# ÇAPAR Hukuk (Çapar Hukuk Bürosu, Çapar Law Office) — Av. Bayram Can Çapar — capar.av.tr: English index

> Denizli attorney Bayram Can Çapar - ÇAPAR HUKUK: Av. Bayram Can Çapar is a lawyer and the founding attorney of ÇAPAR Hukuk (Çapar Hukuk Bürosu, Çapar Law Office) in Denizli (Merkezefendi), Türkiye. capar.av.tr is the official website of Av. Bayram Can Çapar and his law office. He is the author of the site's informative articles in Turkish and English on Turkish law, based on statutory texts and decisions of the Turkish Court of Cassation (Yargıtay) and the Council of State (Danıştay); the site also contains office information and practice areas. At Av. Bayram Can Çapar · ÇAPAR Hukuk, we provide legal services with our team in Denizli, İzmir, Aydın, Muğla, Manisa, Uşak, Afyonkarahisar, Kütahya, İstanbul and Ankara, Türkiye.

This file helps AI assistants and language models understand the site's content and cite it accurately. All articles explain Turkish law (the law of the Republic of Türkiye). The Turkish articles are the originals; the English versions are translations, and in case of any discrepancy the Turkish text prevails. The articles are for general information only; they are not legal advice or a legal opinion, and a lawyer should be consulted for any specific dispute. Statutory provisions are taken from the official Legislation Information System (mevzuat.gov.tr) and court decisions from the Ministry of Justice case-law database (mevzuat.adalet.gov.tr), with links to the source. Unless stated otherwise, the decisions do not concern cases to which the office was a party. When quoting the content, please cite the article's address as the source.

## Av. Bayram Can Çapar

- Who: Av. Bayram Can Çapar is a lawyer and the founding attorney of ÇAPAR Hukuk (Çapar Law Office) in Denizli, Türkiye. He is the author of the articles on this site. "Av." is the Turkish abbreviation of avukat (attorney).
- Also written as: Bayram Can Çapar, Av. Bayram Can ÇAPAR, Avukat Bayram Can Çapar, Bayram Can Capar
- Education: graduate of the Yaşar University Faculty of Law; admitted to the thesis master's programme in Economics at Pamukkale University.
- Expert-witness training: Qualified Calculations in Labour Law, Actuarial Qualified Calculations in Compensation Law and Qualified Calculations in Social Security Law.
- Languages: Turkish; advanced English
- Author page: https://capar.av.tr/yazar/bayram-can-capar
- Profiles: https://www.linkedin.com/in/av-bayram-can-%C3%A7apar/ , https://www.facebook.com/cancpr/

## Courts and Provinces

The office is in Denizli. Below are the competent courts in ten provinces; details and official sources are on the practice area pages.

- Denizli: courthouse Denizli Adliyesi; appeal Denizli Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İzmir Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Denizli Vergi Mahkemesi
- İzmir: courthouse İzmir Adliyesi (Konak); appeal İzmir Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İzmir Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax İzmir Vergi Mahkemeleri
- Aydın: courthouse Aydın Adliyesi; appeal İzmir Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İzmir Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Aydın Vergi Mahkemesi
- Muğla: courthouse Muğla Adliyesi; appeal Denizli Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İzmir Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Muğla Vergi Mahkemesi
- Manisa: courthouse Manisa Adliyesi; appeal İzmir Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İzmir Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Manisa Vergi Mahkemesi
- Uşak: courthouse Uşak Adliyesi; appeal Denizli Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İzmir Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Manisa Vergi Mahkemesi (Uşak dosyaları için)
- Afyonkarahisar: courthouse Afyonkarahisar Adliyesi; appeal Konya Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal Konya Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Konya Vergi Mahkemesi (Afyonkarahisar dosyaları için)
- Kütahya: courthouse Kütahya Adliyesi; appeal Bursa Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal Bursa Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Eskişehir Vergi Mahkemesi (Kütahya dosyaları için)
- İstanbul: courthouse İstanbul Adliyesi (Çağlayan); appeal İstanbul Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal İstanbul Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax İstanbul Vergi Mahkemeleri
- Ankara: courthouse Ankara Adliyesi (Sıhhiye); appeal Ankara Bölge Adliye Mahkemesi; administrative appeal Ankara Bölge İdare Mahkemesi; tax Ankara Vergi Mahkemeleri

## For Turkish citizens abroad

Legal matters in Türkiye for Turkish citizens living abroad, under Turkish law. Av. Bayram Can Çapar handles these matters under a power of attorney; meetings and correspondence can be in Turkish or English. The page is published in Turkish, English and German.

## Articles

### Commercial & Corporate Law

- [Account Closure Notice in Türkiye: How a Bank Accelerates a Loan, Effect of Defective Service (EBL Art. 68/b)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/account-closure-notice): An account closure notice declares the whole bank loan due; if not objected to within one month of notarial service, the account statement becomes final. The article covers its content, service at… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hesap-kat-ihtarnamesi
  - Q: How many days do I have to object to an account closure notice?
    A: As a general rule, one month from receipt of the account statement; if no objection is made within that period, the statement becomes final for enforcement purposes (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 68/b/2; Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/355, K. 2021/1146). If the loan is secured by a mortgage, for the objection to have effect in judgment-type enforcement by realisation of the mortgage it must be made through a notary within eight days of service (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 150/ı).
  - Q: If the account closure notice was never served on me, can the bank claim interest?
    A: It can claim contractual interest, but as a rule it can claim default interest only from the date of enforcement proceedings. In a decision upheld by the 11th Civil Chamber, it was accepted that the failure to duly serve the closure notice on the principal debtor meant that default interest could not be claimed up to the date of enforcement proceedings (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/8214, K. 2022/3568).
  - Q: My address changed; is a closure notice sent to my old address valid?
    A: If the new address in Türkiye was not notified to the bank through a notary, the date on which the notice reaches the old address in the loan agreement is deemed the date of service (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 68/b/1). If you notified the new address to the bank in a later loan agreement, this presumption does not operate; default begins at the end of the payment period given after service at the new address (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/13200, K. 2023/1132).
  - Q: Is the notice invalid if it does not state the objection period?
    A: No. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, art. 68/b contains no rule requiring the objection period to be stated in the closure notice; failure to state the one-month period does not prevent a notice not objected to from being treated as an art. 68/1 document (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/355, K. 2021/1146).
  - Q: Can I never again dispute an account statement I did not object to?
    A: For enforcement purposes the statement becomes final and the bank may seek definitive removal of objection; inaccuracy may be sued for only after the debt has been paid (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 68/b/2). However, if the bank brings an action for annulment of objection, according to the decision upheld by the 11th Civil Chamber the amount in the notice is not final and binding; the claim is determined according to the agreement and the payment record (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/5089, K. 2026/1811).
- [Altered Promissory Note Valid for Original Sum: Turkish Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/altered-promissory-note-valid-for-original-sum-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, a figure and words later added to the amount of a promissory note without the issuer’s signature or initials… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bono-bedeline-sonradan-rakam-eklenirse-tahrifattan-onceki-miktar-gecerli
  - Q: What is alteration of a promissory note under Turkish law?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, it is a change made to the text of a negotiable instrument after it has been delivered to the payee. It may concern entries such as the amount, the maturity date, the date of issue, the place of payment or the name of the payee.
  - Q: If a figure is later added to a promissory note, for what amount is the debtor liable?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, if the addition has not been approved by the issuer’s signature or initials and the alteration is established by an expert report, the note is valid for the amount before the alteration. The objection to the debt is upheld and enforcement is stayed as to the increased part and its ancillaries.
  - Q: Where and when is an objection based on an altered amount raised?
    A: In enforcement specific to negotiable instruments, an objection based on alteration is an objection to the debt; according to the decision, it is notified to the enforcement court by petition within five days of service of the payment order.
  - Q: If the amount in words differs from the amount in figures, which prevails?
    A: Under art. 676/1 of the Turkish Commercial Code, the amount stated in words prevails. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, this rule applies where no alteration has been made to the entries in figures and in words.
  - Q: How is an alteration proven?
    A: In this decision, by expert examination. In the file, the forensic medicine report expressing a definite opinion was relied on, rather than the report that found the addition only “highly probable”.
- [Amended Partial Claim Cannot Be Increased Again on Remand: Turkish Court of Cassation on LLC Withdrawal Suits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/llc-severance-claim-second-amendment-after-reversal-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that, in a withdrawal and severance payment action brought as a partial action with further rights reserved, where the claim… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ayrilma-akcesinde-bozmadan-sonra-bedel-artirimi-ikinci-islahtir
  - Q: How many times can a claim in a partial action be increased by amendment?
    A: Under Turkish law, only once in the same action (art. 176(2) of the Code of Civil Procedure, HMK). In this decision, a later increase in a partial action already amended before the reversal was treated as a second amendment; judgment cannot be given on that basis.
  - Q: Does it matter that the petition is called an “increase of the amount in an action for an unquantified claim”?
    A: In this decision it did not. The Chamber relied on the fact that the action had been brought as a partial action with further rights reserved.
  - Q: Can a party amend its claim after a reversal?
    A: Under art. 177(2) of the HMK, amendment is possible until the end of the evidentiary stage if the first-instance court takes a step relating to that stage after the reversal. In this case, however, the single right of amendment had been used before the reversal.
  - Q: What is the position in actions filed on or after 31.07.2026?
    A: Art. 107 of the HMK was repealed by Law No. 7589 with effect from 31.07.2026; it continues to apply to actions filed before that date. Under art. 109(4), the claim in a partial action may be increased once; the relationship between this increase and amendment was not discussed in the decision.
- [Appeals from Kütahya in Türkiye: Bursa Regional Court of Appeal and Bursa Regional Administrative Court](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/kutahya-appeals-bursa-regional-courts-turkiye): Under Turkish law, appeals against civil and criminal judgments given in Kütahya go to the Bursa Regional Court of Appeal, and appeals in administrative and tax cases go to the Bursa Regional… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kutahya-istinaf-bursa-bolge-adliye-ve-bolge-idare-mahkemesi
  - Q: Which court hears an appeal against a decision of a civil court of first instance in Kütahya, Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, appeals (istinaf) against decisions of the civil, labour, family and enforcement courts in Kütahya are heard by the Bursa Regional Court of Appeal. This link was established by decision No. 703 of the Plenary of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors dated 03.08.2017 and covers the courts in the provincial centre and in the district courthouses. Which civil chamber hears the file is set by the Bursa court's allocation of work; the chamber named in the petition is not binding.
  - Q: Is an appeal petition against a decision given in Kütahya, Türkiye, lodged in Bursa?
    A: No. In civil cases the appeal petition is lodged with the Kütahya court that gave the decision or with another local court (Code of Civil Procedure art. 343/1); the receiving court records it in the regional court of appeal register. In criminal cases it is lodged with the court that gave the judgment, by petition or by a statement to the court clerk (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 273/1). The first-instance court sends the file to Bursa. Posting the petition to Bursa may be risky as regards the time limit.
  - Q: Where and within how many days is an appeal lodged against a decision of the Kütahya Administrative Court in Türkiye?
    A: The appellate court is the Bursa Regional Administrative Court, and the time limit is thirty days from service of the decision (Administrative Procedure Law art. 45/1). Its jurisdiction was redefined by a decision published in the Official Gazette of 11.06.2026 as Bursa, Balıkesir, Bilecik, Eskişehir, Kütahya, Sakarya and Yalova. Decisions in certain cases below the statutory monetary limit are final, and no appeal lies in cases subject to the expedited procedure.
  - Q: Which court hears tax cases from Kütahya, Türkiye, and where is the appeal lodged?
    A: For tax courts, Kütahya province has been within the jurisdiction of the Eskişehir Tax Court since 01.09.2021 under decision No. 607 of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors dated 07.07.2021. Because Eskişehir is within the jurisdiction of the Bursa Regional Administrative Court, appeals against these decisions go to that court; the time limit is thirty days from service.
  - Q: When does the time limit start for an appeal against a criminal judgment given in Kütahya, Türkiye?
    A: The time limit is two weeks from the date the judgment is served together with its reasoning (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 273/1). The appeal is lodged with the Kütahya court that gave the judgment, by petition or by a statement to the court clerk, and the appellate court is the Bursa Regional Court of Appeal. Judgments imposing fifteen years' imprisonment or more are reviewed by the regional court of appeal of its own motion (art. 272/1).
- [Appeals in İstanbul and Ankara, Türkiye: Regional Courts of Appeal and Regional Administrative Courts](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/istanbul-ankara-regional-appeal-courts-jurisdiction): Under Turkish law, this guide explains which regional court of appeal and which regional administrative court hear appeals against civil, criminal, administrative and tax court decisions in İstanbul… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/istanbul-ve-ankara-bolge-adliye-ve-bolge-idare-mahkemeleri-yargi-cevresi
  - Q: Which court hears an appeal against a civil decision given at the Bakırköy or İstanbul Anadolu courthouse in İstanbul, Türkiye?
    A: The İstanbul Regional Court of Appeal. The court's official page gives its jurisdiction as İstanbul, and the Court of Cassation's 5th Criminal Chamber has stated that the Bakırköy and İstanbul Anadolu assize courts are within the same regional court of appeal. The petition is lodged with the court that gave the decision or with another local court, within two weeks of service of the decision.
  - Q: Which provinces are within the jurisdiction of the Ankara Regional Court of Appeal in Türkiye?
    A: Under Decision No. 785 of the Plenary of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors dated 17.04.2025, from 01.05.2025 the provinces of Ankara, Bartın, Çankırı, Eskişehir, Karabük, Kastamonu, Kırıkkale, Kırşehir and Zonguldak. Bartın and Zonguldak were moved by that decision from the Sakarya Regional Court of Appeal to Ankara.
  - Q: Which provinces are within the jurisdiction of the Ankara Regional Administrative Court in Türkiye?
    A: Under Decision No. 1069 of the Plenary of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors dated 06.08.2025: Ankara, Bartın, Bolu, Çankırı, Düzce, Karabük, Kastamonu, Kırıkkale and Zonguldak. The decision applies from the date the newly established regional administrative courts begin operating. This list is not the same as the jurisdiction of the Ankara Regional Court of Appeal.
  - Q: In Türkiye, to which regional administrative court does a person living in İstanbul appeal in an action against an act of a ministry in Ankara?
    A: As a rule, to the Ankara Regional Administrative Court. Under art. 32 of the Administrative Procedure Act, the case is heard by the administrative court in Ankara, where the authority that made the act is located; under art. 45(1), the appeal is made to the regional administrative court to which the deciding court belongs, within thirty days of service. In public servants' cases, the special jurisdiction rules of art. 33 must also be examined.
  - Q: What are the time limits for appeal (istinaf) in İstanbul and Ankara, Türkiye?
    A: In civil cases, two weeks from due service of the decision (Code of Civil Procedure art. 345); in criminal cases, two weeks from service of the judgment with its reasoning (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 273(1)); for administrative and tax court decisions, thirty days from service (Administrative Procedure Act art. 45(1)). Different time limits in special laws are reserved.
- [Arbitration Clauses under Turkish Law: Validity, the Arbitration Objection and Lawsuits Filed in Court](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/arbitration-clause-validity-and-objection-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an arbitration clause must be in writing, the dispute must be arbitrable and, according to the Court of Cassation's Assembly of Civil Chambers, the intention to arbitrate must be… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tahkim-sarti-gecerlilik-kosullari-ve-tahkim-itirazi
  - Q: Can a lawsuit be filed in court in Türkiye when the contract contains an arbitration clause?
    A: Under Turkish law nothing in fact prevents the lawsuit from being filed; however, if the defendant raises the preliminary objection of arbitration in the statement of defence and the arbitration agreement is not null, ineffective or incapable of being performed, the court dismisses the case on procedural grounds (CCP art. 413). An objection not raised in time is not heard; in its 2022 decision the Court of Cassation's Assembly of Civil Chambers stated that in that situation the court must examine the merits.
  - Q: What is the arbitration objection under Turkish law and by when must it be raised?
    A: Under Turkish law, the arbitration objection is the preliminary objection a defendant may raise when a dispute covered by an arbitration agreement is brought before a court (CCP arts. 116, 413). It must be raised in the statement of defence; the time limit for the defence is two weeks from service of the statement of claim, and the court may grant an additional period not exceeding one month (arts. 117, 127). Once the statement of defence has been filed, it cannot be raised even if the period has not expired (art. 131).
  - Q: What are the requirements for an arbitration clause to be valid under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, an arbitration clause must be made in writing (CCP art. 412/3) and the dispute must be arbitrable; disputes arising from rights in rem over immovable property or from matters not subject to the will of the two parties are not arbitrable (art. 408). The Court of Cassation's Assembly of Civil Chambers also requires a clear and definite intention to arbitrate; in its 2026 decision it held invalid a clause providing that disputes would be resolved first by arbitrators and, failing that, by the courts. An agent may not resort to arbitration unless specially authorised (Code of Obligations art. 504/3), and an attorney who has not been expressly authorised may not make an arbitration or arbitrator agreement (CCP art. 74).
  - Q: If the main contract is invalid under Turkish law, is the arbitration clause in it also invalid?
    A: Not automatically. Under Turkish law, no objection may be raised against the arbitration agreement on the ground that the main contract is not valid (CCP art. 412/4), and an arbitrator's ruling that the main contract is null does not automatically render the arbitration agreement null (art. 422/1). According to the 2023 decision of the Court of Cassation's Assembly of Civil Chambers, the arbitration clause is an agreement independent of the main contract. It may still be argued separately that the arbitration agreement itself is null, ineffective or incapable of being performed (art. 413).
  - Q: In which court and within what time limit is an annulment action brought against an arbitral award in Türkiye?
    A: In domestic arbitration subject to the Turkish Code of Civil Procedure, the only remedy against an arbitral award is an annulment action. It is brought before the regional court of appeal of the place of arbitration within one month; the period runs from the date on which the award, or the decision on interpretation, correction or completion, is notified to the parties (art. 439). Bringing the action does not stay enforcement of the award; enforcement may be stayed on request and on condition that security is provided.
- [Artist Contracts and Withdrawal in Türkiye: Management, Concert, Album and Advertising Agreements](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/legal-nature-artist-contracts-right-withdrawal): How management, concert, album, publishing and advertising agreements are characterised as works, mandate or service contracts under Turkish law; exit routes under the Code of Obligations and Law No.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sanatci-sozlesmeleri-hukuki-niteligi-ve-cayma-hakki
  - Q: Can I end my agreement with my manager whenever I want?
    A: If the agreement is a mandate, either party may end it unilaterally at any time (Code of Obligations art. 512), and the Court of Cassation holds clauses hindering this right invalid. However, the party ending it at an inopportune time compensates the other’s loss. Because management agreements are often assessed in practice in terms of just cause and penalty clauses, stating concrete just causes in the written notice is the safe course.
  - Q: What can the artist claim if the organiser cancels the concert?
    A: A concert agreement is usually a works contract. The organiser may terminate before completion but must pay for the part performed and compensate all of the artist’s loss (Code of Obligations art. 484). Graduated cancellation fees pre-estimate that loss and may be reduced if excessive.
  - Q: I am ill and cannot perform at the concert; am I liable?
    A: If the contract was concluded in view of the artist’s personal qualities, it ends automatically when the artist, without fault, loses the capacity to complete the work (Code of Obligations art. 486); in blameless impossibility the parties return what they received. The situation must be notified and documented without delay.
  - Q: Can I withdraw within fourteen days from a concert ticket bought online?
    A: As a rule, no. The Distance Contracts Regulation excludes withdrawal, unless otherwise agreed, for entertainment and leisure services that must be performed on a specific date. If the concert is cancelled or postponed, the refund is claimed under the general rules.
  - Q: The producer to whom I assigned my song never uses it; what can I do?
    A: If the acquirer does not properly exploit the right within the agreed or a reasonable time and your interests are substantially harmed, you may withdraw under Law No. 5846 art. 58. A reasonable period is first granted through a notary; if there is no result, withdrawal is completed by a notarial notice, and the other party has four weeks to contest it.
  - Q: Which court hears disputes arising from artist contracts?
    A: If the action relies on a right or ownership arising from the FSEK, the intellectual and industrial property civil court, or the civil court of first instance where none exists, is competent (Law No. 5846 art. 76). Contract disputes not relying on an FSEK right go to the commercial court if the conditions of a commercial case are met; labour jurisdiction and its mediation rules apply to employment relationships.
- [Assigned Progress Payments: Deductions Must Be Itemised](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/assigned-progress-payments-deductions-itemised-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the 6th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, in an action over progress payment receivables a contractor assigned to a bank, the performance security adjustment… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/temlik-edilen-hakedis-alacagindan-idarenin-kesintileri-tek-tek-hesaplanmali
  - Q: Can progress payment receivables be assigned to a bank?
    A: Under Turkish law, in this case the contractor’s accrued and future receivables from the employer had been assigned to the bank by written deeds of assignment stating the amounts. The contract made assignment subject to the employer’s written permission; the employer had approved it up to a maximum of 70% of each progress payment.
  - Q: Can the employer set off penalties and the security adjustment difference against a progress payment assigned to a bank?
    A: The Chamber did not discuss this separately; but it required the performance security adjustment difference, delay penalty and technical personnel penalty to be calculated one by one and set off against the sum of the contractor’s receivable and the unpaid share of the progress payments owed to the bank.
  - Q: Why could the expert report not be relied on?
    A: The report was held incapable of review or of forming the basis of a judgment because it did not clearly show the amounts of the items set off and did not state the result of the set-off as a plus or minus figure.
  - Q: Can the assignee bank claim more than the contractor could?
    A: The decision reviewed here does not say so expressly. In another case, the same Chamber had stated that an assignment cannot give more than the contractor’s rights and that the employer may raise its defences against the assignee as well; art. 188 of the Turkish Code of Obligations is to the same effect.
  - Q: Is this decision final, and is the case over?
    A: The Court of Cassation decision was given unanimously and with final effect, but it is a reversal. The file has returned to the first-instance court; under the reversal, a supplementary expert report must be obtained and the bank’s claim determined on the result of the set-off.
- [Assignment of Claims (Transfer of Receivables)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/assignment-claims): An analysis of the assignment of claims under Turkish law — its requirements, form and effects, and the assigned debtor's position — covering the written-form requirement, the debtor's defences… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/alacagin-temliki-devri
  - Q: Is the debtor's consent required for an assignment of claims?
    A: No. Under Article 183 TCO, unless prevented by statute, agreement, or the nature of the affair, a creditor may assign a claim to a third party without the debtor's consent. The assignment is a bilateral transaction between the creditor and the assignee; the debtor is not a party to it.
  - Q: Can an assignment of claims be made orally?
    A: No. Under Article 184 TCO, an assignment of a claim is valid only if made in writing; this is a statutory requirement of validity that the court applies of its own motion. By contrast, a mere promise to assign a claim in the future is not subject to any formal requirement.
  - Q: If a debtor, unaware of an assignment, pays the prior creditor, is the debtor released?
    A: Yes. Under Article 186 TCO, where the assignment has not been notified to the debtor, the debtor is released from the obligation by performing in good faith to the prior creditor. Payments made after the debtor has been notified of the assignment, however, do not release the debtor from the obligation owed to the assignee.
  - Q: May the debtor raise the same objections against the new creditor as against the old one?
    A: Yes. Under Article 188 TCO, the debtor may raise against the assignee any defence held against the assignor at the time the assignment became known, and may also, under certain conditions, raise a defence of set-off. This rule reflects the principle that an assignment must not worsen the debtor's legal position.
  - Q: Can a sham (collusive) assignment of claims be set aside?
    A: Yes. An assignment of claims made to place assets beyond creditors' reach may be the target of an action to set aside fraudulent transfers under Articles 277 et seq. of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Code; where the transaction is not grounded in a genuine debt relationship, its invalidity may also be asserted for sham (muvazaa) under general principles. The burden of proving collusion rests with the party alleging it.
  - Q: How is a contractor's personal right assigned to a third party under a build-for-land-share contract?
    A: A contractor's personal rights against the landowner — such as the right to demand cancellation and registration of title to designated units — may be assigned to a third party under Articles 183 et seq. TCO, in writing and without the landowner's consent. The Court of Cassation consistently holds that the written-form requirement suffices for such assignments and that no notarial form is required.
- [Attaching Creditor Cannot Annul or Void a Capital Increase: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/share-attachment-creditor-capital-increase-annulment-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held that a personal creditor who has attached a shareholder’s shares cannot seek the annulment of the general assembly… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/payi-haczettiren-alacakli-sermaye-artirimi-kararinin-iptalini-isteyemez
  - Q: Can a creditor who has attached a shareholder’s shares seek the annulment of a general assembly resolution on a capital increase?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Turkish Court of Cassation, no; the creditor is not a shareholder and is not among the persons listed in art. 446 of the Turkish Commercial Code (TTK).
  - Q: Can the creditor seek a declaration that the resolution is null and void?
    A: In this case, no. According to the Assembly, under art. 353 of the TTK, which applies through the reference in art. 456(4), no decision can be given on the nullity or non-existence of general assembly resolutions on a capital increase.
  - Q: Does the creditor have another remedy?
    A: The Assembly did not consider this. Nor did it address the route pointed out by the Chamber, compensation for the loss arising from the difference in value of the attached shares.
  - Q: Is the decision final, and what happened to the case?
    A: The decision was given by majority and is final. The decision of resistance was reversed and the file was sent to the regional court of appeal.
- [Bank Acceleration of Commercial Loans in Türkiye: Acceleration Clauses, Grace Periods, Wrongful Acceleration](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/bank-accelerate-commercial-loan-before-maturity): A Turkish bank may accelerate a commercial loan before maturity if an event of default in the loan agreement occurs, such as an unpaid instalment. The article covers standard-terms review of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kredinin-vadesinden-once-kat-edilmesi
  - Q: Can the bank accelerate my loan even though I have paid my instalments?
    A: If instalments are paid on time, the bank must show that another event of default provided for in the agreement (for example, cessation of business or bankruptcy proceedings) has occurred (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/5458, K. 2022/2601). Paying instalments irregularly and late may, however, justify acceleration (Istanbul Regional Court of Appeal, 43rd Civil Chamber, E. 2020/190, K. 2021/284).
  - Q: Is it lawful for the bank to demand the whole loan after one or two instalments go unpaid?
    A: Yes, if the agreement provides non-payment of instalments as an event of default. The 11th Civil Chamber upheld a decision accepting that the entire remaining debt may become due (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/5065, K. 2026/2229) and a decision finding lawful the acceleration of a KGF-guaranteed loan after two unpaid instalments (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/6186, K. 2024/7492).
  - Q: What happens if enforcement is started before the 7-day period granted in the acceleration notice expires?
    A: Under the 2021–2025 majority decisions of the 11th Civil Chamber, enforcement before the period expires is contrary to art. 2 of the Civil Code and the action for annulment of objection is dismissed (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/314, K. 2024/3941). The Assembly of Civil Chambers held in 2017, however, that this is relevant only to the calculation of interest (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/815, K. 2017/1007).
  - Q: Can damages be claimed from the bank if the loan was wrongfully accelerated?
    A: They may be claimed under the general rules if unlawfulness, loss and causation are proven (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 112). If the acceleration complied with the agreement, the damages claim is dismissed (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/6186, K. 2024/7492). Refund of excess interest may be claimed separately (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/5506, K. 2021/5763).
  - Q: As a merchant, can I challenge the acceleration clause in a general loan agreement?
    A: Yes. Terms of which the other party was not clearly informed, or which are alien to the nature of the contract, are deemed not written; provisions that are contrary to good faith and aggravate the other party’s position may not be included (Turkish Code of Obligations arts. 21, 25). The 11th Civil Chamber has accepted that this review also applies to merchants and that acceleration clauses and clauses on collection after acceleration must be examined within it (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/4499, K. 2022/8102).
- [Bank Loan Promissory Note Is a Security Note: Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/bank-loan-promissory-note-security-note-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, a promissory note bearing the words ‘value received in cash’ and signed in favour of a bank on the same day… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kredi-gunu-bankaya-ayni-tutarda-imzalanan-bono-teminat-senedidir
  - Q: Can a promissory note given to a bank be a security note under Turkish law if it does not say “security”?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, it can. If the note was issued on the same day, for the same amount and between the same persons as the loan agreement, and the agreement authorises the bank to take instruments, the note is a security note for the loan.
  - Q: Does the notation “value received in cash” change the result?
    A: In this decision it did not. According to the Assembly, banks cannot give out money outside a credit relationship; the payee bank’s cash claim can only arise from the credit relationship.
  - Q: What can a debtor pursued on a security note raise?
    A: If the note is in the hands of the payee, the debtor may raise as a personal defence that the conditions of the security have not arisen or that the claim exceeds the secured debt; if the note has been endorsed, it may raise this against a holder who acted knowingly to the debtor’s detriment while aware of its security character.
  - Q: Can a bank enforce a security note without accelerating the account?
    A: According to the majority of the Assembly, if the agreement contains a power to terminate without notice, default begins on the date of enforcement; a notice sent later is not a waiver of that power. According to the dissent, no claim had fallen due at the date of enforcement.
  - Q: Did this decision end the dispute?
    A: No. The decision is final, but the file was sent back to the regional court of appeal; according to the Assembly, the debt position must be assessed on the basis that default began on the date of enforcement.
- [Binding (Handcuff) Contracts for Artists in Türkiye: Exclusivity, Validity Limits, Ways Out (TMK Art. 23)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/binding-contracts-artists): Limits on the validity of long-term, exclusive artist contracts under Turkish law: personality rights, nullity, exploitation, standard terms and future works; Court of Cassation decisions on handcuff… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sanatcilarin-baglanma-sozlesmeleri
  - Q: Is an eight- or ten-year artist contract valid?
    A: Duration alone is not a ground of invalidity. But if a long term combines with a high management share, a termination right for the company only, costs charged to the artist and a high penalty so as to restrict the artist’s economic and artistic freedom excessively, the contract may be held void under Civil Code art. 23 and Code of Obligations art. 27. The Court of Cassation upheld a judgment declaring such an eight-year contract entirely void.
  - Q: What does an “all songs I will write in the future” clause mean?
    A: Assignment of a work not yet written is void, but an undertaking to assign it in the future is valid. Either party may terminate such an undertaking covering all, or a type, of future works without giving reasons, with effect one year after notice (Law No. 5846 art. 50).
  - Q: The producer releases no albums but will not let me go; what can I do?
    A: A producer’s failure to make the albums promised, or to start preparing them at all, has been accepted as just cause in decisions upheld by the Court of Cassation. After granting the producer a reasonable period through a notary without result, the contract may be terminated; for authors, the FSEK right of withdrawal should also be considered.
  - Q: I signed at seventeen with my parents’ consent; am I bound?
    A: A contract made with the legal representative’s consent is, as a rule, binding (Civil Code art. 16). But consent does not exempt the content from review: terms excessively restricting freedom are void even if made on a minor’s behalf, and inexperience is assessed separately for exploitation. Legal advice should be taken before performing the contract after reaching majority.
  - Q: Is it risky to sign with a new producer while my current contract runs?
    A: Yes. Unless the current contract is void or terminated, a new exclusive contract increases the risk of penalties and damages, and the new producer may face an unfair competition claim for inducing a breach of the existing contract. The status of the current contract should be clarified first.
  - Q: Is the whole contract void, or only some terms?
    A: Partial nullity is the rule: invalid terms are removed and the rest stands. But if it is clear that the contract would not have been concluded without those terms, the whole contract is void (Code of Obligations art. 27). In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, the whole contract was held void because it had no meaning without the excessively restrictive terms.
- [Board Deadlock in Turkish Joint-Stock Companies: Remedies, Quorum, Void Resolutions, Court-Appointed Trustee](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/board-deadlock-joint-stock-company): In a Turkish joint-stock company the board, unless the articles of association provide a stricter rule, meets with a majority of its total membership and decides by a majority of the members present… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirkette-yonetim-kurulu-karar-alamiyorsa
  - Q: With how many members does the board meet and decide?
    A: Unless the articles provide a stricter rule, it meets with a majority of its total membership and decides by a majority of the members present (TCC art. 390/1).
  - Q: What happens if the board’s votes are tied?
    A: The matter is deferred to the next meeting; if there is a tie again, the proposal is deemed rejected (TCC art. 390/3). In a limited company, by contrast, the chair has the casting vote (TCC art. 624/3).
  - Q: Can the board take a decision without a meeting?
    A: If no member requests a meeting, a written proposal made to all members may be adopted with the written approval of a majority of the total membership (TCC art. 390/4).
  - Q: What can a shareholder do if the board cannot meet at all?
    A: If the board is continuously unable to meet, a quorum cannot be formed or there is no board, a single shareholder may call the general assembly with court permission; the court’s decision is final (TCC art. 410/2).
  - Q: What is required for a trustee to be appointed to a joint-stock company?
    A: The company must have been left without its necessary organs and its management must be impossible to secure in another way (Civil Code art. 427/4). According to the Court of Cassation, a board whose term has expired can still call the general assembly, so the company is not automatically organless (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2012/13135, K. 2014/3515).
- [Brokerage (Commission) Claims: The Brokerage Contract, Real Estate Commission and Penalty Clauses](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/brokerage-claims): The brokerage contract and the written-form requirement in real estate brokerage (TCO art. 520), conditions for earning the commission and causation (art. 521), determination (art. 522) and reduction… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/komisyon-tellallik-alacagi
  - Q: When does a real estate agent earn the commission?
    A: Three conditions must coexist: a valid brokerage contract (written, for real estate); conclusion of the main contract (sale, lease etc.); and causation between the broker's activity and that conclusion. Performance of the main contract is not required; its valid conclusion suffices.
  - Q: Can a commission be claimed on an oral agreement?
    A: No. A brokerage contract concerning immovables is invalid unless made in writing (TCO art. 520/3); written form is a condition of validity and the document must bear the obligor's signature. E-mail exchanges do not replace a signed contract. Non-real-estate brokerage (e.g. share transfers) is free of form, but the relationship and the activity must be proven.
  - Q: The buyer viewed the property with the agent but bought directly from the owner; is commission payable?
    A: Yes, if a signed viewing form exists. The Court of Cassation holds the bypassing buyer liable under the agreed alternative penalty clause (6% in the cited decision), without requiring the agent to be separately authorised by the owner, and a merchant debtor cannot plead that the penalty is excessive.
  - Q: How is the fee determined if no rate was agreed?
    A: By tariff and, failing that, by custom (TCO art. 522). An excessive agreed fee may be reduced equitably by the judge on the debtor's request (TCO art. 525).
  - Q: If the sale falls through, can the agent claim a fee or expenses?
    A: As a rule, no fee: the broker earns the fee only if a contract is concluded as a result of their activity. If reimbursement of expenses was agreed, expenses are payable even without conclusion (TCO art. 521/3). Art. 521/1 is not mandatory and may be contracted around.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for commission claims?
    A: Five years for claims arising from brokerage contracts (TCO art. 147/5); the commercial brokerage fee is excluded and is subject to the general ten-year period (TCO art. 146).
  - Q: May the agent take commission from both sides?
    A: A broker who, contrary to their obligation, acts in the counterparty's interest or takes a fee promise from the counterparty in bad faith forfeits both fee and expenses (TCO art. 523). Transparent, impartial dual intermediation with both parties' knowledge and consent falls outside this rule.
- [Commercial Books: Retention Period and Certificate of Loss](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/commercial-books-retention-period-loss-certificate): Under Turkish law, the ten-year retention period for commercial books under Turkish Commercial Code art. 82 and the five-year period under Tax Procedure Law art. 253; the conditions for obtaining a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-defterlerin-saklanma-suresi-ve-zayi-belgesi
  - Q: How many years must commercial books be kept?
    A: Under Turkish law, article 82 of the Turkish Commercial Code requires commercial books, inventories and financial statements, commercial correspondence and the documents on which entries are based to be kept for ten years. The period starts at the end of the calendar year in which the entry was made or the document came into being.
  - Q: What is the retention period for books under the Tax Procedure Law?
    A: Under art. 253 of the Tax Procedure Law, those obliged to keep books must keep their books and documents for five years, starting from the calendar year following the year to which they relate. Even when this period has expired, the ten-year obligation under the Turkish Commercial Code continues.
  - Q: What is the time limit for obtaining a certificate of loss?
    A: The merchant must apply to the court within thirty days from the date on which it learns of the loss. The Court of Cassation treats this as a peremptory time limit; it runs not from the date of the event but from the date the loss became known. The fifteen-day period mentioned in older decisions belongs to the previous text of the article.
  - Q: Can a certificate of loss be obtained if the books are lost?
    A: The Code lists disasters such as fire, flood and earthquake, and theft. According to regional courts of appeal, the list is not exhaustive; however, it must be proven that the loss occurred for a reason outside the merchant’s control and without its fault. In one decision, an unexplained loss during a move or a change of partnership was not considered sufficient.
  - Q: In which court is the action for a certificate of loss brought?
    A: It is brought without a respondent before the commercial court of first instance of the place where the commercial enterprise is located (TCC arts. 5/1 and 82/7). An appeal (istinaf) lies against the first-instance decision; according to the Court of Cassation, no appeal on points of law lies against the regional court of appeal’s decision on this matter.
  - Q: Is a certificate of loss sufficient in a tax audit?
    A: Not on its own. According to the Council of State, whether there was force majeure is assessed separately in each case on the basis of the certificate of loss and the other evidence; the existence of a certificate of loss does not by itself prove that the books were lost, and its absence does not show that they were not.
- [Commercial Suretyship in Turkish Law: Periods, Duration and Grounds of Invalidity](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/commercial-suretyship): Commercial suretyship in Turkish law: form and spousal-consent rules by period, the ten-year limit, termination, grounds of invalidity and case law. Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-kefalet-donemleri-sureklilik-ve-gecersizlik
  - Q: What must the surety write in his own handwriting in a suretyship contract?
    A: For suretyships concluded on or after 01.07.2012, the maximum amount, the date and, if he is to be a joint and several surety, that capacity (TCO art. 583). If written by someone else, the suretyship is invalid even with a genuine signature, and the court takes this into account of its own motion.
  - Q: Do I need my spouse’s consent to stand surety for my company’s loan?
    A: Not if you give it as a partner or manager in connection with the company and it was concluded on or after 11.04.2013 (TCO art. 584/3). For suretyships between 01.07.2012 and 10.04.2013, consent was required for partners as well. The exception concerns consent only; the handwriting, amount and date requirements still apply.
  - Q: Does the ten-year rule apply to suretyships before 2012?
    A: Yes. A natural person’s suretyship lapses automatically ten years after the contract (TCO art. 598), and termination is governed by the TCO for older suretyships too. If the ten years had expired on 01.07.2012, the creditor had to start enforcement within a one-year additional period (Law No. 6101 art. 5/2).
  - Q: Can a surety be liable for more than the suretyship limit?
    A: The surety is in all cases liable only up to the maximum amount (TCO art. 589). Liability above it can arise only for interest from the surety’s own default, which requires a separate notice. A higher credit limit does not raise the suretyship limit unless the surety accepts it in his handwriting.
  - Q: Are a bank letter of guarantee and a “surety” signature on a promissory note suretyships?
    A: No. A bank letter of guarantee is a guarantee contract and, since TCO art. 603 covers only natural persons, it is not subject to the handwriting and spousal-consent rules. A “surety” signature on a promissory note is, as a rule, an aval, to which the spousal-consent rules do not apply according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers. A guarantee given by a natural person is, however, subject to the form of suretyship.
- [Company Manager Transfers Funds to a Personal Account in Türkiye: Liability Action, Evidence, Criminal Aspects](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/company-manager-transfers-company-money): If a company manager transfers company money to a personal account without a basis in the company’s interest, the manager is liable to the company, the shareholders and the company’s creditors for… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sirket-muduru-sirket-parasini-kendi-hesabina-aktarirsa
  - Q: What happens if a manager transfers company money to his own account?
    A: If the manager has caused loss to the company by breaching his statutory obligations through fault, he must compensate that loss (Commercial Code art. 553/1; for limited companies Commercial Code art. 644/1-a). Depending on the facts, the act may also be examined as the offence of breach of trust (Law No. 5237 art. 155).
  - Q: Can a partner sue the manager for compensation?
    A: Yes. The company and each partner may claim compensation for the company’s loss, but a partner may only demand payment to the company, not to himself (Commercial Code art. 555/1).
  - Q: Is a general assembly resolution required for an action on behalf of the company?
    A: The Court of Cassation treats a general assembly (partners’ meeting) resolution as a condition of a liability action brought by the company against its manager. If there is none, the court grants a peremptory time limit to cure the defect, failing which the action is dismissed on procedural grounds (Code of Civil Procedure art. 115/2).
  - Q: When does the manager’s liability become time-barred?
    A: The claim becomes time-barred two years after the loss and the liable person are learned and in any event five years after the act causing the loss; if the act is a crime subject to a longer limitation period under the Turkish Penal Code, that period applies (Commercial Code art. 560).
  - Q: What if the manager argues that the money was his salary?
    A: Setting managers’ remuneration is a non-delegable power of the general assembly (Commercial Code art. 616/1-f). The Court of Cassation held that, without a general assembly resolution, not the entire amount withdrawn as salary but only the part exceeding the salary of a person in the same position in a similar company should be treated as loss.
- [Current Account Contracts under Turkish Law: Written Form, Interest, Balance, Limitation (TCC Arts. 89-101)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/current-account-contract): A current account contract is an agreement under which mutual receivables are entered item by item and only the balance resulting when the account is closed may be claimed; it is invalid unless made… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/cari-hesap-sozlesmesi
  - Q: Does a current account statement prove the debt?
    A: Not on its own; it is a printout from the creditor’s own records, and without a written contract the deemed-acceptance rule for the balance does not apply either. The claim is proved by invoices, delivery records and properly kept commercial books; if the debtor admits the relationship and pleads payment, the debtor must prove payment.
  - Q: Must a current account contract be in writing?
    A: Yes; under TCC art. 89/2 a current account contract not made in writing is invalid. Without a written contract the TCC current account rules do not apply, even if the parties call it a “current account”.
  - Q: When does limitation start for a current account claim?
    A: Under a written current account contract, actions for the balance and interest are time-barred five years after the contract ends (TCC art. 101). Without a written contract, limitation runs separately for each receivable from the date it fell due.
  - Q: From when does interest run on the balance?
    A: Under a written contract, where the conditions in TCC art. 8 are met, interest runs on the balance from the date it is determined and entered in the account (TCC art. 95). Without a written contract, unless otherwise agreed, interest on a commercial debt runs from the due date or, if there is none, from the date of notice (TCC art. 10).
  - Q: Which court hears current account disputes, and is mediation mandatory?
    A: Actions arising from a current account contract are commercial cases even if the parties are not merchants and are heard by the commercial court of first instance (TCC art. 4, TCC art. 5). In actions for a sum of money and actions for annulment of objection, applying to a mediator first is a procedural prerequisite (TCC art. 5/A).
- [Default Interest on Turkish Commercial Loans: Determination, Contractual Rate Plus 100%, Central Bank Rate](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/default-interest-rate-commercial-loan-determined): On Turkish commercial loans default interest follows the contract, without the art. 120 cap, on the lower of the bank’s actual and TCMB-reported rates. The article covers Law No. 3095 art. 2 where no… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-kredide-temerrut-faizi-orani
  - Q: What is the default interest rate on a commercial loan in Türkiye?
    A: The law sets no fixed default rate for commercial loans; the rate is determined by the agreement (Turkish Commercial Code art. 8/1). For example, decisions show 39% applied to a loan with a 26% contractual rate and a 50% uplift (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/5009, K. 2025/3418), and 41% applied to a loan with a 20.50% contractual rate actually applied and a 100% uplift (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/6371, K. 2024/7874).
  - Q: Can default interest on a commercial loan exceed the cap in art. 120 of the Code of Obligations?
    A: Yes. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the caps in arts. 88 and 120 of the Code of Obligations do not apply to commercial loans; the decision was taken by majority (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/1650, K. 2019/507). Consumer loans fall outside this freedom (Turkish Commercial Code art. 8/3).
  - Q: What happens if the loan agreement does not state a default rate?
    A: In commercial matters the advance rate may be claimed even without an agreement if it exceeds statutory interest; if the contractual rate is higher, default interest cannot be lower than the contractual rate (Law No. 3095 art. 2/2-3). Where the rates in the agreement are left blank, the contractual rate the bank applies to the same type of loan is also applied as default interest (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/9064, K. 2023/3414).
  - Q: Is a surety liable for the higher default rate in the payment schedule?
    A: Not if the surety did not sign the payment schedule; although the schedule stated 39%, the calculation for sureties who had not signed it followed the default interest clause of the general loan agreement (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/430, K. 2024/3891).
  - Q: Is BSMV added to default interest?
    A: Judgments are seen to award BSMV at 5% of the interest together with default interest (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/5009, K. 2025/3418). If the bank did not claim BSMV in the enforcement proceedings, the court does not add it to the judgment (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/6371, K. 2024/7874).
- [Defective Delivery in a Turnkey (Works) Contract](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/defective-delivery-turnkey-contract): Defective delivery in a turnkey works contract: the definition of the works contract (TCO Art. 470) and the lump-sum price (TCO Art. 480), the apparent/hidden defect distinction, the duty to inspect… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anahtar-teslim-eser-sozlesmesinde-ayipli-teslim
  - Q: A defect appeared in my turnkey construction; what rights can I use?
    A: According to the gravity of the defect you may use one of three optional rights: rescission if the work is defective to a degree that makes it unusable or impossible to be compelled to accept; keeping the work and a price reduction in proportion to the defect; or (if it does not require excessive expense) free repair at the contractor's cost. Your right to damages under the general provisions is also reserved (TCO Art. 475). If the work was made on your immovable and removal would cause excessive harm, rescission cannot be used; in practice the repair cost or a price reduction comes to the fore.
  - Q: What is the difference between an apparent and a hidden defect; when must I notify?
    A: An apparent defect is one visible by ordinary inspection at delivery and must be notified within a reasonable time. A hidden defect is one not detectable by simple inspection that surfaces later; it must be notified without delay (immediately) once it surfaces. If no notice is given, the work may be deemed accepted with that defect (TCO Arts. 474, 477).
  - Q: How long do I have to sue for a hidden defect in a building?
    A: For immovable structures, defective-work actions are subject to a five-year limitation from delivery; for works other than immovable structures, two years. If the contractor is at gross fault, the period is twenty years regardless of the nature of the work (TCO Art. 478).
  - Q: The contractor says I “did not notify the defect in time”; do I lose my right entirely?
    A: Failure to give timely notice is a defence; unless the contractor expressly raises it, the court does not consider it of its own motion. Moreover, if the contract provides a warranty period, remedy may be claimed for defects arising within that period without the notice requirement. Still, to avoid missing deadlines, notifying the defect in writing (notary/registered mail) is the safest course.
  - Q: We agreed a lump-sum price; the contractor now wants extra payment — must I pay?
    A: As a rule, no. Under a lump-sum price the contractor cannot demand an increase even if the work required more labour/expense than foreseen. However, if extraordinary circumstances unforeseeable at the outset rendered performance excessively difficult, the contractor may ask the court for adaptation or, failing that, rescission/termination (TCO Art. 480).
  - Q: If the defect stems from my own instruction, is the contractor still liable?
    A: If the defect arose from your instruction despite the contractor's express warning, or is otherwise attributable to you, you cannot exercise your defect-based rights (TCO Art. 476). This is why it matters that the contractor warned the owner in writing against the problematic instruction/material.
- [Denizli Regional Court of Appeal, Türkiye: Jurisdiction (Denizli, Muğla, Uşak), Appeal Procedure, Time Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/denizli-regional-court-appeal-jurisdiction-turkiye): Since 02.09.2024, the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal has heard appeals against civil and criminal judgments given in Denizli, Muğla and Uşak. This article explains, under Turkish law and with… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/denizli-bolge-adliye-mahkemesi-yargi-cevresi-istinaf
  - Q: Where is an appeal lodged against a decision of a court in Muğla?
    A: The appellate court is the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal, but the petition is not taken to Denizli. In civil cases it is lodged with the court that gave the decision or another local court, in criminal cases with the trial court; the first-instance court sends the file on.
  - Q: What is the time limit for appealing a civil judgment given in Uşak?
    A: Two weeks from proper service of the judgment (Code of Civil Procedure art. 345); different periods in special statutes are reserved.
  - Q: Can every decision be appealed?
    A: No. Decisions in property disputes not exceeding the monetary threshold are final; actions for non-pecuniary damages are excepted. According to the 4th Civil Chamber of the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal, the decision date governs for decisions given before 04.06.2025 and the filing date for decisions given after that date.
  - Q: In a criminal case, does the appeal period start on the day the verdict is pronounced?
    A: No. For decisions given on or after 01.06.2024, the period is two weeks from service of the judgment with its reasoning (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 273(1)).
  - Q: Do decisions from Aydın, Manisa, Afyonkarahisar or Kütahya also go to the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal?
    A: No. Aydın and Manisa go to the İzmir, Afyonkarahisar to the Konya and Kütahya to the Bursa Regional Court of Appeal.
  - Q: Is an appeal against a decision of the administrative court in Denizli lodged with the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal?
    A: No. Appeals against administrative and tax court decisions are lodged within thirty days of service with the regional administrative court (Administrative Procedure Act art. 45(1)); for Denizli, Muğla and Uşak that is the İzmir Regional Administrative Court.
- [Dissolution and Liquidation of a Simple Partnership: Liquidation Suit, Attachment (Turkish CO Arts. 620-645)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/dissolution-liquidation-simple-partnership): A guide to the dissolution and liquidation of a simple partnership under Turkish law: the definition and elements in Code of Obligations art. 620, joint ownership and the partners’ joint and several… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/adi-ortakligin-feshi-ve-tasfiyesi
  - Q: In which cases does a simple partnership end?
    A: On the grounds listed in Code of Obligations art. 639: achievement or impossibility of the purpose; death, guardianship, bankruptcy or enforcement against a partner’s liquidation share (unless the contract provides for continuation); unanimous decision; expiry of the term; notice of termination; and a court decision for just cause. In a partnership of indefinite term, notice must be given six months in advance (Code of Obligations art. 640).
  - Q: Who carries out the liquidation of a simple partnership?
    A: The partners may liquidate by agreement and appoint a liquidator; if they cannot agree, the judge appoints one (Code of Obligations art. 644). If a liquidation action is brought, the court itself carries out the liquidation and renders judgment on the basis of the final balance sheet (Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/1019, K. 2020/25).
  - Q: Can a partner claim a partnership receivable alone before liquidation?
    A: As a rule, no. Partnership receivables belong jointly to all partners; actions and enforcement proceedings must be brought together, and the consent of the partners who did not sue must be obtained or they must be joined (3rd Civil Chamber, E. 2024/1200, K. 2025/2619). A claim for a share against the other partner also covers dissolution and liquidation.
  - Q: Can receivables of the partnership be attached for a partnership debt?
    A: The 2021 decision of the Assembly of Civil Chambers allowed this, whereas its final 2025 decision held that receivables held jointly by the partnership cannot be attached directly and that the debtor partner’s liquidation share may be attached (Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2024/787, K. 2025/367). The most recent Assembly decision should be checked.
  - Q: Which court hears an action for the dissolution and liquidation of a simple partnership?
    A: If the partners are not merchants, the civil court of first instance (Code of Civil Procedure art. 2; Court of Cassation 20th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/1409, K. 2020/1604); for merchant partners, jurisdiction is assessed separately. Appellate review falls to the 3rd Civil Chamber under the division of work of 26.06.2025, as stated in a referral decision (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/5337, K. 2025/6096).
  - Q: How is a simple partnership proved if there is no written contract?
    A: The contract is not subject to any form requirement; however, the burden of proof lies on the party asserting the partnership, and the documentary evidence rule applies to transactions exceeding the monetary threshold (Code of Civil Procedure art. 200). Witnesses may be heard with the express consent of the other party, and no documentary proof is required for transactions between siblings, spouses, descendants and ascendants (Code of Civil Procedure art. 203).
- [Down Payment Refund in Turkish Financial Leasing: Lessee's Recovery of Payments to the Seller on Termination](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/refund-down-payment-financial-leasing): A down payment the lessee paid directly to the seller, if not financed by the lessor, is recoverable once the asset is returned (Court of Cassation, 2025). The decision concerns a 2007 contract under… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/finansal-kiralamada-pesinat-iadesi
  - Q: If the leasing contract is terminated, can I get my down payment back?
    A: It depends on to whom and on what basis the payment was made. According to the Court of Cassation’s 11th Civil Chamber, a price the lessee paid directly to the seller and the lessor did not finance can be claimed from the lessor once the asset is returned to the lessor (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/72, K. 2025/900). If no valid contract was ever formed, a down payment paid to the lessor can be reclaimed as unjust enrichment (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/8251, K. 2022/2555).
  - Q: If the asset is never delivered, from whom is the down payment paid to the seller claimed?
    A: If the asset was not delivered because of the lessor’s fault or negligence, arts. 123, 125 and 126 of the Turkish Code of Obligations apply (Law No. 6361 art. 25). In a case where the lessor had paid the seller on time and was not, under the contract, responsible for delivery, the Court of Cassation upheld the dismissal of the action against the financial leasing company (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/2600, K. 2020/5012). If the parties signed a release and termination protocol, the court must assess it (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/6932, K. 2025/6071).
  - Q: Does Law No. 6361 contain an article on refunding the down payment?
    A: No. The Law’s financial leasing provisions do not separately regulate a payment made by the lessee to the seller; where there is no provision the general provisions apply (Law No. 6361 art. 2/6), and an enrichment whose cause has not materialised or has ceased to exist must be returned (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 77).
  - Q: Within how many years must an action for the refund of a down payment be brought?
    A: If the claim rests on unjust enrichment, it becomes time-barred two years after the date on which the right to reclaim was learned and in any case ten years after the date on which the enrichment occurred (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 82/1). In commercial cases for a sum of money, applying to a mediator before bringing the action is a condition of action (Commercial Code art. 5/A/1).
  - Q: Which law governs a leasing contract made in 2007?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, disputes concerning contracts made before 13.12.2012, when Law No. 6361 entered into force, are governed by the repealed Law No. 3226 and the earlier regulations (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/1647, K. 2020/88). The Court of Cassation’s 19th Civil Chamber treated a judgment based on an expert report calculating under art. 33 of Law No. 6361 for such a contract as a ground for reversal (Court of Cassation, 19th Civil Chamber, E. 2016/7173, K. 2017/2999).
- [Enforcement File Renewal Does Not Interrupt Limitation: Turkish Court of Cassation on the 3-Year Note Bar](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/promissory-note-limitation-enforcement-file-renewal-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, if three years pass after an enforcement based on a promissory note has become final without any enforcement… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bonoya-dayali-icra-takibinde-dosya-yenileme-zamanasimini-kesmez
  - Q: What is the limitation period in enforcement based on a promissory note?
    A: Claims against the maker of a promissory note become time-barred three years from maturity (Turkish Commercial Code arts. 749(1), 778(1)(h), 779(1)).
  - Q: Does a request to renew the enforcement file interrupt limitation?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, it does not; a renewal request is not an enforcement act aimed at keeping the proceedings going. If the renewal petition also contains a request that advances the proceedings, such as attachment, limitation may be interrupted by that request.
  - Q: How is limitation raised after the enforcement has become final?
    A: The debtor applies to the enforcement court for a stay of enforcement; the court decides on the basis of official documents (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law arts. 71(2), 33/a). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, this application is not bound by a time limit.
  - Q: When does an attachment report count as a final certificate of insufficiency?
    A: If no attachable asset is found, the attachment report has the effect of a certificate of insufficiency (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 105(1)). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the debtor must also have no attachable assets outside the place of attachment.
- [Expulsion and Withdrawal of Partners: Limited and Joint-Stock Companies under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/expulsion-withdrawal-partners-commercial-companies): Expulsion and withdrawal of partners in Turkish commercial companies: withdrawal from an LLC for just cause (TCC art. 638), joining a withdrawal (art. 639), expulsion by general assembly resolution… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticaret-sirketlerinde-ortakliktan-cikarma
  - Q: Can one LLC partner have another partner expelled?
    A: No. The expulsion action for just cause may be brought only by the company itself (TCC art. 640/3); an action brought by one partner against another is dismissed for lack of standing. The Court of Cassation applies this rule even in two-partner companies. The Constitutional Court has annulled the general assembly resolution requirement for two-partner limited companies (E.2025/128, K.2025/273; Official Gazette 17.03.2026); the Code does not separately regulate how the action is to be brought in such companies after the annulment.
  - Q: Is a general assembly resolution required for the expulsion action?
    A: As a rule, yes. Applying to the court for a partner’s expulsion is a non-delegable power of the general assembly (TCC art. 616/1-h), and a resolution to that effect is a procedural prerequisite; if the defect is not cured within the definitive period granted, the action is dismissed. For two-partner limited companies, however, the Constitutional Court has annulled the provisions establishing this requirement (E.2025/128, K.2025/273; Official Gazette 17.03.2026).
  - Q: How can a partner leave the company voluntarily?
    A: By exercising a withdrawal right granted in the articles, or, where none exists, by bringing a withdrawal action for just cause (TCC art. 638/2). Where share transfer is prohibited or consent is withheld, the right to withdraw for just cause is preserved (art. 595/5).
  - Q: How is the severance payment calculated and when is it paid?
    A: It corresponds to the real value of the capital share — not book value — determined with regard to the company’s assets and going-concern value (TCC art. 641). Where departure is ordered by the court, it falls due upon finality of the judgment; the unpaid part ranks after all creditors of the company (art. 642/3).
  - Q: Is expulsion possible in a joint-stock company?
    A: There is no general withdrawal/expulsion mechanism. In a dissolution action for just cause (TCC art. 531) the court may, instead of dissolution, order the claimant shareholder’s buy-out at the real value of their shares as at the date closest to the judgment — provided just cause is established.
  - Q: What counts as just cause?
    A: Case law has accepted: failure to hold general assemblies for many years, violation of information and participation rights, never distributing profits, and the irreparable collapse of trust between partners. Abstract discord and facts arising from the claimant’s own fault do not suffice.
- [Expulsion from Cooperative Membership in Turkish Law: Notice Requirements, Competent Body and Annulment Action](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/expulsion-from-cooperative-membership): A guide to expulsion from cooperative membership in Turkish law: grounds that must be expressly set out in the articles of association and the expulsion procedure (Cooperatives Law art. 16), two… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kooperatif-uyeliginden-ihrac
  - Q: On what grounds may a cooperative expel a member?
    A: Only on grounds expressly set out in the articles of association (Cooperatives Law art. 16/1). For unpaid dues the two-request procedure of Cooperatives Law art. 27 applies; where membership conditions are lost, the member must also be given an appropriate period (Court of Cassation 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/112, K. 2024/347).
  - Q: How many notices are required before expulsion for unpaid dues?
    A: Two requests: the first with an appropriate period, the second with a one-month period (Cooperatives Law art. 27). Granting 30 days instead of one month in the second notice was held to breach a mandatory provision (Court of Cassation 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/384, K. 2023/526); the debt in the notices must be actual and consistent and the sanction clearly stated (Court of Cassation 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/1044, K. 2022/1890).
  - Q: What is the time limit for challenging an expulsion decision?
    A: Three months from due service of the decision through a notary (Cooperatives Law art. 16/3). The one-month period for general assembly resolutions does not apply to expulsion decisions, and service at an address other than the one notified by the member does not start the period (Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2013/1522, K. 2015/1196).
  - Q: Can the board of directors decide on expulsion?
    A: As a rule the general assembly decides on the board’s proposal; the articles of association may also authorise the board (Cooperatives Law art. 16/2). In that case the general assembly’s power does not remove the board’s power (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2009/4119, K. 2010/10268).
  - Q: Can an expelled member recover the payments made?
    A: Yes; the rights are calculated, excluding reserves, on the balance sheet of the year of departure and become time-barred after five years (Cooperatives Law art. 17). The balance remaining after deduction of the share of expenses may be claimed, and late-payment interest may not be set off (Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/855, K. 2021/281). Applying to a mediator before suing for the claim is a condition of the action (Commercial Code art. 5/A).
  - Q: Can a member who has ignored the cooperative for a long time have the expulsion annulled?
    A: An annulment action by a member who for a long time paid no dues and had no contact with the cooperative has been held contrary to the rule of honesty (Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2023/1043, K. 2024/537). Whether the period is long enough depends on the facts; in one case more than four years between expulsion and action was not considered sufficient (Court of Cassation 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/1044, K. 2022/1890).
