- [Extraordinary General Assembly in a Limited Company: Who Sets the Agenda? (TCC Arts. 617, 411-413, 416)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/extraordinary-general-assembly-agenda-rule-limited): In a Turkish limited company the managers call the general assembly, and the managers making the call set the agenda. The partners’ right to demand an extraordinary meeting and add agenda items… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirkette-olaganustu-genel-kurul-ve-gundemle-baglilik
  - Q: Who calls the general assembly of a limited company?
    A: The managers call the general assembly (TCC art. 617/1). The call is made at least fifteen days before the meeting; the articles of association may extend this period or shorten it to ten days.
  - Q: Can partners demand an extraordinary general assembly?
    A: Yes. Partners representing at least one tenth of the share capital may ask the managers, through a notary, to call a meeting (TCC art. 411); if the request is rejected or not answered positively within seven business days, they may apply to the commercial court (TCC art. 412).
  - Q: What happens if the manager does not put an item on the agenda?
    A: The partners may apply to the commercial court, which may appoint a trustee to arrange the agenda and make the call (TCC art. 412). A culpable breach causing loss may also give rise to the manager’s liability (TCC art. 644/1-a, TCC art. 553/1).
  - Q: Can a resolution be adopted on a matter not on the agenda?
    A: As a rule, no; matters not on the agenda cannot be discussed or resolved, statutory exceptions being reserved (TCC art. 413/2). At a general assembly without a call attended by all partners, items may be added to the agenda unanimously (TCC art. 416/2).
  - Q: Is a resolution outside the agenda invalid?
    A: In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, a resolution outside the agenda was treated as annullable and subject to the three-month period (TCC art. 445); there is also a view that it is void. Resolutions of a general assembly convened by an unauthorised person are non-existent.
- [FX and FX-Indexed Commercial Loans in Türkiye: Exchange Rate on Acceleration and Enforcement (TCO Art. 99)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/foreign-currency-loan-acceleration-exchange-rate): If an FX loan is not paid when due, a Turkish bank may, absent an effective-payment clause, claim it in kind or in lira at the due-date or payment-date rate. The article covers FX vs FX-indexed… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/doviz-kredisinde-kat-ve-kur
  - Q: On an FX-indexed loan, does the bank apply the rate on the acceleration date or on the payment date?
    A: If there is no effective-payment clause and the debt was not paid on its due date, the choice belongs to the bank; it may choose the due-date or the actual-payment-date rate (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 99/3). A claim converted into Turkish lira in the enforcement request at the due-date or enforcement-date rate becomes a Turkish lira claim, and the bank can no longer claim interest specific to foreign currency after enforcement (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2019/500, K. 2022/1326).
  - Q: What happens if the bank does not show the Turkish lira equivalent of the foreign-currency claim in the enforcement request?
    A: If the foreign-currency claim is demanded in kind, the enforcement office must reject the request; the defect concerns public order and may be raised by complaint without a time limit (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2019/500, K. 2022/1326). The 12th Civil Chamber does not regard merely stating a Turkish lira figure as the fee basis as sufficient (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/9538, K. 2026/1836).
  - Q: I gave a mortgage in Turkish lira; can the bank realise it for a foreign-currency loan?
    A: According to the 12th Civil Chamber, mortgage enforcement for a foreign-currency claim cannot be brought on the basis of a Turkish lira mortgage; this concerns public order and the proceedings are annulled (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/1437, K. 2024/5890). There is no such obstacle if the claim was demanded in Turkish lira in the acceleration notice and in the enforcement proceedings (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/7357, K. 2026/414).
  - Q: At what rate does default interest run on a foreign-currency loan?
    A: The contract is looked at first. If it does not provide for a higher rate, the highest rate paid by State banks on one-year deposit accounts in that currency applies to a foreign-currency debt (Law No. 3095, art. 4/a). A bank that converted the claim into Turkish lira in the enforcement request cannot rely on this rate for the period after enforcement (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2019/500, K. 2022/1326).
  - Q: Can companies use FX-indexed loans today?
    A: Under article 17/A of Decree No. 32, FX-indexed loans may not be extended to persons resident in Türkiye. Foreign-currency loans are subject to the foreign-currency income requirement; the requirement does not apply in exceptions such as an outstanding loan balance of USD 15 million or more on the date of use.
- [Financial Leasing by Participation Banks in Türkiye: Rent, Ownership, Default, Termination, Asset Return](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/financial-leasing-participation-banks): Participation banks may carry out financial leasing, and these transactions are governed by Law No. 6361. This article briefly explains the participation bank’s power to lease (Banking Law No. 5411… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/katilim-bankalarinda-finansal-kiralama
  - Q: Is participation bank leasing interest-bearing?
    A: Legally, what the lessee pays is regulated in the Law as the financial leasing payment (rent), whose amount and payment periods are set by the parties (Law No. 6361 art. 20). The procedures for activities that participation banks may carry out by interest-free methods are set by the Banking Regulation and Supervision Board (Banking Law No. 5411 art. 77); a religious assessment of the transaction is outside the scope of this article.
  - Q: What happens if the rent is not paid?
    A: The bank grants the lessee a thirty-day payment period; where ownership is to pass to the lessee at the end of the term this period may not be less than sixty days, and if payment is not made within it the contract may be terminated (Law No. 6361 art. 31/1). After termination the lessee returns the asset; if it is sold, the rentals not yet due are settled against the sale price (Law No. 6361 art. 33/1).
  - Q: Who owns the asset at the end of the term?
    A: The bank owns the asset throughout the contract; the lessee becomes the owner only if the contract grants a purchase right and the lessee exercises it (Law No. 6361 art. 23/1). A lessee who does not exercise this right must return the asset immediately when the contract ends (Law No. 6361 art. 32).
  - Q: Must the contract be made before a notary?
    A: No; the contract is made in writing or at a distance, or by electronic methods accepted by the Banking Regulation and Supervision Board that allow identity verification, and the provision contains no notarial requirement (Law No. 6361 art. 22/1). For immovables the contract is annotated in the land register; for other assets it is registered in the relevant registry or in the registry of the Association of Financial Institutions.
  - Q: What is sale and leaseback?
    A: It is a transaction in which the lessor buys the asset from the lessee itself and leases it back to the same person by financial leasing (Law No. 6361 art. 18). In this method the lessor cannot be held liable for defects in the asset (Law No. 6361 art. 24/6).
- [General Assembly Annulment in Turkish Joint-Stock Companies: Time Limit, Mediation, Pre-Finality Enforcement](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/annulment-general-assembly-resolution-joint-stock-company): Under Turkish law, an action to annul a joint-stock company's general assembly resolution must be brought within three months of the resolution before the commercial court of first instance at the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirkette-genel-kurul-kararinin-iptali-davasi
  - Q: What is the time limit for challenging a general assembly resolution?
    A: In a joint-stock company, the action for annulment must be brought within three months from the date of the resolution (Turkish Commercial Code art. 445). The period runs from the day the resolution was adopted and is a period of forfeiture. The Code sets no time limit for nullity under art. 447; however, if the ground relied on is in fact a ground for annulment, an action brought after three months is dismissed.
  - Q: Is mediation mandatory in an action to annul a general assembly resolution?
    A: No. Under Turkish Commercial Code art. 5/A, mandatory mediation applies to commercial actions whose subject matter is a sum of money; a claim for annulment or nullity is not of that kind. If compensation is also claimed from the directors in the same statement of claim, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation holds that the claims are separated because they are directed at different defendants, and mediation is a precondition for the compensation claim.
  - Q: Can the annulment of a general assembly resolution be enforced before it becomes final?
    A: No. The annulment judgment takes effect for all shareholders once it has become final (Turkish Commercial Code art. 450). As it contains no performance order, it cannot be the subject of enforcement proceedings; according to the 12th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, once final only court costs and attorney’s fees may be collected through enforcement. Having the judgment registered in the trade registry is the duty of the board of directors.
  - Q: Does bringing the action stop the resolution from being implemented?
    A: Not by itself. The claimant may ask the court to suspend enforcement of the resolution; the court decides after obtaining the opinion of the board members (Turkish Commercial Code art. 449) and, at the company’s request, may require security from the claimant (art. 448(3)).
  - Q: Can a shareholder who did not attend the meeting sue?
    A: Yes, but he or she must allege that the meeting was not properly called, that the agenda was not duly announced, that unauthorised persons voted or that he or she was unjustly kept out of the meeting, and that this irregularity affected the adoption of the resolution (Turkish Commercial Code art. 446/1-b). A shareholder who attended must vote against and have the dissent recorded in the minutes.
  - Q: Can a board member sue against a general assembly resolution?
    A: The board of directors may sue as a body. Individual members may sue on their own only if implementing the resolution would give rise to their personal liability (Turkish Commercial Code art. 446/1-d). The action of a member who is not a shareholder and does not make this allegation is dismissed.
- [Joint Suit Does Not Spare Director Claim from Mediation: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/general-assembly-annulment-director-liability-mediation): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, where annulment of general assembly resolutions is sought against the company and compensation based on… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/genel-kurul-karari-iptali-ve-yonetici-sorumlulugu-davasinda-arabuluculuk
  - Q: Is applying to a mediator mandatory before an action to annul a general assembly resolution?
    A: Turkish Commercial Code art. 5/A(1) limits mediation as a precondition to suit to commercial receivables, compensation, annulment of objection, negative declaratory and restitution actions whose subject is a sum of money; annulment of a general assembly resolution is not in that list. In this decision, too, the mediation requirement was applied to the separated compensation action.
  - Q: What happens if annulment of general assembly resolutions and compensation from the managers are claimed in the same statement of claim?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the claims are not directed against the same defendant, so there is no joinder of claims. The court separates the actions and applies the mediation precondition to the compensation action on its own.
  - Q: Can applying to a mediator after filing the action cure the defect?
    A: If it is found that the action was filed without applying to a mediator, the action is dismissed on procedural grounds without any further step (Law No. 6325 art. 18/A(2)). In this case the first-instance court stated that mediation is not a precondition that can be completed later, and the procedural dismissal was upheld.
  - Q: Is mediation required if all claims are directed against the same defendant?
    A: The Assembly of Civil Chambers did not decide that situation. After its decision, the 11th Civil Chamber upheld, without writing its own reasoning, a regional court of appeal judgment holding that mediation was not required for trademark infringement and compensation claims brought against the same defendant. Applying to a mediator in advance for the monetary claim avoids the risk of a procedural dismissal on this ground.
  - Q: Does the mediation process affect limitation periods?
    A: From the application to the mediation office until the final report is drawn up, limitation is suspended and preclusive periods do not run (Law No. 6325 art. 18/A(15)).
- [LLC Withdrawal With Severance Claim Needs No Mediation: Turkish Appellate Court on Joined Claims](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/turkish-llc-withdrawal-severance-claim-mandatory-mediation): Under Turkish law, according to the 14th Civil Chamber of the Istanbul Regional Court of Appeal, where withdrawal for just cause and the severance payment are claimed together against the same… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ortakliktan-cikma-ve-ayrilma-akcesinde-arabuluculuk
  - Q: Is it mandatory to go to a mediator before filing an action to withdraw from a company?
    A: Under Turkish law, a withdrawal claim on its own does not fall within art. 5/A of the Turkish Commercial Code, since its subject matter is not a sum of money. If the severance payment is also claimed against the same company in the same statement of claim, according to the line of the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation and this decision the claims are examined together without being subject to mediation. The decision is not final; first-instance decisions to the contrary are also seen.
  - Q: Is mediation required if only the severance payment is claimed after leaving the company?
    A: Yes. In that case the action is a commercial receivable action whose subject matter is a sum of money, and applying to a mediator before filing is a condition of action.
  - Q: Can the court separate the claims and dismiss the severance payment claim for lack of mediation?
    A: According to this decision, not if the claims are directed against the same defendant and are connected. If the claims are directed against different defendants (e.g. one against the company and the other against the manager), according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers the actions are separated and the mediation requirement is examined for each action separately.
  - Q: How are court fees and attorney's fees determined in a combined action?
    A: Each claim counts as a separate action: the withdrawal claim is subject to a fixed court fee and attorney's fee, the severance payment claim to a proportional one. According to this decision, if the action is waived, two separate attorney's fees are awarded in favour of the defendant.
- [Legal Effect of an Invoice in Turkish Commercial Matters: Eight-Day Objection, Commercial Books, Delivery Note](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/legal-effect-invoice-commercial-matters): The legal effect of an invoice in commercial matters: the consequence of not objecting within eight days (the acceptance presumption in TCC Art. 21/2), whether an invoice alone proves the contractual… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-islerde-faturanin-hukmu
  - Q: If I do not object to an invoice within eight days, do I become liable?
    A: If you do not object within eight days, you are deemed to accept the invoice's ordinary content, that is, entries relating to performance such as the type, quantity and price of the goods. A late-payment charge entry with no basis in your contract or in commercial custom, however, is not deemed accepted merely because you did not object (Court of Cassation, Unification of Judgments, 2003/1 K.). Moreover, not objecting does not, on its own, prove that a contract truly exists between the parties; the other side must separately prove the contractual relationship.
  - Q: Does an invoice alone prove the receivable?
    A: As a rule, no. According to the Court of Cassation, not objecting finalises the content, but the contractual relationship must be proven by written/conclusive evidence. If the invoice was entered in the other party's commercial books, the booked invoice also proves the contractual relationship.
  - Q: I entered the invoice in my books without objecting; can I later say I received no goods?
    A: In that case the burden of proof shifts to you. If you did not object/return the invoice in time and entered it in your books, you must prove by written or conclusive evidence that you received no goods/services or owe no debt.
  - Q: Why is the delivery note important?
    A: The invoice shows the amount; actual delivery is usually proven by a signed dispatch/delivery note. A signed delivery note is strong evidence against a later “goods not received” defence.
  - Q: Will my commercial book be evidence in my favour?
    A: Only if it was kept in accordance with the law (fully, with opening/closing approvals, entries corroborating one another) and is not contrary to the other party's properly kept book. A book kept improperly is evidence against you.
  - Q: Which court has jurisdiction in an invoice dispute?
    A: Where the dispute concerns the commercial enterprises of both parties, it is a commercial case and the commercial court of first instance has jurisdiction (Turkish Commercial Code Art. 4(1)); the fact that the invoice is regulated in the Turkish Commercial Code does not by itself make the case commercial. Expert examination of the parties' commercial books is often decisive.
- [Liabilities of the Authorised Company Director: Duty of Care, Damages Actions and Public Debts](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/liabilities-authorised-company-director): The duties of the Turkish limited company director under TCC arts. 623-626, damages liability under art. 553 via art. 644, the general assembly resolution as a special procedural prerequisite… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sirket-mudurunun-sorumluluklari
  - Q: Is the company director personally liable for the company's debts?
    A: As a rule, no; only the company's assets answer for company debts. Exceptions: where the director culpably causes loss to the company, shareholders or creditors, damages liability arises under TCC art. 553 via art. 644; and taxes and other public debts that cannot be collected from the company may be collected from the director's personal assets as legal representative under repeated art. 35 of Law No. 6183 and art. 10 of the Tax Procedure Law.
  - Q: What must the company do before suing its director?
    A: The Court of Cassation requires a general assembly (shareholders') resolution authorising the liability action as a special procedural prerequisite. If it is missing, the court grants a definitive period for the resolution to be submitted before examining the merits.
  - Q: When is the director liable for the company's tax debts?
    A: The company is pursued first; if the tax debt cannot be collected, wholly or partly, from the company's assets or is understood to be uncollectible, the legal representative in office when the debt arose and fell due is pursued. The director who pays has recourse against the company.
  - Q: I resigned as director; am I still liable for past debts?
    A: The period principle applies: you are liable for public debts that arose and fell due during your term, not for those arising after your departure. The Council of State has held that a person who was not the legal representative on those dates cannot be held liable. Registration and announcement of your departure in the trade registry matters for proof against third parties.
  - Q: A payment order was issued against me as legal representative; what should I do?
    A: You may file an action with the tax court within 15 days of notification under art. 58 of Law No. 6183, arguing that no such debt exists, that it was partly paid or that it is time-barred; whether the debt falls within your term and whether the company was duly pursued first should also be examined. The period is preclusive.
  - Q: May the director run a business competing with the company?
    A: No; unless the articles provide otherwise or all other shareholders consent in writing, the director may not engage in any activity competing with the company (TCC art. 626/2). Breach gives rise to damages and may constitute just cause for removal.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for the liability action?
    A: Two years from learning of the damage and the person liable, and in any event five years from the act (TCC art. 560, applicable to limited companies via art. 644). Where the act is also an offence subject to a longer criminal limitation period, the longer period applies.
- [Liability of Board Members in Turkish Joint-Stock Companies: Care, Actions, Discharge (TCC Arts. 369, 553–561)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/liability-board-members-joint-stock-companies): A guide to the civil liability of board members in Turkish joint-stock companies: the duty of care and loyalty under Commercial Code art. 369, the conditions of liability under Commercial Code art.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirkette-yonetim-kurulu-sorumlulugu
  - Q: Is a board member liable for every loss of the company?
    A: No. A fault-based breach of an obligation arising from the law or the articles of association, damage and a causal link are required (Commercial Code art. 553/1). According to the Court of Cassation, no member is liable for damage he or she did not cause, and no one may be held liable for breaches beyond his or her control (Commercial Code art. 553/3).
  - Q: Can a shareholder sue board members for damages in its own name?
    A: Yes, if the damage arose directly in the shareholder’s own assets, independently of the company. If the damage arose in the company’s assets (indirect damage), the shareholder may only claim payment of the compensation to the company (Commercial Code art. 555/1); actions by shareholders seeking payment to themselves were dismissed in upheld decisions.
  - Q: Can company creditors sue board members?
    A: A creditor may sue for direct damage suffered in its own assets; in an upheld decision, rendering the company idle and insolvent in order not to pay its debts was treated as direct damage. If the company is bankrupt, creditors may claim payment of compensation to the company; the claims are first asserted by the bankruptcy administration (Commercial Code art. 556).
  - Q: Does the general assembly’s discharge resolution prevent a liability action?
    A: The discharge extinguishes the right of action only for the disclosed facts and only for the company and the shareholders who voted in favour or acquired shares with knowledge of the discharge. The right of action of the other shareholders lapses six months after the discharge (Commercial Code art. 558/2); in an upheld decision this period was held to be taken into account ex officio.
  - Q: When does a liability action become time-barred?
    A: Two years from knowledge of the damage and the person liable and in any event five years from the harmful act; if the act is an offence subject to a longer criminal limitation period, that period applies (Commercial Code art. 560). In an upheld decision, the basic criminal limitation period, not the period extended by interruption, was applied.
  - Q: Which court hears the liability action, and is mediation mandatory?
    A: The action is a commercial case (Commercial Code art. 4/1-a) heard by the commercial court of first instance (Commercial Code art. 5/1); actions against the persons liable may be brought at the commercial court of the place of the company’s registered office (Commercial Code art. 561). In receivable and compensation actions whose subject matter is a sum of money, prior application to a mediator is a precondition to suit (Commercial Code art. 5/A/1).
- [Mandatory Retained Lawyer for Turkish Joint-Stock Companies: Scope and Sanction (Attorneys Act Art. 35/3)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/mandatory-lawyer-joint-stock-companies-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the obligation under Attorneys Act art. 35/3 for joint-stock companies whose share capital reaches five times the statutory minimum to retain a lawyer under contract: scope, the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirketlerde-avukat-bulundurma-zorunlulugu
  - Q: Which companies must retain a lawyer?
    A: Under Turkish law, joint-stock companies whose share capital is five times or more the minimum share capital laid down in the Turkish Commercial Code, and building cooperatives with one hundred or more members (Attorneys Act art. 35/3). Limited liability companies are not listed in the provision.
  - Q: What is the capital threshold for the obligation of joint-stock companies to retain a lawyer?
    A: In a file concerning 2018–2020 examined by the Court of Cassation, the threshold was treated as TRY 250,000 (five times TRY 50,000) in the Chief Public Prosecutor’s request for annulment in the interest of the law; the Chamber did not separately address the threshold. Since Presidential Decision No. 7887 raised the minimum share capital to TRY 250,000 from 1 January 2024, five times that amount corresponds to TRY 1,250,000; no Court of Cassation decision on that period has yet been found.
  - Q: What is the penalty for not retaining a lawyer?
    A: The public prosecutor imposes, for each month in which no contracted lawyer is appointed, an administrative fine equal to two months’ gross minimum wage in force on the date of the offence. According to the Court of Cassation, each month is a separate misdemeanour.
  - Q: Must the lawyer be employed on the company’s staff?
    A: The Act requires “a lawyer under contract”; it does not require employment. The Court of Cassation gave credit to a contract the company had made with a lawyer and accepted that no misdemeanour arose for the months in which the contract was in force.
  - Q: Can a fine be imposed if the bar association does not report the company?
    A: Yes. According to the Court of Cassation, an application or complaint by the bar is not a precondition for the misdemeanour; article 73/C of the Union of Turkish Bar Associations’ Attorneys Act Regulation concerns the bars’ internal functioning.
  - Q: How can the administrative fine be challenged?
    A: Under Misdemeanours Law art. 27, an application is made by petition to the criminal judgeship of peace within fifteen days of notification of the decision. If the time limit is missed, the decision becomes final; in case of force majeure, an application may be made within seven days of the cause ceasing.
- [Merchant’s Own Books Are No Proof Against Non-Merchants: Turkish Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/commercial-books-evidence-against-non-merchant-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held by majority that, save for the exception in art. 222(5) of the Code of Civil Procedure, commercial books may be… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tacir-olmayana-karsi-ticari-defterle-alacak-ispatlanamaz
  - Q: Can a merchant rely on its own commercial books against a non-merchant?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Turkish Court of Cassation, no: unless the non-merchant party itself relies on those books (Code of Civil Procedure art. 222(5)), books may be examined as evidence in their owner’s favour only if both parties are merchants. Against a non-merchant counterparty, examining the books has no effect on the outcome.
  - Q: Can the non-merchant party rely on the merchant’s books?
    A: Yes. Under art. 222(5) of the Code of Civil Procedure, the non-merchant party may rely on the merchant’s books by stating that it will accept their entries; if the merchant avoids producing its books, the party requesting production is deemed to have proved its claim.
  - Q: What does an unexplained bank transfer show?
    A: According to the Assembly, a transfer is a means of payment and is presumed to have been made to pay an existing debt. The transferor who asserts that the money was sent as an advance or for another purpose must prove it.
  - Q: Was the decision unanimous?
    A: No. It was given by majority and is final; the two dissenting opinions argue that the action arising from the transfer is a commercial action and that the defendant’s books should be examined.
- [Minority Shareholder Rights in Turkish Joint-Stock Companies: Agenda, Special Audit, Just-Cause Dissolution](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/minority-shareholder-rights-joint-stock-companies): The conditions and procedure for minority shareholder rights in Turkish joint-stock companies — the right to add agenda items (TTC art. 411), postponement of financial statement discussion (art.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirkette-azinlik-haklari
  - Q: What minimum shareholding is required to exercise minority rights in a joint-stock company?
    A: Most minority provisions of the TCC set the threshold at one-tenth of the share capital (one-twentieth for publicly held companies). This threshold is required for the right to add agenda items (art. 411), postponement of financial statements (art. 420), the request to the court for a special auditor after the general assembly rejects a special audit (art. 439; here shares with an aggregate nominal value of at least one million Turkish lira also suffice) and dissolution for just cause (art. 531). Asking the general assembly for a special audit is a right of every shareholder (art. 438/1). The articles of association may grant the right to call a meeting to shareholders holding fewer shares (art. 411/1).
  - Q: What can be done if the general assembly rejects a request for a special audit?
    A: Under art. 439 of the TCC, the minority may, within three months of the rejection, apply to the commercial court of the place of the company's headquarters for the appointment of a special auditor, provided it convincingly demonstrates that the founders or corporate organs violated the law or the articles of association and thereby caused damage to the company.
  - Q: Can a resolution rejecting a special audit request be challenged by an annulment action?
    A: According to the settled case law of the 11th Civil Chamber, no; because arts. 438/439 of the TCC provide a direct route for the minority to apply to court for the appointment of a special auditor once the request is rejected, there is no legal interest in a separate action to annul the rejecting resolution.
  - Q: Does the court always order dissolution in an action for dissolution for just cause?
    A: No. Under art. 531 of the TCC, even where just cause is established, the court may order that the plaintiff shareholder be bought out at the real value of its shares instead of ordering dissolution, or order any other solution appropriate to the circumstances; Court of Cassation case law emphasizes that dissolution should be applied as a measure of last resort (ultima ratio) under the principle of proportionality.
  - Q: Under what circumstances is the discussion of financial statements postponed?
    A: Under art. 420 of the TCC, at the request of shareholders holding one-tenth of the share capital (one-twentieth for publicly held companies), the discussion of the financial statements and related matters is postponed for one month by decision of the chairman of the meeting, without any need for a resolution of the general assembly; however, according to the Court of Cassation, the election of the independent auditor does not fall within this scope.
  - Q: How many times can a postponement or special audit request be repeated?
    A: Under art. 420/2 of the TCC, a renewed postponement of the discussion of the financial statements, after it has already been postponed once at the minority's request, requires that the points objected to and recorded in the minutes not have been answered in accordance with the standard of honest accountability; otherwise a second postponement request will not be granted.
- [Officer Must Be Summoned Before Bankruptcy After Concordat: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/rejected-concordat-bankruptcy-officer-hearing-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held that an over-indebted capital company whose concordat request is rejected is declared bankrupt of the court’s own… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/konkordato-reddedilirse-iflas-karari-yetkili-dinlenmeden-verilemez
  - Q: Is every company whose concordat request is rejected declared bankrupt?
    A: No. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Turkish Court of Cassation, bankruptcy requires that the debtor be subject to bankruptcy and that one of the grounds for direct bankruptcy exist, for example the over-indebtedness of a capital company; otherwise the court only rejects the concordat request.
  - Q: Must a creditor file a separate bankruptcy request?
    A: According to the Assembly, no. If the conditions are met, the court declares bankruptcy of its own motion; no separate bankruptcy request by a creditor or by the company is required.
  - Q: Must the company officer be summoned to be heard before the bankruptcy decision?
    A: According to the Assembly, yes. Art. 308 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law contains no express provision; however, art. 177, which treats the situation in art. 308 as a ground for direct bankruptcy, provides that the debtor is summoned to court to be heard.
  - Q: Is it enough to serve the hearing date and time on the officer?
    A: In this case it was not: the notices did not warn the officers that they would be heard or of the consequences of not objecting to the expert report. A notice with a warning issued more than two years earlier was not considered sufficient either.
  - Q: Is the decision final, and what happened to the case?
    A: The decision was given unanimously and is final. The decision of resistance was reversed and the file was sent to the first-instance court.
- [One Manager's Call Not Enough Where No LLC Chair Appointed: Turkish Court of Cassation on Non-Existence](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/llc-general-meeting-call-by-one-manager-non-existent-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that, in a limited company with several managers, no chair of the board of managers and no special arrangement in the articles… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirkette-mudurlerden-birinin-cagirdigi-genel-kurul-yok-hukmunde
  - Q: Who calls the general assembly of a limited company?
    A: Under art. 617(1) of the Turkish Commercial Code (TTK), the managers. If there is more than one manager, the general assembly appoints one of them as chair (art. 624(1)); the manager who is chair, or the sole manager, is authorised to call it (art. 624(2)).
  - Q: Is a resolution of a general assembly called by only one of several managers valid?
    A: In this decision, no. Where no chair had been appointed and the articles of association contained no special arrangement, a call by one of the two managers was not treated as a legal call; the resolution should have been declared non-existent. The dispute dates from the period of the former Commercial Code No. 6762.
  - Q: Is a criminal conviction just cause for removing a manager?
    A: In this case, yes. For a manager whose conviction for breach of trust in the course of service, based on the finding that he had unjustly indebted the company, had become final, the Chamber, treating the material-fact finding in the criminal judgment as binding (art. 74 of the Turkish Code of Obligations, TBK), held that just cause was established.
  - Q: What happened to the case?
    A: The judgment on the joined action was upheld and the decision in the main action was reversed unanimously; the file was sent back to the first-instance court.
- [Partner's Right to Information and Inspection: Books and Accounts of Turkish LLCs and Joint-Stock Companies](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/company-partner-inspect-books-accounts): Yes: in a limited company every partner may ask the managers for information on all of the company’s affairs and accounts and may carry out an inspection on specific matters, and if the general… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sirket-ortaginin-bilgi-alma-ve-inceleme-hakki
  - Q: Can a limited company partner inspect the company’s books?
    A: Yes. Every partner may request information from the managers on all of the company’s affairs and accounts and may carry out an inspection on specific matters (TCC art. 614/1).
  - Q: Can a joint-stock company shareholder see the commercial books?
    A: Yes, but inspection of the parts of the books and correspondence that concern the shareholder’s question requires the general assembly’s express permission or a board resolution (TCC art. 437/4).
  - Q: Within how many days must the court be asked if no information is given?
    A: In a joint-stock company the shareholder may apply to the commercial court within ten days following a refusal, and in other cases after a reasonable period (TCC art. 437/5). For limited companies the law sets no separate time limit.
  - Q: Can the decision in an information case be appealed?
    A: By law the court’s decision is final (TCC art. 614/3, TCC art. 437/5). The majority of the 11th Civil Chamber has treated a dismissal as final too; the dissents take the opposite view.
  - Q: Can a special audit be requested if information is not enough?
    A: In a joint-stock company, a shareholder who has already exercised the right to information or inspection may ask the general assembly for a special audit; if it refuses, shareholders meeting the statutory threshold may apply to the court within three months (TCC arts. 438-439).
- [Proving an Invoice-Based Receivable in Türkiye: Is the Claim Real? (Delivery, Commercial Books, BA-BS Forms)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/proving-invoice-based-receivable): Does an invoice alone prove a receivable? The source of an invoice claim in commercial matters and between companies, proof of delivery and performance, the burden of proof (Civil Code art. 6, Code… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/faturaya-dayali-alacagin-ispati
  - Q: Is an invoice evidence on its own?
    A: An invoice is evidence, but on its own it does not prove the receivable. According to decisions upheld by the Court of Cassation, the seller must separately prove that the goods in the invoice were delivered or the service was performed.
  - Q: Does an invoice without a delivery note prove the claim?
    A: Without a signed delivery note, delivery must be proven by other evidence. In upheld decisions, delivery was accepted where the invoice was recorded in the buyer’s commercial books or notified by the buyer on the BA form.
  - Q: Does recording an invoice in one’s books mean accepting the debt?
    A: An entry in the buyer’s own commercial books is evidence against the buyer; in upheld decisions, delivery was accepted for invoices recorded in the books. A buyer claiming that it did not receive the goods or that the invoice was recorded as an accommodation must prove this itself; the burden of proving payment also lies with the buyer.
  - Q: Are BA-BS forms evidence?
    A: Yes, but their weight depends on who made the notification. An invoice notified by the buyer on the BA form was treated in upheld decisions as a presumption of purchase; the seller’s own BS notification alone does not prove the receivable.
  - Q: If no objection is made to an invoice, does the debt become final?
    A: No, not automatically. Failure to object within eight days has a consequence regarding the content of the invoice (Commercial Code art. 21/2), but, as emphasised in an upheld decision, it does not mean that the goods were delivered; the seller must prove delivery separately.
- [Registered Share Transfer in Turkish Joint-Stock Companies: Approval, Refusal Grounds, Share Register Entry](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/transfer-registered-shares-joint-stock-companies): In a Turkish joint-stock company, registered shares are freely transferable unless the law or the articles of association provide otherwise; where certificates exist, the transfer is made by… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirkette-nama-yazili-pay-devri
  - Q: Does a share transfer in a joint-stock company require the company’s approval?
    A: As a rule, no; registered shares are freely transferable unless the law or the articles of association provide otherwise (TCC art. 490). Approval is required by law for shares not fully paid up, and otherwise only where the articles of association so provide.
  - Q: How is a registered share certificate transferred?
    A: The certificate is endorsed and possession is passed to the transferee (TCC art. 490/2). To be treated as a shareholder by the company, the transferee must also be entered in the share register.
  - Q: On what grounds may the company refuse a share transfer?
    A: For unlisted shares, the company may refuse on an important ground set out in the articles of association, or by offering to acquire the shares from the transferor at their real value at the time of the application (TCC art. 493/1).
  - Q: What happens if the company does not respond to the request for approval?
    A: For unlisted shares, if the company does not reject the request within three months at the latest from receiving it, or if the rejection is unjustified, approval is deemed given (TCC art. 494/3). For listed shares, if the company does not reject the request for recognition within twenty days, the transferee is deemed recognised as shareholder (TCC art. 498).
  - Q: Is a person not entered in the share register a shareholder?
    A: No; in relations with the company, only the person entered in the share register is recognised as shareholder (TCC art. 499/4). If registration is unjustifiably refused, an action for registration may be brought before the commercial court of first instance.
- [Removing a Limited Company Manager under Turkish Law: General Assembly Resolution and Court Action](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/limited-company-manager-removed): The general assembly of a Turkish limited company may remove its manager: appointing and removing managers is a non-transferable power of the general assembly, and unless the articles of association… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirket-mudurunun-gorevden-alinmasi
  - Q: Who removes the manager of a limited company?
    A: The general assembly removes the manager; appointing and removing managers is a non-transferable power of the general assembly (Commercial Code art. 616/1-b, Commercial Code art. 630/1). Unless the articles provide otherwise, the resolution is adopted by a simple majority of the votes represented at the meeting (Commercial Code art. 620/1).
  - Q: What can a partner do if there is no majority at the general assembly?
    A: If just causes exist, any partner may ask the commercial court to revoke or restrict the manager’s management rights and representation authority (Commercial Code art. 630/2). The claimant partner must prove the just cause.
  - Q: What is just cause for removing a manager?
    A: A serious breach of the duty of care and loyalty or of obligations under the law and the articles of association, or the loss of the ability needed for proper management, is a just cause (Commercial Code art. 630/3).
  - Q: Against whom is the removal action brought?
    A: The action is brought against the manager whose removal is sought. According to the Court of Cassation, joining the company or all partners is not mandatory.
  - Q: Can a trustee be appointed while the action is pending?
    A: Suspension of the manager and appointment of a trustee during the proceedings may be requested as an interim measure; the ground must be stated and the claim shown to be justified on a prima facie basis (Code of Civil Procedure art. 389/1, Code of Civil Procedure art. 390/3). The Court of Cassation held that a trustee requested only for the duration of the action cannot be appointed for the period after the judgment becomes final.
- [Sale after Financial Lease Termination in Türkiye: Who Pays the Price Difference? (Law No. 6361 Art. 33)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/financial-lease-termination-sale-price-difference): When a terminated lease asset is sold in Türkiye, the price is set against unpaid future rent and loss; the lessee pays a shortfall, the bank a surplus. The contract may agree another value (Law No.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/leasing-fesih-sonrasi-satis-deger-farki
  - Q: The leasing company or bank sold the asset cheaply; do I have to pay the difference?
    A: Unless the contract agrees a different value, if the sale price is lower than the total of the rentals not yet due and any further loss of the lessor, the lessee pays the difference (Law No. 6361 art. 33/1). You must prove that the sale fell below market value with appraisals, advertisements and comparable sales (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/3469, K. 2021/683).
  - Q: If the sale price exceeds the remaining rentals, is the surplus paid to the lessee?
    A: Yes. If the sale price is higher than the total of the rentals not yet due and any further loss of the lessor, the lessor pays the difference to the lessee; the same principles apply if the asset is re-leased (Law No. 6361 art. 33/1). The contract may also bring overdue rentals, insurance premiums and interest into this account.
  - Q: After taking the asset back, can the bank also claim all the remaining rentals?
    A: If the asset is sold, the rentals not yet due are compared with the sale price and only the difference may be claimed for them; rentals that fell due and remained unpaid before termination are a separate debt (Law No. 6361 art. 33/1). The Court of Cassation’s 6th Civil Chamber reversed a judgment admitting a claim to the estate given without investigating whether the machine had been handed over (Court of Cassation, 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/3797, K. 2025/4496). The rule differs for contracts from the Law No. 3226 era.
  - Q: In 2026, can an action for an unquantified claim be brought for the leasing price difference?
    A: No. Art. 107 of the Code of Civil Procedure was repealed by Law No. 7589 and applies only to actions filed before the date of repeal (Law No. 7589 provisional art. 1/10). In new actions the receivable is claimed by a partial action; the claim may be increased once in the same action until the end of the evidentiary stage, and limitation is deemed interrupted from the date of the action also for the increased part (Code of Civil Procedure art. 109/4). Prior application to a mediator is a condition of action (Commercial Code art. 5/A).
  - Q: Can the bank sell the leased asset while the case is still pending?
    A: If the asset has been left to the lessor or a third party by an interim injunction, the lessor may dispose of it by depositing with the court security equal to its market value, and must compensate the lessee’s loss if the termination proves unjustified (Law No. 6361 art. 31/3). Where the lessee voluntarily returned the asset and consented to its sale, it was held that the lessor had no legal interest in the return action it brought later (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/5993, K. 2023/997).
- [Sale and Leaseback or a Disguised Loan: Simulation (Muvazaa) and Fiduciary Transfer Claims under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/sale-leaseback-really-loan): A sale and leaseback is expressly regulated in art. 18 of Turkish Law No. 6361; a financing purpose alone does not make it a sham (simulated) transaction. A lessee must, as a rule, prove simulation… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sat-geri-kirala-muvazaa-iddiasi
  - Q: Is a sale and leaseback really a loan, and can it be annulled for simulation?
    A: A sale and leaseback is a form of financial leasing expressly provided for in Law No. 6361 (Law No. 6361 art. 18), and the Law defines financial leasing as a transaction aimed at providing financing (Law No. 6361 art. 3/1-ç). A financing purpose is not in itself simulation, and buying the asset from the lessee and leasing it back has not been treated as evidence of simulation (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/249, K. 2025/5017). Annulment requires proof that the parties created an appearance not corresponding to their true intention in order to deceive third parties.
  - Q: Can I prove by witnesses that a leaseback contract was simulated?
    A: As a party to the contract, as a rule, no; a simulation claim against a written or official contract to which one is a party is proven by written evidence (Code of Civil Procedure art. 201; Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2023/214, K. 2023/246). If a document issued or sent by the lessor that makes the claim probable qualifies as prima facie written evidence, witnesses may also be heard (Code of Civil Procedure art. 202). Witnesses may be heard on simulation claims raised by third parties such as creditors (Code of Civil Procedure art. 203/1-d).
  - Q: I could not pay the rent and the contract was terminated; can I now claim the transaction was simulated?
    A: It may be raised, but the burden of proof is on the lessee and written evidence is required. Appeal grounds against a regional appellate reasoning that found it contrary to good faith for a lessee in default to assert invalidity three years later were dismissed (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/72, K. 2025/900). In an invalidity action brought after the finding of termination for just cause had become final, it was held that there was no legal interest (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/3778, K. 2024/597).
  - Q: How do I get back property I had registered in someone else’s name so that a loan could be taken out?
    A: This relationship is characterised as a fiduciary transaction, and a claim for re-transfer depends on proof of the fiduciary agreement. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the written form of the fiduciary agreement is a requirement of proof; where there is no written evidence, any evidence including witnesses may be used if there is prima facie written evidence, and failing that, admission and oath (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2025/329, K. 2026/413).
  - Q: Is land registry fee payable in a sale and leaseback?
    A: Transactions relating to financial leasing contracts are as a rule exempt from fees; however, land registry transactions for the acquisition of leased immovables by the lessor are excluded from this exemption (Law No. 6361 art. 37/1). Registration in the lessee’s name, at the end of the contract term, of an immovable leased by the sale and leaseback method is exempt from land registry fees (Law No. 6361 art. 37/2).
- [Same Issuer and Payee: Ordinary Note, Not a Promissory Note](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/promissory-note-same-issuer-payee-ordinary-note-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, a note whose issuer and payee are the same person is not a negotiable instrument but may serve, as an ordinary… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kesideci-ile-lehtar-ayni-kisi-olan-senet-adi-senet-olarak-takibe-konu-olabilir
  - Q: Is a note on which the issuer and the payee are the same person a promissory note under Turkish law?
    A: No. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, since art. 778 of the Turkish Commercial Code does not refer to art. 673, which allows a bill of exchange to be drawn to the drawer’s own order, the issuer of a promissory note cannot name himself as payee; where the capacities of payee and issuer merge, the document is not treated as a promissory note.
  - Q: Can enforcement proceedings be based on such a note that lacks the character of a promissory note?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, enforcement by way of attachment specific to negotiable instruments requires the claim to be tied to a negotiable instrument. According to the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, however, such a note may be made the subject of enforcement proceedings without judgment as an ordinary instrument given to the creditor.
  - Q: If the note bears no endorsement or written assignment, is the creditor’s action dismissed?
    A: It was not dismissed in this decision. Since there was no third person on the note other than the creditor and the debtor, the Chamber found it incorrect to dismiss the action to annul the objection on the ground that there was no valid endorsement specific to negotiable instruments.
  - Q: What happens if the debtor says the signature on the note is not theirs?
    A: According to the Chamber, the court must decide after assessing the debtor’s objection to the signature on the note.
  - Q: When is an action to annul an objection brought?
    A: Under art. 67/1 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, a creditor whose request for enforcement has been objected to may, within one year from service of the objection, apply to the court and bring an action to annul the objection by proving the existence of the claim under the general provisions.
- [Security Note Defence Does Not Lift Precautionary Attachment](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/security-note-defence-precautionary-attachment-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the 4th Civil Chamber of the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal, where the source of the claim is a promissory note there is prima facie proof that the claim is not… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/teminat-senedi-iddiasi-bonoya-dayali-ihtiyati-haczi-kaldirmaz
  - Q: Does the claim that a promissory note is a security note lift a precautionary attachment?
    A: Not in this decision: under Turkish law, the Chamber treated the claim based on the promissory note as shown on a prima facie basis and adopted the first-instance reasoning that the security-note claim required a trial. There is also an appellate decision upholding the lifting of an attachment in a case where a written document identified the note and expressly stated its security purpose.
  - Q: Is a promissory note sufficient evidence for a precautionary attachment?
    A: Under art. 258(1) of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, the creditor must show evidence that will satisfy the court as to the claim. In this decision a promissory note that had fallen due was held sufficient as prima facie proof of a claim not secured by a pledge and fallen due.
  - Q: Can a note given to a partner in a simple partnership that has not been dissolved be attached by way of precaution?
    A: In this case the debtors’ objection that there can be no claim that has fallen due in a simple partnership that has not been dissolved and liquidated did not lift the attachment. Whether the debt has fallen due is a matter to be examined at trial.
  - Q: Can a precautionary attachment be requested if the claim is secured by a mortgage?
    A: Art. 257(1) of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law allows a precautionary attachment for a claim not secured by a pledge. In this case, relying on the note, the Chamber treated it as shown on a prima facie basis that the claim was not secured by a pledge; it did not separately discuss the mortgage argument.
  - Q: Can this decision be appealed to the Court of Cassation?
    A: No. Under art. 265 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, an appeal (istinaf) may be lodged against the decision given on an objection to a precautionary attachment, and the regional court of appeal’s decision is final.
- [Share Ownership Suit Needs Sole Shareholder as a Party: Turkish Court of Cassation on Company-Only Claims](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/share-ownership-action-sole-shareholder-party-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, a share ownership action brought only against a joint-stock company can concern the interests of a non-party. That is… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/pay-sahipliginin-tespiti-davasi-yalniz-sirkete-karsi-sonuclandirilamaz
  - Q: Against whom is an action for a declaration of share ownership brought?
    A: In the case behind this decision, the action had been brought only against the company and, at the date of the action, all of the company’s shares appeared to be held by a non-party. According to the Chamber, since the judgment will concern that person’s interests, the claimant must be given time to bring a separate action against that person and the actions must be consolidated.
  - Q: If the sole shareholder is not a party, is the action dismissed?
    A: In this decision the Chamber did not call for dismissal; it required that the claimant be given time and that the actions be consolidated, and the decision given without these steps was reversed for incomplete examination.
  - Q: What must the court examine after the actions are consolidated?
    A: According to the Chamber, taking into account the attendance lists for 2022 and earlier years and the allegation that the documents notified to the Central Registry Agency were forged, the court must determine, in a manner open to review, whether the claimant was still a shareholder at the date of the action and, if so, in what amount.
  - Q: Did this decision treat the claimant as a shareholder?
    A: No. It is a reversal decision; the file was sent to the first-instance court, and whether the claimant is a shareholder was left to the examination to be carried out.
- [Share Transfer in a Turkish Limited Company: Notarised Form, General Assembly Approval and Refusal of Approval](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/share-transfer-limited-company): In a Turkish limited company a share is transferred by a written agreement with notarised signatures, and unless the articles of association provide otherwise the transfer becomes valid only with… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirkette-pay-devri-ve-genel-kurul-onayi
  - Q: Must a limited company share transfer be made before a notary?
    A: Yes. The transfer of a capital share and transactions creating an obligation to transfer are made in writing and the parties’ signatures are notarised (TCC art. 595/1). A contract concluded without the form prescribed by law produces no effect (Code of Obligations art. 12/2).
  - Q: Is a general assembly resolution needed for a share transfer in a limited company?
    A: Unless the articles provide otherwise, general assembly approval is required and the transfer becomes valid with it (TCC art. 595/2). This also applies to transfers between partners (TCC art. 593/1).
  - Q: What happens if the general assembly refuses to approve the transfer?
    A: Unless the articles provide otherwise, the general assembly may refuse without giving reasons and the transfer does not become valid (TCC art. 595/3). The partner wishing to transfer retains the right to withdraw from the company for just cause (TCC art. 595/5).
  - Q: Does the transfer take effect if the general assembly does not respond within three months?
    A: Yes. If the general assembly does not refuse within three months of the application, approval is deemed given (TCC art. 595/7). In decisions upheld by the Court of Cassation, a refusal adopted after the period did not change the outcome (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/2161, K. 2025/7060).
  - Q: Which action can be brought if the company does not enter the transfer in the share register?
    A: The transferee may sue the company before the commercial court of first instance for a declaration of partnership, entry in the share register and registration in the trade registry. In decisions upheld by the Court of Cassation these claims were granted because the three-month period had passed without a refusal (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/1690, K. 2025/6595).
- [Singer and Producer Agreements in Türkiye: Validity and Penalty Clauses (Law No. 5846 Arts. 52, 80; TBK)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/singer-producer-agreements): Album, single, master and distribution agreements between singers and producers under Turkish law: written form and itemisation of rights, performer and producer rights, collecting societies and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sarkici-produktor-sozlesmeleri-ve-cezai-sart
  - Q: We agreed orally with the producer; have my rights in the recordings passed to the producer?
    A: Contracts and dispositions concerning economic rights must be in writing and specify each right separately (Law No. 5846 art. 52); a performer’s permissions must also be in writing. An oral agreement, or a general text not listing the rights individually, may not transfer the rights not listed.
  - Q: Can the producer claim the penalty without proving loss?
    A: Yes. A valid penalty may be claimed even if the creditor suffered no loss (Code of Obligations art. 180). But if the main obligation is invalid, or became impossible for a reason for which the debtor is not responsible, the penalty cannot be claimed, and the court reduces an excessive penalty of its own motion.
  - Q: The contract says “I accept that the penalty is not excessive”; is reduction still possible?
    A: Yes. The Assembly of Civil Chambers holds that the possibility of reducing an excessive penalty is a matter of public order and cannot be waived in advance. If the debtor is not a merchant, the court reduces an excessive penalty of its own motion.
  - Q: Is an artist a merchant; can the artist seek reduction?
    A: Personal artistic performance does not in itself mean operating a commercial enterprise; in that case the artist benefits from reduction. If the artist’s company signed the contract, the debtor is a merchant and cannot seek reduction under Commercial Code art. 22; even then, if the penalty would cause the company’s economic ruin, it may be held partly void for immorality.
  - Q: Album delivery was late but the producer accepted the recording; can it claim the penalty later?
    A: Under a penalty for late performance (cumulative penalty), a producer who accepted performance without reservation cannot claim the penalty (Code of Obligations art. 179/2). The reservation must be made expressly at the time of performance; the result may differ if the contract provides that no reservation is needed or the right was reserved by an earlier warning.
  - Q: The producer made no album and I terminated; can it claim the penalty from me?
    A: In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, a producer who took no steps to prepare the album was held to be the party in breach and at fault, the artist’s termination was justified and the producer could not claim the penalty. Keeping correspondence and warnings showing the justification for termination is important.
- [Start Date of a Surety’s Default Interest: Sureties Not Served the Account Closure Notice (TCC Art. 7)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/surety-never-received-account-closure-notice): In Turkish case law, a surety not served with the account closure notice is in default from the enforcement date and owes contractual interest until then. In an 18.02.2026 decision the Assembly of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kefilin-temerrut-faizi-sorumlulugu
  - Q: If the surety never received an account closure notice, must the surety pay default interest?
    A: Not for the period before enforcement. In suretyship for a commercial debt, default interest cannot run unless the surety has been notified (Turkish Commercial Code art. 7/1). In decisions affirmed by the 11th Civil Chamber, such a surety falls into default on the enforcement date and is liable for contractual interest until then (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/5089, K. 2026/1811); in a 2026 decision of the Assembly of Civil Chambers, service of the payment order was taken as the starting point instead (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2025/61, K. 2026/92).
  - Q: If the notice was served on the company, is the surety also in default?
    A: No. The principal debtor’s default is not enough for the surety; a separate notice to the surety is required, and in affirmed regional appellate decisions the İİK art. 68/b rule on the address in the loan agreement is applied only to the principal debtor (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/484, K. 2024/8888). The absence of a notice does not, however, prevent the bank from pursuing the surety for the principal (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2020/697, K. 2022/1386).
  - Q: My address changed and the account closure notice went to the old address and was returned; am I in default?
    A: It depends on the suretyship clauses. If the contract contains only a general address clause aimed at the principal debtor, a returned notice does not put the surety in default. If there is an address and service clause specific to the surety and the change of address was not notified in the form prescribed by that clause, the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation may treat the date on which the notice reached the address in the contract as the date of service (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/1922, K. 2025/6800; E. 2024/5553, K. 2025/3957).
  - Q: Can a surety end up paying more than the suretyship limit?
    A: Not for the principal debt; the surety is in all cases liable up to the maximum amount in the suretyship contract (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 589/1). The 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, however, accepts that the surety is also liable above the limit for default interest running after the surety’s own default (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2020/8370, K. 2022/3497).
  - Q: Which interest can be claimed from the surety between account closure and enforcement?
    A: If the surety was not in default during that period, contractual interest may be claimed, but default interest may not (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/305, K. 2025/5809). If that contractual interest was claimed neither in the notice nor in the enforcement request, the court cannot add it to the principal (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2018/1101, K. 2021/1693); unless otherwise agreed, the surety is liable for contractual interest accrued for one year and for the current year (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 589/2).
- [Tera, Pusula and Liquidated Fund Investors in Türkiye: Legal Roadmap on Liquidation, Claims, Full Remedy](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/legal-roadmap-investors-harmed-tera-pusula): A guide for investors harmed in the Tera, Pusula and liquidated fund cases: CMB decisions, compensation, criminal complaints, bankruptcy claims, full-remedy actions. Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tera-pusula-fon-magdurlari-hukuki-yol-rehberi
  - Q: Will the Investor Compensation Center cover my loss in a liquidated fund?
    A: As a rule, no. The ICC covers claims arising from an investment firm’s failure to deliver cash or instruments held on your behalf after a compensation decision; losses from price movements are excluded (Law No. 6362 art. 84). The fund claim is paid first from the liquidation; damages may be sought from those responsible for the remaining loss.
  - Q: I received an interim payment; what should I do about the rest?
    A: The interim payment is an advance on the final liquidation amount. Follow the final distribution and MKK reconciliation and do not sign releases. For uncovered loss, use mediation and litigation before limitation expires; a partial action may be increased once (Code of Civil Procedure art. 109/4).
  - Q: Can I complain directly to the prosecutor?
    A: Yes, but for offences under the Capital Markets Law such as market fraud the investigation depends on the CMB’s written application (Law No. 6362 art. 115). Address your complaint to both the CMB and the prosecutor. No such condition applies to Penal Code offences such as aggravated fraud or laundering.
  - Q: When and where must I bring the claim registration action?
    A: Within 15 days of the announcement of the ranking table, before the commercial court of the place of bankruptcy; if you advanced notification costs, the period runs from notification (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 235). The action is against the estate if your claim was rejected, or against the other creditor if you challenge that creditor’s claim. Mediation is not a precondition.
  - Q: Can I bring a full-remedy action against the CMB, and when does time start?
    A: Yes. First apply in writing to the CMB within one year of learning of the act and in any event within five years; if there is no reply within thirty days the request is deemed rejected, and the action is brought in the administrative court within 60 days of rejection (Law No. 2577 art. 13). Service fault, actual loss and causation must be shown concretely.
  - Q: Which court hears a damages action?
    A: As a rule the commercial court of first instance, subject to prior mediation (Commercial Code art. 5/A). The Assembly of Civil Chambers did not regard the consumer court as competent for an investment contract expressly stated to be commercial; whether a retail investor is a consumer depends on the specific contract.
- [The Evidential Nature of Commercial Books (CCP Art. 222): Evidence For and Against the Owner](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/evidential-nature-commercial-books): The evidential nature of commercial books: production of books in commercial cases (Code of Civil Procedure Art. 222), the three conditions for books to be evidence in favour of their owner… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-defterlerin-delil-niteligi
  - Q: Will my commercial book be evidence in my favour?
    A: Only if it was kept properly (fully, with opening/closing approvals, entries corroborating one another) and is not contrary to the other party's properly kept book. Also, the contrary must not have been proven by deed/conclusive evidence.
  - Q: What if I kept my book improperly?
    A: A book lacking opening/closing approvals or whose entries do not corroborate one another is evidence against its owner. Moreover, in a properly kept book the favourable and unfavourable entries cannot be separated.
  - Q: What if the other party does not submit its book to the court?
    A: Where a non-merchant party states it will accept the entries in the merchant's book and the merchant refrains from producing it, the party requesting production is deemed to have proven its claim.
  - Q: Which is stronger evidence, the invoice or the commercial book?
    A: If the invoice is entered in the other party's book, the evidence is the commercial book; a booked invoice also proves the contractual relationship. A party asserting the contrary must prove it by written/conclusive evidence.
  - Q: How are books examined and which court has jurisdiction?
    A: Books are examined by an expert; conformity with the law and consistency of entries are reported. In a commercial dispute the commercial court of first instance has jurisdiction.
- [The Fraudulent Return Invoice: Does It Extinguish the Debt? (Proof and the Tax-Criminal Dimension)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/fraudulent-return-invoice): The fraudulent (sham) return invoice: a return invoice not based on a genuine return does not extinguish the debt; a return invoice alone does not prove the return; a genuine return must be proven by… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hileli-iade-faturasi
  - Q: Does issuing a return invoice automatically erase my debt?
    A: No. The party claiming the debt ended by return must prove that the goods were truly returned (actual delivery). Merely issuing a return invoice is not enough; per the Court of Cassation a return invoice alone does not prove the return, and a court may not accept the return on the basis of the return invoice alone.
  - Q: How do I prove a genuine return?
    A: By a signed return/dispatch delivery note, delivery-receipt documents, transport records, and properly kept commercial-book entries consistent with them. The Court of Cassation requires the return to be proven by proper evidence; the mere entry of the return invoice in the issuer's own books is not sufficient.
  - Q: Is there a penalty for issuing a return invoice that is not genuine?
    A: There may be. A document not based on reality may be deemed fake or misleading in content; this may constitute a smuggling offence requiring imprisonment under Tax Procedure Code Art. 359, and gives rise to a tax-loss penalty and penalised assessment.
  - Q: The other side says it closed its debt with a return invoice; what should I do?
    A: You may assert the invalidity of a return invoice not based on a genuine return; demand from the other side the signed documents showing actual delivery; and request examination of the commercial books and, if necessary, of the document's falsity.
  - Q: Which court hears this dispute?
    A: Because the receivable/debt side is a commercial case, the commercial court of first instance has jurisdiction. The tax offence concerning the document's falsity is the subject of separate criminal proceedings.
- [Trademark Infringement in Türkiye (IPC Art. 29): Infringing Acts, Defences, Injunction, Removal, Damages](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/trademark-infringement): Trademark infringement under the Turkish Industrial Property Code (Law No. 6769): the scope of trademark rights (art. 7), infringing acts (art. 29), likelihood of confusion and use as a trademark… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/marka-hakkina-tecavuz
  - Q: Which court hears a trademark infringement action?
    A: The intellectual and industrial property civil court or, where none exists, the designated civil court of first instance (Industrial Property Code art. 156/1); venue lies at the claimant’s domicile or where the act occurred or had its effects (Industrial Property Code art. 156/3).
  - Q: Can an infringement action be brought if the other party also has a registered trademark?
    A: Yes. A later registration is no defence against the holder of an earlier right (Industrial Property Code art. 155), and the later mark may also be invalidated.
  - Q: Is using the same word in a trade name trademark infringement?
    A: Use as a trade name may be prohibited (Industrial Property Code art. 7/3-e); what matters is use as a trademark. In an upheld judgment, use of the name as registered was lawful, while highlighting its core element infringed (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/1561, K. 2025/5659).
  - Q: Does a proprietor who stays silent for a long time lose its rights?
    A: A proprietor who knew or should have known of a later mark’s use and acquiesced for five successive years cannot rely on its mark for invalidity unless the later registration was in bad faith (Industrial Property Code art. 25/6). For infringement, acquiescence rests on Civil Code art. 2 and requires the later user’s good faith (Court of Cassation Civil General Assembly, E. 2024/376, K. 2025/798); the defendant must prove the conditions (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/1561, K. 2025/5659).
  - Q: How are damages for trademark infringement calculated?
    A: Damage comprises actual loss and loss of profit, the latter calculated at the right holder’s choice on probable income, the infringer’s net profit or a licence fee (Industrial Property Code art. 151/2); compensation for loss of reputation (Industrial Property Code art. 150/2) and non-pecuniary damages (Industrial Property Code art. 149/1) may be added.
  - Q: Is trademark infringement a criminal offence?
    A: Yes. Infringement by copying or creating confusion, such as manufacturing, selling, importing or exporting goods, carries one to three years’ imprisonment and a judicial fine of up to twenty thousand days (Industrial Property Code art. 30/1); the mark must be registered in Türkiye and prosecution requires a complaint (Industrial Property Code art. 30/5-6).
