- [Travel Agency Status and Receivables in Turkish Law: Licence, Hotel Allotments, Commission, Limitation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/legal-status-receivables-travel-agencies): The legal status and receivables of travel agencies under Turkish law: the operating licence, mandatory membership of the Association of Travel Agencies and the guarantee under Law No. 1618 and the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/seyahat-acentalarinin-hukuki-durumu-ve-alacaklari
  - Q: Is a licence mandatory to open a travel agency?
    A: Yes. An operating licence from the Ministry of Culture and Tourism is mandatory and is granted only to legal entities (Law No. 1618 art. 4). Membership of the Association of Travel Agencies is also mandatory (Law No. 1618 art. 32).
  - Q: How does a hotel collect accommodation charges from an agency?
    A: The hotel may start enforcement proceedings on the basis of the allotment contract and invoices; if the debtor objects, an action for annulment of objection is filed within one year (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 67). The case is heard by the commercial court, and mediation must be attempted first (Commercial Code art. 5/A).
  - Q: When does the agency’s commission claim arise?
    A: The contract comes first. Absent a provision, in a continuous agency relationship the fee arises when and to the extent that the concluded transaction is performed (Commercial Code art. 114); for one-off brokerage, only when a contract is concluded as a result of the brokerage (Code of Obligations art. 521).
  - Q: Which court hears a dispute between an agency and a hotel?
    A: As a commercial case, it falls within the jurisdiction of the commercial court of first instance (Commercial Code art. 4 and art. 5). Mediation is a procedural prerequisite in money claims (Commercial Code art. 5/A).
  - Q: What is the limitation period for claims arising from an allotment contract?
    A: In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, the allotment contract was not treated as an agency contract and the ten-year period (Code of Obligations art. 146) was applied to the advance claim arising from it (11th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/7191, K. 2024/3360). Accommodation charges and claims arising from commission and agency contracts are subject to the five-year period (Code of Obligations art. 147).
- [Turkish Commercial Enterprise Transfer: Integrity, Registration, Debt Liability (TCC Art. 11, TCO Art. 202)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/transfer-commercial-enterprise): A guide to the transfer of a commercial enterprise under Turkish law: the principle of integrity in Commercial Code art. 11 and the artisan–merchant distinction, the written form, registration and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-isletme-devri
  - Q: Is a written agreement required to transfer a commercial enterprise?
    A: Yes. The transfer agreement is made in writing, registered with the trade registry and announced (Commercial Code art. 11/3). The 11th Civil Chamber treated the written form as a condition of validity and reversed a judgment holding the transferee liable under Code of Obligations art. 202 without a written agreement (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/620, K. 2023/3244).
  - Q: From when is the transferee liable for earlier debts?
    A: A person who takes over an enterprise with its assets and liabilities is liable to the creditors for the business debts from the date on which it notifies them of the transfer or announces it, for commercial enterprises by a notice in the Trade Registry Gazette (Code of Obligations art. 202).
  - Q: Is the transferor released from the debts immediately?
    A: No. The previous debtor remains jointly and severally liable with the transferee for two years; the period runs, for debts already due, from the date of notification or announcement and, for debts falling due later, from their due date. Without notification or announcement, the two-year period does not begin to run (Code of Obligations art. 202).
  - Q: Do the lease and the trade name pass with the enterprise?
    A: Unless otherwise provided, the transfer agreement also covers the leasehold right and the trade name (Commercial Code art. 11/3). The 3rd Civil Chamber accepted that, in a transfer as a whole, the leasehold right passes without the landlord’s consent (Court of Cassation 3rd Civil Chamber, E. 2025/3400, K. 2025/4478). Unless expressly agreed otherwise, the transfer of the enterprise also results in the transfer of the trade name (Commercial Code art. 49).
  - Q: Who is liable for employees’ receivables when a workplace is transferred?
    A: The transferor and the transferee are jointly liable for debts that arose before the transfer and are payable on the date of transfer; the transferor’s liability is limited to two years from the date of transfer (Labour Law art. 6). According to the 9th Civil Chamber, for severance pay the transferor is liable for its own period on the basis of the last wage at the transfer date, without the two-year limit; the last employer bears notice pay and unused leave pay (Court of Cassation 9th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/12198, K. 2022/14570).
  - Q: Which court hears disputes arising from an enterprise transfer, and is mediation mandatory?
    A: Actions arising from Code of Obligations art. 202 and Code of Obligations art. 203 are commercial cases regardless of whether the parties are merchants (Commercial Code art. 4/1-c) and are heard by the commercial court of first instance (Commercial Code art. 5/1). In actions for receivables, compensation, annulment of objection, negative declaratory relief and restitution whose subject matter is a sum of money, applying to a mediator before filing suit is a condition of action (Commercial Code art. 5/A).
- [Turkish Joint-Stock Company General Assembly: How It Is Convened, the Call, Agenda and Quorums](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/general-assembly-joint-stock-company-convened): In a Turkish joint-stock company the general assembly is, as a rule, called by the board of directors at least two weeks in advance, excluding the days of the announcement and of the meeting, by an… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anonim-sirkette-genel-kurul-cagrisi-gundem-ve-nisaplar
  - Q: What is the meeting quorum for the general assembly of a joint-stock company?
    A: Unless a heavier quorum applies, it is at least one quarter of the capital; the quorum must be maintained throughout the meeting, no quorum is required at a second meeting, and resolutions are adopted by a majority of the votes present (TCC art. 418).
  - Q: What quorum is required to amend the articles of association?
    A: As a rule, a majority of the votes present at a meeting where at least half of the capital is represented; one third suffices at a second meeting. Seventy-five per cent affirmative votes are required for decisions such as a complete change of the field of activity, and unanimity for moving the registered office abroad (TCC art. 421).
  - Q: How many days before the meeting must the call be made?
    A: At least two weeks, excluding the days of the announcement and of the meeting; the announcement is published on the company website and in the Turkish Trade Registry Gazette, and registered shareholders receive a registered letter (TCC art. 414/1).
  - Q: Is a resolution annulled if the call was defective?
    A: Yes, if the defect affected the adoption of the resolution; a shareholder may bring an annulment action within three months from the resolution even without attending the meeting (TCC art. 445, TCC art. 446/1-b).
  - Q: Are resolutions of a general assembly convened without authority valid?
    A: If all shareholders attend, do not object before the agenda is taken up and do not leave, the conditions of a general assembly without a call are met (TCC art. 416). Otherwise, especially where an objection was made before the meeting, the resolutions may be treated as non-existent.
- [Turkish Limited Company General Assembly Resolutions: Annulment, Nullity, Three-Month Period, Recorded Dissent](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/annulment-general-assembly-resolution-limited-company): Annulment, nullity and non-existence of a limited-company general-assembly resolution: which resolutions, who may sue, the recorded-dissent requirement, the three-month period and court — in light of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirkette-genel-kurul-karari-iptali
  - Q: What is the period to annul a resolution?
    A: The annulment action must be brought within three months of the resolution; this is a forfeiture period. For nullity and non-existence there is no time limit.
  - Q: Which court hears the action?
    A: The commercial court of first instance at the company's seat.
  - Q: I attended but did not have my dissent recorded; can I still sue?
    A: For an attending member, a negative vote and recorded dissent are a condition of action. Without them, no annulment action lies; but nullity/non-existence grounds are not subject to the period or dissent requirement.
  - Q: What is the difference between annulment and nullity?
    A: Breach of the law, articles or good faith is a ground of annulment, subject to the three-month period. Breach of mandatory rules is nullity — not time-barred and considered by the court ex officio.
  - Q: I just acquired the share; can I sue?
    A: Only a person who has become a shareholder/member may bring the annulment action. Until the transfer is complete, the status of shareholder does not arise.
  - Q: Do these rules apply to limited companies?
    A: Yes. Under Art. 622 TCC, the rules on annulment and nullity of joint-stock company resolutions apply by analogy to limited companies.
- [Unapproved LLC Manager Pay Needs a Reasonableness Review: Turkish Appellate Court on General Assembly Approval](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/turkish-llc-manager-salary-no-general-assembly-resolution): Under Turkish law, according to the 14th Civil Chamber of the Istanbul Regional Court of Appeal, in a liability action against a limited company manager who took remuneration without a general… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/genel-kurul-karari-olmadan-sirket-mudurune-odenen-maas
  - Q: Who sets the salary of a manager in a Turkish limited company?
    A: Under Turkish law, determining the remuneration of managers is one of the non-transferable powers of the general assembly (Turkish Commercial Code art. 616/1-f).
  - Q: Can the entire salary taken without a general assembly resolution be reclaimed?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to this decision and a 2024 decision of the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, not automatically. The court determines through an expert examination whether the payments are reasonable; the loss is the part exceeding a reasonable remuneration.
  - Q: Can a manager be entitled to remuneration even without a general assembly resolution?
    A: The 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation stated that, because of the prohibition of forced labour, it is inconceivable for the manager to work without receiving any remuneration. In another action between the same parties, the first-instance court relied on the custom rule in art. 502/3 of the Turkish Code of Obligations; the dismissal survived appellate and Court of Cassation review with general reasoning.
  - Q: When appointing a trustee to the company is sought, can the company be joined to the action later?
    A: According to this decision, no: a company not named as a defendant in the statement of claim cannot later become a party as a “joined defendant”; if the claim must be directed against the company, a separate action should be brought and the actions consolidated.
- [Unauthorised Moratorium Debts Fall Under the Concordat: Turkish Court of Cassation on Partial Annulment](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/concordat-partial-annulment-unauthorised-moratorium-debt): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation unanimously held that a claim arising from a contract made during the concordat moratorium without the commissioner’s… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/komiser-izni-olmadan-dogan-alacak-icin-konkordatonun-kismen-feshi
  - Q: Is a claim arising from a contract made during the concordat moratorium without the commissioner’s permission subject to the concordat?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Turkish Court of Cassation, yes. Under art. 308/c/2 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, a concordat that has become binding is also mandatory for claims arising during the moratorium without the commissioner’s permission; debts contracted with the commissioner’s permission, by contrast, are not subject to the terms of the concordat in an ordinary concordat under art. 308/c/4.
  - Q: Do “routine” commercial transactions during the moratorium also require the commissioner’s permission?
    A: The Assembly did not answer this question in general terms. It required that whether a transaction counts as routine be assessed separately in each specific case, and it did not regard the sales contract worth EUR 526,320.00 as a routine commercial activity for the debtor.
  - Q: Can a creditor who did not register its claim in the concordat seek partial annulment?
    A: In this case the Assembly accepted the legal interest of a creditor whose claim is subject to the concordat and has not been paid under the project; that claim had been established by an arbitral award. According to a decision of the 6th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation dated 08.04.2026, however, a creditor who registered no claim at all cannot, as a rule, seek partial annulment; it may do so if the claim is established by a judgment in the action it brings and is not paid under the project despite a demand.
  - Q: What is the effect of partial annulment of the concordat?
    A: Under art. 308/e of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, the creditor has the concordat annulled in respect of itself only, while retaining the new rights acquired under the concordat. According to the Assembly, the creditor can thereby return, as regards itself, to the position before the concordat.
  - Q: Was the concordat annulled in this case?
    A: No. The Assembly reversed the decision of resistance; the regional court of appeal will assess whether the conditions for partial annulment are met.
- [Uncertificated Shares Need Written Assignment: Ankara Regional Court of Appeal Voids a Board Transfer](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/uncertificated-shares-transfer-written-assignment-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Ankara Regional Court of Appeal decision upheld by the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, uncertificated (bare) registered shares are transferred under… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/pay-senedi-basilmamis-hisse-yazili-temlik-olmadan-devredilemez
  - Q: How are shares transferred in a joint-stock company that has not printed share certificates?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to this decision, registered shares not embodied in a certificate (bare shares) are transferred under the rules on assignment of claims and in written form (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 184); a fully paid share is transferred by handing the transferee a written declaration of assignment. Shares that are not fully paid were not discussed in this decision.
  - Q: Can shares be transferred by a board resolution?
    A: Not in this case: the Chamber held void a transfer made by a resolution signed only by the chairman of the board, without the shareholder’s signature or any document of authority. Situations where there is a document signed by the shareholder are not the subject of this decision.
  - Q: What happens to later transfers based on an invalid transfer?
    A: According to the Chamber, they are also void. In this case the 2014 transfer between the brothers was also held invalid, and the shares were ordered to be entered in the share register in the shareholder’s name.
  - Q: Can a shareholder who stayed silent for years bring an action later?
    A: In this case, yes: as there was no information or document at all showing that the transfer had been ratified or accepted, the Chamber did not treat bringing the action almost eight years later as an abuse of rights.
  - Q: Is this decision final?
    A: Yes. It is a decision of a regional court of appeal (istinaf); the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation upheld it on 04.03.2026, unanimously and with final effect, without writing separate reasoning.
- [Unfair Competition Actions in Türkiye: Unfair Acts, Claims, Competent Court and Limitation (TCC Arts. 54-62)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/unfair-competition-actions): A guide to unfair competition actions under Turkish law: the principle of honest and undistorted competition in Commercial Code art. 54, the acts of unfair competition in art. 55 (disparagement… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/haksiz-rekabet-davasi
  - Q: What can be claimed in an unfair competition action?
    A: A declaration that the act is unfair, prohibition of the unfair competition, removal of its consequences and correction of false statements, if unavoidable the destruction of instruments and goods, pecuniary damages if there is fault and non-pecuniary damages under the conditions of Code of Obligations art. 58 (TCC art. 56/1).
  - Q: What is the limitation period for an unfair competition action?
    A: One year from learning of the accrual of the rights and in any event three years from their accrual; if the act is an offence subject to a longer criminal limitation period, that period applies (TCC art. 60/1). “Learning” means learning of the damage and of the person responsible (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/5830, K. 2024/2078).
  - Q: Which court hears an unfair competition action?
    A: As a rule, the commercial court of first instance (TCC art. 4/1-a and TCC art. 5). In actions based on the unauthorised use of another’s news, however, the civil court of intellectual and industrial property rights has jurisdiction (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/4292, K. 2025/5784).
  - Q: Is mediation mandatory in an unfair competition action?
    A: It is a precondition to suit for claims whose subject is a sum of money, such as damages (TCC art. 5/A). For non-monetary claims such as a declaration, prevention and deletion of a trade name, the mediation objection was rejected (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/4340, K. 2025/2920).
  - Q: Is it unfair competition for a limited company partner to compete with the company?
    A: As a rule, no, unless the articles of association impose a non-compete obligation; an appeal judgment to that effect was upheld (Court of Cassation 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/2256, K. 2025/2270). The non-compete obligation applicable to directors is assessed separately.
  - Q: How are pecuniary damages calculated in unfair competition?
    A: The basis is the profit the claimant lost because of the unfair competition, not gross revenue; as a rule it is determined by expert examination with commercial books and other evidence. The judge may also award the equivalent of the benefit the defendant could possibly have obtained (TCC art. 56/1).
- [Unfair Competition by Confusion in Türkiye: Trade Names, Packaging and Domain Names (TCC Arts. 55/1-a-4, 52)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/passing-off-unfair-competition-trade-name-packaging-domain): Under Turkish law, measures causing confusion with another's business are unfair competition without any registration requirement (Turkish Commercial Code art. 55/1-a-4), and art. 52 additionally… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/iltibas-yoluyla-haksiz-rekabet-ticaret-unvani-ambalaj-alan-adi
  - Q: Does passing off as unfair competition under Turkish law require a registered trademark or trade name?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, art. 55/1-a-4 of the Turkish Commercial Code treats measures that cause confusion with another's goods, work products, activities or business as unfair competition without requiring registration. However, the sign or get-up must be identified with the claimant in the market. The Court of Cassation has required a high level of distinctiveness for unregistered packaging and product designs and has held that there is no confusion where the later product is sold under a different trademark.
  - Q: Is using the core word of another company’s trade name in one’s own trade name passing off under Turkish law?
    A: It can be. Under art. 52 of the Turkish Commercial Code, a holder whose trade name is used contrary to commercial honesty may seek a declaration, a prohibition, amendment or deletion of the registered trade name and, depending on fault, damages. The Court of Cassation has held that using a trade name registered in bad faith is unfair competition. By contrast, an upheld decision dismissed a claim by an earlier holder who had remained silent for fifteen years, on grounds of good faith.
  - Q: Is using the same colour on packaging enough for passing off under Turkish law?
    A: As a rule, no. The 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that packaging must be assessed by its overall impression and that mere colour similarity between products with different overall compositions is not unfair competition; that decision was taken by majority. Where colour, lettering and shapes together create a near-identical composition, another upheld decision found unfair competition.
  - Q: Can a competitor's similar domain name and website be passing off under Turkish law?
    A: It can. In an upheld regional court of appeal decision, using the core element of a trade name beyond its registered form, in a domain name and website in a way that created a trademark-like effect, was held to be unfair competition, and access to the domain name was blocked. In the same case the request to delete the trade name was dismissed because of long silence and because the defendant’s trade name registration predated the claimant’s trademark registrations, so the domain name and the trade name were assessed separately.
  - Q: Can a registered trademark owner in Türkiye also rely on unfair competition rules against imitation of the mark?
    A: According to the view taken by majority in the 11th Civil Chamber’s decision of 26.02.2026, for events after the Turkish Commercial Code No. 6102 entered into force, only the Industrial Property Code applies to a registered trademark, and no separate unfair competition ruling is made for the same use. One member dissented, arguing that both protections should apply together. The decision reached this result for a case where the scope of the registered trademark and that of unfair competition overlapped; that unfair competition rules may be relied on for elements outside the registration, such as the overall get-up of packaging, is this article’s inference.
- [Unpaid Dividends in Turkish Companies: What Can a Shareholder Do? (Limited and Joint-Stock Companies)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/shareholder-dividends-not-paid): A dividend can be claimed only after the general assembly has resolved to distribute profit; the Court of Cassation holds that, without such a resolution, a court cannot take the general assembly’s… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sirket-ortagina-kar-payi-odenmiyorsa
  - Q: If the company made a profit, can a partner claim the dividend directly?
    A: No. The general assembly decides on dividends (TCC art. 408/2-d, TCC art. 616/1-e); the Court of Cassation holds that, without a distribution resolution, a court cannot take the general assembly’s place and fix a dividend.
  - Q: When must an action against a resolution not to distribute profit be brought?
    A: An annulment action is brought within three months of the resolution (TCC art. 445). A partner attending the meeting must vote against and have the objection recorded in the minutes.
  - Q: Can the general assembly transfer all profit to reserves?
    A: Reserves not provided for in the law or the articles may be set aside only under the statutory conditions (TCC art. 523/2; for limited companies TCC art. 608/3); otherwise the resolution may be challenged by an annulment action.
  - Q: What if a distribution was resolved but the dividend is not paid?
    A: The dividend is claimed from the company by a debt action or enforcement proceedings. If the company objects to enforcement, an action for annulment of the objection may be brought within one year of service of the objection (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 67/1).
  - Q: If no profit has been distributed for years, can a partner leave the company?
    A: In a joint-stock company, shareholders representing at least one tenth of the capital may sue for dissolution for just cause; instead of dissolution, the court may order that the plaintiffs be paid the real value of their shares and excluded (TCC art. 531). In a limited company any partner may bring an action for dissolution or withdrawal for just cause.
- [Voting Quorum in a Turkish Limited Company: Ordinary and Important Resolutions (Commercial Code arts. 620–621)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/voting-quorum-limited-company): In a Turkish limited company, unless the law or the articles of association provide otherwise, general assembly resolutions are adopted by an absolute majority of the votes represented at the meeting… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirkette-karar-nisaplari
  - Q: What is the voting quorum in a limited company?
    A: Unless the law or the articles of association provide otherwise, general assembly resolutions, including elections, are adopted by an absolute majority of the votes represented at the meeting (TCC art. 620). For the important resolutions listed in TCC art. 621, at least two thirds of the votes represented and an absolute majority of the entire share capital carrying voting rights are required together.
  - Q: What majority is needed for a capital increase in a limited company?
    A: A capital increase is an important resolution; at least two thirds of the votes represented and an absolute majority of the entire share capital carrying voting rights are required together (TCC art. 621/1-d). Pre-emption rights may be restricted or abolished only for just cause and with the same quorum (TCC art. 591/2).
  - Q: Can a manager vote on his own discharge?
    A: No. Persons who have taken part in the management of the company in any manner may not vote on resolutions concerning the discharge of managers (TCC art. 619/1). If the quorum is not met once the excluded votes are deducted, the discharge resolution is non-existent (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/560, K. 2025/1186).
  - Q: Can a resolution be adopted without holding a general assembly meeting?
    A: Yes. Unless a partner requests oral deliberation, a resolution may be adopted by obtaining the other partners’ written consent to a partner’s proposal on an agenda item; submitting the same proposal to all partners is a condition of validity (TCC art. 617/4).
  - Q: What happens if a 50–50 company cannot reach a decision?
    A: Equal votes produce no absolute majority, and the capital majority required for important resolutions cannot be reached with one partner’s vote. If the deadlock persists, actions for dissolution for just cause (TCC art. 636/3) and for withdrawal (TCC art. 638/2) come into play; the provisions requiring a general assembly resolution for an expulsion action in two-partner limited companies were annulled by the Constitutional Court (Constitutional Court, E.2025/128, K.2025/273).
- [What a Limited Company Manager Can Do Alone in Türkiye: Authority, Including Selling Company Real Estate](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/limited-company-manager-sole-authority): A Turkish limited company manager decides on management matters not reserved to the general assembly and, if registered with sole signing authority, may alone carry out any transaction within the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/limited-sirket-muduru-tek-basina-hangi-islemleri-yapabilir
  - Q: Can a limited company manager sign contracts alone?
    A: Yes; if registered with sole signing authority, the manager may carry out on the company’s behalf any business within the scope of business (TCC art. 371/1). If joint signature has been registered and announced, that limitation is also effective against third parties.
  - Q: Can the manager sell company real estate without a general assembly resolution?
    A: For an ordinary sale within the scope of business, a general assembly resolution is not a condition of validity. For a transfer of real estate that would end the company’s activity and lead to its de facto liquidation, however, the Assembly of Civil Chambers requires a general assembly resolution and has held a transfer without one void even where the transferee acted in good faith.
  - Q: Which decisions are reserved to the general assembly?
    A: Amending the articles, appointing and removing managers, financial statements and dividends, managers’ remuneration and discharge, approval of share transfers and dissolution are non-transferable powers of the general assembly (TCC art. 616/1).
  - Q: How do several managers take decisions?
    A: The managers decide by majority; in a tie the chair of the board of managers has the casting vote, and the articles may provide otherwise (TCC art. 624/3).
  - Q: What happens if the manager exceeds their authority?
    A: A manager who culpably breaches their duties is liable to the company, the partners and the company’s creditors (TCC art. 553/1, applicable through TCC art. 644/1-a). The general assembly may remove the manager, and any partner may, for just cause, ask the court to withdraw the manager’s right to manage and power to represent (TCC art. 630).
- [When Two Partners of a Company Disagree: 50–50 Deadlock, Withdrawal and Dissolution under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/two-partner-company-partners-disagree): If the two partners of a limited company hold equal shares and vote against each other, the general assembly cannot pass resolutions that require a majority and the company is deadlocked. If no share… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/iki-ortakli-sirkette-ortaklar-anlasamazsa
  - Q: How does the general assembly of a two-partner limited company with equal shares adopt resolutions?
    A: As a rule, resolutions are adopted by an absolute majority of the votes represented at the meeting (TCC art. 620); if two equal partners vote against each other, no such majority exists. Important resolutions also require an absolute majority of the entire share capital (TCC art. 621/1).
  - Q: What can a partner do if it wants to leave the company?
    A: It may transfer its share; the transfer is made in writing with notarised signatures and, unless the articles provide otherwise, requires general assembly approval (TCC art. 595/1-2). Failing agreement it may sue to withdraw for just cause (TCC art. 638/2) and, on leaving, claim a leaving payment corresponding to the real value of its share (TCC art. 641/1).
  - Q: If a dissolution action is brought, will the company necessarily be wound up?
    A: No. Instead of dissolution the court may order that the claimant be paid the real value of its share and leave, or another appropriate solution (TCC art. 636/3); in a decision on a joint-stock company the Assembly of Civil Chambers stated that this may be ordered of the court’s own motion even without a request.
  - Q: Can one partner expel the other in a two-partner company?
    A: Where there is just cause, expulsion by court order is available (TCC art. 640/3). The Constitutional Court has annulled, for two-partner limited companies, the provisions requiring a general assembly resolution and a qualified majority for this route; the decision was published in the Official Gazette of 17.03.2026.
  - Q: Is a limited company wound up if one partner leaves?
    A: No. A limited company may continue with a single partner (TCC art. 573/1); if the number of partners falls to one, the managers are notified within seven days and have the single-partner status registered and announced (TCC art. 574/2).
- [When a Company Cannot Pay Its Debts: Capital Loss, Over-Indebtedness and Directors’ Duties under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/company-cannot-pay-debts): If the last annual balance sheet shows that half of the total of capital and statutory reserves is no longer covered because of losses, the general assembly must be called immediately. The board of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sermaye-kaybi-ve-borca-batiklikta-yonetici-sorumlulugu
  - Q: What must be done if half of the capital is lost?
    A: The board of directors, or in a limited company the managers, must immediately call the general assembly and propose the remedial measures they consider appropriate (Commercial Code art. 376/1, art. 633/1).
  - Q: Does the company dissolve if two-thirds of the capital is lost?
    A: Yes, unless the general assembly, convened without delay, resolves to settle for one-third of the capital or to restore it (Commercial Code art. 376/2).
  - Q: What must a director do if the company is over-indebted?
    A: Draw up an interim balance sheet and, if the assets do not cover the claims, notify the commercial court where the head office is located and request bankruptcy (Commercial Code art. 376/3).
  - Q: Can a company creditor sue the manager or a board member?
    A: For the company’s loss, a creditor may sue only if the company is bankrupt and the bankruptcy administration does not bring the action (Commercial Code art. 556). A loss suffered directly by the creditor is assessed separately (Commercial Code art. 553/1).
  - Q: Is failing to request the company’s bankruptcy a crime?
    A: Yes. Persons charged with management and representation, or liquidators, who fail to request bankruptcy are punished upon the complaint of a creditor (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 345/a).
- [When a Former Employee Sets Up a Competing Business: Poaching, Trade Secrets, Unfair Competition in Türkiye](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/former-employee-poaching-trade-secrets-unfair-competition): Poaching customers and employees and using trade secrets when a former employee sets up or joins a competing business under Turkish law: unfair competition under Commercial Code art. 55/1-b and d… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/calisan-ve-musteri-ayartma-ticari-sir-haksiz-rekabet
  - Q: Is it unfair competition under Turkish law for a former employee to set up a company in the same sector?
    A: The decisions reviewed did not treat this as unfair competition in itself. In an upheld decision, employees who left of their own accord starting work at a competitor for a higher salary was seen as part of the freedom to work; unfair competition required proof of specific conduct such as inducing customers to breach their contracts, systematically poaching employees or unlawfully using trade secrets (Commercial Code art. 55/1-b and d). In one case where the employee, while still employed, set out to exploit the former employer’s customer portfolio for a competitor, unfair competition was found.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, can a former employee take customers to a new company if the employment contract has no non-compete clause?
    A: Without a written non-compete clause, a former employee is as a rule free to compete after the contract ends; customers moving of their own choice does not by itself lead to a sanction. However, inducing customers to breach their contracts with the former employer or using unlawfully obtained information may be unfair competition (Commercial Code art. 55/1-b and d). The duty of confidentiality also continues after the contract to the extent necessary (Code of Obligations art. 396).
  - Q: When is an employee non-compete clause invalid or terminated under Turkish law?
    A: An employee non-compete clause is not valid if it is not in writing, if the employee cannot obtain knowledge of the customer base, production secrets or the employer’s business, or if the use of that knowledge could not cause the employer significant harm (Code of Obligations art. 444). An excessive restriction may be narrowed by the judge; the duration may not exceed two years save in special circumstances (art. 445). The restriction ends if the employer has no real interest left or if the contract is terminated by the employer without just cause or by the employee for a reason attributable to the employer (art. 447).
  - Q: Which court hears an action against a former employee for breach of a non-compete clause in Türkiye?
    A: According to decisions of the Court of Cassation 9th Civil Chamber, the unification decision of 13.06.2025 held commercial courts of first instance competent for breaches of the non-compete obligation occurring after the employment relationship has ended; Commercial Code art. 4/1-c also treats these actions as commercial cases. For a penalty claim arising from a breach while the employment continued, the same Chamber held the labour court competent.
  - Q: Are agreements between employers not to hire each other’s staff (no-poach or gentlemen’s agreements) lawful in Türkiye?
    A: The Turkish Competition Board has found agreements between competing employers not to employ each other’s staff contrary to Law No. 4054 art. 4 and, in its decision of 13.04.2023, fined an undertaking; the agreement need not be in writing. According to the Board, in project-based activities a clause limited to the relevant personnel may qualify as an ancillary restraint if it is in writing and also meets conditions such as proportionality, necessity and duration. A Board decision is not a court judgment.
- [Which Court Hears Commercial and Consumer Cases in Türkiye: Muğla, Manisa, Uşak, Afyonkarahisar, Kütahya](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/commercial-consumer-court-missing-where-to-sue-turkiye): Under Turkish law, where there is no commercial or consumer court, the civil court of first instance hears commercial and consumer cases in the relevant capacity, and Council of Judges and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticaret-ve-tuketici-mahkemesi-olmayan-yerlerde-dava-nerede-acilir
  - Q: Is there a commercial court in Uşak in Türkiye, and where is a commercial case filed in Uşak?
    A: There is no commercial court in central Uşak. A commercial case is filed with the Uşak civil court of first instance, which hears it in the capacity of a commercial court. Applying the rule of Council of Judges and Prosecutors Decision No. 1235 to today's six civil courts, courts Nos. 5 and 6 hear these cases; since the decision named court No. 4 for Uşak at the time, the number should be confirmed with the allocation office.
  - Q: Is there a consumer court in Kütahya in Türkiye, and where is a consumer case filed in Kütahya?
    A: According to the Kütahya Courthouse's 2025 activity report, there is no consumer court in the provincial centre. A consumer case is filed with the Kütahya civil court of first instance, which hears it in the capacity of a consumer court. Applying the rule of Council of Judges and Prosecutors Decision No. 1236 to today's four courts points to court No. 4; the number should be confirmed with the courthouse's allocation office. Depending on the amount, the consumer arbitration committee or mediation may have to come first.
  - Q: Which court hears consumer cases in Manisa and Muğla in Türkiye?
    A: The provincial centres of Manisa and Muğla have a commercial court but no consumer court. Consumer cases are heard by the civil court of first instance in the capacity of a consumer court. In central Manisa, applying the rule of Decision No. 1236 to today's five courts points to court No. 4, to be confirmed with the courthouse's allocation office; in central Muğla two civil courts are listed, so the decision's numbering rule does not apply.
  - Q: Is there a commercial court in Afyonkarahisar in Türkiye, and which court hears commercial cases in Afyonkarahisar?
    A: Central Afyonkarahisar has a consumer court but no commercial court. Commercial cases are heard by the civil court of first instance in the capacity of a commercial court; applying the rule of Council of Judges and Prosecutors Decision No. 1235 to today's six courts points to courts Nos. 5 and 6; the number should be confirmed with the courthouse's allocation office. Consumer cases are filed with the Afyonkarahisar Consumer Court.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, where there is no commercial court, is a commercial case filed with the civil court without stating its capacity dismissed for lack of jurisdiction?
    A: No. Under article 5(4) of the Turkish Commercial Code, in a judicial district without a commercial court the fact that the jurisdiction rule was not invoked does not require a decision of lack of jurisdiction; the civil court continues with the case. The court must, however, hear the case in the capacity of a commercial court; the Court of Cassation listed omitting this capacity among the reversal grounds in one decision and merely criticised it in another.
- [Wrong Article in Minutes Alone Does Not Prove No Call: Turkish Court of Cassation on Uncalled Assemblies](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/general-assembly-without-a-call-minutes-wrong-article): Under Turkish law, according to the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, a statement in the general assembly minutes that the meeting was held under Article 370 of the former Commercial Code… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/genel-kurul-tutanaginda-yanlis-madde-cagrisiz-genel-kurul-saymaya-yetmez
  - Q: What are the conditions of a general assembly without a call?
    A: The holders or representatives of all shares must be present and none of them may object; in that case the meeting may be held without observing the calling procedure, and resolutions may be adopted as long as the meeting quorum exists. Items may be added to the agenda only unanimously (Turkish Commercial Code art. 416).
  - Q: Does a statement in the minutes that the meeting was held under Article 370 of the Commercial Code make it a general assembly without a call?
    A: According to the Chamber in this decision, not on its own. If the meeting was held under Turkish Commercial Code No. 6102, it must be taken into account that the reference to an article of the former code is based on a material error, and whether a call was made must be examined.
  - Q: What happens to the resolutions if a shareholder does not attend a general assembly without a call?
    A: In this case the first-instance court and the regional court of appeal had held that the resolutions were non-existent because two shareholders were not present. The Chamber did not discuss that principle; it required first a determination of whether the meeting was in fact held without a call.
  - Q: How is it shown that the general assembly was called?
    A: The call is made in the manner set out in the articles of association, by an announcement on the company’s website and in the Turkish Trade Registry Gazette at least two weeks before the meeting; a registered letter with return receipt is sent to shareholders registered in the share ledger and to shareholders who have notified their addresses by previously giving the company share certificates or documents proving their shareholding (Turkish Commercial Code art. 414(1)). These announcement and mailing records are the documents of the call.
  - Q: Did this decision bring the case to an end?
    A: No. The decision is a reversal; the file was sent to the first-instance court. The court will decide again after assessing the defence that the meeting was held with a call.
- [İzmir Regional Court of Appeal, Türkiye: Jurisdiction (İzmir, Aydın, Manisa), Appeal Procedure, Time Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/izmir-regional-court-appeal-jurisdiction-turkiye): The İzmir Regional Court of Appeal hears appeals against civil and criminal court decisions given in İzmir, Aydın and Manisa. This article explains, under Turkish law and with statutory texts and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/izmir-bolge-adliye-mahkemesi-yargi-cevresi-istinaf
  - Q: Which court hears an appeal against a civil court decision given in Aydın, Türkiye?
    A: The appellate court is the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal. The petition is not taken to İzmir; it is lodged with the Aydın court that gave the decision or with another local court, and the court that gave the decision sends the file to the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal (Code of Civil Procedure art. 343).
  - Q: Where is the appellate court for decisions of the courts in Manisa, Türkiye?
    A: The province of Manisa lies within the jurisdiction of the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal; for decisions of civil and criminal courts, the appeal is reviewed by the relevant chamber of that court in İzmir. For decisions of the administrative and tax courts in Manisa the appellate court is different: the İzmir Regional Administrative Court.
  - Q: What is the time limit for an appeal to the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal in Türkiye?
    A: In civil cases it is two weeks from proper service of the decision (Code of Civil Procedure art. 345); time limits in special statutes are reserved. In criminal cases, for decisions given on or after 01.06.2024, it is two weeks from service of the judgment together with its reasons (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 273(1)).
  - Q: Do court decisions given in Muğla and Uşak still go to the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal in Türkiye?
    A: No. Muğla and Uşak were brought within the jurisdiction of the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal, which, as recounted in a Court of Cassation decision, began operating on 02.09.2024. Appeals against decisions given before that date, and files previously reviewed by the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal, nevertheless stay in İzmir (Law No. 5235, additional art. 1).
  - Q: If a petition of appeal against a decision given in Aydın or Manisa, Türkiye, names the wrong chamber or a different regional court of appeal, is the appeal invalid?
    A: No, it is not invalid if it was made in time. In civil cases the file is sent to the relevant regional court of appeal without being bound by the chamber indicated in the petition (Code of Civil Procedure art. 343(4)), and if the review must be carried out by another regional court of appeal this is decided at the preliminary examination (art. 352). In criminal cases an error as to the competent authority does not extinguish the applicant's rights (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 264).
  - Q: Is an appeal against an administrative or tax court decision in İzmir, Türkiye, lodged with the İzmir Regional Court of Appeal?
    A: No. Except for decisions the statute makes final or excludes from appeal, an appeal against a decision of an administrative or tax court is lodged with the regional administrative court within thirty days of service of the decision (Administrative Procedure Act art. 45(1)). For İzmir, Aydın and Manisa that court is the İzmir Regional Administrative Court.

### Work Accident Compensation Law

- [Calculation of Work-Accident Compensation (Actuarial Method)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/calculation-work-accident-compensation): Actuarial calculation of permanent-incapacity and loss-of-support compensation in work accidents; TRH 2010 life table, active/passive and known/unknown periods, 10% increase-discount, fault… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/is-kazasi-tazminatinin-hesaplanmasi-akturyal
  - Q: SGK granted me income; can I also claim from the employer?
    A: Yes. For the part of the damage not covered by the SGK income, you may bring a pecuniary compensation action against the employer.
  - Q: Which life table is used in the calculation?
    A: The Court of Cassation has adopted the TRH 2010 National Mortality Table, which contains current data specific to Türkiye.
  - Q: What do active and passive periods mean?
    A: The active period is calculated, as a rule, until age 60 on real income; the passive period after 60, over remaining life, on the minimum wage.
  - Q: Does my own fault affect the compensation?
    A: Yes. Compensation is reduced in proportion to your contributory fault; the judge sets it considering the gravity of fault (TCO Arts. 51-52).
  - Q: Is all SGK income deducted from the compensation?
    A: No. Only the recourse-eligible portion of the first capital value of the granted income is deducted; non-recoverable payments cannot be deducted (TCO Art. 55).
  - Q: What is the limitation period for work-accident compensation?
    A: As it rests on the employer's breach of the contractual duty of care, a ten-year limitation period applies as a rule (TCO Art. 146).
- [Damages in Fatal Work Accidents: Calculating Loss-of-Support Compensation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/damages-fatal-work-accidents): A guide to damages in fatal work accidents under Turkish law: who may claim loss-of-support compensation; heads of loss (TCO art. 53); actuarial principles - the TRH 2010 mortality table, support… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/olumlu-is-kazasinda-tazminat-hesabi
  - Q: Who may claim damages after a fatal work accident?
    A: Those actually supported by the deceased, or who would in all likelihood have been supported — in practice the spouse, children and parents. Non-pecuniary damages may be claimed by the spouse, children, parents and siblings. Heirship is not required.
  - Q: How are loss-of-support damages calculated?
    A: Actuarially, on the deceased’s real income: actual data for the known period and the TRH 2010 mortality table for remaining life expectancy. After a personal share for the deceased, the remainder is apportioned to the spouse and children; fault reduction and deduction of the recourse-eligible portion of the SSI income follow.
  - Q: Is the SSI survivor’s income deducted from the damages?
    A: Only the recourse-eligible portion of the initial capitalised value of the granted income is deducted. Non-recourse social security payments may not be deducted (TCO art. 55).
  - Q: Is mediation mandatory, and which court is competent?
    A: No; actions for pecuniary and non-pecuniary damages arising from work accidents, and the related determination, objection and recourse actions, are exempt from mandatory mediation (Law No. 7036 art. 3/3). The action is brought before the labour court, or the civil court of first instance sitting in that capacity.
  - Q: What is the prescription period?
    A: Claims against the employer are, as a rule, subject to a ten-year period from the date of the accident (TCO art. 146). Different or extended periods may apply to third parties or where the act is also a criminal offence; verify for the specific case.
  - Q: How are non-pecuniary damages assessed?
    A: By reference to the date of the accident, the parties’ social and economic circumstances, the purchasing power of money, the degrees of fault and the intensity of grief; the award must not enrich but must provide solace, assessed on equity (TCO art. 56).
- [From PMF 1931 to TRH 2010: The Life Table in Bodily Injury and Loss-of-Support Damages under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pmf-1931-to-trh-2010-life-table-compensation): The life table that fixes remaining life expectancy in bodily injury and loss-of-support damages under Turkish law: the chronology of the move from PMF 1931 to TRH 2010 in work-accident and traffic… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/pmf-1931-den-trh-2010-a-tazminat-hesabinda-yasam-tablosu
  - Q: What is the difference between PMF 1931 and TRH 2010?
    A: PMF 1931 is a table built from French population data of 1931 that draws no distinction between women and men; it puts the average life expectancy of a newborn at 56.64 years. TRH 2010 was prepared from Turkish data separately for women and men (for a newborn, 78.02 years for women and 71.93 for men). Remaining life expectancy is shorter under PMF 1931.
  - Q: Which life table is used in damages calculations today?
    A: Under Turkish law, the Court of Cassation as a rule applies TRH 2010 in work-accident, traffic-accident and other tort cases. The exceptions are certain older traffic accidents, vested procedural rights and a party’s express request.
  - Q: Does PMF 1931 apply to accidents before 2012?
    A: There is no fixed threshold. The Assembly of Civil Chambers applied PMF 1931 by majority to a traffic accident of 18.11.2010; the “PMF before 2012, TRH after” formula appears in the dissenting opinion. In work-accident cases TRH 2010 has also been applied to incidents of 2009 and 2010.
  - Q: Does the date of a work accident affect the choice of table?
    A: The decisions examined draw no distinction by date of incident in work-accident cases; PMF 1931 was applied only where a vested procedural right existed.
  - Q: What happens if an earlier report in the file used PMF 1931?
    A: Judgments based on PMF 1931 reports are, as a rule, reversed; however, where a vested procedural right has arisen in favour of the other party regarding that report, that limit is observed. For this reason the 10th Civil Chamber, in its decision of 17.12.2024, relied on the amount in the PMF 1931 report.
  - Q: Is the 1.8% technical interest rate still applied?
    A: After the Constitutional Court’s decision of 17.07.2020 (E. 2019/40, K. 2020/40), the chambers of the Court of Cassation no longer apply the 1.8% technical interest rate and have the future period calculated with the progressive annuity (10% increase, 10% discount). A 1.8% calculation that has created a vested procedural right may be preserved.
- [SGK Recourse Against Employers for Work Accidents in Türkiye: Conditions, Fault, Amount and Limitation Period](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/sgk-recourse-action-employer-work-accident): Under Turkish law, where a work accident results from the employer’s intent or from a breach of occupational safety legislation, the SGK claims from the employer the payments it has made and the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/is-kazasinda-sgk-nin-isverene-rucu-davasi
  - Q: Under what conditions can the SGK bring a recourse action against the employer after a work accident in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, the SGK may have recourse against the employer if the work accident occurred as a result of the employer’s intent or of an act contrary to the legislation on the protection of insured persons’ health and on occupational safety (Law No. 5510 art. 21/1). The principle of unavoidability is taken into account in determining the employer’s liability. The 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation has defined this principle as situations and consequences that are impossible to prevent even where the measures prescribed by the legislation have been taken (in a recourse action concerning an occupational disease). If the insured person was not notified to the Institution in time, these grounds of liability are not required (art. 23).
  - Q: What is the limit on the amount the SGK can claim from the employer in a recourse action under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, the employer is asked for the payments made and to be made by the Institution and the initial capitalised value of the income awarded, as at the date the income started; this total is limited to the amount the insured person or the beneficiaries could claim from the employer (Law No. 5510 art. 21/1). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, the court calculates both the Institution’s expenditure (inner ceiling) and the real damage (outer ceiling), takes the lower and, as a rule, applies the degree of fault. Against a third party at fault, recourse is based on half of the initial capitalised value (art. 21/4).
  - Q: What is the limitation period for an SGK recourse action arising from a work accident in Türkiye, and when does it start?
    A: Under Turkish law, actions for compensation and recourse brought by the Institution on the basis of Law No. 5510 are subject to a ten-year limitation period (art. 93/3). The period starts from the Institution’s approval date for the income and pensions that are the subject of recourse, and from the date of the expense or payment for expenses and payments; it does not start from the date of the accident. In a 2024 decision the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation treated as a ground for reversal the dismissal of an action on the basis of a limitation objection raised not during the proceedings but for the first time in the appeal petition.
  - Q: Does the fault report in the worker’s compensation action bind the SGK’s recourse action under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law it does not bind as conclusive evidence, but it is strong evidence. In its decision of 20.05.2026 (by majority on this issue) the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation stated that, because the subject matter and the parties of the two actions are not the same, the fault report in the compensation action cannot be regarded as conclusive evidence in the recourse action, but will constitute strong evidence. If the reports contradict each other, the compensation file is obtained and the contradiction is resolved by a new expert panel report. According to the same decision, to whom fault belongs and in what proportion requires special and technical knowledge; the court cannot determine it of its own motion by setting the expert reports aside.
  - Q: If a worker whose employment entry declaration was not filed in time has a work accident in Türkiye, is the employer liable to the SGK even without fault?
    A: Yes, under Turkish law the employer is liable if the insured employment entry declaration was not filed in time. Under Law No. 5510 art. 23, in that case the expenses incurred and to be incurred by the Institution and the initial capitalised value of the income awarded are collected from the employer without the grounds of liability in art. 21/1 being required. In a 2021 decision the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation treated this as strict liability and stated that a real-damage ceiling calculation is not mandatory and that the Institution’s loss is determined by making an equitable reduction of not less than fifty per cent of the insured person’s fault.
  - Q: Which court hears the SGK’s recourse action against the employer in Türkiye, and is mediation mandatory?
    A: Under Turkish law the action is heard by the labour court. Disputes arising from the application of Law No. 5510 are resolved by the labour courts unless the Law provides otherwise (art. 101); Law No. 7036 also assigns to the labour court, apart from the listed exceptions, disputes arising from labour and social security legislation to which the SGK is a party (art. 5/1-b). The court with venue is the court of the defendant’s domicile or of the place where the work or transaction was performed (art. 6). In recourse actions arising from a work accident, applying to a mediator is not a condition for bringing the action (art. 3/3).
- [Work Accidents in Turkish Mines and Marble Quarries: Liability of Employer, Subcontractor, Licence Holder](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/mine-quarry-work-accident-liability-turkiye): Under Turkish law: liability of the employer, subcontractor, royalty operator and licence holder for work accidents in mines and marble and travertine quarries; joint and several liability, the fault… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/maden-ve-mermer-ocaginda-is-kazasi-sorumluluk
  - Q: I work for a subcontractor in a marble quarry. From whom can I claim damages?
    A: Under Turkish law, you may claim from your own employer (the subcontractor) and from the principal employer operating the quarry, jointly or separately. By statute the principal employer is liable together with the subcontractor (Labour Act No. 4857 art. 2); the Court of Cassation treats this liability as joint and several and accepts an action against both or only one (10th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/5939).
  - Q: Can the licence holder also be held liable?
    A: Where a royalty (rödovans) contract exists, liability arising from the Labour Act and occupational health and safety rests, as a rule, with the operator; the licence holder’s liability under the Mining Law remains (Law No. 3213 additional art. 7). The Court of Cassation requires an inquiry into whether the licence holder acted beyond the royalty arrangement and an assessment of its status as principal employer and its fault.
  - Q: How is the degree of fault determined?
    A: The court obtains a report from an expert panel familiar with the branch of work and with occupational health and safety rules. The report must discuss which measure had to be taken by whom and whether the worker complied, and resolve any conflict with the SSI inspection report.
  - Q: What is the time limit for bringing an action?
    A: Claims against the employer and the principal employer are subject to the ten-year limitation period (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 146). According to the Court of Cassation, the period runs from the date on which the permanent incapacity rate is definitively determined. For persons with no employment relationship, the tort limitation period (TCO art. 72) applies.
  - Q: Is mediation required before a work accident damages action?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, mediation is not a precondition to suit for pecuniary and non-pecuniary damages arising from a work accident (Labour Courts Law No. 7036 art. 3/3).
- [Work-Accident Compensation: Your Rights and Legal Remedies](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/work-accident-compensation): The pecuniary and non-pecuniary damages and loss-of-support compensation a worker and their relatives may claim after a work accident — employer liability, unavoidability, competent court, limitation… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-12; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/is-kazasi-tazminati-haklar-ve-hukuki-yollar
  - Q: Is an SSI income award required to claim work-accident compensation?
    A: No. The recoverable portion of the first capitalised value of any SSI income is deducted from the award, but this does not bar a claim. A pecuniary and non-pecuniary action may be brought against the employer for the loss not covered by the Institution.
  - Q: Which court hears a work-accident case?
    A: Labour courts have jurisdiction. As a rule the competent court is that of the defendant's domicile or of the place of the workplace.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for work-accident compensation?
    A: As a rule, ten years from the date of the event. Where the act also constitutes a criminal offence carrying a longer period, the extended (criminal) limitation period applies.
  - Q: Can I increase the non-pecuniary compensation later?
    A: Non-pecuniary compensation is indivisible and must, as a rule, be claimed fully and at once. Determining the claim correctly when filing is therefore very important.
  - Q: Does the employer still pay if the accident was entirely unavoidable?
    A: Yes, in part. The Court of Cassation allocates part of the loss to the employer even in wholly unavoidable accidents, by virtue of the social-state principle and the protection of the worker.
  - Q: From when does interest run?
    A: Interest runs from the date of the accident giving rise to the compensation; the same start date applies to amounts increased by amendment of the claim.

### Traffic Accident Compensation Law

- [Claiming from the Guarantee Fund in Türkiye: Uninsured, Hit-and-Run or Stolen Vehicles (Law 5684 Art. 14)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/guarantee-fund-claim-uninsured-hit-and-run-vehicle-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a claim may be made to the Guarantee Fund for bodily harm caused by an uninsured, unidentified or stolen vehicle; the Fund pays up to the cover amounts and, according to Court of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/guvence-hesabina-basvuru-sigortasiz-veya-kacan-aracin-verdigi-zarar
  - Q: In which cases can a claim be made to the Guarantee Fund under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, for traffic accidents a claim may be made to the Guarantee Fund for bodily harm where the vehicle involved cannot be identified, where it has no compulsory traffic insurance, or where it was stolen or seized and the keeper cannot be held liable (Law No. 5684 art. 14/2). Where the insurance company is bankrupt or its licence in all branches is revoked, a claim may be made for property and bodily losses. The Regulation also allows a claim for the difference between the policy cover and the compulsory cover.
  - Q: What must be proven to obtain compensation from the Guarantee Fund in Türkiye when a hit-and-run vehicle causes injury?
    A: A person claiming from the Guarantee Fund for harm caused by a hit-and-run vehicle in Türkiye must, as a rule, prove that the injury resulted from a traffic accident involving a motor vehicle. In its 2026 decision the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers found the police statement taken immediately after the event and the hospital and medical records sufficient for this. Since, according to Court of Cassation decisions, the Fund is liable in proportion to the keeper’s fault, fault is also determined separately.
  - Q: Is a written application to the Guarantee Fund mandatory in Türkiye before bringing a court action?
    A: Yes; under Turkish law, Law No. 2918 art. 97 requires, for claims within the limits of compulsory traffic insurance, a written application to the relevant insurance institution before a court action or arbitration and the fifteen-day reply period to be awaited. In a 2021 decision the Court of Cassation’s 17th Civil Chamber applied this requirement to a claim against the Guarantee Fund, holding that the defect does not lead to immediate dismissal and that the claimant must be given a final time limit to apply.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a compensation claim against the Guarantee Fund under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, a compensation claim against the Guarantee Fund is, as a rule, time-barred two years from learning of the loss and the person liable to pay and, in any event, ten years from the accident (Law No. 2918 art. 109). If the accident is also a crime and the criminal law provides a longer limitation period, that longer period applies; the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers has not required a criminal case to have been brought, and the 17th Civil Chamber has applied the extended period in an action against the Guarantee Fund.
  - Q: From whom does the Guarantee Fund in Türkiye claim back the compensation it has paid?
    A: Under Turkish law, for compensation it has paid, the Guarantee Fund may seek recourse against the keeper who did not take out compulsory insurance, persons liable for the loss, a liable person who is identified later, or the obliged insurance company (Guarantee Fund Regulation art. 16/1). In a 2024 decision the Court of Cassation’s 4th Civil Chamber accepted that, if the driver at fault has died, the Fund may also seek recourse against the driver’s heirs.
  - Q: Does the Guarantee Fund in Türkiye pay for car damage caused by an uninsured or hit-and-run vehicle?
    A: As a rule, no. Under Turkish law, Law No. 5684 art. 14/2 lists only bodily harm for losses caused by uninsured, unidentified or stolen vehicles; property loss is listed for the insurer’s bankruptcy or the revocation of its licence in all branches. The Law gives the President power to decide on damage to property; whether such a decision exists should be checked separately.
- [Contributory Fault Need Not Cut Non-Pecuniary Damages: Turkish Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/contributory-fault-non-pecuniary-damages-reduction): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held by majority that a claimant’s own fault in a tort causing serious injury does not automatically require a reduction of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/zarar-gorenin-kusuru-manevi-tazminattan-indirimi-zorunlu-kilmaz
  - Q: Are non-pecuniary damages reduced where there is contributory fault?
    A: Under Turkish law, not automatically. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the injured party’s fault is one of the factors the judge takes into account in assessing non-pecuniary damages; having taken that fault into account, the judge may still find the amount claimed appropriate.
  - Q: Does the fault reduction applied to pecuniary damages also apply to non-pecuniary damages?
    A: According to the Assembly, it need not be carried over as a percentage. In this case the claimant’s 20% fault was taken as the basis in calculating pecuniary damages, whereas the full amount of non-pecuniary damages claimed was awarded, and the Assembly upheld that outcome.
  - Q: What happens if a claimant who said they were not at fault is later found at fault?
    A: According to the Assembly, the assertion of no fault by a claimant who cannot know the degrees of fault in advance is general and abstract; a later finding that the claimant was at fault does not change the relief sought, but it may influence the judge’s discretion.
  - Q: Can the judge award more non-pecuniary damages than claimed?
    A: No. Under Code of Civil Procedure art. 26, the judge is bound by the relief sought; the Assembly also referred to the claimant’s claim as the upper limit for non-pecuniary damages.
  - Q: Is this decision final?
    A: Yes. The decision of resistance was upheld by majority and with final effect. The dissenting opinion concerns not the merits but the preliminary issue of whether the decision could be appealed in view of the amount.
- [Damages in Fatal Traffic Accidents: Loss of Support and Calculation Principles](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/damages-fatal-traffic-accidents): A guide to damages in fatal traffic accidents under Turkish law: the operator's strict liability and the chain of liable parties; the independent nature of loss-of-support claims and the effect of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/olumlu-trafik-kazasinda-tazminat-hesabi
  - Q: Who can be sued after a fatal traffic accident?
    A: The at-fault driver, the vehicle’s operator/owner (strict liability), the compulsory motor liability insurer (within policy limits) and, where the vehicle is uninsured or unidentified, the Guarantee Account. Non-pecuniary damages fall outside the insurance cover.
  - Q: Does the deceased driver’s own fault defeat the relatives’ claim?
    A: Loss-of-support damages are an independent right arising in the claimant’s own person. For policies issued after 01.06.2015, support claims corresponding to the provider’s fault are excluded, and the Assembly of Civil Chambers held that the relatives of a fully at-fault driver-provider cannot claim from the insurer; for earlier policies it held the claim to be within the cover.
  - Q: How are the damages calculated?
    A: Actuarially, on the deceased’s real income, with remaining life expectancy under the TRH 2010 table, without technical interest, using the progressive annuity formula; support shares are apportioned, contributory fault is deducted and payments received are set off.
  - Q: Must I apply to the insurance company before suing?
    A: Yes. A written application to the insurer is a prerequisite (HTC art. 97). If the insurer does not respond within 15 days or the response does not satisfy the claim, you may sue or apply to the Insurance Arbitration Commission.
  - Q: Which court is competent?
    A: Liability actions under the Highway Traffic Code are heard by the civil judiciary (art. 110): the civil court of first instance for claims against the driver and operator, and the commercial court of first instance where the insurer is sued. Venue lies with the courts of the accident place, the defendant’s domicile or the insurer’s branch/agency.
  - Q: What is the limitation period?
    A: Two years from knowledge and in any event ten years (art. 109/1). In fatal accidents the extended criminal period applies: as a rule 15 years (negligent homicide) where a third party died; 8 years in current case law where only the driver-provider died in a single-vehicle accident. Verify on the facts.
- [Knee Pads Not Required, No Contributory Fault Reduction: Turkish Court of Cassation on a Motorcyclist](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/motorcycle-knee-pads-contributory-fault-reduction): Under Turkish law, the 4th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation unanimously held that, because the Highway Traffic Regulation does not regulate knee pads as mandatory protective equipment for… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/motosiklet-surucusunun-dizlik-takmamasi-muterafik-kusur-indirimi-sebebi-degildir
  - Q: Is a motorcyclist’s compensation reduced if they did not wear knee pads?
    A: Under Turkish law, the reduction was not upheld in this decision. The 4th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation found the 20% reduction for contributory fault, based on the failure to use knee pads, erroneous because the Highway Traffic Regulation does not regulate knee pads as mandatory protective equipment for motorcycle drivers. In a 2022 reversal decision, however, the Chamber had required a 20% reduction in this situation; the date of the decision relied on should be borne in mind.
  - Q: What protective equipment is mandatory for a motorcycle driver?
    A: The text of the Regulation in force at the time of the accident, as quoted by the Chamber, listed a protective helmet and protective goggles for the driver and a protective helmet for passengers. The current text, following the amendment of 4.11.2025, also lists protective gloves for motorcycle drivers and passengers carried; knee pads do not appear in the current text either.
  - Q: Is it not enough that the injury was to the knee?
    A: It was not considered enough in this decision. The Objection Arbitration Panel had relied on the injury being to the knee; the Chamber, however, found the reduction erroneous because knee pads are not regulated as mandatory protective equipment in the Regulation.
  - Q: Is not wearing a helmet always a ground for reduction?
    A: This decision made no ruling on helmets. Code of Obligations art. 52/1 makes a reduction depend on the injured party having contributed to the occurrence or increase of the loss; so even where mandatory equipment was not used, the effect of the omission on the actual injury is assessed separately.
- [Rights in a Property-Damage Traffic Accident](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/rights-property-damage-traffic-accident): A comprehensive guide to the injured party's rights in a property-damage traffic accident: the claimable losses (repair cost, diminished value, replacement vehicle/loss of use, loss of earnings… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/maddi-hasarli-trafik-kazasinda-haklar
  - Q: Besides the repair cost, what else can I claim in a property-damage accident?
    A: In addition to the repair cost, you may claim the diminished value remaining in your vehicle, the replacement (rental) vehicle or loss-of-use cost for the period the vehicle is under repair, loss of earnings for a commercial vehicle, and necessary expenses such as towing and appraisal. If the vehicle is an economic total loss, the loss is the difference between the market value and the salvage value.
  - Q: From whom do I claim the vehicle's diminished value?
    A: Diminished value may be claimed from the driver at fault, the vehicle's operator, and their compulsory traffic insurer (limited to the policy limit). If the application to the insurer is rejected, you may go to court or to the Insurance Arbitration Commission.
  - Q: How is diminished value calculated?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation's criterion, diminished value is the difference between the vehicle's undamaged second-hand market value at the accident date and its second-hand value after repair. The calculation takes into account the vehicle's make, model, age, mileage, the extent of the damage and any prior damage; an expert (mechanical engineer) report is therefore required.
  - Q: Must I apply to the insurance company before suing?
    A: Yes. Before bringing an action against the compulsory financial-liability (traffic) insurer, making a written application to the insurance company is a condition of the action. If the insurer does not reply within 15 days or its reply does not meet the claim, you may sue or apply to the Insurance Arbitration Commission (Art. 97).
  - Q: What if the other party has no traffic insurance or fled the scene?
    A: For bodily injury caused by uninsured or untraced (hit-and-run) vehicles, the Assurance Account comes into play; for property (material) losses the Assurance Account's liability is limited. In that case compensation is sought primarily from the driver and operator at fault; the specific situation must be assessed separately.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a compensation claim?
    A: For property losses, as a rule, a two-year period from learning of the loss and the person liable, and in any event a ten-year period from the day of the accident, applies. If the accident also constitutes a crime and the criminal law provides a longer period, this extended (criminal) limitation also applies to property compensation (Art. 109).
