- [Vehicle Diminished-Value Compensation: How to Claim It After a Traffic Accident under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/vehicle-diminished-value-traffic-accident): Who pays for your vehicle's loss of value after a no-fault accident (operator, driver, traffic insurer), within what period and how — application, insurance arbitration and Court of Cassation… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-15; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/trafik-kazasi-arac-deger-kaybi-tazminati
  - Q: From whom can I claim diminished value?
    A: From the operator and driver of the at-fault vehicle, and from that vehicle's compulsory traffic insurer within the policy limit. They are jointly and severally liable.
  - Q: Does my kasko policy cover diminished value?
    A: As a rule, no. Diminished value is outside kasko cover; it falls under the other party's compulsory traffic insurance and the operator/driver's liability.
  - Q: Must I apply to the insurer before suing?
    A: Yes. A written application to the compulsory traffic insurer is required before suit/arbitration; if no positive result follows within 15 days, you may sue or apply to the Insurance Arbitration Commission.
  - Q: How is diminished value calculated?
    A: An expert, considering make, model, age, mileage and damage, determines the difference between the undamaged pre-accident second-hand value and the post-repair second-hand value.
  - Q: Is diminished value available in every accident?
    A: No. The other party must be at fault and the repair economical; in a total loss or minor repairs not affecting value, diminished value may not arise.
  - Q: What is the limitation period?
    A: Two years from learning of the damage and the liable party, and in any event ten years from the accident; an extended criminal limitation period may apply where the act is an offence.

### Customs Law

- [Additional Customs Assessment and Penalty (VEZA) Decisions: Objection and Grounds for Annulment](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/additional-customs-assessment-penalty-decisions): A comprehensive guide to additional customs assessment and penalty (VEZA) decisions: what additional assessment is and how it arises (post-clearance control; differences in customs value, tariff and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/gumrukte-ek-tahakkuk-ve-ceza-karari
  - Q: What does a VEZA decision mean?
    A: In practice the additional assessment (tax) and penalty decision that the customs administration notifies together is called, in short, a “VEZA” (tax and penalty) decision. The under-collected customs duty is demanded by additional assessment, and the related fine is imposed by the penalty decision.
  - Q: Can I bring an action directly against the additional-assessment and penalty decision?
    A: No. The administrative objection route under CL Art. 242 must first be exhausted within 15 days of notification. Only upon rejection of the objection may an action for annulment be brought in the tax court.
  - Q: What is the time bar for additional assessment?
    A: The additional assessment must be notified within three years of the date the customs obligation arose (as a rule, the registration of the declaration). An additional assessment notified after that period is time-barred and must be annulled.
  - Q: What is the relationship between settlement and the action period?
    A: A settlement request is made within 15 days of notification and suspends the objection/action period. If settlement is reached, the matters settled cannot be made the subject of an action; if not, the period resumes.
  - Q: On what grounds can an additional assessment be annulled?
    A: The principal grounds: the passing of the three-year time bar, defective/incomplete notification, determination of the customs value contrary to the methods of Arts. 23-31, non-fulfilment of the penalty conditions (e.g. Art. 234/1-b), and matters relating to the reservation and the binding nature of the declaration.
  - Q: In which court are customs disputes heard?
    A: The action for annulment is brought in the tax court; the appeal in the tax chamber of the regional administrative court, and cassation before the 7th Chamber of the Council of State.
- [Foreign-Plated Cars in Türkiye: Exceeding the Time Limit, Temporary Import, Duties, Penalties, Objections](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/foreign-plated-car-turkiye-time-limit-customs): Under Turkish law, the foreign-plated private car brought to Türkiye by a person living abroad: the 185-day residence condition and the twenty-four-month period (Decision No. 2009/15481), letting… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yabanci-plakali-arac-sure-asimi-ve-gumruk
  - Q: How long may my foreign-plated car stay in Türkiye?
    A: Decision No. 2009/15481 grants private land vehicles twenty-four months and the vehicles of persons retired abroad two years; under certain conditions, residents of the EU, EFTA and the United Kingdom are granted one month once a year. For current periods, the announcements of the Ministry of Trade should be checked.
  - Q: May my relative in Türkiye drive my car?
    A: Only with your permission, not continuously, and while you are in Türkiye (Decision art. 18). Letting others use it while you are abroad breaches the regime; taxes are claimed, the article 238 fine is imposed and the driver may also be fined.
  - Q: May I leave the car in Türkiye and fly back?
    A: Before departure you must give the customs authority an undertaking that the car will not be used by others. Leaving without the car and without informing customs attracts an irregularity fine under art. 241/6-e; provisional art. 11 extends this to departures before 2022.
  - Q: I exceeded the period; what do I have to pay?
    A: Customs duty, special consumption tax and VAT are claimed by additional assessment and a fine of one quarter of the customs duties is imposed (art. 238/1-b). If the vehicle was taken out within three months after the period ended, an irregularity fine applies instead (art. 241/5-b).
  - Q: How do I object to the penalty decision?
    A: Within fifteen days of service, an objection is filed by petition with the higher authority or, if there is none, with the same authority (art. 242). If the objection is rejected, an action is brought before the tax court within thirty days (Administrative Procedure Law art. 7).
  - Q: What if the decision was served by public notice without reaching me?
    A: Service by public notice without researching the foreign address is defective; this was accepted in the decision upheld by the 7th Chamber. Where notice is given by publication, the time limit for the action starts fifteen days after the day following the last publication date (Administrative Procedure Law art. 7/3).
- [Seized Goods and Vehicles in Smuggling Cases in Türkiye: Release, Confiscation, Objection (Law No. 5607)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/seized-vehicles-goods-smuggling-law-5607-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a vehicle used in a smuggling offence is, as a rule, seized by an annotation on its register; a vehicle impounded in one of three cases is returned if security equal to its value… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kacakcilikta-el-konulan-esya-ve-tasit-5607-sayili-kanun
  - Q: How can a vehicle seized in a smuggling case in Türkiye be recovered against security?
    A: Under Turkish law (Law No. 5607 art. 10/2), an impounded vehicle is returned if the owner delivers security equal to the vehicle’s value (for a land vehicle, its comprehensive insurance value) to the customs administration within thirty days of the date of impoundment; otherwise it is liquidated without waiting for the outcome of the proceedings. Impoundment is possible in only three cases: a hidden compartment, a vehicle not registered in Türkiye, or repeated use for smuggling while proceedings are pending. In other cases the rule is an annotation on the register.
  - Q: When is a vehicle carrying smuggled goods confiscated under Law No. 5607 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, confiscation of the vehicle requires one of the three conditions in art. 13/1: the smuggled goods were carried in a hidden compartment; the goods form the whole or the predominant part of the vehicle’s load, or their carriage requires the use of that vehicle; or the goods are prohibited from entering or leaving Türkiye or are harmful to public or environmental health. In addition, the vehicle must not belong to a bona fide third party. The court may refrain from confiscation where it would be inequitable in relation to the offence (Turkish Criminal Code arts. 54/1 and 54/3).
  - Q: Is a vehicle used for smuggling confiscated under Turkish law if it belongs to someone else?
    A: As a rule, no, if the vehicle belongs to a bona fide third party: under Turkish law, art. 54/1 of the Criminal Code makes confiscation conditional on the property not belonging to a bona fide third party. In 2024 the 7th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation upheld, having regard also to the goods not forming the predominant part of the load and to equity, a decision not to confiscate a company-owned tractor unit in which the driver carried smuggled cigarettes. In 2017 the Constitutional Court held that continuing to impound a coach after the good faith of its owner and of its financial lessee had been established violated the right to property. The outcome may differ where the vehicle belonged to the defendant at the offence date and was sold later; a 2026 decision of the same Chamber then referred to confiscation of the insurance value.
  - Q: If the defendant is acquitted in a smuggling case in Türkiye, are the proceeds of liquidated goods returned?
    A: Yes, if return is ordered. Under Turkish law (Law No. 5607 art. 16/2), the sale proceeds are paid to the right holder with statutory interest for the period from the date of seizure to the date of return; if the goods were destroyed, their value is paid from the general budget. In a 2025 decision the 12th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation accepted that, where the sale proceeds fall below the market price of the goods at the date of seizure, the difference may be claimed in an action for compensation for protective measures. In one decision, however, the 7th Criminal Chamber removed the reference to interest from the criminal court’s judgment without stating why.
  - Q: How and within what time can a seizure decision in a smuggling investigation be challenged under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, a person whose property has been seized may at any time ask the judge for a decision on the matter (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 127/4) and may request its return (art. 131). An objection against the judge’s seizure decision or a judge’s refusal of a request for return is made to the authority that gave the decision within two weeks of the day the decision is learned of (art. 268/1). An objection against a decision of a criminal judgeship of peace is reviewed, where there is more than one judgeship in that place, by the judgeship next in number. Smuggled goods that may be confiscated, however, may not be returned (Law No. 5607 art. 16/1).
- [Unfulfilled Export Commitment under Inward Processing in Türkiye: Duties, Article 238 Customs Fine, Objection](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/inward-processing-export-commitment-breach-turkiye): Under Turkish law: how duties secured by collateral are collected when the export commitment under an inward processing certificate or permit is not fulfilled in time, the fine under Article 238 of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/dahilde-isleme-rejiminde-ihracat-taahhudunun-yerine-getirilmemesi
  - Q: What happens if the export commitment under an inward processing authorisation certificate is not fulfilled in time?
    A: The commitment is closed with sanctions at the closing stage. Under Article 45 of the Inward Processing Regime Communiqué, the duties secured by collateral at importation for the unexported portion are collected as of the import date under the Customs Law and Law No. 6183; in addition, an administrative fine is imposed under Article 238 of the Customs Law.
  - Q: When must the application to close the commitment be made?
    A: For an inward processing authorisation certificate, at the latest within three months of the end of the certificate period to the relevant regional directorate; for an inward processing permit, within three months of the end of the permit period to the customs administration (Communiqué Art. 37).
  - Q: Is the Article 238 fine always twice the duty-paid value?
    A: No. The rule is twice the duty-paid value of the goods (Art. 238/1-a). But where the regime has been breached yet the goods are found not to have been removed from customs supervision contrary to the legislation, the fine equals interest calculated on the customs duties at the late-payment surcharge rate (Art. 238/1-c). In that case, if the goods are not assigned to a customs-approved treatment or use within sixty days of notification, a further fine equal to the customs duties is imposed (Art. 238/2).
  - Q: Where is an objection to closure with sanctions lodged?
    A: The holder of an inward processing authorisation certificate may object in writing to the Ministry of Trade (Directorate General of Exports) within one month of notification of the closure with sanctions. For inward processing permits, the objection route under Article 242 of the Customs Law applies (Communiqué Art. 38/12). The fifteen-day Article 242 objection period against the tax and penalty decisions notified by the customs administration must be observed separately.
  - Q: Why are the duties sometimes demanded by additional assessment and sometimes by a payment-period notice?
    A: According to the Council of State, the decisive factor is the status of the collateral. If the collateral is still held by the administration, the duties are pursued by setting a due date under Article 37 of Law No. 6183 and from the collateral under Article 56. If the breach is found after the commitment was closed and the collateral returned, the duties are assessed and notified under the Customs Law and the Article 242 objection route opens. There is a dissenting opinion within the Council of State against this distinction.

### Criminal Law

- [Aggravated Fraud via IT Systems and Banks in Türkiye: Penalty and the Line with Theft (TCK Art. 158/1-f)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/aggravated-fraud-information-systems-banks-turkiye): Under Turkish law: the penalty for aggravated fraud committed by using information systems, banks or credit institutions as instruments (Turkish Criminal Code art. 158/1-f), its difference from… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/nitelikli-dolandiricilik-tck-158-bilisim-sistemleri-ve-banka-araciligiyla
  - Q: What is the penalty for aggravated fraud under art. 158/1-f of the Turkish Criminal Code?
    A: For aggravated fraud committed by using information systems, banks or credit institutions as instruments, Turkish law provides for three to ten years’ imprisonment and a judicial fine; under this sub-paragraph the minimum term is four years and the judicial fine may not be less than twice the benefit obtained. If the act is committed by three or more persons the penalty is increased by one half, and if within the activities of an organisation it is doubled. Reductions such as effective repentance and art. 158/4 may also apply.
  - Q: Is fraud committed through an online advertisement aggravated fraud under art. 158/1-f of the Turkish Criminal Code?
    A: In a 2021 decision, the Court of Cassation’s Criminal General Assembly held, in a case where a complainant who saw an online sales advertisement was persuaded by telephone and transferred money to a bank account, that both the use of information systems as an instrument and the use of press and broadcasting means were present, and held that sub-paragraph (f), which carries the heavier penalty, should apply. Whether the system was used as an instrument of the deceit is assessed separately in each case.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a person who poses as a bank officer, obtains a password and transfers the money punished for fraud or for theft through information systems?
    A: Court of Cassation practice on this point is not settled. In its decision of 07.10.2025, the 2nd Criminal Chamber held that the victim was deceived only into giving information and that the money was transferred without the victim’s knowledge, and treated the act as theft through information systems (art. 142/2-e of the Turkish Criminal Code). In its decision of 02.02.2026, given by majority, the same chamber treated a similar case as aggravated fraud. Both files have been sent to the Criminal General Assembly.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is withdrawing money from an ATM with another person’s bank card aggravated fraud?
    A: As a rule, no. Where a benefit is obtained by using another person’s card without consent and without anyone being deceived, the offence of misuse of bank cards under art. 245/1 of the Turkish Criminal Code arises. In its decision of 14.01.2026, given by majority, the Criminal General Assembly treated a withdrawal with a card forgotten in an ATM as this offence. Where the cardholder is at the ATM and possession of the card continues, the General Assembly stated that the bank card offence does not arise.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, how much does paying back the loss reduce the penalty for aggravated fraud?
    A: Under art. 168 of the Turkish Criminal Code, if the loss is fully made good by returning the property in kind or by compensation before prosecution begins, the penalty is reduced by up to two thirds; if it is made good after prosecution begins but before judgment, by up to one half. In the case of partial payment, the victim’s consent is additionally required for effective repentance to apply. The court sets the reduction within these limits.
- [Aggravated Injury by Result (Turkish Criminal Code Art. 87): Bone Fractures, Danger to Life, Higher Sentences](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/aggravated-injury-bone-fracture-turkish-criminal-code-87): Under Turkish law, Criminal Code art. 87 on intentional injury resulting in a bone fracture, danger to life, a permanent facial scar or death: one- and two-fold increases, increases of up to one half… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/neticesi-sebebiyle-agirlasmis-yaralama-tck-87
  - Q: How much does a bone fracture increase the penalty for intentional injury?
    A: Under Criminal Code art. 87/3 the penalty is increased by up to one half according to the effect of the fracture on vital functions. The Criminal General Assembly found a 1/4 increase reasonable for a moderate (2) fracture, while the 1st Criminal Chamber considered a 1/3 increase excessive for the same degree.
  - Q: Who determines the degree of a bone fracture?
    A: The degree is determined by the forensic medical report: mild (1), moderate (2-3), severe (4-5-6). If the report does not state the degree in figures, the Court of Cassation reverses judgments rendered without obtaining a new report for incomplete investigation.
  - Q: If there is both a bone fracture and danger to life, is the penalty increased twice?
    A: No. According to the 1st Criminal Chamber, the offender is liable for the ground of increase producing the most serious result; once art. 87/1-d is applied for danger to life, no further increase under art. 87/3 may be made. The fracture is instead taken into account by setting the basic penalty above the minimum.
  - Q: What do the four- and six-year minimums in Criminal Code art. 87/1 mean?
    A: The penalty reached after the one-fold increase may not be less than four years in cases under art. 86/1 and six years in cases under art. 86/3 (current text). According to the Criminal General Assembly, this sentence is a minimum rather than a separate ground of increase; if the indictment cites art. 87/1-d, the absence of the last sentence does not require additional defence. The decision was adopted by majority. These minimums were raised from three and five years by Law No. 7550, in force since 4 June 2025; for offences committed before that date, the more favourable law is identified by comparison.
  - Q: If the injured person later dies, under which provision is the offender liable?
    A: Criminal Code art. 87/4 applies if the offender acted with intent to injure, the injury went beyond what simple medical intervention can cure, there is a causal link between the act and the death, and the offender acted at least negligently as to the death. Article 87/4 does not apply to injuries curable by simple medical intervention; if the offender intended death, the provisions on intentional killing come into play.
- [Challenging a Traffic Fine and Drink-Driving in Türkiye: 15-Day Period, Judgeship of Peace, Licence](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/challenging-traffic-fine-drink-driving): Challenging a traffic administrative fine under the Misdemeanours Law (Law No. 5326 art. 27): the fifteen-day period from service or pronouncement, electronic notification, force majeure, petition to… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/trafik-cezasina-itiraz-ve-alkollu-arac
  - Q: How long do I have to challenge a traffic fine?
    A: Fifteen days at the latest from service or pronouncement of the decision; if no application is made within that period, the decision becomes final (Law No. 5326 art. 27). Electronic notification is deemed made at the end of the fifth day after it reaches the electronic address (Law No. 5326 art. 26). In case of force majeure, an application may be made within seven days of its ceasing.
  - Q: Where do I challenge a traffic fine?
    A: By a petition to the criminal judgeship of peace, stating clearly the details of the decision and the evidence relied on (Law No. 5326 art. 27). Decisions of lack of jurisdiction have been given in actions brought before administrative courts or civil courts of first instance; an application made in time to the wrong authority does not, according to the Court of Cassation, cause loss of rights (Law No. 5271 art. 264).
  - Q: If I pay the fine, do I lose the right to challenge it?
    A: No. Under the Misdemeanours Law, payment does not affect the right to apply for a remedy against the decision; payment within the payment period attracts a 25% reduction (Law No. 5326 art. 17). For traffic fines, the payment period is one month from service of the record (Highway Traffic Law art. 115).
  - Q: What is the sanction for drink-driving?
    A: A driver of a private car above 0.50 per mille is given an administrative fine and has their licence withdrawn for six months on the first occasion; for vehicles other than private cars, the lower limit is applied as 0.21 per mille. Within five years counting back from the latest violation, the licence is withdrawn for two years on the second occasion and for five years each time on the third and subsequent occasions (Highway Traffic Law art. 48). Above 1.00 per mille, criminal proceedings for endangering traffic safety are also brought (Law No. 5237 art. 179). Fine amounts are not given here because they are increased every year by the revaluation rate.
  - Q: What happens if I refuse the breath test?
    A: A driver who does not submit to the test for the police to determine alcohol or drugs is given an administrative fine and has their licence withdrawn for five years. A driver involved in an accident with injury or death, or in a property-damage accident attended by the police, must be examined; if they dispute the device reading or refuse the device test, samples such as blood, saliva or urine are taken at a health facility (Highway Traffic Law art. 48).
  - Q: When can I get my licence back?
    A: When the withdrawal period has expired; however, for the licence to be returned, all administrative fines imposed under the Highway Traffic Law must have been collected (Highway Traffic Law art. 48/13). Those whose licence is withdrawn a second time within five years undergo a driver behaviour improvement training, and those whose licence is withdrawn three or more times undergo a psycho-technical assessment and an examination by a psychiatrist (Highway Traffic Law art. 48/11). Driving before the licence is returned attracts a separate administrative fine (Highway Traffic Law art. 36).
- [Commercial Blackmail (Turkish Penal Code 107): Elements, Threats to Report or Disclose, vs. Threat and Robbery](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/blackmail-commercial-life): Blackmail in commercial life (Penal Code art. 107): definition and two forms (art. 107/1 coercion into an unjust benefit by invoking a right/duty; art. 107/2 obtaining a benefit by threatening to… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ticari-santaj-sucu-tck-107
  - Q: Is saying “pay your debt or I will report you” blackmail?
    A: Turning one’s genuine right to report an actual illegality to the authorities into a tool of pressure to obtain an unjust benefit may constitute blackmail. The line between blackmail and a genuine, proportionate legal warning to collect a real debt is drawn on the facts of the case.
  - Q: Must money be demanded for blackmail?
    A: No. According to the Court of Cassation the concept of “benefit” covers not only material gains but any situation advantageous to the offender or a third party — for instance, escaping an investigation, forcing a contract to be signed, or putting a competitor in difficulty.
  - Q: What is the difference between blackmail and threat?
    A: Threat is the announcement of a future harm. Blackmail ties that threat to obtaining a benefit or coercing specific conduct. Without a benefit aim, a threat to disclose matters harmful to honour and reputation constitutes ordinary threat.
  - Q: Is blackmail a complaint-based offence?
    A: No. As a rule blackmail is investigated and prosecuted ex officio by the public prosecutor; the victim’s withdrawal of complaint does not by itself end the case.
  - Q: What is the penalty for blackmail?
    A: Imprisonment from one to three years and a judicial fine of up to five thousand days, applied together. The actual sentence is set according to the manner of commission, the benefit sought and other circumstances.
  - Q: How should I document blackmail messages?
    A: Preserve the threatening and demanding messages, e-mails, voice recordings and correspondence together with screenshots, and submit them to the prosecutor with information identifying the account/line. The integrity of the evidence is decisive in correctly classifying the offence.
- [Deferred Verdict (HAGB) and Criminal Records in Türkiye: Conditions, Supervision, Appeal, Erasure of Records](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/deferral-announcement-verdict-criminal-records): Deferral of the announcement of the verdict under Code of Criminal Procedure art. 231: the two-year limit, absence of a prior conviction for an intentional offence, compensation of material damage… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hagb-ve-adli-sicil-kaydi
  - Q: Is HAGB entered in the criminal record?
    A: No. A HAGB decision is recorded in a dedicated system and may be requested only by the public prosecutor, the judge or the court in connection with an investigation or prosecution (Law No. 5271 art. 231/13; Law No. 5352 art. 6/1). If the supervision period is breached and the judgment is announced and becomes final, the conviction is entered in the criminal record.
  - Q: How long is the HAGB supervision period?
    A: Five years; the limitation period for prosecution is suspended during this period, and probation obligations not exceeding one year may be imposed (Law No. 5271 art. 231/8). For HAGB decisions concerning children, the supervision period is three years (Law No. 5395 art. 23/1).
  - Q: Is a HAGB decision challenged by objection or by appeal?
    A: An appeal (istinaf) lies against HAGB decisions rendered on or after 1.6.2024; if the decision was rendered by a regional court of appeal or the Court of Cassation acting as a first-instance court, an appeal on points of law is available (Law No. 5271 art. 231/12). The objection procedure continues for HAGB decisions rendered before that date (Law No. 5271 provisional art. 6/2).
  - Q: What happens if a new offence is committed during the supervision period?
    A: If a new intentional offence is committed or the obligations are breached during the supervision period, the court announces the judgment; for a defendant unable to fulfil the obligations, it may also order that part of the sentence not be executed, suspend it or convert it into an alternative sanction (Law No. 5271 art. 231/11). If no new intentional offence is committed and the obligations are complied with, the case is dismissed (Law No. 5271 art. 231/10).
  - Q: When is a criminal record erased?
    A: Upon execution of the sentence, expiry of the limitation period for execution, a general amnesty, or withdrawal of the complaint or effective repentance eliminating the conviction with all its consequences, the entry is deleted from the criminal record and transferred to the archive (Law No. 5352 art. 9). The archive record is deleted entirely after five years for other convictions, and for convictions causing a forfeiture of rights under special laws after fifteen years with a decision restoring forfeited rights or thirty years without it (Law No. 5352 art. 12/1).
  - Q: How long must one wait for the restoration of forfeited rights?
    A: As a rule, three years must have elapsed since the execution of the sentence was completed, with no new offence and good conduct during that time; if execution ended for a legal reason other than a general amnesty or effective repentance, five years from finality are required (Law No. 5352 art. 13/A). According to the Court of Cassation, execution means execution of the entire sentence, including the supervision period of conditional release.
- [Effective Repentance in Drug Trafficking in Türkiye: Early Disclosure, Later Information (Criminal Code 192)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/effective-repentance-drug-trafficking-turkiye-tck-192): Under Turkish law, effective repentance in drug trafficking (Criminal Code art. 192): no penalty for disclosure made before the official authorities learn of the offence, and a reduction of one… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-06. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/uyusturucu-ticaretinde-etkin-pismanlik-tck-192
  - Q: Does effective repentance remove the penalty entirely in drug trafficking?
    A: Only in the case in Criminal Code art. 192/1: if a person who participated in the offence informs the authorities of the accomplices or the place where the substance is stored or manufactured before the official authorities learn of the offence, and the information leads to a capture or the seizure of the substance, no penalty is imposed. Cooperation after the offence has been discovered does not remove the penalty but reduces it under art. 192/3.
  - Q: By how much is the sentence of a defendant who gives information after arrest reduced?
    A: By one quarter to one half according to the nature of the assistance (art. 192/3). The rate is not fixed; the 10th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation stated that for a defendant who revealed the offences of persons against whom there was no evidence, the reduction may be set even at the upper limit.
  - Q: Is there a reduction if the information produces no result?
    A: The Criminal General Assembly requires the information to be true, effective for the outcome and useful; vague, fictitious or misleading information is not enough. However, where true and sufficient information produced no result because no effective investigation was conducted, the Assembly held that this adverse consequence cannot be imposed on the defendant.
  - Q: Does confessing one’s own offence count as effective repentance?
    A: Not always. If the offence is already proven by other evidence, a confession on its own may not be considered sufficient. The Criminal General Assembly accepted that where the other evidence was not sufficient to prove the offence, a confession at the trial stage served to reveal the offence.
  - Q: Must the court investigate the persons named?
    A: The 10th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation treated giving judgment without inquiring into the outcome of the investigation against the person named as a ground for quashing. The 8th Criminal Chamber, on the other hand, accepted that if the statements are later found to be true, effective repentance may be applied through a retrial.
- [Effective Repentance in Embezzlement under Turkish Law: Penal Code Art. 248, Reductions, Full Compensation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/effective-repentance-embezzlement-turkiye-tck-248): Effective repentance in embezzlement under Turkish Penal Code art. 248: reductions of two thirds before the investigation, one half before the prosecution and one third before judgment where the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-06. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/zimmet-sucunda-etkin-pismanlik-tck-248
  - Q: How large is the effective repentance reduction in embezzlement?
    A: If the embezzled property is returned in kind or the loss fully compensated before the investigation begins, two thirds of the penalty is reduced; if this happens voluntarily before the prosecution begins, one half; and if it happens before judgment, one third (Turkish Penal Code art. 248). The rates are fixed; the court determines the stage at which the return or compensation took place and applies the corresponding rate.
  - Q: Does paying part of the loss reduce the penalty?
    A: Art. 248 requires the return of the property in kind or full compensation of the loss and provides no separate rate for partial payment. The 5th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation has treated the application of effective repentance to a defendant who did not compensate the whole embezzled amount as a ground for reversal.
  - Q: Must interest also be paid for effective repentance?
    A: According to the 5th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation, default interest or late-payment surcharges, the loss suffered by the administration and lost income are not taken into account in determining the value of the embezzled property; the value at the time of the offence is decisive. In embezzlement by use, however, the subject of the offence is the yield of the money; effective repentance does not apply unless the yield is paid.
  - Q: Does effective repentance apply if the institution recovers the loss by salary deductions or enforcement?
    A: The Court of Cassation does not treat money collected through enforcement as a voluntary return, and in one case it held the application of effective repentance unlawful where the loss had been made good through salary deductions made by way of enforcement. By contrast, a payment made directly into the institution’s account without the pressure of enforcement must be assessed after verifying the authenticity of the bank receipt.
  - Q: Does a payment made after a reversal by the Court of Cassation reduce the penalty?
    A: According to a decision of the 5th Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation, it does if made before the new judgment: in a case where the loss was fully paid after the reversal judgment but before judgment, it was held that a one-third reduction must be applied under the last sentence of art. 248/2.
- [Effective Repentance in Theft under Turkish Law: Art. 168, Partial Return, Property Damage, Breach of Trust](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/effective-repentance-theft-turkish-criminal-code-168): Under Turkish law, effective repentance under Criminal Code art. 168 in theft, damage to property and breach of trust: reductions of up to two-thirds at the investigation stage and up to one half at… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-06. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hirsizlik-sucunda-etkin-pismanlik-tck-168
  - Q: How large is the effective repentance reduction for theft?
    A: If the loss is fully redressed before the indictment is accepted, up to two-thirds of the penalty is reduced; if it is redressed after the prosecution starts but before judgment, up to one half (Criminal Code art. 168/1-2). According to the Criminal General Assembly, the reduction under the first paragraph must be more than 1/2.
  - Q: If the police find the stolen property and return it to the owner, does effective repentance apply?
    A: If the property was seized by the police and returned without the offender showing its location or making any other contribution, the Court of Cassation does not apply effective repentance. The offender himself showing where he hid the property and thus securing its return may, however, count as effective repentance.
  - Q: Can effective repentance apply if my family pays the loss?
    A: If the defendant has at least made statements or engaged in conduct showing remorse or consent to the payment and has not opposed it, redress by third persons on the defendant’s behalf may count as effective repentance depending on the circumstances. Payment made without the defendant’s knowledge or against his will is not enough.
  - Q: I paid part of the loss; what if the victim does not accept it?
    A: For partial return or compensation, the victim’s consent is required (Criminal Code art. 168/4). Without consent, no reduction can be based on the partial payment; if the remaining loss is fully redressed before judgment, it is assessed under the second paragraph.
  - Q: If I pay at the appeal stage after the judgment, will I get a reduction?
    A: As a rule, no; payment after the first-instance judgment does not lead to a reduction. If the judgment is reversed, payment before the new judgment is taken into account; if judgment was rendered without giving the opportunity to a defendant who declared that he wished to redress the loss, payment at the appeal stage may also be regarded as made before judgment.
  - Q: Is settlement (uzlaşma) available for theft?
    A: Under Code of Criminal Procedure art. 253/1-b, theft under Criminal Code art. 141 is subject to settlement; art. 142 is not on the list. Breach of trust (except the third paragraph) is also listed, and damage to property under Criminal Code art. 151/1, being prosecuted upon complaint, is subject to settlement.
- [Fair Trial and Press Freedom for Journalists in Türkiye: Detention, Sources, Access Bans, AYM and ECtHR](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/fair-trial-rights-press-freedom-journalists): Fair trial rights and press freedom in proceedings against journalists in Türkiye: Constitution arts. 26 and 28, Press Law No. 5187 (source protection, time limits for prosecution, news websites and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/gazetecilikte-adil-yargilanma-hakki-ve-basin-ozgurlugu
  - Q: Can a journalist be compelled to disclose a source?
    A: No. The owner of a periodical, the responsible editor and the author cannot be compelled to disclose any news source, including information and documents, or to testify on this matter; non-disclosure cannot be held against the journalist.
  - Q: Can a journalist be detained solely because of a news report?
    A: Detention requires concrete evidence of strong suspicion and a ground for detention. The Constitutional Court and the ECtHR have held that detentions ordered without any concrete fact other than published reports violate the right to liberty and press freedom.
  - Q: Is it a crime to report on an ongoing investigation?
    A: As a rule, no. Reporting on investigative and trial proceedings without exceeding the limits of news reporting is not an offence (Law No. 5237 art. 285/6). The offence of breach of confidentiality requires that the disclosure violate the presumption of innocence, confidentiality of communications or privacy, or be capable of preventing the discovery of the material truth.
  - Q: What is the time limit for prosecuting press offences?
    A: Four months for daily periodicals and news websites and six months for other printed works; for news websites the period runs from the criminal complaint concerning the report and may not exceed the limitation period for prosecution.
  - Q: How can an access ban on a news report be challenged?
    A: Decisions concerning catalogue offences may be challenged under the Code of Criminal Procedure (Law No. 5651 art. 8) within two weeks of learning of the decision (Law No. 5271 art. 268). The provision on blocking for personality rights was annulled, with effect from 10.10.2024. After the objection is rejected, an individual application to the Constitutional Court may be lodged within thirty days.
  - Q: What are the time limits for applying to the Constitutional Court and the ECtHR?
    A: An individual application to the Constitutional Court must be lodged within thirty days of exhaustion of remedies (Law No. 6216 art. 47/5). The time limit for applying to the ECtHR is four months for final domestic decisions given after 01.02.2022.
- [Indirect Report Not False Accusation: Telling a Site Manager Who Alerts Police (Turkish Court of Cassation)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/false-accusation-report-to-competent-authority-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Criminal Chambers of the Court of Cassation unanimously held that a defendant who falsely accused another of theft before a construction site manager, rather than… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yetkili-makama-yapilmayan-suc-isnadi-iftira-degildir
  - Q: What are the elements of the offence of false accusation?
    A: Under Turkish law, the offender imputes an unlawful act to a person knowing that the person did not commit it, makes the accusation by a report or complaint to the competent authorities or through the press and broadcasting, and aims to have an investigation, prosecution or administrative sanction initiated against that person (Penal Code art. 267/1). According to the Assembly of Criminal Chambers, the imputed act need not be an offence; the offence is committed with direct intent and this specific aim.
  - Q: Which are the competent authorities for false accusation?
    A: Relying on Code of Criminal Procedure art. 158, the Assembly listed the Chief Public Prosecutor’s Office, the law enforcement authorities, the governorship, the district governorship, the courts and, abroad, embassies and consulates; it did not mention the institution’s administration under art. 158/4.
  - Q: Is it false accusation if the person who heard the accusation informs the police?
    A: According to the Assembly of Criminal Chambers, no. The report must be made directly to the competent authority; it is not enough for the authorities to learn of the accusation indirectly through an intermediary.
  - Q: If there is no false accusation, can the offence of insult arise?
    A: It can; however, for words uttered in the victim’s absence the Assembly required communication with at least three persons. Since that condition was not met in this case, insult was not committed either.
- [Insult and Social Media Insults in Türkiye: Elements, Publicity, Complaint, Prepayment, Cassation Criteria](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/insult-offence-social-media): The offence of insult under the Turkish Penal Code (arts. 125-131): imputing a concrete act or fact, swearing and the line between insult and merely rude speech; publicity in insults on social media… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hakaret-sucu-ve-sosyal-medyada-hakaret
  - Q: Is every insult on social media considered public?
    A: No. Publicity requires that the content be visible to an indeterminate number of people and open to everyone without restriction. The Court of Cassation has held that no increase for publicity applies to a comment in a closed Facebook group or to a message sent to the victim through a phone application.
  - Q: Is an insult sent by message (SMS or messaging application) an offence?
    A: Yes. An insult committed through an audio, written or visual message addressed to the victim carries the same penalty as a face-to-face insult (Law No. 5237 art. 125/2). However, since such messages are not public, the penalty is not increased for publicity.
  - Q: What is the complaint period for insult?
    A: The complaint must be filed within six months from the day the act and the offender became known; for insult, this period may in any event not exceed two years from the date of the act (Law No. 5237 art. 73/1-2). The two-year cap does not apply to files that had reached the investigation or prosecution stage as at 14.11.2024. Insult against a public official because of their duty is not subject to complaint.
  - Q: Is mediation available for insult?
    A: No. Under the current Law No. 5271 art. 253/3, mediation is not available for the offence of insult. Prepayment applies instead, except for insult against a public official because of their duty; if the suspect pays the determined amount within ten days of notification, no public prosecution is brought (Law No. 5237 art. 75).
  - Q: What is the penalty for insult?
    A: The basic penalty is imprisonment from three months to two years or a judicial fine (Law No. 5237 art. 125/1). For insults against a public official because of their duty, because of beliefs and opinions or by reference to sacred values, the minimum penalty may not be less than one year; committing the insult publicly increases the penalty by one sixth (Law No. 5237 art. 125/3-4).
  - Q: Is a penalty imposed for reciprocal insults?
    A: Depending on the nature of the incident, the court may reduce the penalty for both or one of the parties by up to one third or decide not to impose a penalty (Law No. 5237 art. 129/3). The Court of Cassation accepts that a defendant who insulted first may also benefit from this reduction depending on the circumstances.
- [Intentional Injury (Turkish Criminal Code Art. 86): Minor and Aggravated Forms, Complaint, Settlement, Penalty](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/intentional-injury-turkish-criminal-code-86): Under Turkish law, intentional injury under Criminal Code art. 86: elements, the complaint requirement and settlement for injury remediable by simple medical intervention, aggravated forms and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kasten-yaralama-sucu-tck-86
  - Q: What is the penalty for minor intentional injury?
    A: If the effect of the injury is so minor that it can be remedied by simple medical intervention, Criminal Code art. 86/2 applies: imprisonment of six months to one year and six months or a judicial fine. Where the offence is committed against a woman, the lower limit is nine months. These ranges apply from 4 June 2025; for earlier acts, the former range (four months to one year) applies.
  - Q: What is the time limit for filing a complaint for intentional injury?
    A: For minor injury, which depends on a complaint, the period is six months from the day on which the act and the offender become known and may not exceed the limitation period (Criminal Code art. 73). The basic offence and the aggravated forms do not depend on a complaint; the six-month complaint period does not apply to them.
  - Q: If I withdraw my complaint, will the case be dropped?
    A: Only for minor injury that depends on a complaint; a defendant who does not accept the withdrawal is not affected. In the basic offence and in injury committed with a weapon, against a spouse or because of a public duty, no complaint is required, so withdrawal does not terminate the case.
  - Q: Is settlement possible for intentional injury against a spouse?
    A: No. Injury against a spouse falls under art. 86/3-a; Code of Criminal Procedure art. 253 subjects intentional injury to settlement “except the third paragraph”. The Court of Cassation reversed, in the interest of the law, a decision terminating such a case after settlement.
  - Q: Which court hears an intentional injury case?
    A: As a rule, the criminal court of first instance; the provisions on the prosecution of children are reserved. If the act is considered possibly to be attempted intentional killing, the high criminal court is competent.
  - Q: Who decides whether an injury can be remedied by simple medical intervention?
    A: This is established by the forensic examination report, which the court assesses. Where reports conflict, the Court of Cassation regards giving judgment without a report from the relevant specialised department of the Forensic Medicine Institute as incomplete examination.
- [Metabolite Findings in Drug-Use Offences: The Evidentiary Force of Blood and Urine Tests](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/metabolite-findings-drug-use-offences): The evidentiary force of metabolite findings in the Turkish drug-use offence (Criminal Code art. 191), through the case law of the 10th Criminal Chamber: the effect of positive tests (THC, 6-MAM… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/uyusturucu-kullanma-sucunda-metabolit
  - Q: Is a metabolite finding alone sufficient for conviction?
    A: Under the 10th Criminal Chamber’s practice, detection of the substance or its metabolite in a sample taken at the time of the offence establishes use by technical means and, as a rule, suffices for conviction — even if nothing is seized on the person. Defences must be substantiated with concrete evidence.
  - Q: What if the test is clean but the person confessed?
    A: Where no substance is seized and the panel is entirely negative, a bare confession cannot ground conviction; acquittal is required under in dubio pro reo.
  - Q: What is the penalty, and is prosecution immediate?
    A: Two to five years’ imprisonment (art. 191/1). As a rule, prosecution is deferred for five years with at least one year of probation (and treatment if needed); compliance leads to a non-prosecution decision.
  - Q: What happens if a test is positive during probation?
    A: Use during the deferral period is a violation (art. 191/4-c) and the original prosecution is instituted; under art. 191/5 the new use is not prosecuted separately.
  - Q: How does effective remorse work?
    A: No penalty is imposed on a user who, before the authorities learn of it, discloses from whom, where and when they obtained the substance, facilitating apprehension or seizure (art. 192/2), or who requests treatment before an investigation is opened (art. 192/4).
  - Q: Can Forensic Medicine reports be challenged?
    A: Yes — on the scope of the panel, the chain of custody, the type/level of the metabolite and its relation to the offence date; re-analysis and expert opinions may be requested. Irregular sampling raises an unlawful-evidence issue.
- [Provocation and Self-Defence in Intentional Injury: Reduction, First Unjust Act, Fights, Excess (TCK 29, 25)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/unjust-provocation-self-defence-intentional-injury-turkiye): Under Turkish law, unjust provocation (Criminal Code art. 29) and legitimate self-defence (art. 25) in intentional injury: a reduction of 1/4 to 3/4, minimum provocation where the first unjust act in… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kasten-yaralamada-haksiz-tahrik-ve-mesru-mudafaa
  - Q: How much is the unjust provocation reduction in intentional injury?
    A: Under Criminal Code art. 29, between one quarter and three quarters of the penalty for intentional injury is deducted. The judge sets the rate according to the gravity of the unjust act and its effect on the offender’s will; in fights where it cannot be established from whom the first unjust act came, the Court of Cassation applies the minimum rate.
  - Q: What happens if it is unclear who started the fight?
    A: Under the principle that doubt benefits the defendant, the doubt is interpreted in the defendant’s favour and unjust provocation is applied at the minimum rate. Where the first unjust act cannot be established at all, the 1st Criminal Chamber of the Court of Cassation holds that the conditions of self-defence and of exceeding the limits are not met; where evidence such as video footage shows who started the physical attack, self-defence is assessed for the party who responded.
  - Q: If I started the fight, can I benefit from unjust provocation?
    A: As a rule, no: an offender who provoked the other party by his unjust act cannot claim to have acted under provocation because of the reaction he met. If the other party’s reaction was clearly disproportionate and excessive, that reaction may constitute provocation in the offender’s favour.
  - Q: What is the difference between self-defence and unjust provocation?
    A: Self-defence is a ground of justification and leads to acquittal; unjust provocation does not remove the offence but only reduces the penalty. Self-defence means repelling an unjust and present attack at that moment and proportionately, whereas provocation means committing an offence under the influence of anger or severe distress caused by an unjust act.
  - Q: If I responded more than necessary out of fear, will I be punished?
    A: If, while the conditions of self-defence existed, proportion was exceeded because of excusable excitement, fear or panic, no penalty is imposed (Criminal Code art. 27/2) and a decision that there is no room for a penalty is rendered. If the limits were exceeded for reasons such as anger, this provision does not apply; unjust provocation may, however, come into question.
- [Renting Out a Bank Account (IBAN) in Türkiye: The Offence and Effective Remorse at the Investigation Stage](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/renting-out-bank-account-effective-remorse): The criminal liability of letting fraud or crime proceeds pass through your own bank account (IBAN): aggravated fraud (Turkish Criminal Code Art. 158/1-f, four to ten years' imprisonment), the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/iban-kiralama-ve-sorusturmada-etkin-pismanlik
  - Q: I rented out my IBAN but only withdrew and handed over the money; is it still a crime?
    A: Yes. Letting the money pass through your account makes the bank a tool of the offence, so the act may be assessed as aggravated fraud (Art. 158/1-f). The “I only withdrew it” defence does not, on its own, remove liability; it depends on whether intent is present.
  - Q: How much reduction do I get if I pay the loss at the investigation stage?
    A: If you fully compensate the loss (principal) before the indictment is accepted — i.e. at the investigation stage — the penalty is reduced by up to two thirds under Art. 168/1. If you compensate after prosecution begins, the reduction is limited to up to one half.
  - Q: I paid only the principal, not the interest; can I benefit from effective remorse?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation, full payment of the principal suffices for effective remorse; not also paying interest (supplementary loss) does not bar the reduction. However, where payment is partial, the victim's consent is required.
  - Q: If there are several victims, how should I compensate the loss?
    A: Effective remorse applies in respect of the victim whose loss is compensated. To benefit fully, the loss of the relevant victim(s) must be fully compensated; for partial payment the victim's consent is required.
  - Q: What if I prove my account was used without my knowledge?
    A: Fraud is committed intentionally. If you can establish with concrete evidence that your account was seized and used outside your knowledge and will, acquittal may follow for lack of intent. Building this defence at the investigation stage is important.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, which court hears IBAN (account) renting cases?
    A: The current text of art. 12 of Law No. 5235 does not list aggravated fraud among the offences heard by the assize court; provisional art. 7 added by Law No. 7571 provides that files then pending before the assize court or under appeal continue under the old rules until a final judgment. Accordingly, IBAN renting cases filed afterwards are, as a rule, heard by the criminal court of first instance (asliye ceza mahkemesi); jurisdiction is assessed separately where more than ten years’ imprisonment is required. MASAK processes and account freezing may also arise from suspicious money movements.

### Administrative Law

- [Appeals in Afyonkarahisar Cases in Türkiye: Konya Regional Court of Appeal and Regional Administrative Court](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/afyonkarahisar-appeals-konya-regional-courts-turkiye): Under Turkish law, appeals against civil and criminal decisions in Afyonkarahisar go to the Konya Regional Court of Appeal, and appeals against administrative and tax decisions go to the Konya… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/afyonkarahisar-istinaf-konya-bolge-adliye-ve-bolge-idare-mahkemesi
  - Q: Which regional court of appeal hears appeals against a civil court decision given in Afyonkarahisar in Türkiye?
    A: The Konya Regional Court of Appeal. Its official jurisdiction page lists Konya, Afyonkarahisar, Aksaray and Karaman. The petition is not taken to Konya; it is lodged with the court that gave the decision or another local court, and the first-instance court forwards the file (Code of Civil Procedure art. 343).
  - Q: On what date is an appeal petition filed in Afyonkarahisar in Türkiye deemed lodged?
    A: In civil cases the appeal is deemed lodged on the date the petition is registered. Article 343(3) of the Code of Civil Procedure refers to article 118, and the Court of Cassation 3rd Civil Chamber therefore treated the registration date as the filing date. The time limit is two weeks from proper service of the judgment (art. 345).
  - Q: What is the time limit for appealing a criminal judgment of a court in Afyonkarahisar in Türkiye, and where is the appeal lodged?
    A: Two weeks from service of the judgment together with its reasons. The appeal is made by petition to, or by a statement before the clerk of, the court in Afyonkarahisar that gave the judgment (Code of Criminal Procedure art. 273(1)); the criminal chambers of the Konya Regional Court of Appeal review it.
  - Q: Which regional administrative court hears appeals against decisions of the Afyonkarahisar Administrative Court in Türkiye?
    A: The Konya Regional Administrative Court. By decision no. 1069 of 06.08.2025 of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors (Official Gazette, 12.08.2025), its jurisdiction covers the provinces of Konya, Afyonkarahisar, Aksaray, Karaman and Niğde. The time limit is thirty days from service of the decision (Administrative Procedure Law art. 45(1)).
  - Q: Is an appeal against an Afyonkarahisar administrative court decision invalid in Türkiye if the petition is mistakenly addressed to the Council of State?
    A: No. Under article 45(2) of the Administrative Procedure Law, files concerning decisions subject to appeal are sent to the regional administrative court regardless of how the petition is addressed or framed. The Council of State 13th Chamber rejected such a cassation request on jurisdictional grounds and sent the file to the administrative court that gave the decision, for transmission to the regional administrative court competent to hear the appeal. The time limit remains thirty days.
  - Q: Which court hears appeals in tax cases from Afyonkarahisar in Türkiye?
    A: According to the administrative judiciary guide of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors (file dated 18.09.2026 on its website), tax disputes in Afyonkarahisar are heard by the Konya Tax Court. Appeals against tax court decisions, like those against administrative court decisions, go to the Konya Regional Administrative Court (Administrative Procedure Law art. 45(1)).
- [Building, Facilities and Mining in Olive Groves in Türkiye: Law No. 3573, Prohibitions and Legal Remedies](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/olive-groves-facilities-mining-law-3573-turkiye): Under Turkish law, Law No. 3573 art. 20 prohibits facilities that leave chemical waste or emit dust and smoke within olive grove areas and within 3 kilometres of them; olive grove areas may not be… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/zeytinlik-sahalarda-yapi-tesis-ve-madencilik-3573-sayili-kanun
  - Q: Can a factory or other facility be built in or near an olive grove in Türkiye?
    A: As a rule, no. Under Turkish law (Law No. 3573 art. 20), no facility that leaves chemical waste or emits dust and smoke such as to hinder the development of olive groves may be built or operated within olive grove areas or within 3 kilometres of them. Olive oil factories and small-scale agricultural industry enterprises are subject to Ministry permission. In a 2025 decision the 4th Chamber of the Council of State required the court first to establish whether the facility is of that kind.
  - Q: What is an olive grove area under Law No. 3573 in Türkiye, and is a land registry entry enough?
    A: The Law does not define an olive grove area. The definition in the Regulation, which required at least 25 decares, was annulled by the 8th Chamber of the Council of State. On 19.02.2026 the same Chamber annulled, by majority, the relevant part of a Ministry letter stating that the Law also applies to land registered as an olive grove but bearing no olive trees; that decision may not yet be final. No single settled criterion appears in the decisions read.
  - Q: Is permission needed to cut down or uproot an olive tree in Türkiye, and what is the penalty?
    A: Under Turkish law, uprooting olive trees in olive grove areas requires Ministry permission based on technical grounds; a tree may not be cut down or uprooted unless there is a definite necessity (Law No. 3573 art. 20). Cutting or uprooting without permission attracts an administrative fine per tree. The amount in the text of the Law is sixty Turkish liras; administrative fines are applied as increased each year by the revaluation rate. As the provisions of the Law that were read show no separate remedy, on this article’s own inference the fifteen-day application under art. 27 of the Misdemeanours Law applies against the fine.
  - Q: Can mining be carried out in an olive grove area in Türkiye?
    A: According to the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, absent a separate statutory provision, mining in olive grove areas in Türkiye may be carried out only within the framework of Law No. 3573; the permission clause added to the Mining Regulation in 2022 was annulled on that ground. Those decisions rest on the text of art. 7 of the Mining Law before it was amended in 2025. Provisional article 45, added by Law No. 7554 of 19/7/2025, allows the Ministry to grant permission only in the areas listed in the annex to the Law, for mining aimed at the country’s electricity needs by those who were licence holders or royalty contractors on that date, and provided that no other area is available.
  - Q: Can an olive grove area within municipal boundaries be opened to development under Turkish law?
    A: Under Law No. 3573 art. 20, olive grove areas may not be reduced. The exception in the Law is where an olive grove area within municipal boundaries is brought within the zoning limits; in that case total construction, including infrastructure and social facilities, may not exceed 10% of the olive grove area. The article treats zoning plans that became final before its publication as valid; art. 24 of the Regulation explains that date as the publication of Law No. 4086 of 28.2.1995.
  - Q: What is the time limit for challenging a facility, mining permit or EIA decision affecting an olive grove in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an annulment action against an administrative act is, as a rule, brought within sixty days from the day following written notification (Law No. 2577 art. 7). Decisions taken as a result of environmental impact assessment and urgent expropriation acts are subject to the expedited procedure; the time limit is thirty days and a decision on a stay of execution is not open to objection (art. 20/A). For regulatory acts that must be announced, time runs from the day following the announcement.
- [Challenging a Refusal of Veteran or Martyr Status in Türkiye: Disability, Laws No. 3713 and 2330, Time Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/challenging-refusal-veteran-or-martyr-status): Challenging refusals of service-disability, martyr or veteran status in Türkiye: Laws No. 3713, 2330, 5510 and 5434, the 2026 amendments by Law No. 7594, administrative versus labour courts, time… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25; updated: 2026-11-06. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/gazi-veya-sehit-sayilmama-islemine-karsi-dava
  - Q: Which court hears a challenge to a refusal of martyr or veteran status?
    A: As a rule, the administrative court. However, service-disability disputes of persons first insured as public servants after 2008 are heard by the labour courts under Law No. 5510 art. 101. Service-disability claims of conscripts who died during military service and acts under Laws No. 3713 and 2330, the veteran title, martyrdom certificates, medals and employment belong to the administrative courts. In cases concerning military service, the competent court is the administrative court located where the regional administrative court of the person’s place of duty sits.
  - Q: What is the time limit for bringing an action?
    A: Sixty days from written notification of the refusal. If an application is not answered within thirty days, it is deemed rejected and the sixty days run from then. For compensation claims, the administration must be approached within one year of learning of the action and in any event within five years; the Council of State accepts that these periods may run from the date the administrative character became known, for example from notification of a service-disability annulment judgment.
  - Q: Can relatives of a conscript who took his own life benefit from service-disability rights?
    A: As a rule no, because disability resulting from attempted suicide is excluded by law. However, the Council of State has held that an assumption of suicide cannot be relied on where the cause of death was not clarified, and that the State bears the burden of a plausible explanation. It has also upheld the annulment of a refusal where there was service fault, such as putting a soldier detained for drugs on armed guard duty without examination. The outcome depends on the facts.
  - Q: What changed in 2026 for those injured in counter-terrorism duty but not classified as disabled?
    A: Law No. 7594 raised the indicators for degree-based pensions under Law No. 3713 Additional art. 3 and extended to their recipients the other rights granted under Law No. 1005. In addition, under the new Law No. 3713 Provisional art. 20, personnel not classified as disabled and given no degree, who suffered non-minor injuries from firearms, explosives or close-combat attacks, may receive a pension based on indicator 17,135. The application must be made within one year of entry into force to the institution of service at the time.
  - Q: Does receiving cash compensation prevent a separate damages action?
    A: No. If the administration is liable, a full-remedy action may be brought. However, the updated value of the cash compensation paid under Law No. 2330 and the twenty-five per cent increased part of the pension are deducted from pecuniary damage. After the Constitutional Court’s annulment, cash compensation counts only against pecuniary damage and is not deducted from non-pecuniary damages.
- [Damages for Delayed Healthcare in Turkish Prisons: Jurisdiction, Time Limits, Service Fault and Interest](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/delayed-prison-healthcare-state-liability-claim-turkiye): Under Turkish law, harm caused by delayed examination, treatment or hospital referral in a penal institution is claimed through a full-remedy action against the administration: the ordinary and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-06. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/cezaevinde-saglik-hizmeti-gecikmesi-tazminat-davasi
  - Q: In which court is an action brought for a convict or remand prisoner whose treatment in prison was delayed?
    A: As a rule, a full-remedy action is brought before the administrative court. However, the 10th Chamber of the Council of State, treating the dispute as one concerning the execution of the sentence, held the ordinary courts competent in a case based on a delayed hospital referral; the same Chamber continues to examine on the merits cases based on fault in supervision and in the operation of the health service.
  - Q: What are the time limits for applying to the administration and bringing an action?
    A: An application must be made to the administration within one year of learning of the damage and in any event within five years of the date of the action. For bodily harm, the date of learning is as a rule the date on which the final report is obtained; for injuries whose harm was apparent at the time of the incident, the period runs from the date of the incident. If the administration does not reply within thirty days, the request is deemed rejected and the action must be brought within sixty days.
  - Q: Which heads of damage may be claimed?
    A: Treatment, medication and device costs, transport and accompanying-person costs, carer costs, temporary incapacity, loss of working capacity (effort), impaired economic prospects and, in case of death, loss of support may be claimed; non-pecuniary damages are claimed separately. Awarding a head that was not claimed is treated as exceeding the claim.
  - Q: Do I lose interest if I increase the amount claimed later?
    A: Under the decision of the Council of State Assembly for the Unification of Judgments, interest runs on the increased amount from the same date as on the amount in the statement of claim, as a rule the date of the application to the administration. The increase may be made once only, until the final judgment.
  - Q: Do I pay attorney’s fees if part of my non-pecuniary claim is rejected?
    A: The Constitutional Court annulled the rule dividing costs according to the extent of success as regards actions for non-pecuniary damages. The 10th Chamber of the Council of State therefore holds that no costs or attorney’s fees may be awarded against the claimant for the rejected part of a non-pecuniary claim. The proportionality rule still applies to pecuniary damages.
- [Debarment from Public Tenders in Türkiye: Prohibited Acts, Periods, Shareholders, Annulment (Laws 4734, 4735)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/public-tender-debarment-decision-annulment-turkiye): Under Turkish law, those who commit prohibited acts in a public tender or during contract performance are debarred from all public tenders for one to two years, and those who fail to sign the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ihalelere-katilmaktan-yasaklama-karari-ve-iptal-davasi-4734-4735
  - Q: How long does a debarment from public tenders last under Law No. 4734 in Türkiye?
    A: For the prohibited acts in art. 17 and the contract-stage acts in Law No. 4735 art. 25, the ban is not less than one year and up to two years. For those who, except in force majeure, do not duly sign the contract after being awarded the tender, it is not less than six months and up to one year. The deciding authority sets the period according to the nature of the act; the Law provides no further standard.
  - Q: Does a debarment from public tenders in Türkiye also apply to the company’s shareholders?
    A: Yes, under the statutory conditions. If a partnership company is debarred, all partners are also debarred; if a capital company is debarred, only partners holding more than half of the capital are (Law No. 4734 art. 58/2; Law No. 4735 art. 26/2). A partner without a majority holding is not caught; the Council of State’s 13th Chamber upheld a regional court decision annulling the debarment of a forty-eight per cent partner.
  - Q: Within what period must a debarment decision from public tenders be given under Turkish law?
    A: Under the Law, the decision is given within forty-five days at the latest after the act requiring debarment is established and sent to the Official Gazette within fifteen days at the latest. The Law does not say whose finding counts; in a first-instance decision upheld by the Council of State’s 13th Chamber, the period was started from the date the act was reported to the competent ministry. That assessment was specific to the case.
  - Q: Within how many days must an annulment action against a debarment from public tenders be brought in Türkiye?
    A: As a rule within sixty days in the administrative court: Administrative Procedure Law art. 20/A excludes debarment decisions from the expedited procedure, and Law No. 4734 art. 58 and Law No. 4735 art. 26 set no separate time limit, so the general period in art. 7 applies. The period starts on the day after written notification. Since the effect of Official Gazette publication is not settled, it is prudent to use the earlier date.
  - Q: Can a stay of execution be obtained in a case against a debarment from public tenders in Türkiye?
    A: Yes, but bringing the action does not suspend the ban by itself. The court may stay execution where both conditions are met: implementation would cause damage that is difficult or impossible to remedy and the act is manifestly unlawful (Administrative Procedure Law art. 27/2). Stay orders are, as a rule, granted against security, which may be waived depending on the circumstances.
  - Q: Can the regional administrative court’s decision in a debarment case be appealed to the Council of State in Türkiye?
    A: The Council of State’s Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers said no in two decisions of 2024 and 2025: since these cases are not listed in Administrative Procedure Law art. 46, they become final with the regional court’s decision. The decisions were by majority. However, the 13th Chamber appears to have carried out appellate review in some debarment cases in 2025; practice is not uniform.
- [Foreign-Currency Military Service, Deferment Abroad in Türkiye: Conditions, Cancellation, Multiple Nationals](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/turkish-military-service-foreign-currency-citizens-abroad): Under Turkish law, deferment abroad (art. 38) and military service by foreign-currency payment (art. 39) under the Military Recruitment Law No. 7179 for male Turkish citizens living abroad: the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/dovizle-askerlik-ve-yurt-disindaki-yukumlunun-askerligi
  - Q: How many years must I work abroad for military service by foreign-currency payment?
    A: At least three years of actual work, not counting time spent in Türkiye. For multiple nationals living abroad and persons born abroad who live there with a permanent residence permit, no work requirement applies; three years of actual presence abroad is sufficient (Law No. 7179, art. 39).
  - Q: How much does it cost?
    A: The Law sets a formula rather than a fixed amount: the indicator figure is multiplied by the civil servant salary coefficient and converted into euros at the rate on the day of payment. The amount changes as the coefficient changes; the current figure is available from the consulate. Transfer fees and costs are charged separately.
  - Q: Until what age does deferment abroad last?
    A: Military service procedures may be deferred until the end of the year in which you turn 35. If you spend more than half of any calendar year in Türkiye during deferment, the deferment is cancelled (art. 38).
  - Q: What can I do if my application is refused?
    A: An annulment action against the refusal may be brought before the administrative court within the time limit for administrative actions. An action against a Ministry decision was heard by the Ankara Administrative Court; in some cases the regional administrative court’s decision may be final.
  - Q: I served in the country of my other nationality; am I exempt in Türkiye?
    A: Law No. 7179 ties exemption to bilateral agreements between the two countries. If you served before 26.06.2019, under the former Law No. 1111, the 10th Chamber of the Council of State held that your application must be assessed under the former Law and that the regulation limit tying applications to 26.06.2019 is unlawful; the decision may not yet be final.
- [Judicial Review of Disciplinary Penalties](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/judicial-review-disciplinary-penalties): The legal framework and judicial review of disciplinary penalties imposed on civil servants and public officials (warning, reprimand, deduction from salary, suspension of step advancement, dismissal… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/disiplin-cezalarinin-yargisal-denetimi
  - Q: What disciplinary penalties are there?
    A: Article 125 of Law No. 657 provides for five penalties increasing in severity: warning, reprimand, deduction from salary, suspension of step advancement and dismissal from the civil service. The corresponding acts and situations are listed one by one; the same kind of penalty may also be imposed for acts of similar gravity.
  - Q: A disciplinary penalty was imposed without taking my defence; what can I do?
    A: No disciplinary penalty may be imposed without taking the defence (Art. 130), a guarantee protected by Article 129 of the Constitution. If the defence was not taken at all, or not taken in due form (by notifying the allegations, the evidence and the characterisation of the act), this is in itself a ground for annulment. An action may be brought before the administrative court to annul the penalty.
  - Q: Should I object first, or bring an action directly?
    A: The objection route is optional. You may object within seven days against warning, reprimand and deduction-from-salary penalties to the disciplinary board, and against the suspension-of-step-advancement penalty to the high disciplinary board.
