You may also bring an annulment action before the administrative judiciary directly, without objecting; in any event the judicial route is open (Art. 135).
  - Q: Within what period does a disciplinary penalty become time-barred?
    A: There are two periods: the period to open an investigation (one month for warning/reprimand/deduction from salary/suspension of step advancement, six months for dismissal) and the period to impose a penalty (at most two years from the date the act was committed). If these periods are exceeded, the power to impose a penalty lapses (Art. 127).
  - Q: I was acquitted in the criminal case; is my disciplinary penalty automatically lifted?
    A: No, it is not lifted automatically. Disciplinary law and criminal procedure are separate disciplines; the official's not being convicted under criminal law does not by itself prevent a disciplinary penalty. However, the criminal court's finding as to the proof of the same act (whether or not it occurred) must be respected in the administrative judiciary.
  - Q: In which court and within what period is the action brought?
    A: The action to annul a disciplinary penalty is, as a rule, heard before the administrative court. The period to bring the action is sixty days from the day following service (Administrative Procedure Law, Art. 7). Bringing the action does not by itself stay the application of the penalty; if the conditions are met, a stay of execution must be requested separately (Art. 27).
- [Tax Cases from Uşak, Afyonkarahisar and Kütahya: Which Court, Time Limits and Appeal in Türkiye](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/tax-court-usak-afyonkarahisar-kutahya-turkiye): There is no tax court in Uşak, Afyonkarahisar or Kütahya; tax cases are heard by the Manisa, Konya and Eskişehir Tax Courts respectively. This article explains, under Turkish law and with official… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/usak-afyonkarahisar-kutahya-vergi-davasi-hangi-mahkemede-acilir
  - Q: Is there a tax court in Uşak, and which court hears tax cases from Uşak in Türkiye?
    A: There is no tax court in Uşak; the only administrative-jurisdiction court in the province is the Uşak Administrative Court. According to the Administrative Judiciary Guide of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors (file dated 18.09.2026 on the Council's website), Uşak lies within the territorial jurisdiction of the Manisa Tax Court; cases against acts of a tax office or municipality in Uşak are brought before that court. The petition may also be lodged through the Uşak Administrative Court.
  - Q: Where is a tax case from Afyonkarahisar filed in Türkiye?
    A: A tax case against an act of a tax office or municipality in Afyonkarahisar is filed with the Konya Tax Court; Afyonkarahisar has no tax court. The Administrative Judiciary Guide of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors (file dated 18.09.2026 on the Council's website) lists the province within the jurisdiction of the Konya Tax Court.
  - Q: In which province is the tax court for Kütahya, and which court hears tax cases from Kütahya in Türkiye?
    A: Kütahya has no tax court; tax cases from Kütahya are heard by the Eskişehir Tax Court. By Decision No. 607 of the Plenary of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors dated 07.07.2021, Kütahya was removed from the jurisdiction of the Manisa Tax Court and attached to the Eskişehir Tax Court with effect from 01.09.2021.
  - Q: What is the time limit for challenging a tax and penalty assessment notice in Uşak, Afyonkarahisar and Kütahya in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, a case against a tax and penalty assessment notice must be filed with the tax court within thirty days from the day after service (Law No. 2577 art. 7). For a payment order the time limit is fifteen days (Law No. 6183 art. 58). An electronic notification is deemed served at the end of the fifth day after transmission (Tax Procedure Law art. 107/A).
  - Q: Do I have to travel to Manisa, Konya or Eskişehir to file a tax case from Uşak, Afyonkarahisar or Kütahya in Türkiye?
    A: No. Under art. 4 of Law No. 2577, the petition may be handed to another administrative or tax court for forwarding to the competent tax court and, where there is neither, to the civil court of first instance. Each of the three provincial centres has an administrative court; in the districts the civil court of first instance may also be used. A case may also be filed through UYAP with a secure electronic signature.
  - Q: Which regional administrative court hears appeals against decisions of the Manisa, Konya and Eskişehir tax courts in Türkiye?
    A: The appeal (istinaf) goes to the regional administrative court in whose jurisdiction the deciding court sits: İzmir for decisions of the Manisa Tax Court, Konya for the Konya Tax Court and Bursa for the Eskişehir Tax Court. The time limit is thirty days from service of the decision (Law No. 2577 art. 45(1)); decisions in tax cases not exceeding the monetary threshold are final.
- [The Annulment Action in Administrative Jurisdiction](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/annulment-action-administrative-jurisdiction): A comprehensive guide to the annulment action in administrative jurisdiction: the definition of the action and the elements of an administrative act (competence, form, cause, subject-matter, purpose)… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/idari-yargida-iptal-davasi
  - Q: What is the difference between an annulment action and a full-remedy action?
    A: An annulment action seeks the annulment of an administrative act on the ground of unlawfulness in terms of competence, form, cause, subject-matter or purpose, and is brought by those whose interests are violated. A full-remedy action is a claim for compensation (or for the fulfilment of a right) by those whose personal rights are directly harmed by an administrative action or act. Where the conditions are met, the two actions may also be brought together.
  - Q: Who may bring an annulment action?
    A: Anyone who has a personal, legitimate and current relationship of interest with the act in question may bring it (subjective standing). On matters closely concerning the public interest — such as the environment, zoning, and the protection of historical and cultural values — associations, trade unions and professional bodies may also bring actions within the framework of their founding purposes.
  - Q: Can an action be brought against any administrative letter?
    A: No. Only final and enforceable (executory) administrative acts may be challenged. The administration's opinions, preparatory acts or preliminary-act letters may not be challenged on their own; they are reviewed only together with the final act.
  - Q: What is the filing period?
    A: Where there is no separate period in their special laws, it is sixty days before the administrative courts and the Council of State and thirty days before the tax courts. The period, as a rule, begins from the day following service of the act. Under Article 11, an application to the superior authority suspends the period; in case of rejection or implied rejection it continues from where it left off.
  - Q: Does bringing an action stay the application of the act?
    A: No, bringing an action does not stay the act by itself. If the application of the act will cause harm that is difficult or impossible to remedy and the act is manifestly unlawful (the two conditions together), a stay of execution must be requested from the court (Art. 27).
  - Q: What happens if an annulment decision is given?
    A: An annulment decision removes the act from the moment it was issued (retroactively); the act is deemed never to have been made. Under Article 138 of the Constitution and the Administrative Procedure Law, the administration must implement the requirements of the annulment decision without delay.
- [The Blue Card and Dual Citizenship in Türkiye: Rights of Former Turkish Citizens Who Renounced with Permission](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/blue-card-rights-former-turkish-citizens): Under Turkish law, the rights in Türkiye of persons who renounced Turkish citizenship with permission and of their descendants under art. 28 of Law No. 5901: the Blue Card, the exceptions (elections… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/mavi-kart-ve-cifte-vatandaslik-vatandasliktan-cikanlarin-haklari
  - Q: Can I buy property and inherit in Türkiye with a Blue Card?
    A: Yes. Blue Card holders enjoy the rights granted to Turkish citizens in the same way, apart from the exceptions (Law No. 5901, art. 28/1); acquiring property and inheriting are not among those exceptions.
  - Q: Which rights do Blue Card holders not have?
    A: No right to vote or stand for election, no right to import vehicles or household goods duty-free, no military service obligation, and no permanent civil-service posts based on a cadre. They may work in public institutions as workers, temporary or contract staff (art. 28/2-3).
  - Q: Can my children and grandchildren also get a Blue Card?
    A: Yes; descendants up to the third degree benefit. They must document their descent from the ascendant (art. 28/5).
  - Q: My citizenship was taken away for military service reasons; can I get a Blue Card?
    A: According to the judgment upheld by the Council of State, no; the Blue Card is only for those who were citizens by birth and renounced with permission. For persons in this position, art. 43 of Law No. 5901, as quoted in the decision, provides a route to reacquire citizenship.
  - Q: My Blue Card was cancelled; what can I do?
    A: Cancellation is an administrative act; an annulment action can be brought before the administrative court within the time limit for administrative actions. However, the Council of State has accepted that a card issued by mistake to someone who does not meet the conditions creates no acquired right.
- [Union Standing Not Judged on Members' Interest Alone: Turkish Constitutional Court on Union Annulment Action](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/trade-union-standing-annulment-action-access-to-court): Under Turkish law, the Constitutional Court unanimously held that administrative court decisions dismissing a trade union's annulment action for lack of standing, on the basis of the members' common… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sendikanin-davasi-yalniz-uyelerin-ortak-menfaatine-bakilarak-ehliyetten-reddedilemez
  - Q: Can a trade union bring an annulment action against an act concerning its members?
    A: Yes, but under Turkish law, Article 2/1-a of the Administrative Procedure Law requires that the act infringe its interest. In this decision, the Constitutional Court accepted that, where the union argues that the act also affects its own legal personality, the court must discuss that interest not only in terms of the members’ common interest but also from that angle.
  - Q: If the members have sued separately, will the union’s action be dismissed?
    A: In this case, the administrative court made the members’ separate actions one of its grounds for dismissal. The Constitutional Court accepted that this approach pursued a legitimate aim, but found disproportionate a dismissal given without discussing the effect on the union’s own legal personality.
  - Q: Did the Constitutional Court decide that the union won its case?
    A: No. The Court only found that the right of access to a court had been violated. Whether the act was lawful and whether the union was actually affected by it will be decided by the administrative court in the retrial.
  - Q: What happens after the finding of a violation?
    A: The decision was sent to the administrative court for a retrial. According to the Constitutional Court, what must be done is to give a new decision removing the reasons that led to the violation (Law No. 6216, art. 50/2).
- [Unlawful Processing of Personal Data in Türkiye: Application, KVKK Complaint, Damages, Criminal Code Art. 136](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/personal-data-breach-kvkk-complaint-compensation-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a person whose personal data has been processed unlawfully first applies to the data controller and, if this fails, may complain to the Personal Data Protection Board within the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kisisel-verilerin-hukuka-aykiri-islenmesi-kvkk-sikayet-tazminat-ve-ceza
  - Q: What is the time limit for a complaint to the Personal Data Protection Board in Türkiye if the data controller does not respond?
    A: Under Turkish law, if the application is rejected, the response is insufficient or no response is given in time, the data subject may complain to the Personal Data Protection Board within thirty days of learning of the data controller’s response and in any case within sixty days of the application date (Law No. 6698 art. 14/1). As a rule, the data controller must respond within thirty days at the latest (art. 13/2).
  - Q: Can a complaint be made directly to the Personal Data Protection Board in Türkiye without first applying to the data controller?
    A: No. Under Law No. 6698 art. 14/2, the complaint route may not be used before the application to the data controller under art. 13 has been exhausted. This requirement concerns the complaint to the Board; on the wording of arts. 13 and 14, the same requirement is not imposed for an action for compensation or for a criminal report under Turkish Criminal Code arts. 135–136. This is an interpretation drawn by this article from the provisions.
  - Q: Under which provisions is an action for compensation for unlawful processing of personal data brought under Turkish law?
    A: Law No. 6698 art. 14/3 reserves the right to compensation under general provisions of those whose personality rights have been violated. According to a 2025 reversal decision of the Court of Cassation 4th Civil Chamber, this refers to the Turkish Civil Code provisions on the protection of personality; liability is fault-based and requires unlawfulness, loss and an adequate causal link. In the decision summarised, the action against a bank was heard by the ordinary courts.
  - Q: Which court hears an action against an administrative fine imposed by the KVKK Board in Türkiye?
    A: Under Law No. 6698 art. 18/3, added by Law No. 7499, an action may be brought before administrative courts against administrative fines imposed by the Board. The Council of State 10th Chamber accepted that this provision entered into force on 01.06.2024 and also applies to pending cases. As no special time limit is provided, the time limit is, as a rule, the sixty days in Administrative Procedure Law art. 7.
  - Q: Does the offence of unlawfully giving or disseminating personal data under Turkish Criminal Code art. 136 depend on a complaint?
    A: No. Under Turkish Criminal Code art. 139, recording personal data (art. 135), unlawfully giving or obtaining data (art. 136) and failure to destroy data do not depend on a complaint; the prosecutor investigates of its own motion. The penalty under art. 136/1 is imprisonment from two to four years. The Criminal General Assembly stated that personal data that is not secret and is known to everyone also benefits from this protection.
- [İzmir Regional Administrative Court: Jurisdiction (İzmir, Aydın, Denizli, Manisa, Muğla, Uşak) and Appeals](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/izmir-regional-administrative-court-jurisdiction-turkiye): The İzmir Regional Administrative Court hears appeals against decisions of the administrative and tax courts in İzmir, Aydın, Denizli, Manisa, Muğla and Uşak. This article explains, under Turkish law… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/izmir-bolge-idare-mahkemesi-yargi-cevresi-istinaf
  - Q: Which court hears an appeal against a decision of the Denizli Administrative Court in Türkiye?
    A: The appellate (istinaf) court is the İzmir Regional Administrative Court; the Denizli Regional Court of Appeal hears only appeals from civil and criminal courts. The petition does not have to be taken to İzmir: it may be lodged with the Denizli administrative court that gave the decision, and the file is sent to the regional administrative court (Law No. 2577 arts. 45/2 and 48/3).
  - Q: Where and within what time limit is a decision of the Muğla Administrative Court challenged in Türkiye?
    A: The remedy against a final decision of an administrative court in Muğla is an appeal (istinaf) to the İzmir Regional Administrative Court. The time limit is thirty days from service of the decision (Law No. 2577 art. 45/1). The petition may be lodged with the court in Muğla that gave the decision. In cases under the expedited procedure, such as tenders or urgent expropriation, there is no appeal; the application goes directly to the Council of State within fifteen days.
  - Q: Which provinces fall within the jurisdiction of the İzmir Regional Administrative Court in Türkiye?
    A: İzmir, Aydın, Denizli, Manisa, Muğla and Uşak. The court's 2025 annual report describes its jurisdiction as these six provinces, and the administrative-justice guide published on the website of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors as a file dated 18.09.2026 lists the administrative courts of the same provinces under the İzmir Regional Administrative Court. Afyonkarahisar belongs to the Konya, and Kütahya to the Bursa, Regional Administrative Court.
  - Q: Is there a tax court in Uşak, Türkiye, and where do appeals in Uşak tax cases go?
    A: There is no tax court in Uşak; according to the administrative-justice guide of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors, tax disputes from Uşak fall within the jurisdiction of the Manisa Tax Court. Appeals against decisions of the Manisa Tax Court are heard by the İzmir Regional Administrative Court. The same court hears appeals against decisions of the Uşak Administrative Court.
  - Q: Can every decision of an administrative court in Aydın or Manisa, Türkiye, be appealed to the İzmir Regional Administrative Court?
    A: No. Decisions in tax cases, full-remedy actions and annulment actions against administrative acts whose subject matter does not exceed the statutory monetary threshold are final (Law No. 2577 art. 45/1). The threshold rises each year by the revaluation rate, and the amount in force on the date the case was filed applies (Additional art. 1). There is also no appeal in cases under the expedited procedure; those decisions go directly to the Council of State on cassation.
  - Q: Can an appeal decision of the İzmir Regional Administrative Court be taken to the Council of State in Türkiye?
    A: Only in the cases listed in art. 46 of Law No. 2577, within thirty days of service of the decision. These include annulment actions against regulatory acts, cases above the statutory amount, removal from public office and zoning-plan cases. For regional administrative court decisions given after 31.07.2026, cassation is as a rule also available where the first-instance decision was set aside and a new decision given. Other decisions are final.

### Higher Education Law

- [Actions Against YÖK and University Acts in Türkiye: Which Administrative Court Has Territorial Jurisdiction?](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/yok-university-acts-competent-administrative-court-turkiye): Under Turkish law, which administrative court has jurisdiction over actions against acts of YÖK, the Inter-University Board and universities: Administrative Procedure Law arts. 32 and 33 for… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yok-ve-universite-islemlerinde-yetkili-idare-mahkemesi
  - Q: Which administrative court hears an action against an act of the Council of Higher Education (YÖK) in Türkiye?
    A: The 8th Chamber of the Council of State has found the administrative court in Ankara competent for the rejection of an application tied to YÖK's regulatory power and for an ÜAK act concerning the title and certificate of associate professor; for a post examination conducted by an ÜAK jury, it relied on the place of the university that advertised the post. Actions against regulatory acts to be applied nationwide, such as YÖK regulations, are brought before the Council of State as a court of first instance. If the dispute concerns a public servant's appointment, discipline or personnel rights, the duty-station rules in art. 33 must also be considered.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, in which administrative court is an action against a student disciplinary penalty imposed by a university brought?
    A: Under the general rule in art. 32(1) of the Administrative Procedure Law, the action is, as a rule, brought before the administrative court of the place where the superior or board that imposed the penalty is located; no separate decision determining the competent court on this point is cited here. An objection may be made to the university executive board within fifteen days, but it is not mandatory and an action may be brought directly (Law No. 2547 art. 54(10)). The time limit is, as a rule, sixty days from the day after written notification.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, which administrative court hears an academic's disciplinary penalty or appointment case?
    A: Academics are public servants, so art. 33 of the Administrative Procedure Law applies. In appointment and transfer cases the administrative court of the new or former duty station, in dismissal and suspension cases that of the last duty station, and for disciplinary penalties not ending employment and for advancement, promotion and personnel rights that of the current duty station has jurisdiction. In a file where a university in another city rejected an ÖYP research assistant's request for extra time in a doctorate, the Council of State found the duty-station court competent because the act could end the employment relationship.
  - Q: Where is an action against an Inter-University Board (ÜAK) act on an associate professorship application brought in Türkiye?
    A: The 8th Chamber of the Council of State has found the administrative court in Ankara, where the Presidency of the Inter-University Board that made the act is located, competent for ÜAK acts concerning the title and certificate of associate professor. By contrast, failing an oral examination conducted by an ÜAK jury for appointment to an associate professor post advertised by a university was treated as a rejection of an application for appointment, and the administrative court of the place where that university is located was found competent.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, what happens if an administrative action is filed in an administrative court that lacks territorial jurisdiction?
    A: The action does not lapse. At the preliminary review the court dismisses it for lack of jurisdiction and sends the file to the competent court (Administrative Procedure Law art. 15(1)(a)); no appeal lies against that decision. If the court to which the file is sent also considers itself to lack jurisdiction, the regional administrative court or the Council of State resolves the conflict with a final decision (art. 43). Because this process can take months, filing in the right place from the outset saves time.
  - Q: In Türkiye, where is an appeal lodged against a decision of the administrative court in Denizli in a university case?
    A: The appeal (istinaf) is lodged with the İzmir Regional Administrative Court within thirty days of service of the decision (Administrative Procedure Law art. 45(1)). Decisions in cases not exceeding the statutory monetary threshold are final. For decisions of the administrative court in Ankara the appellate court is the Ankara Regional Administrative Court; for decisions of the Council of State at first instance, the remedy is an appeal on points of law to the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers.
- [Annulling Faculty Appointments in Türkiye: Appointment and Promotion Criteria, Non-Appointment, Non-Renewal](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/faculty-appointment-annulment-action-turkish-universities): Under Turkish law, annulment actions against non-appointment to assistant professor, associate professor and professor posts and against non-reappointment: criteria under Law No. 2547 and the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ogretim-uyesi-atamasi-iptal-davasi
  - Q: What is the time limit for challenging a faculty appointment?
    A: Unless a special law provides otherwise, the time limit before the administrative courts is sixty days and runs from the day following written notification (Law No. 2577 art. 7).
  - Q: My score is higher than the appointee’s; is my non-appointment unlawful?
    A: Not on that basis alone. In quashing decisions of 2025, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State found the act unlawful where, despite a clear score advantage, no academic reason was shown for preferring the lower-scoring candidate. In a 2023 decision, however, it explained its quashing of an annulment judgment based on an even larger score difference by stating that activities such as thesis supervision, projects and teaching must also be assessed as a whole. The Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers did not base its quashing on score advantage in a similar case.
  - Q: If the court annuls the act, will I be appointed?
    A: Not automatically. Administrative courts may not render judgments that remove the discretion of the administration (Law No. 2577 art. 2/2). The administration carries out a new assessment in line with the reasons of the judgment; in one case the Council of State stated that, since the first-ranked candidate had not challenged the act, the administration would cancel the appointment and announce the post again.
  - Q: What can an assistant professor do if the term is not extended?
    A: An annulment action may be brought against the non-reappointment. The Council of State has held lawful the non-reappointment of a faculty member who did not meet an institutional requirement known at the time of first appointment, while for a new publication or points requirement introduced during the term it has accepted that the period must be calculated from the requirement’s entry into force.
  - Q: Can the university’s appointment criteria be challenged separately?
    A: Yes. The criteria are regulatory acts; they may be challenged directly within the time limit from their publication or together with the individual act applying them (Law No. 2577 art. 7/4). Under the Law, additional conditions must be approved by YÖK, aimed exclusively at raising scientific quality, and objective and verifiable.
- [Annulling Tailor-Made Academic Job Postings in Türkiye: University Vacancies Drafted for One Candidate](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/annulment-tailor-made-academic-job-postings): Annulment actions against academic job postings tailored to one candidate: Constitution, Law No. 2547, regulations, time limits, standing, Council of State rulings. Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/universitelerde-kisiye-ozel-kadro-ilaninin-iptali
  - Q: May a university attach special conditions to a posting?
    A: Yes. The Law permits objective and verifiable additional conditions with YÖK approval aimed at scientific quality, and the courts accept post-specific conditions provided they rest on service needs. What is prohibited is writing a thesis title into the posting and conditions that identify only a particular candidate.
  - Q: Can I bring an action if I did not apply?
    A: In the decisions reviewed, the merits were examined in an action brought by a person who could not apply because she did not meet the condition. You need to document that you meet all other requirements and that the condition excluded you from competition. Actions by non-candidate faculty members may be dismissed for lack of interest.
  - Q: When does the time limit start?
    A: The time limit is sixty days. Where appointment results were published on the university website, the Council of State upheld a judgment treating that date as the date of knowledge. For an action against the posting itself, the most prudent course is to file within sixty days of its publication.
  - Q: If I win, will I be appointed to the post?
    A: As a rule, no. The court cannot appoint in the administration’s place; where the condition is found tailor-made, the administration may issue a new objective posting. If the non-appointment of a qualified candidate is found unlawful, the administration must act in accordance with the judgment.
  - Q: Can the appointment be suspended during the proceedings?
    A: A stay may be ordered if manifest unlawfulness and damage difficult to remedy are shown. In appointment cases such a decision is given after the administration’s defence has been received or the defence period has expired.
  - Q: Is cassation available against the regional court’s decision?
    A: It is available in the types of case listed in the Law; the Council of State treated an action against another candidate’s appointment as open to cassation because it could result in removal from public service. In addition, regional court decisions given after 31.07.2026 that quash the first-instance judgment and rule anew can now be appealed in cassation.
- [Appointing a Markedly Lower Scorer Is Unlawful: Turkish Council of State on Assistant Professor Rankings](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/assistant-professor-appointment-objective-score-ranking): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, holding unlawful the appointment of a candidate with a markedly lower score to an assistant professor post by departing from the objective… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/objektif-puan-siralamasi-disinda-doktor-ogretim-uyesi-atamasi
  - Q: Must the candidate with the highest score be appointed to an assistant professor post?
    A: Under Turkish law, the decision does not lay down such a general rule. The Chamber held unlawful the appointment of a candidate with a markedly lower score made by departing from the objective ranking. According to the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers, where the candidates’ scientific work is very close or equal, the administration’s discretion becomes important.
  - Q: What happens if the top-ranked candidate does not sue?
    A: In this case, according to the Chamber, the administration annuls the appointment, re-advertises the post and makes an appointment after assessing the applicants objectively.
  - Q: Is the claimant appointed directly to the post?
    A: Not in this decision. The claimant was not first in the expert’s ranking; the outcome is not the claimant’s appointment but a new advertisement and a fresh assessment of the candidates.
  - Q: Is a higher score decisive in every case?
    A: No. In a decision of 31.12.2025, the Chamber did not find unlawful the non-appointment of a higher-scoring candidate in a file where it was established that the score advantage did not stem from publications in the advertised field.
- [Art. 54 Annulment Alone Does Not Lift Student Penalties: Turkish Council of State, Review Under Law No. 7437](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/constitutional-court-annulment-student-discipline-penalty): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State did not regard the Constitutional Court’s annulment of Article 54 of Law No. 2547 as sufficient on its own to… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anayasa-mahkemesi-iptali-ogrenci-disiplin-cezasini-kendiliginden-kaldirmaz
  - Q: Does the Constitutional Court’s annulment of Article 54 automatically lift a student disciplinary penalty?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the decision of the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State of 17.09.2025, it does not do so automatically. The Assembly accepted that the annulment left the penalty without a legal basis; however, since Article 54 was re-enacted by Law No. 7437 before the annulment took effect, it quashed the decision of insistence that had annulled the penalty solely on the basis of the annulment decision. The case will be examined on the merits under that regulation.
  - Q: What will the court examine in a penalty imposed under the former Regulation?
    A: According to the Assembly, if a new statutory regulation has been made after the annulment, the dispute is resolved according to that regulation. In this file, the regional administrative court will examine whether the act is proven, whether it warrants a disciplinary penalty and whether there is a more favourable provision.
  - Q: Does the decision apply to every student disciplinary penalty?
    A: The decision concerns an expulsion penalty imposed under the former Regulation and based on a final conviction for membership of an organisation. The Assembly’s standard rests on a new statutory regulation having been made after the annulment; in this file, the fate of the penalty will be decided by the regional administrative court’s review on the merits.
  - Q: Is the Assembly’s decision final, and where did the file go?
    A: The Assembly’s decision is final and was given by majority. The file was sent back to the regional administrative court for a new decision; the new decision given after the quashing could not be located.
- [Assoc. Prof. Appointment Unlawful Over Co-Author Juror: Turkish Council of State on Joint Publications](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/associate-professor-appointment-jury-joint-publications): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State finally held, by majority, that an associate professor appointment was unlawful where it relied on the reports… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/docent-atamasinda-adayla-ortak-yayini-olan-juri-uyesi
  - Q: Can a professor with joint publications with the appointee sit on the jury for an associate professor post?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly’s decision, the presence of such a member on the jury may create a negative impression as to objectivity. The jury should be determined without that member; otherwise an appointment based on that jury’s reports may be held unlawful.
  - Q: If the jury is flawed, is the claimant appointed directly to the post?
    A: No. The Assembly required a new appointment act aimed at scientific quality to be established between the two candidates.
  - Q: If the jury says that one candidate does not meet the minimum conditions, can the administration appoint the other?
    A: In this case the appointment was made on the basis of the jury reports as if there were a single candidate. Since the expert examination ordered by the court showed that both candidates met the conditions, the Assembly required this matter first to be clarified in a manner leaving no room for doubt and the act to be established accordingly.
  - Q: Must the candidate with the higher score be appointed?
    A: The Assembly did not base the quashing on the higher score; it stated that where the candidates’ scientific work is very close or equal, the administration’s discretion becomes important. One member wrote a dissenting opinion stating that the quashing should have been based on the higher score.
- [Associate Professorship in Türkiye: Application Requirements and Annulment Action Against a Negative Result](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/associate-professorship-requirements-and-annulment-action): Under Turkish law, an application for associate professorship requires a doctorate, at least 55 points in a central foreign language examination and original publications set for each field; the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/docentlik-basvuru-sartlari-ve-docentlik-sonucuna-karsi-iptal-davasi
  - Q: What are the requirements for applying for associate professorship in Türkiye?
    A: Under Law No. 2547 art. 24/a, a doctorate or an equivalent title listed in the Law, at least 55 points in a central foreign language examination (or an equivalent score in an examination accepted by YÖK) and original publications of the number and quality set for each field by YÖK on ÜAK’s opinion are required. Point tables by field are set out in the Associate Professorship Application Requirements published by ÜAK.
  - Q: When are applications made, and when is the result deemed served?
    A: Unless ÜAK sets another date, applications are made from the twentieth day of March and October until the close of working hours on the last working day of the month (Regulation art. 4/1). The result and the reports taken as the basis of assessment are deemed served on the fifth day after they are made accessible electronically (Law No. 2547 art. 24/b).
  - Q: When may an unsuccessful candidate apply again?
    A: A candidate found unsuccessful by the jury may apply again at the earliest in the third following period, provided the deficiencies are remedied (art. 6/8). A candidate whose application was cancelled for failing the minimum requirements may apply in the following period after remedying the deficiencies (art. 6/1-a).
  - Q: Where and within what time limit is an action against the result brought?
    A: The action is brought against the Presidency of the Interuniversity Board within sixty days from the day following service (İYUK art. 7). According to the Council of State’s jurisdiction rulings, the competent courts are the administrative courts of Ankara, where ÜAK is located. In the decisions cited, the regional administrative court’s appellate decision was treated as final.
  - Q: Can the court change the jury’s scientific assessment?
    A: The court does not take the jury’s place; it reviews, where necessary with experts, whether the reports are detailed and reasoned and whether the act is lawful. According to recent regional administrative court decisions, it is not enough that an expert finds the candidate successful; it must be shown that the jury’s reasons are wrong or that the jury assessed the file contrary to academic and scientific principles. An act based on a vague criterion, on the other hand, is unlawful according to İDDK.
  - Q: Can a candidate who has sued apply again for associate professorship?
    A: Yes. The regulatory provision making a new application dependent on the judgment becoming final was annulled as contrary to the right to seek legal remedies, and İDDK upheld that decision. However, a candidate whose application is still pending may not make a new application (art. 4/7).
- [Disciplinary Penalties for Student Cheating: Unlawfulness and Annulment under Turkish Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/unlawfulness-annulment-disciplinary-penalties-imposed): Unlawfulness and annulment of disciplinary penalties imposed for a student cheating in an exam: the disciplinary treatment of cheating (Higher Education Law No. 2547, art. 54 — reprimand for… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ogrenci-kopya-disiplin-cezasi-iptali
  - Q: What is the disciplinary penalty for cheating in an exam?
    A: Under Article 54 of Higher Education Law No. 2547, cheating or enabling cheating in exams requires one-semester suspension from the institution. Attempting to cheat is punished by a reprimand, while cheating by threat, preventing the removal of cheating students from the exam hall, having another person sit the exam or sitting it for another person requires two-semester suspension. These rules were contained in the Higher Education Institutions Student Discipline Regulation until 2023; the Regulation was repealed on 11.03.2023.
  - Q: Is a penalty imposed without taking my defence valid?
    A: No. The student must be informed in writing, at least seven days before the defence date, of what the imputed offence consists of, and the investigation must allow the student to defend themselves properly (Law No. 2547, art. 54/5). A penalty imposed without duly requesting the defence is annulled before the administrative courts for breach of the right of defence. However, a student who fails to respond to the summons without a valid excuse is deemed to have waived the right of defence.
  - Q: How must cheating be proven?
    A: The allegation must be established by concrete, legally acceptable and doubt-free evidence. The investigator may hear witnesses, conduct an inspection and consult experts (Law No. 2547, art. 54/6-b). Especially in online exams, technical claims such as the authorship of the answer sheet may require expert examination; if the act is not found established, the penalty is annulled.
  - Q: How and within what time can I challenge a suspension?
    A: An objection may be lodged with the university executive board within fifteen days; it is not mandatory, and an annulment action may be brought directly (Law No. 2547, art. 54/10). The annulment action is brought before the administrative court, as a rule within sixty days of notification (Administrative Procedure Code, art. 7). To suspend the penalty, a stay of execution should be requested together with the action.
  - Q: Is cassation available against a suspension?
    A: Since actions concerning a one-semester suspension are not listed in Article 46(1) of the Administrative Procedure Code, the regional administrative court’s decision dismissing the appeal is final; the 8th Chamber of the Council of State upheld the rejection of the appeal on points of law even in a file where it was argued that consecutive penalties would end enrolment. Cassation is, however, available against expulsion from the institution (Administrative Procedure Code, art. 46/1-d). For decisions given after 31.07.2026, if the regional administrative court quashes the first-instance decision and decides anew, that decision may also be appealed on points of law, save for the statutory exceptions (Administrative Procedure Code, art. 46/2).
  - Q: If I am acquitted in the criminal case, is the disciplinary penalty lifted?
    A: Disciplinary and criminal processes are independent; the initiation of criminal prosecution, or the student’s conviction or acquittal, does not bar a disciplinary penalty (Law No. 2547, art. 54/6-ğ). Acquittal therefore does not by itself remove the disciplinary penalty, but the penalty is separately reviewed by the administrative courts as to the proof of the act and the lawfulness of the procedure.
- [Disciplinary Penalties on Academics and Annulment Actions](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/disciplinary-penalties-academics-annulment-actions): A guide to disciplinary penalties on academics in Türkiye and annulment actions: the special regime of Law No. 2547 and its differences from the civil-servant regime; penalty types (art. 53/b); the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/akademisyen-disiplin-cezalari-iptal-davasi
  - Q: Which disciplinary penalties may be imposed on academics?
    A: Under art. 53/b of Law No. 2547: warning, reprimand, salary/wage deduction, suspension of grade advancement or repeated wage deduction, removal from the university teaching profession and dismissal from public service. The acts warranting each penalty are exhaustively enumerated.
  - Q: May a penalty be imposed without taking a defence?
    A: Not without affording the right of defence (Article 129/2 of the Constitution). Since 09.08.2026, under art. 53/A of Law No. 2547, the competent authority's invitation to defend must state the act on which the proposal is based and the corresponding penalty, grant at least seven days and indicate that the file may be examined before the defence. A person who does not defend within that period despite a proper invitation is deemed to have waived the right of defence; the penalty may then be imposed on the available evidence without a defence. In removal cases the person may also examine the file, call witnesses and defend before the board orally or in writing, in person or through counsel.
  - Q: What are the limitation periods?
    A: The investigation must be commenced within one month of knowledge for minor penalties and six months for removal penalties; no penalty may be imposed after two years from the act (six years for removal from the teaching profession) (art. 53/C). Re-use or republication of a scientific work restarts the periods.
  - Q: What safeguards apply in plagiarism investigations?
    A: A prior ethics-board examination is mandatory (art. 53/A-b). In the annulment action the court must order an expert examination of its own motion; the expert fee cannot be imposed on the claimant and is requested from the administration or covered by the Treasury.
  - Q: Where and within what period is the annulment action brought?
    A: Before the administrative court within 60 days of notification; regional appeal lies against the judgment. Appeal decisions dismissing the regional appeal on warnings and reprimands are final; cassation before the Council of State is available, as a rule, for removal from the profession or public service (APL art. 46(1)). Regional decisions rendered after 31/7/2026 that set aside the first-instance judgment and rule anew may, save for the listed exceptions, be appealed in cassation (APL art. 46(2)).
  - Q: May an academic be deemed to have resigned for absenteeism?
    A: As a rule, no. In a case the 8th Chamber held that deemed resignation under art. 94 of Law No. 657 cannot be applied to teaching staff; absenteeism may be addressed only through a disciplinary investigation under Law No. 2547. Failing to take up duty in time at the place of assignment is a separate matter: in assignments under art. 41 (and art. 40/b) of Law No. 2547 and provisional art. 78 (ÖYP research assistants), this is treated as resignation.
- [Doctoral Withdrawal Ends a 50/d Research Assistant Post: Turkish Council of State; 33/a Request Is Separate](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/50-d-research-assistant-doctoral-withdrawal-post-ends): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State held that a post under Article 50/d of Law No. 2547 is support limited to the duration of postgraduate education and that ties with the post… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/doktora-kaydini-sildiren-50-d-arastirma-gorevlisinin-ilisigi-kesilir
  - Q: Are the ties of a 50/d research assistant with the post severed if the doctoral registration is deleted?
    A: Under Turkish law, in the decision of the 8th Chamber of the Council of State of 24.11.2025, the severance of the ties with the post of the claimant, who had the doctoral registration deleted at their own request, was regarded as a requirement of the statutory rule. The Chamber saw the 50/d post as support limited to the duration of postgraduate education and found it sufficient that the education had ended for any reason.
  - Q: Does an application for transfer to a 33/a post prevent the severance of ties?
    A: It did not in this case. Stating that the claimant’s request for appointment to a 33/a post was not the subject of the action and that the administration’s will concerning that request could not be assessed in this file, the Chamber found the severance lawful. What became of the request was not discussed in the decision.
  - Q: Does the decision also apply to research assistants in 33/a posts?
    A: The reasoning of the decision rests on the link between the 50/d post and postgraduate education; the position of research assistants in 33/a posts was not addressed in the decision. Under Article 33/a of Law No. 2547, appointment to that post is for at most three years and a reappointment may be made by the same procedure.
  - Q: Where does the file go after the quashing decision?
    A: The quashing decision is final and was given by majority. The file was sent back to the regional administrative court for a new decision; the new decision given after the quashing could not be located.
- [Excluding Associate Degree Courses Is Unlawful: Turkish Council of State on 2023 Associate Professor Criteria](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/associate-professor-application-associate-degree-courses): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State annulled the omission of associate degree courses from the 2023 associate professorship application requirements and upheld the other… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/docentlik-basvurusunda-on-lisans-dersinin-dislanmasi
  - Q: Do associate degree courses count for an associate professorship application?
    A: The 2023 requirements did not include associate degree courses. The 8th Chamber of the Council of State annulled this omission; however, the decision was given subject to appeal in cassation and may not be final.
  - Q: Has the teaching requirement for associate professorship been abolished?
    A: No. The claim against the requirement to teach undergraduate or graduate courses for four semesters or two years (at least 2 points) was dismissed unanimously. Those who have served on the permanent teaching staff for at least two years after their doctorate are deemed to meet the requirement.
  - Q: On what condition is a work presented at a scientific meeting scored?
    A: The requirement that the organising committee include an officially appointed academic representative was found lawful. The rule that at most one work presented at the same meeting is scored was also upheld.
  - Q: Is this decision final?
    A: The decision was given by the Council of State acting as a first-instance court and is subject to appeal in cassation to the Plenary Session of the Administrative Law Chambers. No cassation decision could be found; the decision may not be final.
- [Lecturer Term Non-Extension Held Lawful on Combined Grounds: Turkish Council of State on Three Facts](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/lecturer-term-non-extension-upheld-combined-grounds): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State held lawful the non-extension of a lecturer’s term of office, assessing together that the precondition in Article 31 of Law No. 2547 had… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ogretim-gorevlisi-gorev-suresi-uzatilmamasi-alana-ogretim-uyesi-atanmasi
  - Q: Can a lecturer’s term of office be left unextended?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law, Article 31 of Law No. 2547 provides that a lecturer’s duties end automatically at the end of the term of appointment and that the lecturer may be reappointed. According to the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, the discretion as to non-extension is limited by the public interest and the requirements of the service and is subject to judicial review in terms of reason and purpose.
  - Q: Is the appointment of a faculty member to the field in which a lecturer teaches enough on its own not to extend the term?
    A: This decision does not say so. The Chamber accepted that the precondition in Article 31 ceased to exist when an assistant professor was appointed to the field; however, it reached its conclusion together with the disciplinary penalty, the action against which was dismissed on appeal, and the fulfilment of only 6 hours of the 12-hour teaching load.
  - Q: How does this decision differ from the decision on the research assistant contract?
    A: In the Chamber’s decision of 25.11.2025, no disciplinary investigation had been opened into the alleged disruption of work, no concrete findings had been made and relying on a single delay alone was held disproportionate. In this decision, by contrast, the action against the disciplinary penalty had been dismissed on appeal, the shortfall in the teaching load had been established in numbers and an assistant professor had been appointed to the field.
  - Q: Where does the file go after the quashing decision?
    A: The quashing decision is final and was given by majority. The file was sent back to the regional administrative court for a new decision; the new decision given after the quashing could not be located.
- [Missing Rules on Deleting Student Penalties Unlawful: Turkish Council of State on Disciplinary Records](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/student-disciplinary-penalty-record-deletion): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, ruling at first instance and by majority, held that the absence of a clear rule on keeping higher education students’ disciplinary… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ogrenci-disiplin-cezalarinin-sicilden-silinmesi
  - Q: Is a disciplinary penalty received at university deleted from the record?
    A: Under Turkish law, Law No. 2547 contains no clear provision on how long a penalty is kept or when it is deleted; Article 54 only states that penalties are entered in the student’s file. The 8th Chamber of the Council of State held that this gap should not be applied to the detriment of students; the decision may not be final.
  - Q: Is a penalty for cheating entered in the student’s file?
    A: Yes. Under Article 54 of Law No. 2547, disciplinary penalties are entered in the student’s file. Under the current text, attempting to cheat in an exam calls for a reprimand, while cheating or helping others cheat calls for suspension for one semester, and cheating by threats for two semesters.
  - Q: Did the Council of State order the penalty to be deleted?
    A: No. The Chamber annulled YÖK’s implied refusal of the request to regulate the matter and the dean’s refusal of the request to implement the Ombudsman Institution’s recommendation; it did not itself rule that the penalty be deleted.
  - Q: Is the decision final?
    A: The decision was given at first instance, with an appeal open to the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State within 30 days of notification. No decision of the Assembly in this file could be found; the decision may not be final.
- [No Age-35 Limit on Research Assistant Reappointment: Turkish Council of State on Article 33 of Law No. 2547](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/research-assistant-age-limit-reappointment): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State unanimously and finally held that the age-35 condition in Article 33 of Law No. 2547 applies only to the first application for a research… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/arastirma-gorevlisinde-35-yas-siniri-yeniden-atamada-aranmaz
  - Q: Is there an age limit for becoming a research assistant?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law, Article 33 of Law No. 2547 requires that, to apply for the post, a candidate must not have reached the age of thirty-five as of 1 January of the year in which the examination is held. The condition was added to the Law by Law No. 7243 of 15.04.2020.
  - Q: Can a research assistant who has turned 35 have their contract not renewed for that reason?
    A: According to the reasoned decisions of the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, no. The age condition applies to the first application; it does not apply to the reappointment of a research assistant who met the condition when taking up the post.
  - Q: Can the contract be left unrenewed on another ground?
    A: Yes. The administration has discretion in reappointment; however, a legally acceptable concrete reason must be shown, such as neglect of duties, indiscipline, academic insufficiency or the post no longer being needed.
  - Q: Is there a Council of State decision to the contrary?
    A: On 19.06.2025 the Chamber upheld, without giving reasons, a regional administrative court decision that had applied the age condition to the extension of a term of office as well. The upheld decision also had a basis independent of age; the Chamber’s reasoned decisions, by contrast, hold that age is not required on reappointment.
- [No Regulatory Gap for Staff Already Over the Norm Cadre: Turkish Council of State on University Staff](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/norm-cadre-surplus-teaching-staff-keep-posts): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State upheld the 8th Chamber’s decision rejecting the claim of incomplete regulation, because provisional Article 1 of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/norm-kadro-fazlasi-ogretim-elemanlari-kadrolarinda-gorev-yapmaya-devam-eder
  - Q: What happens to teaching staff who were in excess of the norm cadre when the Regulation entered into force?
    A: Under Turkish law, provisional Article 1 provides that they continue to serve in the posts they hold; those appointed to posts advertised before the Regulation also remain in their posts even if they create a surplus over the norm cadre. The Council of State did not accept the claim of incomplete regulation because of this provision.
  - Q: Does the decision also cover those who later became surplus to the norm cadre?
    A: No. The upheld reasoning rests on provisional Article 1; surpluses arising after the Regulation entered into force were not addressed in the decision.
  - Q: What is the maximum ratio for the same title today?
    A: Under the current text, in appointments exceeding the minimum cadre number at most 3/4 of the posts may be used for the same title; the requirement does not apply in certain units, research universities and vocational schools. The 2023 and 2025 amendments were not assessed in the decision.
  - Q: What became of the allegation that the underlying Presidential Decree provision is unconstitutional?
    A: The Assembly did not consider the allegation serious and did not refer the matter to the Constitutional Court. One member argued in a dissenting opinion that norm cadres must be regulated by statute.
- [Rector Holds Power Not to Extend Assistant Professor Terms: Turkish Council of State on Vice-Rector Decision](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/assistant-professor-term-non-extension-rector-authority): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, in a final and unanimous decision quashing a regional administrative court judgment that had rejected the appeal, held that an act not… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/doktor-ogretim-uyesinin-gorev-suresini-uzatmama-yetkisi-rektore-aittir
  - Q: Who decides not to extend an assistant professor's term of office?
    A: Under Turkish law, Article 23 of Law No. 2547 provides that an assistant professor (doktor öğretim üyesi) is appointed by the rector for at most four years; those whose term has ended may be reappointed. The 8th Chamber of the Council of State held that, as no express provision allows delegation, the non-extension act must also be adopted by the rector.
  - Q: Why is a non-extension act signed by a vice-rector unlawful?
    A: According to the Chamber, delegation of authority is possible only where the law expressly permits it; Article 23 contains no such provision. An act of an unauthorised authority does not become lawful through subsequent acceptance or ratification by the competent authority.
  - Q: Is a defect of authority examined at later stages of the case too?
    A: Yes. According to the Chamber, the element of authority concerns public order; the administrative judicial authorities must take a defect of authority into account at every stage of the case, including review by way of legal remedy.
  - Q: Does quashing on grounds of authority mean the reason for non-extension was wrong?
    A: No. The Chamber stated that where a defect of authority is found, the other elements of the act cannot be examined, and it made no assessment of the merits of the act. Whether the minimum-score requirement was lawfully applied to the claimant was not answered in this decision.
  - Q: Are lost salary and personnel rights paid immediately?
    A: In this decision, no. The Chamber stated that, since the merits of the act had not been examined and there was no new contractual relationship, no direct decision granting losses that were not definite and clear could be given at this stage on the assumption that the contract was renewed.
- [Research Assistant Non-Renewal Needs Concrete Reasons: Turkish Council of State Requires Proportionate Grounds](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/research-assistant-contract-non-renewal-concrete-reasons): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State held, by majority and as a final decision, that not renewing a research assistant's contract requires concrete and proportionate reasons… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/arastirma-gorevlisi-sozlesmesinin-yenilenmemesi-somut-gerekce
  - Q: Can a research assistant's contract be left unrenewed?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law, Article 33/a of Law No. 2547 provides that research assistants are appointed for at most three years and that their duties end automatically at the end of the term; reappointment is at the administration’s discretion. According to the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, however, the reason for non-renewal must be shown concretely and must be proportionate.
  - Q: Does a research assistant at a foundation university enjoy the same protection?
    A: The Chamber stated that no distinction is made between the professional security of academic staff at foundation and state higher education institutions. Article 23 of the Regulation on Foundation Higher Education Institutions also subjects their working principles to the provisions for state universities; salary and other personnel rights are governed by the Labour Law.
  - Q: Is a written warning or being late to an examination enough for non-renewal?
    A: In this decision, no. The Chamber found that relying on a single 20-minute delay, which was met with a written warning and did not recur, as the sole ground for non-renewal was contrary to the principle of proportionality and to equity. The outcome depends on the concrete circumstances of each case.
  - Q: Can the administration raise a new ground during the proceedings?
    A: According to the Chamber, a ground that was not stated when the act was adopted and is raised while the case is pending does not on its own render the act lawful. For this reason, the decrease in student numbers added at the appeal stage did not save the act in this case.
- [Research Assistant Requirements, 50/d and 33/a Posts, Term](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/research-assistant-requirements-50-d-33-a-posts-term): Under Turkish law, applying for a research assistant post requires at least 70 in ALES, at least 50 points in a central foreign language examination, not having reached the age of 35 on 1 January of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/arastirma-gorevlisi-olma-sartlari-50-d-33-a-gorev-suresi
  - Q: What are the requirements for becoming a research assistant in Türkiye?
    A: The general conditions in Article 48 of Law No. 657, at least 70 in ALES, at least 50 points (or the equivalent) in a central foreign language examination accepted by YÖK, not having reached the age of thirty-five on 1 January of the year of the entrance examination and, at state universities, being a postgraduate student (Central Examination Regulation art. 6 and art. 7). The senate may require higher scores.
  - Q: Is there an age limit for becoming a research assistant?
    A: Yes. Under Article 33/a of Law No. 2547 and Article 7/1 of the Regulation, the candidate must not have reached the age of thirty-five on 1 January of the year of the entrance examination. The 8th Chamber of the Council of State found the Regulation provision lawful; the decision may not be final. The age requirement does not apply to the reappointment of a serving research assistant.
  - Q: What is the difference between a 33/a and a 50/d research assistant?
    A: A 50/d post is for postgraduate students; the appointment is for one year at a time and ties with the post are severed when postgraduate education ends, once the periods in additional Article 38/2 have run. In a 33/a post the appointment lasts at most three years and may be renewed by the same procedure.
  - Q: What happens to a 50/d research assistant’s post after the doctorate or master’s degree?
    A: For those who complete a doctorate, the ties with the post are extended by one year; the post of those meeting the institution’s performance criteria may continue for one more year, after which the ties are severed automatically. For those who complete a master’s with thesis, the ties continue for at most six more months and are severed if the assistant does not begin a doctorate in the field at a state university within that period (additional art. 38/2).
  - Q: Who is exempt from the ALES requirement?
    A: The central examination requirement does not apply to those who have completed a doctorate, specialty training in medicine, dentistry, pharmacy or veterinary medicine, or proficiency in art, or to those who have worked or are working in a teaching staff post (art. 14/1); their ALES score is taken as 70.
  - Q: Within what time limit can an action be brought against non-appointment or non-extension of the term?
    A: An annulment action is brought before the administrative court within sixty days from the day following written notification (Law No. 2577 art. 7). In cases concerning the termination of duties, the administrative court of the last place of duty has jurisdiction (Law No. 2577 art. 33/2).
- [Residence Rule Upheld for Assoc. Prof. Language Exams Abroad: Turkish Council of State on the 2022 Guide](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/associate-professorship-language-exam-abroad-residence-rule): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State found the provision of the 2022 March Term Associate Professorship Application Guide requiring associate professor candidates who sit a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law).   - Q: What is the foreign language requirement for an associate professorship application?
    A: Under Turkish law, Article 24 of Law No. 2547 requires the candidate to obtain at least 55 points in a central foreign language exam determined by YÖK, or an equivalent score in an exam whose international validity is accepted by YÖK.
  - Q: Is a foreign language exam taken abroad accepted in an associate professorship application?
    A: Under the 2022 March Term Guide examined by the 8th Chamber of the Council of State, it was accepted if residence of at least six months in the exam country, up to and including the exam date, was documented. The Chamber held this requirement lawful; the decision may not be final, and later guides could not be verified for this article.
  - Q: Why did the Council of State find the six-month residence requirement lawful?
    A: The Chamber stated that the requirement seeks not short trips but actual presence of a certain continuity, provides for a substantive rather than formal control and aims to protect equality and merit, and found nothing contrary to the public interest and the requirements of the service.
  - Q: What if the requirement was introduced after the exam was taken?
    A: In this case the claimant argued that the requirement was first introduced in the 2021 September Guide, that the exam process had begun before that guide was announced, and that no transitional provision had been made. Noting that the rule was expressly included in the 2021 September and 2022 March guides, the Chamber did not accept the allegation of being unaware of the rule and dismissed the action; it did not separately address the transitional provision argument.
- [Split Experts in Professor Case Need New Panel: Turkish Council of State on Conflicting Expert Reports](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/professor-appointment-conflicting-expert-reports-new-panel): Under Turkish law, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State stated that, in a professor appointment where both candidates met the advertised requirements, the administration must compare them on… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/profesor-kadrosu-atama-iptal-davasinda-bilirkisi-ayrismasi-yeni-heyet
  - Q: If both candidates meet the requirements, may the administration appoint whichever candidate it wishes to a professor post?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the Chamber in this decision, no: the administration may make a choice in the exercise of its discretion, but it cannot rely only on the formal requirements and must compare the candidates on objective and verifiable criteria in terms of merit and scientific competence.
  - Q: What happens if the expert panel is divided on which candidate is superior?
    A: In this case, the Chamber stated that the divergence of opinion raised doubt as to the adequacy and soundness of the report on which the judgment was based, and considered an examination by a new expert panel of different persons necessary.
  - Q: What should the new expert panel examine?
    A: According to the Chamber, the panel should examine the candidates’ works and studies in terms of quality, taking the requirements of the advertisement into account, compare them on objective criteria and, where necessary, apply scoring based on concrete criteria.
  - Q: Does this decision mean that the claimant is appointed to the professor post?
    A: No. The Chamber did not decide which candidate is superior; the file went back to the regional administrative court. If that court complies with the quashing decision, it decides on the basis of the new expert report; if it insists on its earlier decision, the insistence decision is reviewed on appeal by the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers.
- [YÖK Paragraph Barring Court-Backed Appointments Annulled: Turkish Council of State on YLSY (Law No. 1416)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/ylsy-scholar-lecturer-appointment-court-rulings-yok-letter): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State upheld, on the same reasoning, the annulment of the paragraph of a YÖK general letter stating that those deemed… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ylsy-bursiyeri-mahkeme-karariyla-da-olsa-atanamaz-yok-yazisi
  - Q: May YÖK state in a general letter that scholarship holders deemed unsuccessful cannot be appointed even by court decisions?
    A: Under Turkish law, in this case no: the 8th Chamber of the Council of State annulled that paragraph as contrary to the administration’s duty to comply with court decisions, and the Assembly upheld the annulment on the same reasoning.
  - Q: Does the decision mean that every scholarship holder who did not complete a doctorate must be appointed?
    A: No. The decision only upheld the annulment of the fifth paragraph; it made no ruling on any individual right to appointment or on the compensation obligation.
  - Q: May a scholarship holder who has repaid the doctoral costs challenge the whole general letter?
    A: According to the Assembly, in this case yes: the whole letter concerns the claimant’s interest, and repayments made for the uncompleted doctorate do not remove the link of interest.
  - Q: At what stage is the file now?
    A: The parts concerning annulment and no need to decide were upheld finally. The dismissal for lack of standing was quashed, and the file was sent back to the 8th Chamber of the Council of State for a new decision on the other paragraphs of the general letter.

### Real Estate Law

- [1956–1983 Takings Valued at Suit Date by Character at Taking: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/de-facto-expropriation-1956-1983-character-at-taking-date): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held that compensation for land taken without expropriation between 09.10.1956 and 04.11.1983 is determined under… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kamulastirmasiz-el-atma-tazminati-el-atma-tarihindeki-nitelik
  - Q: By reference to which date is compensation for land taken between 1956 and 1983 calculated?
    A: According to the Assembly, the character of the land is determined as at the date of taking and its value as at the date of the action. Provisional art. 6 likewise provides that, if an action is brought, the value at the date of the action is determined on the basis of the characteristics at the date of seizure.
  - Q: If land taken between 1956 and 1983 is a zoned plot today, can it be treated as a building plot?
    A: In this case the Assembly did not consider sufficient a comparable-sales valuation based on the land’s status as a zoned plot at the date of the action. For takings within provisional art. 6, what is decisive is whether the land was a building plot at the date of taking.
  - Q: What happens if the file does not show the land’s character at the date of taking?
    A: According to the Assembly, that character must first be clearly determined, and only then may the land be valued under provisional art. 6.
  - Q: Does this rule also apply to takings after 04.11.1983?
    A: Provisional art. 6 covers only takings between 09.10.1956 and 04.11.1983. In another decision concerning land taken after 04.11.1983, the Assembly stated that provisional art. 6 did not apply.
  - Q: Is the decision final, and what happened to the file?
    A: The decision was given by majority and is final. The decision of resistance was reversed and the file was sent to the first-instance court.
- [Action for Annulment of Title and Re-Registration: Incapacity, Fraud, Abuse of Authority (Turkish Case-Law)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-annulment-title-re-registration): An action for annulment of title and re-registration is an in rem claim to cancel a wrongful land-registry record and re-register the immovable in the true right-holder's name. This article explains… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tapu-iptali-ve-tescil-davasi
  - Q: Which court hears this action?
    A: The Civil Court of First Instance. Venue is exclusive: the action is brought where the immovable is located (HMK arts.2 and 12).
  - Q: Is the action time-barred?
    A: As an action founded on ownership it is, in principle, not subject to limitation. The Court of Cassation holds that no forfeiture or limitation period applies in cases of forgery and abuse of authority; only where fraud alone is invoked may a period arise.
  - Q: What if the buyer is a good-faith third person?
    A: A third person acquiring in good-faith reliance on the register is protected (TMK art.1023). But one who knew or ought to have known of the defect is not (art.1024); a first acquirer through forgery cannot plead good faith.
  - Q: My agent sold my property below value; what can I do?
    A: This may be abuse of authority. A price far below market value or purchase without inspection may show the buyer was not in good faith. You may sue the buyer for annulment and re-registration, or failing that for the value.
  - Q: How is incapacity proved?
    A: Through medical records referable to the transfer date and, ultimately, a Forensic Medicine Institute report. The court must examine capacity before other grounds such as fraud.
  - Q: Whom should I sue?
    A: As a rule, the current registered owner. In chain transfers, intermediate owners alleged not to be in good faith may also be joined. For heirs, some grounds require the action to be brought jointly.
- [Action for Determination of Rent: The Increase Ceiling (CPI Limit), Equity After Five Years, Time to Sue](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-determination-rent): The action for determination of rent is a public-order action by which the court fixes the renewed-period rent in residential and roofed-workplace leases. This article explains, under TBK… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kira-bedelinin-tespiti-davasi
  - Q: Who may bring the rent-determination action?
    A: Both landlord and tenant. The landlord usually sues to increase the rent, the tenant to have it fixed/reduced to market. Even without an increase clause, determination under TBK art.344 may always be sought.
  - Q: What is the maximum new-period increase?
    A: The increase in renewed periods cannot exceed the twelve-month average CPI change of the previous lease year (TBK art.344); agreements exceeding it are void as to the excess. The temporary 25% ceiling on residential rents expired on 01.07.2024.
  - Q: How is the rent fixed after five years?
    A: After five years and at the end of each five-year period, the rent is fixed by the judge according to equity — no longer bound solely by the CPI — having regard also to the property's condition and comparable rents. This is when the rent may be brought closer to current market value.
  - Q: When should I file the rent-determination action?
    A: It may be filed at any time. But for the fixed rent to apply from the start of the new period, the action must be filed at least thirty days before the new period, or written notice of increase (ihtar) must be given to the tenant within that time. If the contract has an increase clause, an action within the new period suffices.
  - Q: Which court is competent?
    A: Disputes arising from a lease fall within the civil court of peace irrespective of the amount (HMK art.4). Venue is the defendant's domicile or the place of performance (as a rule, where the property is located).
  - Q: Can I bring an adaptation action before five years because inflation is high?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation, no. In residential and roofed-workplace leases TBK art.344 is a special and mandatory provision, so an art.138 adaptation action cannot be brought before five years merely on grounds of inflation/inadequate rent. But where genuine extraordinary circumstances (earthquake, epidemic, war) exist, an adaptation action may always be brought.
- [Action for Prevention of Interference with Property: Conditions, Co-Owners, Encroachment, Mesne Profits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-prevention-interference-property): Conditions of the action for prevention of interference with property under Turkish law; Articles 683, 693, 725, 983-984 and 995 of the Turkish Civil Code; interference between co-owners and the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/elatmanin-onlenmesi
  - Q: Is the action subject to a limitation period?
