    A: A claim based on ownership is not subject to prescription. Claims based solely on protected possession lapse after two months from knowledge of the act and its author, and in any event one year after the act (Civil Code Art. 984).
  - Q: My co-owner uses the whole property. Can I sue?
    A: Yes, you may at any time seek prevention of interference with your share. But if you peacefully use an area corresponding to your share, the action will not be entertained; partition or judicial dissolution is the remedy. A de facto partition binding on all co-owners will also be protected.
  - Q: My neighbour's building encroaches on my land. Can I obtain demolition?
    A: If the builder was not in good faith, the encroaching part is demolished regardless of the extent of the loss. If he was in good faith and you did not object within fifteen days of learning of the encroachment, he may claim an easement or transfer of the encroached parcel against payment (Civil Code Art. 725).
  - Q: Which court is competent?
    A: The civil court of first instance (asliye hukuk). Actions solely for the protection of possession belong to the civil court of peace. Venue lies exclusively with the court of the place where the immovable is situated.
  - Q: Can I claim mesne profits in the same action?
    A: Yes; this is a joinder of claims. Fees are levied on the aggregate value, and since the Assembly of Civil Chambers' decision of 01.04.2026 the aggregate is also used to determine the appeal threshold.
  - Q: My tenant refuses to leave. Should I bring this action?
    A: No. Where a valid lease exists the occupation is not unlawful; the correct route is an eviction action or eviction through enforcement proceedings.
- [Action for Release of a Mortgage (İpoteğin Fekki) in Türkiye: Loan Repaid but the Bank Will Not Lift It?](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-release-mortgage): Once the secured debt ends, the owner may ask the bank to cancel the mortgage; but a maximum-amount mortgage tied to a general loan agreement does not lapse just because one loan is closed. The… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ipotegin-fekki-davasi
  - Q: The loan is paid off but the bank will not lift the mortgage; what should I do?
    A: First ask the bank in writing to release the mortgage and to provide a statement of any cash and non-cash debts. If the receivable has ended, the owner may request cancellation of the mortgage (Turkish Civil Code art. 883/1); if the bank does not issue a release letter, an action for release is brought before the commercial court of first instance. If the bank’s non-cash risk continues on the filing date, there is a risk of bearing the costs (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2022/87, K. 2023/133).
  - Q: The loan I secured has been closed; why is the mortgage still in place?
    A: A mortgage tied to a general loan agreement can be created to cover all of the company’s existing and future debts, non-cash risks such as letters of guarantee and cheques, and suretyship debts; its scope is determined by the mortgage deed. The court therefore looks not at a single loan but at all debts covered by the mortgage deed (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/4111, K. 2026/1119).
  - Q: If I serve a notice on the bank, will the mortgage stop covering new loans?
    A: As a rule it continues to cover them. The Assembly of Civil Chambers holds that no statutory provision allows a mortgagor to escape the mortgage debt by a unilateral declaration (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/3128, K. 2021/1224); in 2026 the 11th Civil Chamber also reversed a release judgment based on such a notice (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/5314, K. 2026/2478).
  - Q: My loan was sold to an asset management company; who lifts the mortgage?
    A: The assignment of a mortgage-secured receivable does not require registration in the land register, and the mortgage passes to the assignee together with the receivable (Turkish Civil Code art. 891; Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/381, K. 2022/4548). The release request should therefore also be addressed to the assignee company.
  - Q: Is mediation mandatory before an action for release of a mortgage?
    A: Not if only release of the mortgage is sought; that claim is not included in the list in Turkish Commercial Code art. 5/A/1 (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/2809, K. 2025/7367). If a negative declaratory or compensation claim concerning a sum of money is added, a mediator must first be approached for that claim; otherwise that claim may be dismissed on procedural grounds (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/6375, K. 2025/3913).
- [Annulment of Title and Re-Registration for Fraud in Türkiye: Elements, One-Year Period, Proof, Third Parties](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/annulment-title-re-registration-fraud): An owner who sold immovable property as a result of deceit (fraud) is not bound by the contract under art. 36 of the Turkish Code of Obligations; within one year of discovering the deceit, the owner… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hile-nedeniyle-tapu-iptali-ve-tescil
  - Q: Can a title transfer obtained through fraud be cancelled?
    A: Yes. An owner who sold as a result of deceit by the other party, or by a third person whose deceit the other party knew or was in a position to know of, is not bound by the contract; as the registration based on it is wrongful, cancellation of the title entry and re-registration in the owner’s name may be claimed (Civil Code art. 1024). If a price was paid, the seller returns it.
  - Q: Within what period must a fraud action be filed?
    A: Within one year of discovering the deceit, the deceived party must declare that it is not bound by the contract or reclaim what it performed; otherwise the contract is deemed ratified (Code of Obligations art. 39). The period runs from discovery, not from the date of sale, and no separate upper limit running from the date of the contract is provided for deceit. As the declaration may also be made by filing suit, filing within that year is the safest course.
  - Q: How is fraud proved?
    A: By any evidence, including witnesses; witnesses may be heard on allegations of defective consent as an exception to the requirement of documentary proof (Code of Civil Procedure art. 203). Witness statements are assessed together with the gap between the price and the market value, the parties’ relationship, transactions after the transfer, medical records and, where necessary, a report of the Forensic Medicine Institute. If there is no other evidence, the oath should be relied on expressly.
  - Q: What happens if the property has been sold to someone else?
    A: A third party who acquired the property relying on the land register in good faith is protected (Civil Code art. 1023); a person who knew or ought to have known of the wrongful registration is not, and cancellation may also be sought against that person. Where the property remains with a person in good faith, compensation is claimed from the deceiver. To prevent a sale during the proceedings, an interim injunction should be sought and annotated in the land register.
  - Q: Can the heirs bring a fraud action if the seller has died?
    A: Yes, but the action must be brought on behalf of the estate and, as a rule, by all the heirs together or with the consent of the other heirs; if the heirs cannot agree, the civil court of peace may be asked to appoint a representative for the community of heirs (Civil Code art. 640). The Court of Cassation held that an action based on fraud and brought only in proportion to the claimant’s inheritance share could not be heard.
  - Q: Is it mandatory to apply to a mediator before filing suit?
    A: No. An action for annulment of title and re-registration based on fraud is not among the disputes in which mediation is a precondition (Law No. 6325 art. 18/B). However, mediation is a precondition if a sum of money or compensation is claimed in a commercial case between merchants; the parties may also choose to resolve a dispute over the transfer of property through voluntary mediation.
- [Annulment of Title for Gabin (Exploitation) in Türkiye: Elements, Time Limits, Valuation (TBK Art. 28)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/annulment-title-gabin-property-sales): If a property was sold clearly below its value by exploiting the seller’s distress, thoughtlessness or inexperience, the seller may, under Code of Obligations art. 28, declare that he is not bound by… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/gabin-asiri-yararlanma-nedeniyle-tapu-iptali
  - Q: What is gabin?
    A: Gabin (excessive benefit) is a clear disproportion between the performances in a contract created by exploiting the injured party’s distress, thoughtlessness or inexperience (Code of Obligations art. 28). In a property sale the seller may declare that he is not bound by the contract and claim annulment of title and re-registration.
  - Q: Can a property sold far below its value be recovered?
    A: A low price alone is not enough; the seller’s distress, thoughtlessness or inexperience and the buyer’s knowing exploitation of it must also be proven. If the conditions are proven and the right is exercised in time, the property returns to the seller by a judgment of annulment and re-registration and the seller returns the price received; a good-faith third party’s acquisition is, however, protected (Civil Code art. 1023).
  - Q: Within what time must a gabin action be brought?
    A: Within one year from discovery of the thoughtlessness or inexperience or, in the case of distress, from the end of the distress, and in any event within five years from the conclusion of the contract (Code of Obligations art. 28/2). The upper limit is five years, not ten; the Court of Cassation treats this as a preclusive period, not a limitation period, and applies it to both options.
  - Q: How is a clear disproportion established?
    A: The property’s real value at the moment the contract was concluded is determined by site inspection and examination by a qualified expert, taking into account zoning status, location, comparable sales and encumbrances such as attachments, and is compared with the counter-performance actually given to the seller. The law sets no ratio; the disproportion must be high enough to strike anyone familiar with the matter.
  - Q: Can only the difference be claimed without setting the sale aside?
    A: Yes. The injured party may also remain bound by the contract and claim removal of the disproportion between the performances (Code of Obligations art. 28). This is a money claim subject to the same one-year and five-year periods; if the parties are merchants and the case is commercial, recourse to a mediator before filing suit is a precondition.
  - Q: What is the difference between gabin and fraud?
    A: In fraud the seller is induced to contract by deceit and is not bound even if the mistake was not fundamental (Code of Obligations art. 36). In gabin no deceit is required; a clear disproportion and the exploitation of the seller’s distress, thoughtlessness or inexperience are required (Code of Obligations art. 28).
- [Building Declaration Fails Without Legal Interest: Turkish Court of Cassation on Muhdesat Without Partition](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/muhdesat-declaration-legal-interest-partition-expropriation): Under Turkish law, the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that, as no partition action had been brought and no expropriation procedure existed in respect of the immovable, the plaintiff… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/muhdesatin-tespiti-davasinda-hukuki-yarar-ortakligin-giderilmesi
  - Q: Can an action to establish who owns muhdesat always be brought?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, the plaintiff must have a current legal interest under HMK art. 106/2; the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation stated that there was no legal interest in this case, in which there was no partition action or expropriation procedure in respect of the immovable.
  - Q: In which situations is a legal interest accepted?
    A: The Chamber referred to a pending partition action and an expropriation procedure. In its decision of 23.10.2024, the Assembly of Civil Chambers also listed an urban transformation project among such exceptional situations.
  - Q: Does the result change if some defendants admit the claim?
    A: It did not in this case. According to the Chamber, since the determination of muhdesat is a material fact, no weight can be given to the statements of some defendants admitting the claim.
  - Q: Did this decision end the case?
    A: No. The Chamber reversed the first-instance judgment and sent the file back to the first-instance court; it also stated that the claim labelled as ecrimisil had to be examined as a rent receivable.
- [Co-owner Who Approved the Sale Cannot Claim Pre-emption: Turkish Cassation Assembly on Joining Negotiations](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pre-emption-waiver-co-owner-consented-to-sale): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation found that a pre-emption action brought later by a co-owner who took part in the sale negotiations, approved the sale of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04; updated: 2026-11-17. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/satis-pazarligina-katilip-onay-veren-paydas-onalim-hakkini-kullanamaz
  - Q: How is the right of pre-emption waived?
    A: Under Turkish law, a waiver must be made in official form and annotated in the land register. Renouncing the exercise of the right in a specific sale is subject to written form and may be made before or after the sale (Civil Code art. 733/2).
  - Q: Can a co-owner who negotiated and approved the sale bring a pre-emption action?
    A: The Assembly of Civil Chambers found that the exercise of pre-emption by a co-owner who personally took part in and approved the pre-sale dealings and made no claim to the share sold is contrary to Civil Code art. 2; the outcome depends on the circumstances and the evidence.
  - Q: Can a co-owner who took part and bid in an auction later exercise pre-emption?
    A: In 2023 the Assembly of Civil Chambers held by majority that they can; having taken part in the auction was not regarded as a bar to pre-emption.
  - Q: At what stage can the objection of bad faith be raised?
    A: At any stage of the proceedings; the court also considers it of its own motion.
  - Q: Within what period must a pre-emption action be brought?
    A: Within three months from notification of the sale to the right holder and, in any event, within one year from the sale (Civil Code art. 733). For sales made before the amendment entered into force, the outer limit is two years (Civil Code provisional art. 1/1).
- [Court Must Offer a Choice When Property Is Sold Mid-Case: Turkish Court of Cassation on CCP Art. 125](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/land-sold-during-title-annulment-case-plaintiff-options): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held by majority vote that if the immovable in dispute is sold through enforcement proceedings while an action for… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/muris-muvazaasi-davasi-surerken-tasinmaz-cebren-satilirsa-hmk-125
  - Q: What happens if the immovable is sold while an action for annulment of title is pending?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly, the subject matter may be transferred during the action unless there is a restricting reason, but the transferring defendant no longer has standing as a party. Under Code of Civil Procedure art. 125/1, the plaintiff continues the action against the transferee or converts it into an action for compensation against the transferor.
  - Q: Must the court ask the plaintiff which option it chooses?
    A: Yes. A court that learns of the transfer reminds the plaintiff of the alternative rights of its own motion and asks which one is chosen; without that choice, no decision may be rendered between the former parties.
  - Q: Does a sale through enforcement proceedings also count as a transfer?
    A: It did in this decision. Under Civil Code art. 705/2, ownership is acquired before registration in the case of enforcement; proceedings under Code of Civil Procedure art. 125 must also be carried out for an immovable whose auction has become final.
  - Q: What happens if the sale comes to light at the Court of Cassation stage?
    A: The reminder rule concerns public order and is applied at every stage, including appellate review. In this case the sale was reported before the Assembly of Civil Chambers, and the judgment was reversed for that reason.
- [De Facto Expropriation Claims in Türkiye: Compensation, Injunction and Mesne Profits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/de-facto-expropriation-claims): A guide to de facto expropriation (kamulastirmasiz el atma) claims under Turkish law: the physical vs regulatory taking distinction and competent jurisdiction; the owner's alternative remedies… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kamulastirmasiz-el-atma-davasi
  - Q: What is de facto expropriation?
    A: The administration’s seizure of privately owned land without a lawful expropriation and without compensation — physically (roads, buildings, parks, power lines) or legally, by designating the land for public services in the zoning plan and leaving it unexpropriated for years.
  - Q: What is the difference between physical and regulatory taking, and which court hears each?
    A: In a physical taking the administration actually seizes the land; the action is brought before the civil court of first instance where the land is situated. In a regulatory taking the land is merely restricted by the zoning plan; the compensation action is brought before the administrative courts.
  - Q: Which actions can I bring against the administration?
    A: You have alternative remedies: an action to stop the interference and demolish the installations, or, accepting the situation, an action for payment of the land’s value. In both cases, mesne profits (ecrimisil) may be claimed for the period of wrongful use.
  - Q: How is compensation calculated and when does interest start?
    A: The land is valued as at the date of action — by comparable sales for building plots and by the net-income method for agricultural land. Statutory interest generally runs from the date of action, and the land is registered in the administration’s name or struck from the registry.
  - Q: Is there a time limit for de facto expropriation actions?
    A: After the annulment of article 38, ownership-based actions to stop the interference and for compensation are subject to no prescription or forfeiture period while the taking continues. Ecrimisil is limited to the preceding five years; the three-month period applies to 1956–1983 takings after the disagreement report, and administrative time limits apply to regulatory takings.
  - Q: Must I attempt settlement with the administration before suing?
    A: Only for takings between 09.10.1956 and 04.11.1983 is the settlement procedure a procedural prerequisite (Provisional art. 6). For takings after 04.11.1983, no settlement requirement applies and the action may be brought directly.
- [Dissolution of Co-ownership (Partition): Division in Kind and Sale](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/dissolution-co-ownership): The action for dissolution of co-ownership (partition): each co-owner's right to demand partition (Civil Code Art. 698), division in kind as the priority and, where impossible, sale by public auction… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ortakligin-giderilmesi-izale-i-suyu
  - Q: Can I dissolve the co-ownership even if the other co-owners do not want to?
    A: As a rule, yes. Each co-owner may demand partition; this right may be limited only by agreement (at most ten years) or by dedicating the asset to a permanent purpose. A demand at an inopportune time is not heard.
  - Q: Can the court order a sale directly?
    A: No. First a site inspection and expert examination determine whether division in kind is possible. If it is not, or the asset cannot be divided without significant loss of value, a public-auction sale is ordered.
  - Q: Can the sale be conducted only among the co-owners?
    A: A sale conducted only among the co-owners (closed) depends on the consent of all co-owners. Without consent, the sale is by public auction open to everyone.
  - Q: Against whom should I bring the action?
    A: All co-owners must be parties; all co-owners other than you are shown as defendants. The action cannot proceed with a co-owner missing.
  - Q: Which court has jurisdiction?
    A: In partition actions the competent court is the magistrates' court (sulh hukuk); the court of the place where the immovable is located has jurisdiction. Applying to a mediator before suing is a precondition to suit.
- [Early Need-Based Eviction Mediation Valid If It Ends in Time: Turkish Court of Cassation on the Time to Sue](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/eviction-for-need-mediation-before-time-to-sue): Under Turkish law, the 3rd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, resolving a conflict between decisions of a regional court of appeal, unanimously held that a mandatory mediation application made… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ihtiyac-nedeniyle-tahliyede-erken-arabuluculuk-basvurusu
  - Q: When must an action for eviction for need be filed?
    A: Within one month starting, in fixed-term contracts, from the end of the term and, in indefinite-term contracts, from the date determined according to the termination and notice periods (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 350). If the landlord has notified the tenant in writing, at the latest within that period, that it will sue, the time to sue is deemed extended by one lease year (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 353). In lease disputes, applying to a mediator before filing suit is a precondition to suit (Law No. 6325 art. 18/B).
  - Q: Is the precondition met if mediation is sought before the time to sue begins?
    A: According to the 15.06.2026 decision of the 3rd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, the application is deemed timely if the process concluded within the time to sue. If the process ended before the time to sue began, the precondition is not met according to the Chamber’s 2025 decision.
  - Q: Does the time spent in mediation shorten the time to sue?
    A: Preclusive periods do not run from the application to the mediation office until the date on which the final record is drawn up (Law No. 6325 art. 18/A(15)).
  - Q: Does this decision apply to all eviction actions?
    A: The decision was given for actions for eviction for need to be brought under Turkish Code of Obligations art. 350 and rests on two cases arising from fixed-term leases; it does not assess other grounds for eviction.
- [Ecrimisil (Compensation for Unlawful Occupation): Bad-Faith Possessor, Co-owners, Prescription, Calculation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/ecrimisil): Ecrimisil is the compensation for unlawful occupation that a bad-faith possessor occupying property without lawful ground or consent must pay to the owner. This article explains, under TMK arts.683… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ecrimisil-haksiz-isgal-tazminati
  - Q: How far back can an ecrimisil action be brought?
    A: Ecrimisil actions are subject to a five-year prescription running backwards from the filing date, so as a rule at most the five-year period preceding the action (or amendment) may be claimed. But because prescription is a plea, if the defendant does not raise it in time, ecrimisil may be awarded for older periods too.
  - Q: Can I claim ecrimisil from my co-owner (e.g. my sibling)?
    A: As a rule, co-owners may claim ecrimisil from one another only if the prohibition-of-use condition has been met. Before the claimed period you must have notified your co-owner of your wish to benefit from the property or its income; demanding rent also serves as such notice.
  - Q: What is the difference between ecrimisil and rent?
    A: Rent rests on a contract and confers a right to use. Ecrimisil is compensation for unlawful occupation without any contract; it confers no right to use and, even if paid, the occupation remains unlawful.
  - Q: How is the ecrimisil amount determined?
    A: By determining the market rent the property could generate in the first period through comparable leases and an on-site expert examination. For later years the amount is computed forward by reflecting the PPI increase onto the first-period figure.
  - Q: Which court is competent for an ecrimisil action?
    A: Because ecrimisil is a monetary (compensation) claim, the civil court of first instance is competent irrespective of the amount (HMK art.2). As to venue, besides the defendant's domicile, the court of the place where the property is located may also be competent given the tortious character.
  - Q: Must a good-faith occupier pay ecrimisil?
    A: Ecrimisil arises only for a bad-faith possessor. A possessor genuinely believed to have used the property in good faith, relying on an existing right, is as a rule not liable to compensate under TMK art.993. Whether the occupier acted in good faith is therefore the decisive issue.
- [Eviction for Personal Need on Permanent Return to Türkiye: Conditions, Proof, Time Limits, Cassation Decisions](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/eviction-for-personal-need-returning-from-abroad-turkiye): Under Turkish law, how a landlord living abroad can evict a tenant on grounds of need on permanently returning to Türkiye under Code of Obligations arts. 350 and 351: the genuine, sincere and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disindan-kesin-donuste-ihtiyac-nedeniyle-tahliye
  - Q: I am returning permanently from abroad; can I evict my tenant?
    A: Yes, on grounds of need: if your need for a home is genuine, sincere and compelling, you can terminate a fixed-term lease by an action brought within one month from the end of the term (Code of Obligations art. 350/1). Mediation comes first in lease disputes.
  - Q: How do I prove my permanent return?
    A: The “has returned permanently” endorsement in the passport, proof of leaving your job abroad or retirement, a change-of-residence document and witnesses. The Assembly of Civil Chambers held a resignation letter, a change-of-residence document and witness statements sufficient.
  - Q: Can I sue while I am still abroad?
    A: You can, but the need must be real and imminent and must continue throughout the proceedings. The Court of Cassation accepted as proven by witnesses the need of a retired landlord living in Sweden; on the other hand, a need not yet arisen or depending on a long period is not a ground for eviction.
  - Q: My child will return from abroad; can I seek eviction for him or her?
    A: Yes; art. 350 also covers the need of descendants. But it must be proven that the need is genuine, sincere and compelling for your child and that the permanent return has happened or is very near.
  - Q: I recently bought a home to return from abroad; the tenant will not leave.
    A: A new owner may notify the tenant in writing of the need within one month of acquisition and sue after six months, or sue within one month from the end of the lease term (art. 351). The Assembly of Civil Chambers held proven the need of a claimant who bought a home to return from the USA.
- [Eviction of Leased Property: Terminating a Lease for Default, Need, an Undertaking to Vacate, Two Warnings](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/eviction-leased-property): The exhaustive grounds for evicting a tenant of residential or roofed business premises under Turkish law: non-payment of rent (TCO Art. 315), the landlord's need and reconstruction (Art. 350), the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kiralananin-tahliyesi
  - Q: The lease term has expired. Can I evict the tenant?
    A: No. In residential and roofed business leases the landlord cannot terminate merely because the term has expired (TCO Art. 347/1); the lease is extended for one year on the same terms. Termination without cause is possible only at the end of the ten-year extension period, on three months' notice before the end of an extension year.
  - Q: When can a tenant who fails to pay rent be evicted?
    A: The landlord must give written notice granting at least thirty days and stating that the lease will be terminated if payment is not made (TCO Art. 315). An action filed before that period expires will be dismissed. Alternatively, eviction may be sought through enforcement proceedings (EBL Art. 269/a).
  - Q: Is an undertaking to vacate signed together with the lease valid?
    A: As a rule, no. To be valid the undertaking must be given AFTER the property has been handed over to the tenant, be in writing and state a definite date.
  - Q: I have bought a property. When can I evict the tenant?
    A: Either notify the tenant in writing within one month of acquisition and sue six months later, or sue within one month of the expiry of the existing lease. The one-month notice requirement cannot be cured later.
  - Q: Whose need counts under the 'need' ground?
    A: Only the landlord, his spouse, descendants, ascendants and persons he is legally obliged to support. Siblings, nephews or in-laws do not count.
  - Q: May I re-let the property immediately after eviction for need?
    A: No. Without just cause, the property may not be let to anyone other than the former tenant for three years; otherwise compensation of at least one year's rent is payable (TCO Art. 355).
  - Q: Which court has jurisdiction?
    A: The civil court of peace, irrespective of the value of the claim (CCP Art. 4/1-a).
- [Expropriation Price Checked Against Final Same-Scheme Awards: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/expropriation-price-consistency-similar-plots-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held that, although the main principle for the expropriation price is real value, the price must be reviewed for… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kamulastirma-bedeli-benzer-tasinmaz-kesinlesen-bedellerle-uyum
  - Q: Is the final price awarded for neighbouring properties in the same expropriation taken into account in my case?
    A: Yes, as a yardstick for review. According to the Assembly, the main principle is real value; however, the price must be reviewed, taking particular characteristics into account as well, for consistency with the prices finalised in the same expropriation area, and there should be no excessive differences in value between properties in similar locations. The Assembly required the files that had passed the Special Chamber’s review to be evaluated.
  - Q: At what rate is an objective value increase applied?
    A: The decision sets no fixed rate. Features such as ease of transport and proximity to residential areas may increase the price, but the effect of each criterion must be explained. In this case the 25% rate applied on abstract grounds was not considered sufficient.
  - Q: May the regional court of appeal depart from the experts’ price and calculate the price itself?
    A: In this case the Assembly did not find it correct to give a decision by making its own calculation, in a manner not open to review, without obtaining a supplementary report on the reasons for departing from the expert panel’s price based on TAMSİS data.
  - Q: Is the decision final, and what happened to the case?
    A: The decision was given unanimously and is final. The decision of resistance was reversed; the file was sent to the regional court of appeal that gave the decision.
- [Expropriation Price Determination and Registration in Türkiye](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/expropriation-price-determination-registration-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the stages of the action for determination of the expropriation price and registration under art. 10 of Law No. 2942: summons and time-limits, the settlement hearing, site… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kamulastirma-bedelinin-tespiti-ve-tescil-davasi
  - Q: Who brings the action for determination of the expropriation price and registration, and which court hears it?
    A: The expropriating administration brings it when no agreement is reached under the purchase procedure. The action is heard by the civil court of first instance where the immovable is located; cassation review is carried out by the 5th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation.
  - Q: What time-limits does an owner who receives the summons have?
    A: The owner notifies the court in writing of defences and evidence on the value of the immovable within ten days of service. The period for an annulment action before the administrative courts against the expropriation measure, and for a correction action before the civil courts for material errors, is thirty days from service or notice.
  - Q: As of which date and how is the expropriation price determined?
    A: Value is determined by an expert panel of at least three persons as of the day the administration filed its documents with the court. Net income is used for land, comparable sales for building plots, and official unit prices and depreciation for buildings; increases in value caused by the project requiring the expropriation are not taken into account.
  - Q: What happens if the administration does not deposit the price in a bank?
    A: The administration is given fifteen days, which may be extended once. If the price is not deposited despite properly given periods, the action may be dismissed; dismissal directly at the end of the first period, however, has been treated by the Court of Cassation as a ground for reversal.
  - Q: Does the expropriation price bear interest?
    A: In 2023 the Constitutional Court annulled the provision on statutory interest after four months. In its decision of 08.04.2026 the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held by majority that the annulment also applies to actions not yet final and that the price bears the highest interest rate applied to public receivables from the date of the action to the date of judgment.
  - Q: Can the registration ruling be challenged, and what happens if the price is reduced on appeal?
    A: The registration ruling is final; appeal and cassation concern the price only. If the price that becomes final on appeal is less than the amount paid to the owner, the difference is claimed from the owner; however, no interest is charged for the period between the date of payment and the date of service of the repayment letter.
- [Expropriation and Easements in Geothermal Resource Areas in Türkiye: The Landowner's Rights](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/geothermal-expropriation-easement-landowner-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a geothermal licence holder gains access to land by agreement, easement, expropriation or urgent expropriation, and only an easement may be requested under an exploration licence.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/jeotermal-kaynak-sahasinda-kamulastirma-ve-irtifak-hakki
  - Q: Can a geothermal exploration licence holder expropriate my field under Turkish law?
    A: No. Under Article 12 of Law No. 5686, if no agreement is reached with the owner during the exploration licence period, only an easement may be requested. The Council of State Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers upheld an annulment decision holding that, since expropriation is not possible at the exploration licence stage, urgent expropriation cannot be applied either. Expropriation is possible only during the operating licence period and for necessary places such as drilling sites, transmission lines and capture works.
  - Q: Does a geothermal operating licence holder company in Türkiye expropriate my land itself?
    A: No. The licence holder applies to the administration, which is the Investment Monitoring and Coordination Directorate in metropolitan cities and the special provincial administration in other provinces. If the request is found appropriate, the administration adopts the public interest decision and the proceedings follow the Expropriation Law No. 2942. The licence holder pays the compensation and costs. The expropriated property is registered in the name of the administration and allocated to the licence holder while the activity continues.
  - Q: How is compensation calculated for an easement established for a geothermal pipeline in Türkiye?
    A: The compensation for an easement is the loss in value that the easement causes to the property (Law No. 2942 art. 11, last paragraph). In a Court of Cassation 5th Civil Chamber decision on a non-geothermal pipeline, the agricultural land was valued on the basis of net income and the easement consideration was determined by stating the rate of loss in value; the rate was set by taking into account the nature of the property, its total surface area, its geometric shape and the route of the line.
  - Q: When and where is an annulment action filed against a geothermal expropriation in Türkiye?
    A: The annulment action is filed before the administrative courts within thirty days of the notification made by the civil court of first instance under Article 10 of Law No. 2942, or, for those who could not be notified, of the newspaper announcement (art. 14). Proof of filing must be submitted to the civil court; otherwise the expropriation becomes final. The compensation is determined by the civil court of first instance where the property is located, in the determination and registration action brought by the administration.
  - Q: Is land expropriated for a geothermal activity in Türkiye returned to the former owner?
    A: If the administration determines that the land is no longer needed for the activity, under Article 12(5) of Law No. 5686 the licence holder and the former owner are notified that the property will be returned to the former owner on payment of its current market value determined under the procedure of the Expropriation Law. If the former owner does not wish to take it back within six months, the property remains with the administration.
- [Expropriation and the Owner's Rights](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/expropriation-owners-rights): A comprehensive guide to expropriation and the owner's rights: the constitutional basis and the real-value principle; the purchase-by-agreement procedure (Law No. 2942, Art. 8); the court… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kamulastirma-ve-malikin-haklari
  - Q: What can I do if the expropriation price is too low?
    A: You are not obliged to accept the price offered by the administration. If no agreement is reached at the purchase (reconciliation) stage, the price is determined by the court through an independent panel of experts. If the property is a building plot, the real value is determined by the comparable-sales method; if it is land, by the net-income (capitalisation) method, with objective value-increasing factors added according to location.
  - Q: What can I do if the administration took my property without expropriation?
    A: This is de facto expropriation. If it has been physically taken and a facility (road, pylon, school, etc.) built on it, relying on your right of property you may claim the price (compensation) of the part taken before the civil courts, and the property is registered in the administration's name. You may also assert your right where the property has been allocated to a public area by the zoning plan without implementation (legal taking).
  - Q: What is the time-limit to challenge the expropriation act?
    A: An annulment action before the administrative courts (for unlawfulness) and a correction action before the civil courts (for material errors) may be brought within thirty days of notification or publication (Art. 14). This period is a forfeiture period; if it is missed, the act becomes final. As to price, the principal safeguard is the real value the court will determine.
  - Q: When does ownership pass to the administration, and when do I receive the price?
    A: Ownership passes to the administration by the registration decision given by the court (Art. 25). The price determined is, as a rule, paid in advance and in cash; the court has the determined price blocked in a bank and ensures its payment to the right-holder.
  - Q: What is urgent expropriation, and does it affect my price?
    A: In urgent expropriation the court decides on seizure within seven days after the value determined by the experts has been deposited in a bank; the proceedings other than valuation are completed afterwards (Art. 27). This does not extinguish your right to the price; the actual price-determination and registration action is conducted separately.
  - Q: Can I repurchase my expropriated property?
    A: If the property is not used in accordance with the purpose of the expropriation or is not allocated to a public service within the period provided by law, you may acquire a right of repurchase under the conditions of Article 23. The time-limits and conditions must be assessed separately in the specific case.
- [Forest Cadastre Title Suits Must Consider Additional Art. 22](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/forest-cadastre-title-deed-cancellation-art-22-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the 8th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation reversed a dismissal in a title cancellation action based on the forest cadastre so that the effect of additional Article 22 of the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/orman-kadastrosuna-dayali-tapu-iptalinde-orman-kanunu-ek-22-madde
  - Q: Can land whose title was cancelled because it is forest be returned?
    A: Under Turkish law, if the land has been registered in the name of the Treasury, it is returned to the person concerned if the former owner or their successors apply to the administration within two years of the entry into force of additional Article 22 and repay to the Treasury the total of any payment made. The amount to be repaid may not be less than the current market value; the exceptions in the article remain applicable.
  - Q: Can the title of registered land placed within forest boundaries be accepted as valid?
    A: It can. If the owner’s application is approved by the General Directorate of Forestry, or if an ex officio examination establishes the accuracy of the title record, the title is accepted as valid without charge and any forest annotation is cancelled.
  - Q: What happens to a pending action for cancellation of title?
    A: The procedures under the article are treated as a preliminary question; for this reason, the 8th Civil Chamber of the Turkish Court of Cassation reversed the dismissal. Actions waived by the parties become devoid of subject matter and no attorney’s fee is awarded.
  - Q: When did additional Article 22 enter into force?
    A: The provision, added by Law No. 7584, was published in the Official Gazette of 20.06.2026 and entered into force on the same date; the two-year application period for return runs from that date.
  - Q: Are compensation actions brought because of the cancellation of title affected?
    A: Such actions waived by the parties also become devoid of subject matter; once land is returned, no compensation or ecrimisil may be claimed from the administrations. The Assembly of Civil Chambers sent a compensation action based on art. 1007 of the Turkish Civil Code to the 5th Civil Chamber for examination in light of the new situation.
- [General Rules for Multi-Parcel Sites Until Estate Management: Turkish Court of Cassation on Common Areas](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/multi-parcel-housing-estate-common-area-action-jurisdiction): Under Turkish law, the 5th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that, where a site built on more than one parcel has not yet transitioned to housing-estate management, the general provisions… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/toplu-yapi-yonetimine-gecilmemis-sitede-ortak-yer-davasi-asliye-hukukta
  - Q: In which court is an action over interference with a common area of a multi-parcel site brought?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the Chamber in this decision, if the site has not yet transitioned to housing-estate management, the general provisions apply to the dispute and the action is heard by the civil court of first instance. Sites that have transitioned to housing-estate management were not addressed in the decision.
  - Q: What happens if a civil court of peace decides such an action on the merits?
    A: According to the Chamber, jurisdiction is a matter of public order and is observed of the court’s own motion. In this case a judgment that had passed appellate review was reversed on appeal for lack of jurisdiction.
  - Q: Can a site manager bring an action concerning a common area on their own initiative?
    A: According to the Chamber, this action is based on the right of ownership and is not among the manager’s duties listed in KMK art. 35. A manager who is not a condominium owner may bring this action only if authorised by the board of condominium owners.
  - Q: If the manager is a condominium owner, is separate authority from the board required?
    A: The decision does not say so expressly; the Chamber made the review of authority conditional on the manager not being an owner in the property in dispute.
- [Good-Faith Buyers from the Contractor Keep Their Title: Turkish Court of Cassation on Flat-for-Land Deals](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/title-annulment-flat-bought-from-contractor-good-faith): Under Turkish law, the 6th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that a third party who, relying on the land register, buys an independent unit from the contractor in a build-for-land-share… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yukleniciden-bagimsiz-bolum-alanin-tapusu-iptal-edilemez
  - Q: Can the title of a flat bought from the contractor be annulled?
    A: In an action by the landowner, as a rule, no; the acquisition of a person in good faith who, relying on the land register, buys an independent unit from the contractor is protected by Civil Code art. 1023. According to the Chamber, if the landowner proves that the buyer knew or should have known that the transfer was made under the construction contract, annulment of title may be allowed.
  - Q: Does the buyer lose the title if the contract is terminated retroactively?
    A: According to the Chamber, no; even if the termination makes the registration in the contractor’s name wrongful, the transfer to the person in good faith remains valid.
  - Q: Who must prove the buyer’s good faith?
    A: Good faith is presumed (Civil Code art. 3(1)); the landowner must prove bad faith. A person who has not exercised the care required cannot claim good faith (Civil Code art. 3(2)).
  - Q: Is the title given to the contractor an “advance title”?
    A: According to the Chamber, no; the concept has no place in the Turkish law of property, and a transfer of title cannot be made conditional.
- [Good-Faith Third Parties in Title Annulment Actions: Turkish Civil Code Art. 1023, Bad Faith, Annotations](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/good-faith-third-party-annulment-of-title-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the protection under Civil Code art. 1023, in an action for annulment of title, of a third party who acquired a property or a mortgage relying on a wrongful registration: the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tapu-iptali-ve-tescil-davasinda-iyiniyetli-ucuncu-kisi
  - Q: Is a good-faith third party protected in an action for annulment of title?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law, a third party who acquires ownership or another right in rem such as a mortgage relying in good faith on a wrongful registration is protected (Civil Code art. 1023). A person who knew or should have known of the irregularity is not protected (art. 1024).
  - Q: Who must prove that the third party acted in bad faith?
    A: Because good faith is presumed, the claimant must prove bad faith, relying on circumstantial presumptions such as frequent changes of hands, a sale far below market value, a close relationship between the parties or buying without seeing the property. The allegation of bad faith is an objection and is taken into account by the court of its own motion.
  - Q: Is a person who buys while a “subject of litigation” (davalıdır) annotation is on the register in good faith?
    A: No. No one may claim not to know an entry in the land register. The Assembly of Civil Chambers accepted as undisputed that a last owner who bought the property with the “subject of litigation” annotation after the action had been filed cannot benefit from the protection of Civil Code art. 1023.
  - Q: What happens if the property is sold while the annulment action is pending?
    A: Under Code of Civil Procedure art. 125, the claimant may either continue the action against the transferee or convert it into a compensation action against the transferor. Whether the transferee is protected depends, as a rule, on his good faith, in particular on whether an annotation was on the register; however, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, if the chain of bad faith was broken at an earlier transfer, the last owner’s acquisition may still be protected.
  - Q: Does a bank’s mortgage survive the annulment of title?
    A: The Assembly of Civil Chambers protected a bank mortgage that had been established before the interim-injunction annotation and in respect of which the bank’s bad faith had not been proven, and upheld the removal of the attachment annotations placed after the injunction annotation. The outcome depends on the date of the annotation and the creditor’s good faith.
  - Q: What can the true owner do if the property stays with a good-faith acquirer?
    A: Civil Code art. 1025 protects the rights of the good-faith third party while reserving all claims for compensation. If the property was transferred while the action was pending, the action may be converted into a compensation action against the transferor under Code of Civil Procedure art. 125/1-b; the State’s liability arising from the land register is regulated separately in Civil Code art. 1007.
- [Mining Revenue and Tax Do Not Justify Urgent Expropriation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/urgent-expropriation-mining-revenue-tax-eia): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State annulled, in respect of the immovable in dispute, an urgent expropriation decision taken for a gold mine… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/maden-projesinde-gelir-ve-vergi-gerekcesi-acele-kamulastirmayi-hakli-kilmaz
  - Q: How can an urgent expropriation decision be challenged?
    A: Under Turkish law, an action is brought before the administrative courts for annulment of the presidential urgent expropriation decision in respect of the immovable. In this case the 6th Chamber of the Council of State heard the action at first instance and the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers reviewed the appeal.
  - Q: Do revenue, tax and employment data justify urgent expropriation?
    A: According to the Assembly, they do not. Such data may show the need to expropriate the immovable, but they do not show the extraordinary circumstances required for the urgent procedure or the public interest pursued by that procedure.
  - Q: Can urgent expropriation be carried out if the project's EIA positive decision has been annulled?
    A: The Assembly held in this case that it cannot: in view of the uncertainty created by the administrative court's annulment of the EIA positive decision, the urgent expropriation method cannot be applied. The decision does not state whether the annulment of the EIA decision had become final.
  - Q: Was the decision unanimous?
    A: No. The decision is final and was given by majority on both the merits and the reasoning. One dissenting opinion favours upholding the dismissal; the other favours annulment on the EIA ground alone.
- [Pre-emption Lost If Partition Sale Ends Co-ownership: Turkish Court of Cassation on Pending Suits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pre-emption-lost-partition-sale-co-ownership-ends): Under Turkish law, the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that a plaintiff whose co-ownership ends because the immovable is sold through enforcement proceedings while a pre-emption… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/onalim-davasi-surerken-ortakligin-giderilmesi-satisi
  - Q: What happens if the property is sold at a partition auction while a pre-emption action is pending?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, if the sale becomes final and the plaintiff no longer holds a share in the immovable, the plaintiff loses the right of pre-emption and the action is dismissed. The Chamber referred to the sale in this case as a sale through enforcement proceedings.
  - Q: Is it enough to be a co-owner on the date the action is brought?
    A: No. According to the Chamber, the right of pre-emption exists as long as the co-ownership relationship continues, and the plaintiff must retain co-ownership while the proceedings continue.
  - Q: Does the result change if the action has been converted into a claim for compensation under Code of Civil Procedure art. 125?
    A: It did not in this case. The plaintiff had converted the action into a monetary claim and the first-instance court had awarded the difference between the pre-emption price and the auction price; the Chamber reversed that judgment, stating that the action should have been dismissed because co-ownership had ended.
  - Q: Can co-owners exercise pre-emption against a partition auction?
    A: Under Turkish Civil Code art. 733/1, the right of pre-emption cannot be exercised in sales by compulsory auction. This decision, however, did not concern pre-emption against the auction; it concerned the fate of an action brought over a sale of shares made before the auction.
- [Pre-emption Price Valued at Filing Date or 25.12.2025: Turkish Court of Cassation on Law No. 7571](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pre-emption-price-market-value-valuation-date-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, the market value forming the pre-emption price is assessed as at the filing date in actions brought after 25.12.2025… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04; updated: 2026-11-17. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/onalimda-rayic-bedel-hangi-tarihe-gore-belirlenir
  - Q: How is the pre-emption price determined after Law No. 7571?
    A: The market value of the share in dispute is determined by the judge without delay; the holder of the right must deposit that amount, together with the land registry costs borne by the buyer, in cash within the peremptory period given (Turkish Civil Code art. 734(2)). The Constitutional Court has annulled that rule; since the annulment takes effect on 6 August 2027, it applies until then.
  - Q: As at which date is the market value assessed in a pre-emption action?
    A: According to the 7th Civil Chamber of the Turkish Court of Cassation, the market value at the filing date applies to actions brought after 25.12.2025, and the market value as at 25.12.2025 applies to actions pending on that date. The statute contains no express rule on this point.
  - Q: What happens to the amount already deposited in a pending action?
    A: The amount deposited and its yield are set off against the market value of the share as at 25.12.2025; any shortfall is deposited into the same account (Turkish Civil Code provisional art. 1(2)). Provisional art. 1(2), which applies the rule to pending actions, has also been annulled by the Constitutional Court, with effect from 6 August 2027.
  - Q: Has the Assembly of Civil Chambers fixed the valuation date?
    A: No. In its decision of 01.04.2026, the Assembly of Civil Chambers stated that the new price rule also applies to pending actions, but did not lay down a criterion for the valuation date.
- [Pre-emption in a Neighbouring (Adjacent) Agricultural Parcel: Repealed in 2020 (Soil Protection Law Art. 8/İ)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pre-emption-neighbouring-agricultural-parcel): Pre-emption in a neighbouring (adjacent) agricultural parcel: the scope and conditions of the adjacent-agricultural-landowner pre-emption introduced in 2014 by Art. 8/İ of the Soil Protection Law No.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/toprak-koruma-kanununda-komsu-parsel-onalim
  - Q: My neighbouring field was sold; do I have a pre-emption right?
    A: As a rule, no, today. Adjacent (neighbouring) agricultural-land pre-emption was repealed on 04.11.2020. For sales after that date the neighbouring owner has no pre-emption right. Only if you are a co-owner of the same immovable may co-owner pre-emption (Civil Code Art. 732) come into play.
  - Q: Can I still sue over a sale made before 04.11.2020?
    A: Since vested rights are protected, a pre-emption right that arose in sales made before the repeal may be asserted; but you must have been the full owner of the adjacent immovable on the sale date and comply with the time limits. This must be assessed carefully on the facts.
  - Q: Did neighbouring pre-emption apply to the sale of a share?
    A: No. As the Constitutional Court stated, adjacent pre-emption arose only on the sale of the whole land (parcel); it did not apply to the sale of a share in a parcel.
  - Q: What if more than one neighbour sought pre-emption?
    A: The judge transferred the immovable to the adjacent owner forming an agricultural integrity. Factors such as the type of agricultural activity were investigated ex officio in determining agricultural integrity.
  - Q: Was co-owner pre-emption also repealed?
    A: No. The co-owner's pre-emption right in co-ownership (Civil Code Art. 732) remains in force and is independent of adjacent agricultural-land pre-emption.
- [Promise to Sell Real Estate in Türkiye: Registration Action, Termination, Limitation and Informal Sales](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/promise-to-sell-real-estate-registration-action-turkiye): Under Turkish law: conditions for claiming registration on the basis of a notarised promise to sell, why unilateral termination is ineffective, the ten-year limitation period and the return of the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tasinmaz-satis-vaadi-sozlesmesi-tescil-davasi-ve-harici-satis
  - Q: What can a buyer do if the seller refuses to transfer title despite a notarised promise to sell real estate in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law the promisee may file an action for annulment of title and registration under Civil Code art. 716 and ask the court to transfer ownership by judgment. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the seller's failure to attend the land registry does not prevent this action, but the agreed price must have been paid or the unpaid part deposited.
  - Q: Can a promise to sell real estate be terminated unilaterally under Turkish law?
    A: According to Assembly of Civil Chambers decision K. 2025/85, a promise to sell registered real estate cannot be terminated by a unilateral declaration. If the parties do not agree on termination, termination can only take place by court judgment; without a proper termination the buyer keeps the right to demand registration.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a registration action based on a promise to sell real estate in Türkiye?
    A: According to the chamber view quoted in Assembly of Civil Chambers decision K. 2024/488, since no special period exists, the ten-year limitation period applies and starts when performance of the contract becomes possible. If the property has been delivered to the buyer, a limitation defence raised after ten years is not heard under the good-faith rule. Limitation is not considered by the judge of its own motion; it must be raised as a defence.
  - Q: How is the price paid under an informal (non-notarised) real estate sale recovered in Türkiye?
    A: A contract that does not comply with the official form is invalid, and the parties may reclaim what they gave under the rules on unjust enrichment. According to Assembly of Civil Chambers decision K. 2024/496, the money paid is returned after being updated under the principle of corrective justice, using the average of inflation, foreign currency, gold and wage increases.
  - Q: Can title to a flat bought by an informal sale never be transferred to the buyer under Turkish law?
    A: As a rule it cannot. The unification-of-case-law decision of 30.09.1988 provides that, in the sale of an independent unit in a building subject to the Condominium Law, where the buyer has paid everything, the seller has delivered the unit and the buyer uses it as an owner, the judge may accept the registration action having regard to Civil Code art. 2. For a unit acquired from the contractor by written assignment, the rules on assignment of receivables apply.
- [Property Inside the Shore Edge Line in Türkiye: Title Cancellation, Compensation and Legal Remedies](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/shore-edge-line-title-cancellation-compensation-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the title to land lying on the seaward side of the shore edge line may be cancelled in an action brought by the Treasury, and the owner may claim the loss from the Treasury under… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kiyi-kenar-cizgisi-icinde-kalan-tasinmaz-tapu-iptali-ve-tazminat
  - Q: Is the title to registered land inside the shore edge line cancelled in Türkiye?
    A: Yes, it may be. Under Turkish law, shores are under the authority and disposal of the State (Constitution art. 43; Coastal Law No. 3621 art. 5). The part lying on the shore according to the approved shore edge line is annotated in the land register, and under the Regulation the title cancellation procedures are carried out by the provincial revenue office. The title is not deleted automatically; it is cancelled by court judgment in an action brought by the Treasury.
  - Q: Can an owner whose title is cancelled because of the shore edge line claim compensation under Turkish law?
    A: As a rule, yes. The State is liable for losses arising from the keeping of the land register (Turkish Civil Code art. 1007). The owner may claim the real loss from the Treasury before the civil courts. The position of a person who buys while there is an annotation in the register is contested: the Constitutional Court treated the refusal of compensation as a violation in one decision, whereas the Court of Cassation’s Assembly of Civil Chambers, in a decision on a forest annotation, did not accept compensation for the purchased share.
  - Q: Can a compensation action be brought for land inside the shore edge line in Türkiye before the title is cancelled?
    A: The Plenary of the Constitutional Court held that making the compensation action conditional on cancellation of the title, for land inside a shore edge line that has become final, violates the right to property. In the decisions examined, the 5th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation likewise found compensation lawful for the seaward part of land still registered in the owner’s name, and required the line to be approved by the administration or final by judgment.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a shore edge line compensation action under Turkish law?
    A: The Court of Cassation’s Assembly of Civil Chambers decided that the ten-year general limitation period applies to an action based on art. 1007 of the Turkish Civil Code and, in the case before it, counted the period from the date the title cancellation judgment became final (today’s counterpart is art. 146 of the Turkish Code of Obligations). When the period starts in actions brought before the title is cancelled is not expressly discussed in the decisions examined.
  - Q: As of which date is the land valued for shore edge line compensation in Türkiye?
    A: In the two decisions of the 5th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation examined, the value of the land on the date of the action was taken as the basis; agricultural land is valued by the net income method and building plots by precedent sales. In a file recounted in an Assembly of Civil Chambers decision that ended on procedural grounds, however, the year the cancellation judgment became final had been used; the valuation date may therefore need to be argued separately in the file.
  - Q: How is the determination of the shore edge line objected to in Türkiye, and within what time must an annulment action be brought?
    A: Under the Regulation the line is announced for one month; those concerned may object to the governorship within that period and the Ministry decides the objection. An action for annulment of the line is brought before the administrative court; as the Coastal Law contains no special period, the general time limit is sixty days (Administrative Procedure Law art. 7). The (since closed) 14th Chamber of the Council of State, in a 2016 decision, accepted that, in a matter concerning the right to property, a fresh application may be made to the administration and an action brought within sixty days against the refusal.
- [Rent Case Costs on Landlord If Tenant Exits Before New Term: Turkish Court of Cassation on Early Rent Suits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/rent-determination-action-tenant-vacates-litigation-costs): Under Turkish law, the 3rd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, resolving a conflict between regional courts of appeal, held that where a rent determination action is brought before the period… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kira-tespit-davasi-acildiktan-sonra-kiraci-cikarsa-yargilama-giderleri
  - Q: What happens if the tenant leaves after a rent determination action has been brought?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the 3rd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, if the contract is terminated and the premises are vacated before the new rental period begins, the action becomes devoid of subject matter. The court does not decide on the merits; it determines the costs of proceedings under Code of Civil Procedure art. 331/1 according to the parties’ degree of justification on the date the action was brought.
  - Q: Who pays the costs of proceedings in that case?
    A: If the action was brought before the period for which determination is sought and the tenant left before the new period began, according to the Chamber the tenant is not regarded as having caused the action; the costs of proceedings are left with the landlord.
  - Q: Can the landlord bring a rent determination action before the new period begins?
    A: Yes. Under Code of Obligations art. 345 the action may be brought at any time. According to the Chamber, this rule does not prevent the tenant from exercising its legal right and does not lead to the conclusion that the action brought was justified.
  - Q: What must the tenant do in order not to renew the contract?
    A: The Chamber stated that under Code of Obligations art. 347, in fixed-term leases of residential and roofed business premises, the tenant may decline to renew the contract by giving notice at least fifteen days before the end of its term. The decision’s reasoning rests on the contract being ended in compliance with this statutory period; the operative part, however, does not mention notice and refers only to vacating before the new rental period begins.
  - Q: Is the result the same if the tenant leaves after the new period has begun?
    A: This decision did not address that situation; the conflict was limited to cases where the tenant left before the new rental period began.
- [Revoking a Gift of Real Estate in Türkiye: Conditional Gifts, Grounds and the One-Year Period (TCO 295–297)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/revocation-of-gift-real-estate-turkiye): Under Turkish law, donated real estate can be taken back only on the three grounds in Code of Obligations art. 295 and within one year of learning of the ground; the Court of Cassation strictly… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bagistan-donme-ve-tapu-iptali-sartli-bagis-ve-bir-yillik-sure
  - Q: In which cases can donated real estate be taken back under Turkish law?
    A: Under Code of Obligations art. 295, the donor may revoke the gift if the donee has committed a serious offence against the donor or the donor's relatives, has seriously breached legal obligations towards the donor or the donor's family, or has failed without good reason to perform the burden in a gift subject to a burden. For real estate, revocation is pursued by an action for annulment of title and registration.
  - Q: Within what period must an action to revoke a gift be filed in Türkiye?
    A: The donor may revoke the gift within one year from the day on which the donor learns of the ground for revocation (Code of Obligations art. 297). The 1st Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation treats this period as preclusive and, for gifts subject to a burden, starts it from the date on which it becomes definitely clear that the purpose will not be achieved.
  - Q: Can a conditional gift be proved under Turkish law if the land registry deed says the gift was unconditional?
    A: In a case where the official deed recorded an unconditional gift and no written evidence of a condition was submitted, the 1st Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation did not consider abstract witness statements unsupported by material evidence sufficient. Proof is easier where the condition appears in municipal or institutional decisions, a protocol or the deed.
  - Q: Is land donated for a school taken back in Türkiye if no school is built on it for years?
    A: Not automatically. In decision K. 2026/4182 of the 1st Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, the land remained designated as a high-school area in the zoning plan and had been allocated to the Ministry of National Education as a school site; the Chamber held that there was nothing contrary to the purpose of the gift although no building had yet been erected.
  - Q: Is a home bought with one spouse's money in the other spouse's name a gift under Turkish law?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, joint investments made by spouses during the marriage to secure their common life and the family's future cannot be treated as a gift. The dispute is then resolved not under revocation of gifts but under the matrimonial property rules, as a claim for value-increase share and participation.
- [Risky Building Designation under Law No. 6306 in Türkiye: Owners' Rights, Agreement and Legal Remedies](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/risky-building-designation-law-6306-turkiye): Under Turkish law, owners may object to a risky building designation before a technical board within fifteen days, and the time limit for an action against administrative acts taken under Law No.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/riskli-yapi-tespiti-ve-6306-sayili-kanun-maliklerin-haklari
  - Q: What is the time limit for objecting to a risky building designation under Law No. 6306 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an owner or legal representative may object to a risky building designation within fifteen days; a technical board decides the objection (Law No. 6306 art. 3/1). The designation is announced by posting a report on the building, notification via e-Government and a fifteen-day posting at the neighbourhood headman’s office, and it is deemed notified on the last day of that posting. In the urgent procedure under art. 6/A the objection period is two days.
  - Q: Within how many days must an annulment action be filed in the administrative court against measures under Law No. 6306 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an action may be brought under the Administrative Procedure Law within thirty days of notification against administrative acts taken under Law No. 6306 (art. 6/9). In a first-instance decision that may not yet be final, the Council of State’s 6th Chamber applied this period to a risky building decision and dismissed an action filed out of time. According to the Chamber reasoning upheld by the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers, all owners have the right to sue against the technical board’s decision on an objection.
  - Q: Under Law No. 6306 in Türkiye, what majority of owners is needed to decide on rebuilding a risky building?
    A: Under Turkish law, rebuilding, the sale of shares and methods such as flat-for-land arrangements are decided by an absolute majority of the co-owners in proportion to their shares, whether or not they own part of the building (art. 6/1). The ratio is calculated by land shares, not by head count. This ratio was introduced by Law No. 7471; before 9 November 2023 the text required at least a two-thirds majority.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, what happens to the land share of an owner who does not join the majority decision on a risky building?
    A: Under Turkish law, the majority notifies the dissenting owner of an offer containing the decision and the terms of agreement through a notary or by a fifteen-day posting at the neighbourhood headman’s office. If the offer is not examined or accepted within fifteen days, the land share is sold by public auction to the agreeing co-owners for not less than the market value determined at the instance of the Presidency (art. 6/1). The sale may be challenged in the administrative court; the Council of State has required the market value to be determined through on-site inspection and expert examination.
  - Q: What rights does a tenant in a risky building have under Law No. 6306 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, the designation is deemed notified, through the special procedure in the Law, to holders of real and personal rights (such as tenants) (art. 3/2). Tenants of buildings vacated by agreement may be given temporary housing or business premises or rent assistance (art. 5/1); a tenant who has lived or had a workplace in the building for at least one year may be given housing, premises, credit or a housing certificate (art. 6/3). The Law sets no amounts for this assistance and gives the right of objection to owners.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, which courts hear challenges to the owners’ majority decision and to the sale of land shares in a risky building?
    A: According to the Council of State, the decision taken by a majority of the co-owners is a decision between private persons to which the administration is not a party; it is challenged before the ordinary (civil) courts. Administrative acts based on that decision, such as the sale of a land share by public auction, are challenged before the administrative courts. The 6th Chamber of the Council of State accepted that the outcome of the civil case may be treated as a preliminary issue in the action against the sale.
- [Rooftop Solar Panel Removal Upheld: No Co-owner Consent or Amended Project (Turkish Court of Cassation)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/rooftop-solar-panels-apartment-building-without-consent): Under Turkish law, the 5th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation unanimously upheld the removal of a solar energy system and water tanks installed on an apartment roof without an amended project or… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/apartman-catisina-izinsiz-kurulan-gunes-paneli-sokulur
  - Q: Is the consent of the condominium owners needed to install solar panels on an apartment roof?
    A: Under Turkish law, the roof is a common area; Condominium Ownership Law art. 19/2 requires the written consent of four-fifths of all condominium owners for construction, repairs and installations in common areas. Earlier decisions of the Court of Cassation allowed solar energy systems without consent under certain conditions; in upholding the removal in the 2026 decision, the Chamber relied on the absence of an amended project and sufficient consent; however, it had also been established in the case that the system would adversely affect the load-bearing structure, and the Chamber did not address the earlier practice.
  - Q: Is a panel removed even if it occupies an area in proportion to the land share?
    A: In this case the system, at 6 m², was proportionate to the defendant’s 1/6 land share and there was room on the roof for everyone; nevertheless, the removal order was upheld because of the structural risk and the lack of consent.
  - Q: Is a solar energy system protected because it is useful?
    A: According to the regional court, even a useful work is removed if it damages the structural integrity and load-bearing structure of the main building; the Court of Cassation upheld that decision.
  - Q: What should be considered before installing a system on the roof?
    A: The written consent of four-fifths of the condominium owners, an amended project where required, and a technical examination of whether the building can carry the load. For systems installed without consent, earlier decisions required that the land share not be exceeded, that there be enough room for everyone, that the installation follow good technical practice and that it not damage the load-bearing structure.
- [Shared Title Deeds in Türkiye: Selling a Share, Partition and Conversion to a Single Title](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/shared-title-deed-turkiye-share-sale-partition-single-title): Under Turkish law, a co-owner may sell his or her share alone, but if it is sold to a third party the other co-owners have a right of pre-emption.
