A shared title becomes a single title by gathering… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-04; updated: 2026-11-17. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hisseli-tapu-satis-ayirma-ve-tek-tapuya-cevirme
  - Q: Can a shared title deed be converted into a single title?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law the shares can be gathered in one co-owner, the property can be subdivided in line with zoning legislation if the co-owners agree, or condominium ownership can be established in a building. Failing agreement, dissolution of co-ownership is sought before the civil court of peace after mediation; the court partitions in kind where possible and otherwise orders a sale.
  - Q: Do I need the other co-owners’ consent to sell my share?
    A: No; the share may be transferred (Turkish Civil Code art. 688/3). If it is sold to a third party, the other co-owners may exercise pre-emption. Selling the whole property requires all co-owners’ consent; on agricultural land, transfer of shares requires the Ministry’s permission.
  - Q: Can I sell a particular part of a co-owned property?
    A: No. What can be sold is the fraction in the register. A separate title for a particular area arises only through subdivision, condominium ownership or partition in kind in an action for dissolution of co-ownership.
  - Q: Can a co-owned field be divided?
    A: Only if the resulting parcels do not fall below the minimum agricultural land size and the necessary permission is obtained. Subdivision, splitting into shares and increasing the number of shares or co-owners below the minimum size are prohibited; the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers does not allow this rule to be circumvented by means such as accepting a claim.
  - Q: How is an inherited title converted into a shared title?
    A: Any heir may ask the land registry to convert joint ownership into co-ownership in shares. If the other heirs do not object or bring an action for partition in time, the title is registered in shares in the heirs’ names (Cadastre Law additional art. 3). On agricultural land, this requires the Ministry’s permission.
- [Short-Term Tourist Rentals in Türkiye: The Permit, Flat Owners' Unanimous Decision and Administrative Fines](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/short-term-tourist-rental-permit-fines-turkiye): Under Turkish law, letting a dwelling for one hundred days or less requires a permit under Law No. 7464, and in a building with more than one independent unit a unanimous decision of all flat owners… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/turizm-amacli-kisa-sureli-kiralama-izin-belgesi-ve-idari-para-cezalari
  - Q: Is a permit mandatory for letting a home by the day in Türkiye, and where is the short-term rental permit obtained?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law (Law No. 7464), a permit must be obtained before the contract in order to let a dwelling for one hundred days or less (art. 3/1). The permit is issued by the Ministry of Culture and Tourism, which may also exercise this power through the governorship. Under the implementing regulation, the application is made through e-Government. The application is made by the owner or the holder of a usufruct or a right of construction.
  - Q: Is a unanimous decision of the flat owners required under Turkish law to let a home short-term on platforms such as Airbnb?
    A: As a rule, yes. Art. 3/3 of Law No. 7464 requires the application to include a decision taken unanimously by all flat owners of the building; in residential complexes the decision is required only for that building. The Law exempts high-end residences whose management plan allows short-term rental. The implementing regulation additionally does not require a unanimous decision for timeshare and detached dwellings.
  - Q: How much is the fine for letting a home by the day without a permit in Türkiye?
    A: Under the text of Law No. 7464, a person who lets a dwelling for tourism purposes without a permit is subject to an administrative fine of one hundred thousand Turkish lira for each dwelling. A fine of five hundred thousand Turkish lira is provided for those who continue at the end of the fifteen-day period given, and one million Turkish lira for those who continue despite both (art. 4/1). These are the amounts in the text of the Law; they are increased every year by the revaluation rate.
  - Q: Can a tenant in Türkiye let the home they live in by the day?
    A: No. Art. 3/7 of Law No. 7464 prohibits the tenant of a dwelling rented for use as a residence from letting it to third parties for tourism purposes in their own name and for their own account; the Law contains no exception based on the owner’s consent. The sanction in the text of the Law is an administrative fine of one hundred thousand Turkish lira for each contract (art. 4/1-ç).
  - Q: Where and within how many days can an administrative fine for short-term rental be challenged under Turkish law?
    A: Law No. 7464 does not set out a separate remedy against the fine. Reading arts. 3 and 27 of the Misdemeanours Law together, the application is made to the criminal judgeship of peace (the “criminal court of peace” in the wording of art. 27/1) within fifteen days at the latest from notification. This conclusion rests on the statutory texts; as no decision discussing the competent authority could be found, the authority and time limit in the decision served should be checked.
  - Q: What can the other flat owners do under Turkish law if a neighbouring flat is being let by the day?
    A: An unpermitted rental may be reported to the local administrative authority competent to impose the fine or to the governorship/provincial directorate of culture and tourism that carries out inspections, and an action may be brought before the administrative courts against a permit believed to have been issued irregularly. In addition, under art. 33 of the Condominium Law, a flat owner who suffers harm because of disturbance or a breach of the management plan may request the intervention of the judge from the civil court of peace.
- [State Pays for Land Lost to a Forged Power of Attorney: Turkish Cassation Assembly on Civil Code Art. 1007](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/forged-power-of-attorney-land-sale-state-compensation): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation unanimously held that the State is strictly liable under Civil Code art. 1007 for the owner’s loss where immovables sold… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sahte-vekaletnameyle-satilan-tapu-geri-alinamiyorsa-devlet-tazminat-oder
  - Q: Can land sold under a forged power of attorney be recovered?
    A: Under Turkish law, in this decision the owner recovered part of the immovables through an action for annulment of title and re-registration. However, if the immovable has passed to a third person who acquired it relying in good faith on the land register entry, that acquisition is protected (Civil Code art. 1023). In this case, those protected were the later owners who acquired from the first buyer.
  - Q: If the title cannot be recovered, who pays for the loss?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, the State pays under Civil Code art. 1007. In this case the defendant is the Treasury; the action brought against the land registry directorate was dismissed for lack of standing to be sued.
  - Q: Is the State liable even if the land registry officer was not at fault?
    A: Yes. The State’s liability is strict liability; an unlawfulness in keeping the register and a causal link suffice. Fault matters only for the State’s recourse against the official at fault (Civil Code art. 1007/2).
  - Q: Does the fault of the notary or the fraudsters release the State from liability?
    A: The Assembly of Civil Chambers held that it did not in this case. The Treasury must prove that the causal link has been broken; the outcome depends on the circumstances of the case.
  - Q: In which court is the compensation action brought?
    A: Under Civil Code art. 1007/3, actions concerning the State’s liability are heard by the court of the place where the land register is kept. This case was heard by the ordinary (civil) courts.
- [The Pre-emption (Şufa) Action and Sham Transactions: Constitutional Court Annuls the Market-Value Rule](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pre-emption-action-sham-transactions): The pre-emption (şufa) action and sham transactions: the statutory pre-emption right of other co-owners when a co-owner sells their share to a third party (Civil Code Art. 732), the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-28; updated: 2026-11-17. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/onalim-sufa-davasi-ve-muvazaa
  - Q: Within what period must I exercise the pre-emption right?
    A: If the sale was notified to you through a notary, you must sue within three months of the notification and, in any event, within one year of the sale. These are forfeiture periods; if missed, the right ends.
  - Q: Can I use pre-emption for a share transferred by gift?
    A: As a rule, no. The pre-emption right arises only on a sale of the share; it cannot be used in gift, exchange or forced/auction sale. However, if it is ostensibly a gift but in truth a sale (sham), this may be asserted by proving it.
  - Q: What did the 2025 amendment and the Constitutional Court decision change in pre-emption?
    A: Law No. 7571 of 24/12/2025 amended Art. 734; the right-holder deposits not the registry sale price but the market value set by the judge, together with the land-registry expenses, within a final period. The Constitutional Court has annulled that rule; since the annulment takes effect on 6 August 2027, the market-value rule applies until then.
  - Q: The sale price is shown very high in the registry; what can I do?
    A: Before Law No. 7571 the amount payable was the registry price, so the sham assertion was critical and had to be proven. Since 25.12.2025 the basis is market value, so a high stated price is not decisive on its own; even so, whether the transaction is truly a sale may be discussed. As the market-value rule ceases to have effect on 6 August 2027, a price sham may regain importance under the rules that apply after that date.
  - Q: Against whom and where is the pre-emption action brought?
    A: The action is brought against the third-party buyer, before the civil court of first instance of the place where the immovable is located, and seeks cancellation of title and registration in your name.
- [The Right of Pre-emption (Önalım/Şufa): Co-ownership, De Facto Partition, Period-and-Price Reform (Law 7571)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/right-pre-emption): The statutory right of pre-emption entitles the other co-owners, where a co-owner sells their share to a third person, to acquire it on the same terms; it is a formative right. This article explains… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-17. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/onalim-sufa-davasi
  - Q: Within what time must a pre-emption action be brought?
    A: Within three months of notarial notification of the sale, and in any event within one year of the sale (TMK art.733; Law No. 7571 reduced the outer period from two years to one). Without notarial notification the three-month period does not run.
  - Q: I learned of the sale by other means; does time start to run?
    A: No. According to the Court of Cassation, what is required to start the period is notarial notification by the buyer or seller. Learning by other means does not start the three-month period.
  - Q: How is the pre-emption price determined?
    A: Under the provisions in force, the judge determines the market value of the share, and the claimant must deposit that value plus registration costs in cash within a strict period. The Constitutional Court has annulled that rule; since the annulment takes effect on 6 August 2027, it applies until then. (Before Law No. 7571 the price was the deed price plus costs.)
  - Q: Can the action succeed if there is a de facto partition?
    A: As a rule, no. If the property has in fact been divided among the co-owners and use continues accordingly, the action is dismissed on that ground, which the court examines of its own motion.
  - Q: Can pre-emption be used if the buyer is already a co-owner?
    A: No. Pre-emption applies only to a sale to a non-co-owner. If the buyer was already a co-owner, it cannot be exercised against them.
  - Q: Do gift or capital contribution trigger pre-emption?
    A: No. The object of pre-emption is a genuine sale. Transfers that are not sales — gift, exchange, or contribution as capital to a company — do not give rise to it.
- [Turkish Condominium Management: Owners' Board, Manager, Management Plan, Service Charges, Annulling Decisions](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/condominium-management): Condominium management under the Turkish Condominium Ownership Law (arts. 27-41): the board of condominium owners, voting rights and proxies, notice of meetings and quorums, amending the management… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kat-mulkiyetinde-yonetim
  - Q: Within what time must an action be brought against a decision of the board of condominium owners?
    A: An owner who attended the meeting and voted against may bring an action for annulment before the civil court of peace within one month from the date of the decision, an owner who did not attend within one month from learning of it, and in any event within six months from the date of the decision (Condominium Law art. 33). No time limit applies to decisions that are non-existent or absolutely null and void. Applying to a mediator before suing is a precondition to suit; preclusive periods do not run during mediation.
  - Q: How is a building manager elected, and what happens if none can be elected?
    A: Appointing a manager is mandatory in buildings with eight or more independent units. The manager is appointed by a majority of the owners both in number and in land share and is re-appointed every year; if the owners cannot agree or fail to appoint a manager, the civil court of peace appoints one upon the application of an owner (Condominium Law art. 34). Where a manager alleged to have been irregularly elected is in office, a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation found that the conditions for appointment are not met unless the election decision is annulled.
  - Q: What can be done against an owner who does not pay service charges?
    A: The manager or any of the owners may sue or initiate enforcement proceedings; the defaulting owner pays late-payment compensation of five per cent per month (Condominium Law art. 20). Finalised operating plans and board decisions on operating expenses count as documents under Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 68/1 (Condominium Law art. 37). A tenant is also jointly and severally liable up to the rent he owes; if the debt still cannot be collected, a statutory mortgage may be registered on the unit (Condominium Law art. 22).
  - Q: I do not use the lift or the common area; must I still pay service charges?
    A: As a rule, yes. An owner cannot avoid paying his share of expenses and advances by waiving his right to use a common area or facility or by claiming that, owing to the situation of his unit, he has no need for it (Condominium Law art. 20). Very costly or luxurious innovations and additions are an exception: an owner who does not wish to benefit from them is not obliged to contribute to their cost (Condominium Law art. 43).
  - Q: Can a tenant seek the annulment of a decision of the board of condominium owners?
    A: Tenants and others continuously using a unit may request the intervention of the judge, but they do not have rights as broad as an owner’s. In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation it was accepted that, for a tenant to sue against a board decision, the decision must concern his particular interest and directly harm his rights and interests.
  - Q: How is the management plan amended?
    A: Amending the management plan requires the vote of four fifths of all owners (Condominium Law art. 28). According to the Court of Cassation this majority cannot override matters for which the Law requires unanimity, and the annulment of plan provisions contrary to mandatory provisions of the Law may be sought at any time. In housing estates, following the amendment made by Law No. 7579, the vote of two thirds of the units represented by the members of the estate’s representatives board is required (Condominium Law art. 70).
- [Turkish Court of Cassation 5th Civil Chamber: Most Reviewed Case Types and Current Leading Decisions](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/court-of-cassation-5th-civil-chamber): The 5th Civil Chamber of the Turkish Court of Cassation is the specialised chamber for expropriation disputes. Based on a scan of the official case-law database, this article explains the case types… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yargitay-5-hukuk-dairesi-kamulastirma-davalari
  - Q: Which cases does the 5th Civil Chamber hear?
    A: Determination of expropriation compensation and registration (Art. 10), de facto expropriation compensation and mesne profits, easement compensation and urgent expropriation; ownership-based interference and co-ownership dissolution files are now transferred to the 7th Civil Chamber.
  - Q: How is expropriation compensation assessed?
    A: By an expert panel under the Article 11 criteria: income method for agricultural land, comparable sales for building plots, official unit costs less depreciation for structures; value increases caused by the expropriating undertaking are excluded.
  - Q: Can the registration judgment be appealed?
    A: No — it is final; appeal and cassation lie only against the amount of compensation.
  - Q: What interest applies to the compensation?
    A: The Constitutional Court annulled the interest provision in the ninth paragraph of Article 10 (E. 2022/83, K. 2023/69; Official Gazette 01.08.2023). In its decision of 08.04.2026 the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held by majority that the annulment also applies to cases not yet final and that interest at the highest rate applied to public receivables runs on the differential compensation from the filing date until the date of judgment under the last paragraph of Article 46 of the Constitution, without the need for a request; the 5th Civil Chamber has since ruled in the same direction. The line is new: in September 2026 the Chamber upheld, without giving reasons, a regional court of appeal judgment applying statutory interest from four months after filing.
  - Q: How long does an expropriation case take?
    A: In the reviewed decisions, about 4–4.5 years across three instances; regional-court reversals extend the process.
  - Q: The administration seized my land without expropriation — what can I do?
    A: You may claim the value of the property and mesne profits; see our de facto expropriation analysis for details.
- [Turnkey Home Purchases in Türkiye: Off-Plan Sale Contracts, Late Delivery, Withdrawal and Title](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/turnkey-home-purchase-off-plan-sale-contract-turkiye): Under Turkish law: when buying a turnkey home from a developer, the form of a pre-paid home sale, who may invoke its invalidity, the forty-eight-month delivery limit, penalty clauses and lost rent… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anahtar-teslim-konut-satisi-sozlesmesi-on-odemeli-konut-ve-gec-teslim
  - Q: Is a turnkey home sale contract in Türkiye invalid if it is not made at the land registry or before a notary?
    A: Where the seller acts for commercial purposes and the buyer is a consumer, the contract is a pre-paid home sale; if the form is not met, the seller cannot invoke invalidity against the consumer (Law No. 6502 art. 41). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, only the consumer may rely on the lack of form, and the consumer may also claim registration if the conditions are met.
  - Q: What is the maximum delivery period for a turnkey home in a pre-paid home sale in Türkiye?
    A: The home must be delivered within the contractual period, which may not exceed forty-eight months from the contract date (Law No. 6502 art. 44). For older contracts, the text in force on the contract date should be checked.
  - Q: Can both a penalty and lost rent be claimed if a turnkey home in Türkiye is delivered late?
    A: No. According to the 3rd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, a penalty cumulative with performance and compensation for lost rent cannot be claimed together; a loss exceeding the penalty may be claimed only if the seller’s fault is proved. The right to the penalty must be reserved until delivery.
  - Q: Until when can a consumer withdraw from or rescind a pre-paid home sale contract in Türkiye?
    A: The consumer may withdraw without reason within fourteen days of the contract. Law No. 6502 art. 45 also grants a right of rescission without reason for up to twenty-four months; the seller may then claim costs and compensation of two to eight per cent of the price.
  - Q: Is the bank also liable if a turnkey home bought with a loan in Türkiye is not delivered?
    A: Yes, if the loan is a linked loan; the bank is then liable together with the seller, limited to the loan amount and for one year from the contractual delivery date where the home is not delivered (Law No. 6502 art. 35).
  - Q: Is mediation mandatory in disputes arising from a turnkey home sale in Türkiye?
    A: For monetary claims such as late-delivery damages, a penalty or a refund, applying to a mediator first is a precondition to suit. Disputes arising from rights in rem over real estate, such as annulment of title and registration, and disputes within the consumer arbitration committee’s jurisdiction are excluded (Law No. 6502 art. 73/A).
- [Urgent Expropriation Annulment Does Not End Price Suit: Turkish Cassation Assembly, If Expropriation Stands](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/urgent-expropriation-annulment-registration-action-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, the Council of State’s annulment of an urgent expropriation decision only taints the taking of possession… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/acele-kamulastirma-kararinin-iptali-tescil-davasini-dusurmez
  - Q: If an urgent expropriation decision is annulled, does the price determination and registration action lapse?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, as long as the public-interest decision and the expropriation decision have not been annulled, the action is heard on the merits; the annulment only taints the taking of possession under the urgent procedure.
  - Q: Which action can be brought against the expropriation measure?
    A: An action for annulment of the expropriation measure before the administrative courts; the time limit is thirty days from service or announcement under art. 10. Each co-owner may bring the action separately (Expropriation Law art. 14).
  - Q: What if the expropriation decision has also been annulled?
    A: The 5th Civil Chamber of the Turkish Court of Cassation held, in a case where the expropriation decisions had also been annulled with final effect in an action brought by the defendant owner, that the price determination and registration action should be dismissed.
  - Q: Was the decision unanimous?
    A: No, it was given by majority and is final. The dissenting opinion argues that the administration must return to the ordinary expropriation procedure.
- [Year-Round Beach Occupation: Ecrimisil Due for Whole Year](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/beach-occupation-ecrimisil-whole-year-not-seasonal): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers of the Council of State held that where an area under the sovereignty and disposal of the State is occupied as a beach, the business is… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-09; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/plaj-isgalinde-ecrimisil-sezonluk-degil-tum-yil-icin-hesaplanir
  - Q: Is ecrimisil for public land occupied as a beach claimed only for the summer months?
    A: Not in this case. Since the business was open all year and the occupation continued throughout the year, the Assembly held that ecrimisil must be calculated for the whole year.
  - Q: Is the fact that the area can be used only in summer not reflected in the amount?
    A: It is, but in the unit value rather than the period. According to the Assembly, the possibility of summer use had been taken into account as a negative factor in determining the ecrimisil.
  - Q: What if the use is genuinely limited to the season?
    A: This decision does not resolve that situation. The 4th Chamber of the Council of State decided whether an occupation was seasonal by looking at whether the land was actually used in the winter months.
  - Q: Can the administration directly apply the unit value in a final court decision on a comparable property?
    A: According to the Assembly, no. That decision is one of the data to be taken into account; but a value fixed for a different period cannot simply be updated and used as the amount without regard to the case-specific circumstances.
  - Q: Is the decision final, and what happened to the file?
    A: The decision is final and its reasoning was adopted by majority. The part of the decision of insistence that went against the administration was reversed and the file was sent to the regional administrative court.

### Labor & Social Security Law

- [Action for Determination of Insured Service: Conditions, Time Limit and Proof](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-determination-insured-service): Court determination of work never reported or under-reported to the SGK: the conditions and parties of the action, the competent labour court, the five-year forfeiture period and its exceptions… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/hizmet-tespiti-davasi
  - Q: What is a determination-of-service action for?
    A: It lets a court establish work periods and earnings that your employer never reported, or under-reported, to the SGK. The premium days and earnings are then recorded on your service history, so entitlements such as retirement, disability and survivors’ pensions are calculated correctly.
  - Q: Within what time must the action be brought?
    A: As a rule, within five years from the end of the year in which the work took place. This is a forfeiture period: it does not stop or interrupt and is applied by the court of its own motion.
  - Q: Does the five-year period still run if the employer filed an entry notification?
    A: No. If the employer submitted an employment entry notification or one of the documents listed in the regulation, the Institution is deemed aware of the work and the five-year forfeiture period does not apply. The period likewise does not run where the work was established by a social-security inspector’s report.
  - Q: Against whom is the action brought; is SGK the defendant?
    A: The action is brought against the employer of the workplace. The Social Security Institution is not the defendant but is joined ex officio as an intervening party.
  - Q: Can work be proven by witness testimony alone?
    A: Usually not. Because the action concerns public order the court investigates ex officio; testimony must be corroborated by payrolls, service ledgers, neighbouring-workplace records and official documents.
  - Q: Is mandatory mediation required?
    A: No. Unlike claims for employee receivables, determination-of-service actions to which the SGK is a party are not subject to mandatory mediation as a procedural condition.
- [Court of Cassation: An SGK Fine Not Challenged in Time Cannot Be Disputed in a Payment-Order Action](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/sgk-administrative-fine-objection-payment-order-turkiye): Under Turkish law, in its decision of 15.12.2022 the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held that an SGK administrative fine is challenged by objecting to the Institution within fifteen… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sgk-idari-para-cezasi-kesinlesirse-odeme-emri-davasinda-tartisilamaz
  - Q: Under Turkish law, what is the time limit for objecting to an SGK administrative fine?
    A: An objection to an SGK administrative fine may be made to the Institution within fifteen days of notification; the objection stays enforcement. If the objection is rejected, an action may be brought before the competent administrative court within thirty days of notification of the rejection; if that period is missed the fine becomes final (Law No. 5510, Art. 102).
  - Q: Which court hears a challenge to an SGK administrative fine in Türkiye?
    A: An action against an SGK administrative fine is brought before the administrative court after the Institution rejects the objection (Law No. 5510, Art. 102). For disputes arising from collection of the Institution’s claims under Law No. 6183, that is, objections to a payment order, the labour court of the place where the Institution’s creditor unit is located has jurisdiction (Art. 88).
  - Q: How can a payment order for a final SGK administrative fine be challenged?
    A: An objection to the payment order is made within 15 days of service (Law No. 6183, Art. 58). According to the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, for a final administrative fine the debtor may raise only payment and limitation; the unlawfulness of the fine or the claim that “there is no debt” cannot be raised in that action.
  - Q: Is there a discount if an SGK administrative fine is paid early?
    A: Yes. If the fine is paid in advance within fifteen days of notification, before any objection to the Institution or recourse to the courts, three quarters of it is collected. Advance payment does not affect the right to go to court (Law No. 5510, Art. 102).
  - Q: What happens if the SGK sends a payment order while the objection to the fine is still pending?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, if a payment order is sent before the objection procedure under Article 102 is completed and an action is brought against the payment order, the court must await the outcome of that procedure and treat the administrative court action as a preliminary question.
- [Court of Cassation: Survivors’ Pension Reached by Crediting Days Is Stopped; Recovery Limited as SGK Error](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/survivors-pension-900-days-overpayment-sgk-error-turkiye): Under Turkish law, in its decision of 18.06.2026 the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held that credited periods do not count towards the 5-year and 900-day route for a survivors’… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/olum-ayligi-900-gun-borclanma-kurum-hatasi-yersiz-odeme
  - Q: Under Turkish law, can the 900 days required for a survivors’ pension be completed by crediting military service?
    A: No. In its decision of 18.06.2026, the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held that the phrase “excluding all kinds of credited periods” in Article 32/2-a of Law No. 5510 applies to both the 5-year insurance period and the 900-day premium condition. Credited days may, however, count towards the 1800-day premium condition.
  - Q: Can the Social Security Institution (SGK) stop a survivors’ pension it granted by its own error?
    A: Yes. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, if the statutory conditions for the survivors’ pension were never met, the Institution is right to stop the pension; the fact that it had been paid for years did not change that result. The beneficiaries’ request to have the pension reinstated must be rejected.
  - Q: Can survivors’ pensions paid by the SGK’s own error be reclaimed in Türkiye?
    A: They can, but only to the extent of Article 96/1-b of Law No. 5510. Payments made within at most five years back from the detection of the erroneous act are recovered; no interest runs if the debt is paid within twenty-four months of notification, and statutory interest applies from the end of that period to payments made later.
  - Q: Is the Assembly of Civil Chambers’ survivors’ pension decision No. 2026/386 final?
    A: Yes. The decision was given unanimously and as final on 18.06.2026; the decision to persist was reversed and the file was sent to the Regional Court of Appeal, which will decide again in line with the reversal adopted by the Assembly.
- [Determining Farmer Bağ-Kur Insurance in Türkiye: Farmers’ Service and Insured Status (Laws No. 2926 and 5510)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/determination-agricultural-bag-kur-service-insurance): Retroactive determination of compulsory agricultural Bağ-Kur insurance under Laws No. 2926 and 5510; registration, premium withholding from product prices, commencement, competent court, proof, and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tarim-bag-kur-ciftci-hizmet-sigortalilik-tespiti
  - Q: Is agricultural Bağ-Kur insurance optional?
    A: No. Once the agricultural-activity condition is met, insurance is compulsory and cannot be waived (Art. 5 of Law 2926; Art. 92 of Law 5510).
  - Q: I never registered — do my past years still count?
    A: Even without registration, if premiums were withheld from your product sales, the Court of Cassation starts insurance from the month after the withholding. But the period before any withholding or voluntary payment cannot be determined.
  - Q: I only have chamber registration; is that enough?
    A: Not by itself. Actual agricultural activity (production, sale, withholding) must be shown with concrete evidence alongside the registration.
  - Q: I interrupted my farming; is my insurance terminated?
    A: Interruptions exceeding an acceptable period (about two years) may end insurance; restarting requires registration, voluntary payment, or withholding.
  - Q: My father died; can I have his agricultural insurance determined?
    A: Yes. On the insured's death, the beneficiaries (spouse, child, parents) may sue to determine the deceased's agricultural insurance to obtain a survivor's pension.
  - Q: Which court is competent?
    A: The labour court (Art. 101 of Law 5510); where none exists, the civil court of first instance acts in that capacity.
- [Fictitious Insurance and Cancellation of Insured Status](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/fictitious-insurance-cancellation-insured-status): Legal consequences of fictitious/collusive insurance and fake-workplace notifications; the actual-work requirement, the SGK's audit and cancellation powers, pension suspension and recovery of undue… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sahte-sigortalilik-ve-sigortaliligin-iptali
  - Q: My insurance was paid although I did not actually work; is it valid?
    A: No. The essential condition of insurance is genuine (actual) work. A notification without work is not protected and may be cancelled by the Institution.
  - Q: An SGK inspector drew up a report; can I challenge it?
    A: Yes. Audit officers' reports are valid until the contrary is proven; you may prove otherwise by any evidence before the labour court.
  - Q: My employer admits I worked; is that enough on its own?
    A: No. According to the Court of Cassation, the employer's admission or denial of the work is not decisive on its own; the court investigates ex officio.
  - Q: What happens to my pension if my insurance is cancelled?
    A: The pension based on cancelled periods is suspended; pensions paid after cancellation and health expenses obtained may be reclaimed as undue payments.
  - Q: I was shown as collusively insured with a relative; what is my risk?
    A: Notifications among relatives not based on genuine work are deemed collusive; cancellation, undue-payment recovery and, depending on the case, criminal liability may arise.
  - Q: My insurance was cancelled although I genuinely worked; what can I do?
    A: You may sue in the labour court to annul the Institution's transaction and to determine the service, proving your actual work with payroll witnesses and records.
- [Invalidity Pension in Türkiye: Conditions, 60% Loss of Working Capacity and Challenging the SGK Decision](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/invalidity-pension-conditions-sgk-objection-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an invalidity pension requires the Institution’s Health Board to find a loss of at least 60% of working capacity, at least ten years of insurance and 1800 premium days, and a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/malulluk-ayligi-sartlari-ve-sgk-kararina-itiraz
  - Q: What are the conditions for an invalidity pension under Turkish law?
    A: Under Article 26 of Law No. 5510, the insured must be considered an invalid under Article 25, must have been insured for at least ten years with a total of 1800 premium days reported, and must make a written request to the Institution after leaving work, or closing or transferring the business, because of the invalidity. For those needing another person’s constant care, the ten-year insurance period is not required; 1800 days suffice.
  - Q: What percentage of working capacity must be lost to be considered an invalid in Türkiye?
    A: For employees (4/1-a) and the self-employed (4/1-b), at least 60% of working capacity, or of earning capacity in the profession as a result of a work accident or occupational disease, must be lost. This is determined by the Institution’s Health Board (Law No. 5510, Art. 25).
  - Q: Is an invalidity pension granted for an illness that existed before starting work?
    A: If the insured had already lost 60% of working capacity before first starting work, no invalidity pension is granted for that illness (Art. 25/2). According to the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, however, if the illness had not reached that degree at the start of insurance but progressed during insurance to a degree requiring invalidity, a pension may be granted. In 2025, however, the Assembly of Civil Chambers held that the claim of an insured whose illness already existed before the start of insurance had to be rejected under Art. 25/2.
  - Q: How can an SGK health board decision on invalidity be challenged in Türkiye?
    A: On an objection to the decision of the Institution’s Health Board, the Social Insurance Supreme Health Board decides. According to the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, that Board’s decisions bind the Institution but not the insured. An application to the Institution is mandatory before suing (Law No. 7036, Art. 4); in the decisions cited, the cases were heard by labour courts and the courts obtained reports from the Forensic Medicine Institute.
  - Q: From what date does an invalidity pension start under Turkish law?
    A: It starts from the beginning of the month following the date of the written request if the report on which invalidity is based predates the request, or following the report date if the report is later (Law No. 5510, Art. 27). In a file where the 1800-day condition was met later, the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that the pension should start from the beginning of the month following the date that condition was met.
  - Q: What happens if a person receiving an invalidity pension in Türkiye starts working again?
    A: Under Article 27 of Law No. 5510, the pension of a person who starts working under this Law or under a foreign country’s legislation while receiving an invalidity pension is stopped at the start of the payment period following the start of work. If the person leaves work and makes a new written request, the pension is recalculated and granted again, provided that a control examination shows the invalidity continues.
- [Is a Heart Attack at Work a Work Accident in Türkiye? Law No. 5510 Art. 13 and Court of Cassation Decisions](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/heart-attack-at-work-work-accident-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a heart attack suffered by an insured while at the workplace is, as a rule, treated as a work accident under Article 13 of Law No. 5510 in decisions of the Court of Cassation’s… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/isyerinde-kalp-krizi-is-kazasi-sayilir-mi
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a heart attack at the workplace a work accident?
    A: In decisions of the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, a heart attack suffered while the insured is at the workplace is, as a rule, treated as a work accident under Article 13/1-a of Law No. 5510. The law makes no distinction between heart attacks and other events. In a 2025 decision, however, the Chamber required a report from a cardiology expert to establish medically whether a heart attack had occurred.
  - Q: Is a heart attack in an employer-provided shuttle a work accident in Türkiye?
    A: In a 2023 decision, the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation upheld a judgment treating the death of an insured from a heart attack while travelling to work in a vehicle provided by the employer as a work accident under Article 13/1-e and granting a survivors’ income. Under the upheld reasoning, the SGK circular excluding heart attacks in this situation is contrary to the law.
  - Q: Is a heart attack suffered by a driver sent abroad on duty always a work accident under Turkish law?
    A: Not always. In a 2025 decision, the 10th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation did not treat as a work accident the death of a driver who parked his truck in the customs area, went of his own accord to a friend’s house and died there of a heart attack. The decision was by majority; the dissent argued that the event occurred under the employer’s indirect control and should be treated as a work accident.
  - Q: Must one apply to the SGK before suing to have a heart attack recognised as a work accident?
    A: Yes. Under Article 4 of Labour Courts Law No. 7036, in disputes arising from social security legislation, except for determining insurance periods under an employment contract, an application to the Institution is mandatory before suing. If the Institution does not respond within sixty days, the request is deemed rejected.
  - Q: If a heart attack is recognised as a work accident in Türkiye, is the employer automatically at fault?
    A: No. Recognising the event as a work accident and the employer’s fault are separate questions. The dissenting opinions in the 10th Civil Chamber’s 2025 decisions also state that the link between the work and the event affects the employer’s fault and the amount of compensation, not whether the event is a work accident.
- [Notice Pay: Conditions, Notice Periods and Calculation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/notice-pay): Notice pay: compensation payable under Labour Code art. 17 on the improper (notice-less) termination of an indefinite-term contract; seniority-based notice periods of 2-4-6-8 weeks, the two-sided… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ihbar-tazminati
  - Q: What is notice pay and who pays it?
    A: Notice pay is the compensation the party who terminates an indefinite-term contract without just cause and without giving notice pays to the other side. It is two-sided: if the employer terminates improperly it pays the employee, and if the employee does, he pays the employer.
  - Q: How long are the notice periods?
    A: They are graduated by seniority: 2 weeks for less than 6 months, 4 weeks for 6 months–1.5 years, 6 weeks for 1.5–3 years, and 8 weeks for more than 3 years. These are minimums and may be increased by contract.
  - Q: Can a resigning employee claim notice pay?
    A: No. An employee who leaves without just cause and without giving notice is deemed to have resigned; he does not acquire a right to severance or notice pay and may even owe notice pay to the employer. An employee who proves the resignation letter was taken by pressure or threat may, however, acquire a right to compensation.
  - Q: How is notice pay calculated?
    A: By multiplying the notice period corresponding to seniority (2/4/6/8 weeks) by the dressed gross wage. The dressed wage includes, besides the base wage, regular benefits such as transport, meals and bonuses.
  - Q: What happens if job-search leave is not granted?
    A: Within the notice period the employee must be given at least two hours of job-search leave per day. If it is not given or given short, the wage for that period is paid; if the employer makes the employee work during the leave, it pays that time’s wage with a 100% surcharge. The leave arises only for days actually worked within the notice period.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for notice pay?
    A: Five years (Labour Code add. art. 3). This applies to employment contracts that ended after 25 October 2017; for contracts that ended earlier, the ten-year period may apply under the transitional provisions.
- [Overtime Pay: Conditions, Calculation and Proof](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/overtime-pay): Overtime pay under Turkish law: the 50% surcharge for work over 45 hours per week (Labour Code art. 41), extended-hours work (25%), written consent and the annual 270-hour ceiling, averaging (art.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/fazla-calisma-alacagi
  - Q: How is overtime pay calculated?
    A: For each hour exceeding forty-five hours per week, the normal hourly wage increased by 50% (1.5x) is paid. If the weekly time is set below 45 hours by contract, work exceeding the agreed time up to 45 hours is “extended-hours work” at a 25% surcharge.
  - Q: How do I prove I worked overtime?
    A: First by written documents such as entry/exit records, time-clock/fingerprint data and internal correspondence; failing that, by witness testimony. If a signed payroll shows an overtime accrual and no reservation was entered, any excess must be proven by written document.
  - Q: Does “overtime included in salary” extinguish the claim?
    A: No. Such a clause is valid at most for 270 hours per year and only if the wage is adequate for it. If the wage is set at the minimum wage the clause is invalid; and overtime exceeding 270 hours is always paid separately.
  - Q: Why is a reduction applied to overtime claims?
    A: Where overtime is calculated on witness testimony, continuous identical work every day is contrary to the ordinary course of life (leave, illness, excuse), so a presumption-based reasonable reduction applies. Where the calculation rests on written documents, no reduction is made, and it may not destroy the essence of the right.
  - Q: Can I claim overtime as a manager?
    A: A manager who sets their own hours and works at the highest level cannot, as a rule, claim overtime. But if there is a higher manager/employer from whom you take instructions and you must observe fixed hours, you may be entitled to overtime pay.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for overtime claims?
    A: Five years (Labour Code art. 32). It runs from the date each month’s claim falls due; applying to a mediator is a condition of the action before filing.
- [Principal Employer and Subcontractor in Türkiye: Sham Arrangements, Joint Liability (Labour Law Arts. 2, 112)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/principal-employer-subcontractor-sham-liability-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a subcontracting relationship may be set up for ancillary jobs or for a part of the main work required by operational and business needs and requiring technological expertise; if… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/asil-isveren-alt-isveren-iliskisi-muvazaa-ve-birlikte-sorumluluk
  - Q: When is a principal employer–subcontractor relationship considered a sham under Turkish law?
    A: As a rule, the relationship is treated as sham if a part of the main work not requiring expertise has been given to the subcontractor, if the relationship has been set up with someone previously employed at that workplace, or if the principal employer’s employees have been moved to the subcontractor with their rights restricted (Labour Law No. 4857 art. 2; Subcontracting Regulation art. 3). The Subcontracting Regulation also requires consideration of the subcontractor’s equipment and experience and of whether the principal employer’s employees work in the same job. The decision is based on all the facts in the file.
  - Q: What follows under Turkish law if a sham arrangement is found for a subcontractor’s employee?
    A: The subcontractor’s employee is deemed the principal employer’s employee from the outset (Labour Law No. 4857 art. 2). The employee may then claim the differences arising from the pay scheme of the principal employer’s comparable employees and, where the conditions are met, from the collective labour agreement. Benefiting from the collective labour agreement may depend on separate conditions such as union membership or a solidarity contribution.
  - Q: Is the principal employer liable under Turkish law for the severance pay and other receivables of a subcontractor’s employee?
    A: Yes, in a valid subcontracting relationship the principal employer is jointly liable with the subcontractor, in relation to that workplace, for obligations arising from the Law, the employment contract and the collective labour agreement to which the subcontractor is a party (Labour Law No. 4857 art. 2). The Court of Cassation’s 9th Civil Chamber applies this as joint and several liability. That the statutory words “in relation to that workplace” limit the liability to the period the employee worked at the principal employer’s workplace is this article’s own inference.
  - Q: Who pays the severance pay of a subcontractor’s employee working at a public body in Türkiye under art. 112 of Law No. 4857?
    A: The severance pay of an employee who worked without interruption at the same public body as a subcontractor’s employee within the scope of art. 62/1-e of Law No. 4734, and whose last employment contract ended in a way requiring severance pay, is as a rule paid by the relevant public body into the employee’s bank account (art. 112/2-a). The Court of Cassation’s 9th Civil Chamber has held that this rule does not remove the subcontractor’s liability and that the principal employer and the subcontractor are jointly liable.
  - Q: In a subcontracting relationship under Turkish law, does a sham-arrangement judgment for one tender period also apply to later tender periods?
    A: No, according to the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, final sham-arrangement judgments are binding only for the tender periods found to be sham. The contracts and the work actually done must be examined separately for previous and subsequent tender periods. The Assembly repeated this principle unanimously in its 2025 decision.
- [Reinstatement Action under Turkish Labour Law: Conditions, Valid Reason, Mediation, Time Limits, Compensation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/reinstatement-action-labour-law): The legal regime of the reinstatement action (action to establish the invalidity of a dismissal) available to an employee within the scope of job security whose contract has been terminated without a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ise-iade-davasi
  - Q: Who may bring a reinstatement action?
    A: The action is available to an employee within the scope of job security: the workplace must employ thirty or more employees, the employee must have at least six months' seniority, and the contract must be of indefinite term. Employer's representatives who manage the entire undertaking or workplace and have authority to hire and dismiss do not benefit from this security.
  - Q: Is applying to a mediator compulsory before bringing the action?
    A: Yes. Mediation is a condition of action. Application to a mediator must be made within one month of service of the notice of dismissal; if no agreement is reached, the action must be brought before the labour court within two weeks of the final minutes. An action brought without applying to a mediator is dismissed on procedural grounds.
  - Q: Are a valid reason and a just cause the same?
    A: No. A just cause (Art. 25) is a serious fact that makes the relationship intolerable and permits immediate termination. A valid reason is a less grave one that justifies terminating the contract by notice. A fact may constitute a valid reason even if it does not reach the level of a just cause.
  - Q: How much is the compensation for non-reinstatement?
    A: It is between four and eight months' wages. In the Court of Cassation's practice, by seniority, 4 months' wages apply for 6 months–5 years, 5 for 5–15 years and 6 for more than 15 years; depending on the reason for dismissal it may rise to eight months. The only case permitting the upper limit to be exceeded is a union-related dismissal.
  - Q: I won the case; am I reinstated automatically?
    A: No. You must apply to the employer to start work within ten working days of service of the finalised decision. If you do not apply, the dismissal is deemed valid. If, despite your application, the employer does not reinstate within one month, you become entitled to compensation for non-reinstatement and idle-period wages.
  - Q: Is the dismissal invalid if the employer dismissed me without obtaining my defence?
    A: In dismissals based on conduct or productivity, obtaining a defence is compulsory; a dismissal made without it is invalid solely for that reason. The period allowed for the defence must also be reasonable. In dismissals based on operational requirements, however, obtaining a defence is not required.
- [Retirement Through Crediting of Periods Spent Abroad](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/retirement-through-crediting-periods-spent-abroad): Periods spent abroad working or as a homemaker can be credited under Law No. 3201 and counted toward retirement in Türkiye; scope, amount and three-month payment, status, and the final-return… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disi-borclanmasi-ile-emeklilik
  - Q: Can I credit periods spent abroad as a homemaker?
    A: Yes. Law No. 3201 includes periods spent abroad as a homemaker within crediting.
  - Q: Is final return required for crediting?
    A: Not for crediting itself; but final return is required to obtain a pension based on the credited periods.
  - Q: How is the amount calculated and when is it paid?
    A: It is 45% of the chosen daily earning. The accrued debt must be paid within three months of notification; otherwise a fresh application is needed.
  - Q: Under which status is crediting assessed?
    A: The Court of Cassation fixes status by the last insurance status at the request date (4/1-a or 4/1-b), which affects pension conditions.
  - Q: What if I work abroad again after the pension is granted?
    A: Pensions of those granted under this Law who work abroad again or receive a residence-based benefit may be suspended.
  - Q: If I give up crediting, is my payment refunded?
    A: Those who give up crediting or cannot meet the pension conditions are refunded their payments without interest.
- [Retirement for Homemakers: Voluntary Insurance](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/retirement-homemakers): Homemakers not working under an employment contract can earn pension rights by paying voluntary-insurance premiums; conditions, 4/1-b status, age and premium-day requirements, in light of Law No.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ev-hanimlarinin-emekliligi-istege-bagli-sigorta
  - Q: Can a homemaker who never worked retire?
    A: Yes. By paying voluntary-insurance premiums she joins the long-term branches and, once the statutory age and premium-day conditions are met, becomes entitled to an old-age pension.
  - Q: Under which status is voluntary insurance counted?
    A: As a rule, 4/1-b (formerly Bağ-Kur) insurance. This matters for the conditions of the pension to be granted.
  - Q: How many premium days are needed to retire?
    A: The required age and premium days vary in stages by the start date of insurance. Missing days may be completed by continuing to pay after the application.
  - Q: What if I pay premiums irregularly?
    A: Validity depends on regular payment. Non-payment may end the insurance, and an irregularly granted pension may be cancelled and reclaimed.
  - Q: What happens to my voluntary insurance if I start working?
    A: If work requiring compulsory insurance begins, the overlapping voluntary premiums are cancelled and refunded; compulsory insurance prevails.
  - Q: Can I use periods spent abroad as a homemaker?
    A: Yes; these may be separately assessed through crediting of periods spent abroad under Law No. 3201.
- [Severance Pay on Resignation: Just-Cause Termination](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/severance-pay-resignation): The difference between resignation and the worker's just-cause immediate termination (Labour Law Art. 24); whether a resigning worker can claim severance; non-payment of wages and overtime… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/istifada-kidem-tazminati-hakli-nedenle-fesih
  - Q: I submitted a resignation letter; can I still claim severance?
    A: It is the real reason for leaving, not the letter's heading, that is decisive. If you left because your wages or overtime were unpaid, or your working conditions were substantially changed against you, that is a just-cause termination and entitles you to severance.
  - Q: I left because my wages were unpaid; is that a just-cause termination?
    A: Yes. Non-payment of the wage in accordance with law or contract is a just-cause ground under Art. 24/II-e and gives rise to severance. Non-payment of overtime, weekly rest, and national/general holiday pay produces the same result.
  - Q: I terminated with just cause; can I also claim notice pay?
    A: No. Notice pay is paid only by the party who terminates, to the other party. Because you terminated, you cannot claim notice pay even with just cause; you may claim severance and your other receivables.
  - Q: The employer made me sign a resignation letter promising compensation, then did not pay; what can I do?
    A: A resignation obtained by a promise of compensation or by pressure does not reflect a genuine resignation intent; in that case the termination is deemed the employer's. Proving the claim with witnesses and other evidence is important.
  - Q: Within what time must I exercise a just-cause termination?
    A: For conduct contrary to morality and good faith, you must terminate within six working days of learning the ground and, in any event, within one year of the event. However, in continuing violations such as non-payment of wages the six-working-day period does not run while the violation continues.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a severance claim?
    A: Five years from the date the employment contract ends (Law No. 4857, Additional Art. 3). Applying to a mediator before suit is mandatory.
- [Severance Pay: Conditions and Calculation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/severance-pay): Severance pay's basis (Law 1475, Art. 14), conditions, qualifying termination grounds, the one-year requirement, calculation over the 30-day all-inclusive wage, the ceiling, merging of intermittent… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kidem-tazminati-sartlar-ve-hesap
  - Q: Can I get severance pay if I resign?
    A: As a rule, no. But severance is earned in cases such as just-cause termination (Art. 24), military service, retirement, or a woman worker's termination within one year of marriage.
  - Q: How many years of service are needed?
    A: At least one year of service is required. Pro-rata payment is also made for periods exceeding one year.
  - Q: Which wage is used in the calculation?
    A: The last all-inclusive wage; continuing benefits such as bonuses, regular premiums, transport, meals, fuel, clothing and private health insurance are included. The minimum-living allowance and overtime are not.
  - Q: What is the severance ceiling?
    A: The severance payable per year cannot exceed the maximum retirement bonus payable to the highest civil servant for one year (the ceiling); the ceiling concerns public order.
  - Q: I worked intermittently at the same workplace; are the periods merged?
    A: Yes. Intermittent service at the same employer's workplaces is merged; but a period already settled by severance payment is not counted again.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for severance pay?
    A: Five years from the date the employment contract ends (Law No. 4857, Additional Art. 3).
- [Workplace Mobbing and Compensation: Psychological Harassment under Turkish Labour Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/workplace-mobbing-compensation): Workplace mobbing (psychological harassment) and compensation: the definition and elements (targeting, spread over time, systematic/continuous, intent to intimidate), the employer's duty of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/mobbing-ve-tazminat
  - Q: Is a one-off negative act mobbing?
    A: As a rule, no. Mobbing requires conduct that targets a specific employee, is spread over a period, and is systematically repeated. A one-off rude or unfair act, however serious, is not mobbing on its own.
  - Q: How do I prove mobbing?
    A: Witness statements, e-mail and message correspondence, minutes, health/psychiatric reports, camera footage and complaint applications may serve as evidence. Conclusive evidence is not required; once strong facts raising a suspicion of mobbing are shown, the burden of proving otherwise shifts to the employer. But abstract, non-concrete statements do not suffice.
  - Q: What compensation can I claim for mobbing?
    A: Moral compensation for the violation of personality rights (TBK art. 58) and, where applicable, compensation for pecuniary damage. In addition, if you terminate your contract treating the mobbing as just cause, you may become entitled to severance pay where the conditions are met.
  - Q: Can I get severance pay if I resign due to mobbing?
    A: Mobbing is a just-cause ground for termination under Labour Code art. 24/II. If mobbing is proven, the termination is deemed justified and you may be entitled to severance pay where the conditions are met. If it cannot be proven, the termination is not deemed justified and the claim may be rejected; hence gathering evidence before terminating is important.
  - Q: Is the employer liable for mobbing by another employee?
    A: Yes. The employer is obliged to protect the employee’s personality and prevent psychological harassment (TBK art. 417). If it fails to prevent harassment by a superior, subordinate or other employees, to investigate complaints effectively and to take measures, it is liable for the resulting damage.
  - Q: In which court is a mobbing case filed?
    A: In the labour court. Applying to a mediator before filing is a condition of the action; if no agreement is reached, the action is filed with the final minutes.

### Enforcement & Bankruptcy Law

- [Action for Annulment of Dispositions under Turkish Law: Conditions, Time Limits, 2025 Amendment (EBL 277-284)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-annulment-dispositions): A comprehensive guide to the action for annulment of dispositions: the purpose and relative nature of the action (EBL art. 277); gratuitous dispositions and transactions deemed donations as rewritten… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tasarrufun-iptali-davasi
  - Q: What is an action for annulment of dispositions?
    A: It renders dispositions such as transfers made by the debtor to hide assets ineffective only against the claimant creditor. It does not recover ownership; it gives the creditor the power of attachment and sale over that asset.
  - Q: Is a certificate of insolvency required to bring the action?
    A: Yes; a provisional or definitive certificate is a condition of the action. An attachment record showing no attachable assets has the same nature (EBL art. 105). The defect can be cured even on appeal.
  - Q: Which dispositions are subject to annulment?
    A: Gratuitous dispositions and transactions deemed donations within one year before the certificate of insolvency or bankruptcy, certain pledges and unusual payments in the same period, and transactions made with intent to harm that the other party knew or should have known of.
  - Q: Against whom do I bring the action?
    A: Jointly against the debtor and the third party who acquired the asset (EBL art. 282). Bad-faith subsequent acquirers may be joined; if bad faith is not proven, the action becomes a money claim.
  - Q: What is the time limit for the action?
    A: The action must be brought within five years of the annullable disposition (EBL art. 284). Separately, the one-year look-back periods for the relevant ground and the requirement that proceedings be brought within five years of the transaction must be met.
  - Q: Can a partner’s assets be pursued for a company debt?
    A: As a rule, no. If company assets were moved to a partner or group company, annulment of dispositions, simulation or, exceptionally, piercing the veil may apply. For public receivables, partners and legal representatives may be directly liable under Law No. 6183 art. 35 and repeated art. 35.
- [Action for Annulment of Objection under Turkish Law: Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law Art. 67](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-annulment-objection): A comprehensive guide to the action for annulment of objection: what the action is and when it is brought upon an objection to unsecured (ilamsız) enforcement proceedings (EBL Arts. 66-67); the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/itirazin-iptali-davasi
  - Q: What is an action for annulment of objection?
    A: It is the action the creditor brings in the general court to resume enforcement proceedings stayed by the debtor's objection. If the court allows it, it annuls the objection and orders the continuation of the proceedings.
  - Q: Within what time must the action be brought?
    A: Within one year of service of the debtor's objection on the creditor. This is a time bar examined by the court of its own motion. If the objection has not been served on the creditor, the period does not begin.
  - Q: In which court is the action brought?
    A: Not in the enforcement court but in the general court competent by the nature of the claim (civil, commercial, consumer or labour court). As to venue, as a rule the court of the defendant debtor's domicile is competent.
  - Q: When can enforcement-denial compensation be sought?
    A: Where the debtor's objection is unjustified and the claim pursued is liquid (definite/ascertainable), on the other party's request compensation of at least 20% of the adjudicated sum is awarded. If the claim can be determined only through a trial, it is not liquid and no compensation is awarded.
  - Q: What is the difference between annulment of objection and removal of objection?
    A: Removal of objection is a narrow/formal route by which a creditor holding the qualified documents of Arts. 68/68-a applies to the enforcement court within six months. Annulment of objection is an action brought in the general court within one year, in which the claim is examined on the merits. Where the conditions are met, the creditor may choose one of these routes.
  - Q: What happens if I miss the one-year period?
    A: You cannot seek annulment on the same proceedings; but your right to bring a separate debt action under general provisions is reserved (Art. 67/5).
- [Bank Debits During a Turkish Concordat Moratorium: Set-Off, Netting and Account Blocks (EBL Art. 294)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/bank-debit-company-account-during-concordat): In a Turkish concordat, a bank cannot set off against its loan any money reaching the company account after the temporary moratorium is announced; set-off against an earlier balance requires a due… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/konkordatoda-bankanin-hesaptan-kesinti-yapmasi
  - Q: Can a bank apply money reaching the account of a company in concordat to its loan?
    A: Not money reaching the account after the announcement of the temporary moratorium; in a concordat set-off is subject to arts. 200–201 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, applied by reference to the announcement date (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 294/4). Set-off against money held in the account before the announcement is possible if the bank’s loan claim is also due (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 139/1).
  - Q: Can a bank block a company’s account during a concordat moratorium?
    A: During the moratorium no enforcement proceedings may be taken against the debtor and interim injunctions and precautionary attachment orders are not executed (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 294/1). Concordat courts may order, as a protective measure, the lifting of blocks on funds reaching the account after the moratorium; in a decision upheld by the 11th Civil Chamber, the objection of a bank that withheld a deposit through a block based on a contractual pledge was found unjustified (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/3323, K. 2024/5376).
  - Q: Is interest payable to the bank during the concordat process?
    A: Unless the project provides otherwise, no interest runs on an unsecured bank claim from the date of the temporary moratorium (Court of Cassation, 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/2336, K. 2024/2845). The 6th Civil Chamber also treats a claim secured only by a mortgage over a third party’s real property as an unsecured claim (Court of Cassation, 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/426, K. 2023/488).
  - Q: Can the bank apply the proceeds of customer cheques collected during the moratorium to the loan?
    A: According to the 2025 decision of the 6th Civil Chamber it can, if the cheques were transferred by assignment endorsement and the loan claim arose and fell due before the temporary moratorium. If the company proves that the cheques were given as security by a disguised pledge endorsement, the collected proceeds must be returned to the commissioner (Court of Cassation, 6th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/1755, K. 2025/2890).
  - Q: How is money debited by the bank in breach of concordat measures recovered?
    A: Return is first requested through the commissioner and the concordat court. If the bank does not pay, enforcement proceedings without judgment are started and, if the bank objects, an action for annulment of objection is filed after mediation (Turkish Commercial Code art. 5/A). The court’s written order to the bank is not a sufficient document for removal of objection (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/3470, K. 2024/9880).
- [Compensation in Title Deed Cases Enforceable Before Finality: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/compensation-in-title-deed-case-enforceable-before-finality): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation held by majority that where, in an action for cancellation of title and registration or, alternatively, compensation, the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tapu-iptali-davasinda-hukmedilen-tazminat-kesinlesmeden-icraya-konulabilir
  - Q: Can a judgment given in an action for cancellation of title and registration be enforced before it becomes final?
    A: Under Turkish law, a judgment requiring a change in the register, such as cancellation of title and registration, may not be enforced until it has become final (Code of Civil Procedure arts. 350/2 and 367/2). If, in the same action, the title claim is dismissed and compensation is awarded, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation the compensation award may be put into enforcement without waiting for finality.
  - Q: Does the outcome change if the action was brought with alternative claims?
    A: No. According to the Assembly, what matters for enforcement based on a judgment is not the type of action but the nature of the judgment. The enforcement officer looks at the operative part and does not review the type of action.
  - Q: What if positive damages are the primary claim and cancellation of title the alternative claim?
    A: In its decision of 11.03.2026 the 12th Civil Chamber required finality for such a judgment as well. That decision predates the Assembly’s decision; under the Assembly’s criterion, which looks at the operative part, whether the claim was primary or alternative does not appear decisive for a judgment awarding a money claim.
  - Q: How did the debtor challenge enforcement based on a judgment that was not final?
    A: In this case the debtor filed a complaint with the enforcement court seeking the annulment of the enforcement proceedings. Since the judgment did not need to become final, the complaint was dismissed. Code of Civil Procedure arts. 350/1 and 367/1 provide that an appeal (istinaf or cassation) does not stay enforcement, but reserve the provision of Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 36 on the stay of enforcement.
- [Custodianship in Turkish Enforcement Law: Safekeeping, Liability and Liquidation (EBL Art. 88, TCK Art. 289)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/custodianship-enforcement-law): A guide to custodianship in Turkish enforcement law: what a custodian is and its legal nature; safekeeping of attached movables (EBL art. 88); the debtor, the creditor, a third party or a licensed… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/icra-hukukunda-yedieminlik
  - Q: My attached goods were left with me as custodian; may I use them?
    A: No. Even an owner-custodian may not sell, pledge, lease or relocate the goods; any disposal outside the purpose of safekeeping may be an offence under Law No. 5237 art. 289. For an owner-custodian the penalty is halved.
  - Q: What happens if I do not bring the goods to the sale venue on the sale date?
    A: According to the Criminal General Assembly, the custodian has no obligation to take the goods to the sale venue; this alone is not abuse of the duty of safekeeping. However, failing to return the goods when the enforcement office requests them where they are kept may be an offence and may trigger civil liability under EBL art. 358.
  - Q: Who pays the custodian fee?
    A: Safekeeping costs and advances are first collected from the creditor (EBL art. 95) and are, as a rule, charged to the debtor as enforcement costs (EBL art. 15). But if the attachment lapses because the sale was not requested in time, the creditor who caused this is liable for all costs including safekeeping (EBL art. 110). Licensed depot fees follow the Ministry tariff.
  - Q: The attachment has lapsed but my vehicle is still in a custodian car park; what should I do?
    A: The debtor may collect the vehicle by paying the custodian fee under the tariff; otherwise ex officio liquidation under EBL art. 88/A begins. The debtor is given ten days from service of notice. The goods may ultimately be sold, pass to the custodian, be transferred as scrap to MKE A.Ş., or be transferred free of charge to the Red Crescent.
  - Q: Whom do I sue if goods are lost in a custodian depot?
    A: An action for damages lies under the general rules against the custodian and the licensed depot operator; the enforcement office may also recover the value, of its own motion, from a custodian who cannot prove perishing or loss (EBL art. 358). If enforcement officers were at fault, the action is brought against the State, not the officer (EBL art. 5).
  - Q: How can I challenge enforcement office acts concerning the custodian?
    A: By a complaint to the enforcement court within seven days of learning of the act (EBL art. 16); where a right is not performed or is left pending without reason, a complaint may be made at any time. A compensation order under EBL art. 358 may also be challenged by complaint within the same seven days.
- [Exceeding a Maximum-Amount Mortgage: Are Interest, BSMV, Costs Included? (Turkish Civil Code Arts. 851, 875)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/exceeding-limit-maximum-amount-mortgage): In a maximum-amount (limit) mortgage under Turkish law, principal, interest, BSMV, enforcement costs and attorney’s fees together cannot exceed the limit; the excess can be annulled by a complaint… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ust-sinir-ipoteginde-limit-asimi
  - Q: Can the bank claim interest above the limit of a maximum-amount mortgage?
    A: Not in proceedings for realisation of the mortgage. The total claim, including interest, BSMV, enforcement costs and attorney’s fees, cannot exceed the limit; if the claim exceeds the limit, no interest may be claimed from the date of the proceedings either (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2022/75, K. 2023/1011). The claim above the limit may be pursued against the borrower by general attachment (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/3362, K. 2024/476).
  - Q: Within how many days must a complaint be filed against an enforcement order exceeding the mortgage limit?
    A: A limit complaint is not subject to a time limit; the first-instance court’s dismissal of the complaint as time-barred was reversed by the Court of Cassation (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/8074, K. 2025/1043). If the complaint is upheld, the proceedings are annulled only as to the part exceeding the limit (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/5159, K. 2024/9879).
  - Q: If the bank has two mortgages on the same immovable, are the limits added together?
    A: Yes.
