The limits of mortgages established for the same claim are added together; the 12th Civil Chamber accepted first- and second-degree mortgages of TRY 6,000,000 each and a TRY 2,500,000 mortgage on another immovable as a total limit of TRY 14,500,000 (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/8000, K. 2025/2895). A mortgagor, however, is liable only up to the limits of its own mortgages (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/4750, K. 2024/1808).
  - Q: I both stood surety for the loan and gave a mortgage; am I liable above the limit?
    A: In proceedings for realisation of the mortgage, liability is limited to the limit, and being a surety does not change this (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/615, K. 2024/4920). The bank may, however, separately pursue the claim outside the limit by general attachment in your capacity as surety (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/1307, K. 2025/2865).
  - Q: Does a limit complaint stop the sale of the mortgaged immovable?
    A: Not in itself; even if the complaint is upheld, the proceedings continue for the claim up to the limit (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2026/1392, K. 2026/1335). In a collective mortgage, however, the other immovables must not be sold once the limit has been covered (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/8564, K. 2024/786).
- [Fees in Allowed Negative Declaratory Actions Await Finality: Turkish Court of Cassation; No Complaint Deadline](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/negative-declaratory-attorney-fees-enforcement-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, a negative declaratory judgment is a whole; the litigation costs and attorney's fee awarded when the action… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/menfi-tespit-vekalet-ucreti-kesinlesmeden-icraya-konulamaz
  - Q: Can the attorney’s fee awarded in a negative declaratory action be enforced before the judgment becomes final?
    A: Under Turkish law, where the action has been allowed, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, no. The litigation costs and attorney’s fee are ancillary to the judgment; they cannot be the subject of enforcement based on the judgment until the judgment on the merits becomes final. A judgment dismissing the action was not examined in this decision.
  - Q: What is the time limit for a complaint against enforcement started before finality?
    A: According to the Assembly, the complaint concerns public order and is not bound by the seven-day time limit in art. 16 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law.
  - Q: What happens if the complaint is upheld?
    A: According to the Assembly, the enforcement court annuls the enforcement proceedings.
  - Q: Which judgments cannot be enforced before they become final?
    A: As a rule, an appeal (istinaf or cassation) does not stop enforcement (Code of Civil Procedure arts. 350/1 and 367/1). Decisions concerning the law of persons, family law and rights in rem over immovable property (arts. 350/2 and 367/2) and negative declaratory judgments (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 72) are among the exceptions; special laws contain further exceptions.
  - Q: Was the decision unanimous?
    A: No, it was given by majority; the view that the claim, being a monetary claim, did not concern public order did not obtain a majority.
- [How to Obtain a Stay of Enforcement Pending Appeal in Türkiye: Step-by-Step Procedure, Security, Time Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/stay-of-enforcement-pending-appeal-turkiye): Under Turkish law, appealing a judgment does not by itself stop its enforcement; the debtor must provide security, obtain time from the enforcement office and bring a decision on stay of enforcement… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tehir-i-icra-icranin-geri-birakilmasi-nasil-alinir
  - Q: Does appealing a judgment stop enforcement in Türkiye, or is a stay of enforcement needed?
    A: No, under Turkish law lodging an appeal to the regional court of appeal or to the Court of Cassation does not by itself stop enforcement of the judgment (CCP arts. 350/1 and 367/1). A debtor who wants enforcement stopped must, under EBL art. 36, provide security, obtain time from the enforcement office and bring a decision on stay of enforcement from the enforcement court within that time. Judgments on the law of persons, family law and rights in rem over immovable property cannot be enforced before becoming final in any case.
  - Q: Which court decides on a stay of enforcement pending appeal under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, the decision on stay of enforcement is given by the enforcement court of the place where the proceedings are conducted. The phrase “from the regional court of appeal or the Court of Cassation” in EBL art. 36/1 was, as reported by the 2nd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, replaced in this way by Law No. 7343, adopted on 24.11.2021. Since the amendment, the 2nd Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation finds no legal interest in requesting a stay from it. The enforcement court’s decision cannot be appealed to the regional court of appeal (EBL art. 363/1).
  - Q: How much and what kind of security is required for a stay of enforcement in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, security for a stay of enforcement must be to the value of the money or goods awarded (EBL art. 36/1). The law lists deposit of the money with an official authority, a pledge of movables or mortgage over immovables, shares or bonds and a reliable bank suretyship to be accepted by the enforcement court; attachment of the debtor’s assets covering the debt is also sufficient. The amount should be confirmed against the enforcement office’s file calculation. The State or a person receiving legal aid is not required to provide security.
  - Q: What happens if the time granted for a stay of enforcement expires in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, the period is not fixed in days; the enforcement officer grants “a reasonable period”, which may be extended only in case of necessity (EBL art. 36/1). The law does not state expressly what happens when the period expires; since the warning in the enforcement order (EBL art. 32) states that compulsory enforcement will follow unless a decision is brought, the proceedings can be expected to continue if no decision on stay of enforcement is brought within the period. It is prudent to request any extension before the period expires.
  - Q: What happens to the security for a stay of enforcement in Türkiye if the judgment is reversed?
    A: Under Turkish law, if the judgment is set aside by the regional court of appeal or reversed by the Court of Cassation, enforcement steps stop where they are (EBL art. 40). If the regional court of appeal finds the appeal justified, it decides whether the security is returned; if the Court of Cassation reverses, the court examining the merits after reversal decides with final effect, upon the debtor’s application and according to the nature of the reversal (EBL art. 36/5). If the judgment is upheld, the money held as security is paid to the creditor.
- [Improper Account Closure Notice in Turkish Mortgage Enforcement: Are Proceedings Annulled? (EBL Art. 150/ı)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/improperly-served-account-closure-notice-mortgage): A Turkish bank may use judgment-track mortgage enforcement only after serving an account closure notice via a notary; defective service leads to annulment. The article covers the conditions of art.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ipotekli-ilamli-takip-hesap-kat-ihtari
  - Q: Can a bank bring judgment-type mortgage enforcement without serving an account closure notice?
    A: Not on a maximum-amount mortgage. Judgment-type enforcement requires the account statement or payment notice to be served on the borrower through a notary, or to be deemed served under art. 68/b (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 150/ı). The Court of Cassation treats this as a condition of the proceedings and requires the proceedings to be annulled if the notice was not duly served (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/9381, K. 2026/2739).
  - Q: Within how many days must a complaint of defective service of the account closure notice be filed?
    A: The 12th Civil Chamber examines this complaint without time limit under art. 16/2 (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/1743, K. 2024/7285); the former practice requiring a seven-day period has been abandoned. A complaint without time limit, too, can only be made until the proceedings are concluded (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/1685, K. 2024/6923).
  - Q: Within how many days must I object to the account closure notice, and what if I do not?
    A: The borrower objects through a notary within eight days from service of the notice or from the date it is deemed served under art. 68/b (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 150/ı). Without a timely objection, the amount of debt stated in the notice becomes final for the purposes of enforcement law (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2022/75, K. 2023/1011).
  - Q: My address changed and the bank sent the notice to my old address; is the service valid?
    A: If the new address was not notified to the bank through a notary, the date on which the account statement reaches the old address is deemed the date of service (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 68/b/1). The 12th Civil Chamber has held that the condition of the proceedings is fulfilled in that situation (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/9760, K. 2026/3608).
  - Q: If the mortgagor has died, must the bank serve a new account closure notice on the heirs?
    A: Not where the proceedings were started against the mortgagor, the notice was served on the mortgagor and the proceedings became final (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/8662, K. 2023/1610; by majority). If the death occurred before the proceedings started, the proceedings cannot continue on the basis of a notice served after they were started (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/711, K. 2022/7295).
- [Negative-Declaratory and Recovery Actions: Declaring Non-Liability and Recovering Money Paid](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/negative-declaratory-recovery-actions): Negative-declaratory and recovery actions (EBC art. 72): declaring non-liability, actions before and after enforcement, an interim injunction staying enforcement against 15% security, the condition… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/menfi-tespit-ve-istirdat-davasi
  - Q: Does a negative-declaratory action stay the enforcement?
    A: Not automatically. In an action brought before enforcement the court may, against security of at least 15% of the claim, issue an injunction staying the proceedings. In an action brought after enforcement, the proceedings cannot be stayed by injunction; the debtor may only, against 15% security, request that the money not be released to the creditor.
  - Q: What is the difference between a negative-declaratory and a recovery action?
    A: In a negative-declaratory action no payment has yet been made and non-liability is sought. In a recovery action payment has been made under threat of compulsory enforcement and the aim is restitution. If a debtor who brought a negative-declaratory action obtained no injunction and then paid, the action continues as a recovery action.
  - Q: Is there a time limit for a recovery action?
    A: Yes. A person compelled to pay a sum he did not owe must bring the recovery action within one year of the date of payment (EBC art. 72/7). This period is for payments connected to enforcement (under threat of compulsory enforcement); the return of overpayments outside enforcement is subject to the unjust-enrichment provisions.
  - Q: Who bears the burden of proof?
    A: It depends on the nature of the claims. If the debt is embodied in an instrument and it is alleged to be without consideration or given as security, the party making that allegation must, in principle, prove it by written evidence (CCP art. 200). Without a beginning of written evidence, proof by witnesses may not be possible.
  - Q: In which court are these actions brought?
    A: As to territory, before the court of the place of the enforcement office or of the defendant’s domicile. As to subject matter, depending on the nature of the dispute, the civil, commercial, consumer or labour court.
  - Q: Can I claim damages if the proceedings were unjust?
    A: Yes. If the action succeeds for the debtor and the proceedings are found to be unjust and in bad faith, the debtor’s damage is collected from the creditor; this compensation may not be less than 20% of the claim pursued.
- [Objecting to a Payment Order and Salary Attachment: Time Limits, Late Objection, Protected Wages (Turkish Law)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/objecting-payment-order-salary-attachment-enforcement): Objecting to a payment order under the Turkish Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law (EBL art. 62): the seven-day period, objection to the enforcement office by petition or orally, objections to the debt… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/odeme-emrine-itiraz-ve-maas-haczi
  - Q: How many days do I have to object to a payment order?
    A: In general attachment proceedings without judgment, seven days from service of the payment order, and the objection is made to the enforcement office (EBL art. 62). In proceedings based on a negotiable instrument (promissory note, cheque, bill of exchange), objections to the debt and to the signature must be made within five days by petition to the enforcement court (EBL art. 168).
  - Q: Can I object before the payment order is served on me?
    A: Yes. The 12th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation treats as valid an objection lodged with the enforcement office by a debtor who learned of the proceedings by other means before the payment order was served; as long as the creditor intends to pursue the proceedings, the absence of service does not prevent an objection.
  - Q: I missed the objection period. What can I do?
    A: If the period was missed because of an obstacle for which you were not at fault, you may file a late objection with the enforcement court within three days from the removal of the obstacle, with evidence of your excuse (EBL art. 65). If the payment order was served irregularly, you may ask the enforcement court, by way of complaint, to correct the date of service. Even if the proceedings have become final, a negative declaratory and recovery action should be considered separately.
  - Q: How much of my salary can be attached?
    A: The amount necessary for your and your family’s subsistence is first deducted and the remainder is attached; however, the amount attached may not be less than one quarter of the salary. Where there are several attachments they are ranked, and no deduction is made for a later one until the earlier one has been completed (EBL art. 83). Under Court of Cassation practice, current maintenance is deducted in first rank.
  - Q: Can my state pension be attached?
    A: Income, pensions and allowances paid by the Social Security Institution may not be attached except for the Institution’s own claims and maintenance debts; attachment requests falling within this prohibition are rejected by the enforcement officer unless the debtor consents (Law No. 5510 art. 93). Agreements made in advance that a salary may be attached are invalid (EBL art. 83/a).
  - Q: My home has been attached. How do I argue that it is a home appropriate to my circumstances?
    A: You must file a complaint of non-attachability with the enforcement court within seven days of learning of the attachment (EBL art. 16, EBL art. 82). If the home is worth more than is appropriate to your circumstances, it is sold and a portion of the proceeds sufficient for your needs is left to you; the protection does not apply if the debt arises directly from the price of the home.
- [Physical Attachment When No Custodian Is Available: No Licensed Warehouse or Refusal (EBL Art. 88)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/physical-attachment-no-custodian-available): How does physical attachment proceed in Turkish enforcement law when there is no licensed custodian warehouse, the warehouse staff do not come or they refuse the goods? Based on EBL arts. 79/a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yediemin-bulunmadiginda-fiili-haciz
  - Q: Can attachment not be carried out if there is no custodian warehouse?
    A: It can. A movable is attached once it is recorded in the report; safekeeping is a separate stage. Without a warehouse, the goods may be left temporarily with the debtor or a third party with the creditor’s consent, or delivered to the body assigned to provide warehouse services in the area. At the sale stage, however, the goods must be taken into safekeeping or made ready for delivery to the buyer (EBL art. 88/2).
  - Q: Can the officer leave the attached goods with the debtor without the creditor’s consent?
    A: By law, goods in the debtor’s hands may be left temporarily with the debtor only with the creditor’s consent (EBL art. 88/2). If they are left without consent, the act may be challenged within seven days of learning of it. However, if the safekeeping cost has not been paid in advance, safekeeping is not carried out and the creditor bears the consequence.
  - Q: What should be done if the licensed warehouse does not take the goods?
    A: The licence holder must accept goods ordered into safekeeping. The situation should be recorded and the office asked to write to the operator and set a new date. If the request is not acted upon, a complaint may be filed at any time; if it is expressly rejected, within seven days (EBL art. 16). Warehouses are supervised by the chief public prosecutor and the Ministry.
  - Q: Who pays the safekeeping and warehouse fees?
    A: The creditor pays the safekeeping advance in advance, calculated on at least six months’ storage cost. These are enforcement costs ultimately collected from the debtor (EBL art. 59). The warehouse fee is calculated under the tariff in force when safekeeping ends.
  - Q: The attachment has lapsed but my car is still in the car park; what happens?
    A: Goods left with a custodian after the attachment has lapsed are liquidated ex officio (EBL art. 88/a). The debtor is notified that he may collect the vehicle within ten days on paying the custodian fee; otherwise the stages of pledgee, electronic sale, transfer to the custodian, transfer as scrap to Makine ve Kimya Endüstrisi AŞ and finally free transfer to the Turkish Red Crescent follow.
  - Q: Can I appeal an enforcement court decision rejecting my request for safekeeping?
    A: No. Enforcement court decisions on custodian fees, replacement of custodians and the manner of safekeeping of attached movables are not appealable to the regional court (EBL art. 363/1). The complaint should therefore be complete at the first attempt, documenting payment of the cost and the warehouse or custodian proposed.
- [Pledge-First Rule in Turkish Enforcement Law: Can a Mortgagee Bank Go Straight to Attachment? (EBL Art. 45)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/pledge-first-rule): A bank holding a mortgage must first enforce by realising it; attachment brought directly against the debtor breaches Turkish Enforcement Law art. 45/1. The article explains the public-order nature… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/once-rehne-muracaat-ilkesi
  - Q: Can a bank holding a mortgage attach the company’s accounts or vehicles?
    A: As a rule, no. For a mortgage-secured loan claim the bank must first enforce against the borrower by realisation of the mortgage (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 45/1). Attachment is available for the part of the claim not covered by the pledge or exceeding the mortgage limits (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/3362, K. 2024/476); a bank holding a promissory note or cheque may choose bills-of-exchange enforcement instead (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 167/1).
  - Q: Within how many days must a complaint against proceedings breaching the pledge-first rule be filed?
    A: The Court of Cassation treats this complaint as a matter of public order and does not subject it to a time limit (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/1109, K. 2023/7900). However, a debtor who neither objects to the proceedings nor files a complaint cannot benefit from the rule’s protection (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2024/300, K. 2025/571). The existence and scope of any mortgage should therefore be examined as soon as the payment order is served.
  - Q: If the mortgage does not cover the debt, how does the bank pursue the balance?
    A: If the finalised valuation does not cover the claim, the bank may obtain a provisional pledge shortfall certificate and request attachment of the debtor’s other assets (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 150/f). If the sale price is insufficient, a pledge shortfall certificate is issued; if attachment is requested within one year, no new enforcement order or payment order needs to be served (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 152).
  - Q: Can a bank bring both promissory-note enforcement and mortgage enforcement for the same loan?
    A: Not at the same time. Under the unification decision of 20.01.2023, E. 2021/2, K. 2023/1, as cited by the Assembly of Civil Chambers, two sets of proceedings cannot be brought simultaneously for the same claim even with a “without double recovery” reservation; the creditor chooses one route, and mortgage proceedings started while bills-of-exchange enforcement is pending are annulled as duplicate (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2022/1039, K. 2023/856).
  - Q: I am a surety for the loan and also gave a mortgage; can the bank pursue me directly by attachment?
    A: It depends on the mortgage deed. If the mortgage secures only the company’s debts, the bank may pursue you as a surety by general attachment under the conditions of art. 586 of the Turkish Code of Obligations (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/7479, K. 2021/7971). If the mortgage also covers debts arising from your suretyship, the mortgage must be realised first; attachment is available for the part not covered by the mortgage (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/4387, K. 2025/6585).
- [Precautionary Attachment](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/precautionary-attachment): An analysis of the requirements for precautionary attachment, the procedure for applying to court and for security, the ten-day period for enforcing the order, the seven-day and one-month periods… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ihtiyati-haciz
  - Q: What is precautionary attachment, and under what conditions may it be sought?
    A: Precautionary attachment is a provisional measure by which, on the creditor's application, the court places an advance attachment over the debtor's property and rights to secure collection of a monetary claim that is due and not secured by a pledge. For a claim not yet due, it may be sought only where the debtor has no fixed domicile or is attempting to abscond or conceal assets in order to escape its obligations.
  - Q: Is providing security mandatory to obtain a precautionary attachment order?
    A: As a rule, yes; under Article 259 EBC the creditor must provide security to cover any damages that may arise should the attachment prove unwarranted. However, no security is required where the claim is founded on a judgment; where it is founded on a document having the effect of a judgment, the court decides, in its discretion, whether security is needed.
  - Q: Within what period must a precautionary attachment order be enforced after it is obtained?
    A: Under Article 261 EBC, the creditor must, within ten days of the date the order was rendered, request its enforcement from the enforcement office within the judicial district of the court that rendered it. If this period lapses, the precautionary attachment order falls away automatically.
  - Q: Is there a time limit for bringing an action or commencing enforcement proceedings after precautionary attachment has been carried out?
    A: Yes. Under Article 264 EBC, a creditor who obtained precautionary attachment before bringing an action or commencing enforcement proceedings must, within seven days of the attachment being carried out (or, if carried out in the debtor's absence, of service of the attachment record), file a request to commence enforcement proceedings or bring an action; failing this, the precautionary attachment becomes void.
  - Q: How may a debtor object to a precautionary attachment order made against them?
    A: Under Article 265 EBC, a debtor against whom precautionary attachment was ordered without being heard may object, within seven days — from the carrying out of the attachment where the debtor was present, or otherwise from service of the attachment record — to the grounds on which the attachment rests, to the court's jurisdiction, and to the security, before the court that rendered the order. Third parties whose interests are affected may likewise object within seven days of learning of it.
  - Q: Can the decision rendered upon an objection to precautionary attachment be further appealed?
    A: Yes; under Article 265 EBC, an appeal (istinaf) lies against the decision rendered on objection. The regional court of appeal examines such an appeal as a priority, and its decision is final; the appeal does not suspend enforcement of the precautionary attachment order.
- [Removal of Objection under Turkish Law: Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law Arts. 68, 68/a and 69](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/removal-objection): A comprehensive guide to removal of objection: definitive removal of objection (EBL Art. 68) and its underlying documents (a deed containing an acknowledgment of debt with an admitted or notarised… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/itirazin-kaldirilmasi
  - Q: What is removal of objection?
    A: It is the creditor's request to the enforcement court, on the basis of a qualified document in hand, to remove the objection so that unsecured proceedings stayed by the objection may continue. The enforcement court does not try the merits; it decides by a formal examination.
  - Q: What is the difference between definitive and provisional removal?
    A: Definitive removal (Art. 68) rests on a qualified document such as an acknowledgment of debt with an admitted/notarised signature or an official document. Provisional removal (Art. 68/a) applies where the debtor denies the signature on a private deed, following a signature examination, and gives the debtor the possibility of bringing an action for release from debt.
  - Q: Which documents may support removal of objection?
    A: Deeds containing an acknowledgment of debt with an admitted or notarised signature, and receipts/documents duly issued by official departments and competent authorities. The document must contain an unconditional acknowledgment of debt; synallagmatic contracts conditional in performance are, as a rule, insufficient.
  - Q: What is the time limit for removal of objection?
    A: Six months from service of the debtor's objection on the creditor. This is a time bar examined by the court of its own motion. If no application is made within the period, fresh unsecured proceedings may not be brought for the same claim.
  - Q: Does the enforcement court examine whether the claim actually exists?
    A: No. The enforcement court conducts a narrow, formal examination and decides only on the documents. Disputes requiring a trial — the existence of the claim, performance of the contract — fall within the general court's jurisdiction.
  - Q: What can I do after provisional removal of objection?
    A: The debtor may bring an action for release from debt within seven days of the pronouncement or service of the provisional-removal decision. For this action to be heard, 15% of the claim at issue must be deposited by the first hearing (or security of equal value provided).
- [Return of Leased Assets in a Turkish Concordat: Can It Be Postponed? (EBL Arts. 307 and 294/7)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/return-leased-assets-postponed-concordat): In a Turkish concordat, return of a leased asset can be postponed only in the confirmation decision, at the debtor’s request, for up to one year, if all five conditions of Enforcement Law art. 307/3… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/konkordatoda-finansal-kiralama-malinin-iadesi
  - Q: Can leased machinery be taken back from a company in concordat?
    A: If rent has not been paid and the contract has been terminated by a proper notice, the lessor can bring an action for return; the concordat moratorium alone does not prevent this (Court of Cassation, 11th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/3472, K. 2025/1972). However, if the concordat court has issued a measure preventing the return of the machinery, a contrary delivery injunction may not be granted in the action for return (Istanbul Regional Court of Appeal, 44th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/745, K. 2025/976).
  - Q: For how long can the return of a leased asset be postponed in a concordat?
    A: For at most one year from the confirmation decision. The postponement is ordered in the confirmation decision at the debtor’s request and depends on all five conditions of art. 307/3 (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 307/3). If the debtor voluntarily transfers the asset, goes bankrupt or dies, the postponement automatically becomes void (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 307/5).
  - Q: Is postponement possible if more than three months of lease rent is unpaid?
    A: No. The Law lists as a condition of postponement that the unpaid rent must not exceed three months’ rent (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 307/3-c). This condition applies together with the other four; the absence of any one prevents postponement.
  - Q: Can a leasing company terminate the contract because the company applied for a concordat?
    A: No. The contract may not be terminated on the ground that the debtor applied for a concordat, and obligations under contracts continuing during the moratorium are performed reciprocally (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 296/1). If rent is not paid, however, the notice and termination route of Law No. 6361 applies (Law No. 6361, art. 31/1).
- [Service on Persons Living Abroad under Turkish Law: Consular Service, Public Notice, Unnoticed Judgments](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/service-of-process-abroad-turkish-courts-enforcement): Under Turkish law, service on persons living abroad: the consular route and the thirty-day period under arts. 25 and 25/a of the Notification Law, why service by public notice without researching the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disindaki-kisiye-tebligat-ve-habersiz-kesinlesen-kararlar
  - Q: I live abroad and a judgment was given against me without my knowledge; what can I do?
    A: If service was defective, the judgment is not final. You can lodge an appeal stating your date of knowledge; with defective service, the date of knowledge declared by the addressee counts as the date of service (Notification Law art. 32).
  - Q: What happens if I do not collect a service letter from the consulate?
    A: If the notice reached you under the laws of that country and you do not apply to the consulate within thirty days, service is deemed effected at the end of the thirtieth day (art. 25/a). Time limits run from that date.
  - Q: Is service at my old address in Türkiye valid?
    A: If it is known that you live abroad, or the address registration system shows a foreign address, service at an old address in Türkiye by posting on the door is defective according to Court of Cassation decisions.
  - Q: What is the time limit against enforcement proceedings started without my knowledge?
    A: A complaint against defective service is filed with the enforcement court within seven days of learning of the act (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 16). The time limit to object to the payment order runs from the corrected date of service.
  - Q: Can I sue my spouse or debtor who lives abroad in Türkiye?
    A: Yes; service is made through the competent authority of that country or, if the other party is a Turkish citizen, through the consulate. The foreign address should be stated in the statement of claim from the outset; if it is unknown, the address is researched first and public notice is the last resort.
- [Third-Party Mortgagor Liability for Company Loans: Acceleration Notice, Limit, Subrogation (TMK 887, 884)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/third-party-mortgagor-liability): A third party who mortgages real property for a company loan is liable only with the property and up to the mortgage limit, and must also receive notice. The article covers annulment of enforcement… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-03; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ipotek-veren-ucuncu-kisinin-sorumlulugu
  - Q: I mortgaged my home for the company’s loan; can the bank attach my salary or my other assets?
    A: Not if you only gave a mortgage. A third-party mortgagor is not personally liable for the debt but is liable with the property (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2025/466, K. 2026/289); no pledge shortfall certificate can be issued against you for the claim remaining after the property is sold (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/6305, K. 2021/11636). If you also signed the loan agreement as a joint and several surety, you are personally liable in that capacity (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 586/1).
  - Q: Can the bank put my mortgaged property up for sale without sending me a notice?
    A: No. The demand for payment must be made to the mortgagor as well as to the debtor (Turkish Civil Code art. 887); service of the account closure notice on the mortgagor replaces that demand (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 150/ı). Judgment-based enforcement started without notice is annulled in respect of the mortgagor, and the complaint is not subject to a time limit (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2026/116, K. 2026/5017).
  - Q: If I pay the company’s debt, from whom can I recover the money?
    A: When a mortgagor who is not personally liable pays the debt, the claim passes to them (Turkish Civil Code art. 884) and they may recover the amount from the borrower (Court of Cassation, Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2021/427, K. 2022/1610). You may claim against the sureties only if there is an agreement between you or the pledge was given later by a third party (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 596/4).
  - Q: Can a mortgagor object to the account closure notice?
    A: The objection through a notary under art. 150/ı is for the party using the credit; a mortgagor who is not personally liable cannot object in that way but may raise discharge, extension or limitation (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/8932, K. 2023/2393; affirmed). A surety who also gave a mortgage for the suretyship debt may object to the notice in time (Court of Cassation, 12th Civil Chamber, E. 2023/3924, K. 2024/1291).
  - Q: Can I have the mortgage removed before the loan is closed?
    A: A mortgage does not lapse by itself while the secured claim exists; the owner may request its cancellation once the claim ends (Turkish Civil Code art. 883/1). An owner who is not personally liable may pay the debt on the terms applicable to the debtor and request removal of the mortgage (Turkish Civil Code art. 884). Ending the security under a mortgage given for existing and future debts is discussed in the article on the action for release of a mortgage.
- [Third-Party Ownership Claims in Turkish Enforcement: EBL Arts. 96-99, Burden of Proof, Seven-Day Period](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/third-party-ownership-claims-enforcement-proceedings): Third-party ownership claims over attached property, first in plain language with everyday examples (a son's motorcycle at the family home, a spouse's jewellery, a leased machine, another company at… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/icra-takibinde-istihkak-davasi
  - Q: Enforcement officers attached my goods at my business or home for someone else’s debt. What should I do?
    A: Assert the ownership claim at the attachment itself, recorded in the report, and ensure it is made by an authorised person. Accept custody so that the goods are not removed. Have the lease, tax certificate and the serial numbers of the goods recorded. Then bring the ownership action within seven days of the pronouncement or service of the enforcement court’s decision on continuation or stay.
  - Q: The television attached at my home belongs to my daughter. Should I bring an ownership action?
    A: Often there is no need. The personal belongings of the debtor and family members living under the same roof and household goods used in common are exempt, except money and valuables (EBL art. 82/1-3). If such an item is attached, a complaint should be made to the enforcement court, as a rule within seven days of learning of it (EBL art. 16). The ownership claim is for attachable property belonging to someone else.
  - Q: I hold the invoice for the attached goods. Is that enough?
    A: Usually not. Where the presumption favours the creditor, you must rebut it with evidence that is convincing and free of all doubt: the invoice entered in duly kept commercial books, serial numbers matching the attachment report, financial capacity at the time of purchase, and an explanation of why the goods were where they were.
  - Q: Does bringing the action stop the sale?
    A: Not automatically. If the goods were attached in the debtor’s hands, you must obtain a stay from the enforcement court and provide security (EBL art. 97/3). If the goods were attached in your sole possession (EBL art. 99), the property may not be sold until an action brought in time by the creditor is concluded.
  - Q: If I lose, will I pay damages?
    A: Only if a stay was ordered and the proceedings were in fact stayed. In that case, damages of not less than 20% of the delayed recovery are awarded (EBL art. 97/13), calculated on the lesser of the debt claimed and the value of the attached property. If you win and the objector’s bad faith is established, damages of not less than 15% of the value of the property are awarded against him (EBL art. 97/15).
  - Q: I missed the seven-day period. What happens?
    A: Under EBL art. 96/3 you forfeit the right to raise the ownership claim in the same proceedings. The period is preclusive and examined by the court of its own motion at every stage. The date of awareness is construed broadly — filing a power of attorney in the enforcement file may suffice.

### Negotiable Instruments Law

- [Assumed Burden on a Note Survives a Contradictory Amendment: Turkish Court of Cassation Civil Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/promissory-note-burden-of-proof-amendment-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation unanimously held that, in a negative declaratory action concerning promissory notes allegedly given as an advance, a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bono-bedelsizlik-davasinda-ispat-yukunu-ustlenen-davali-islahla-kurtulamaz
  - Q: Who bears the burden of proof when lack of consideration is alleged against a promissory note?
    A: Under Turkish law, it lies as a rule with the drawer who alleges lack of consideration, because a promissory note contains an acknowledgment of debt that does not depend on its cause. Where the note states the cause of the debt as “goods” or “cash”, the burden of proof falls on the party asserting the contrary of that entry.
  - Q: When is the creditor deemed to have assumed the burden of proof?
    A: Merely denying the debtor’s allegation is not enough. If the creditor, contrary to what appears from the text of the note, explains that the note was given for a different underlying relationship and submits its evidence, it must prove this new fact.
  - Q: Can the defence set out in the statement of defence be changed by amendment?
    A: As a rule, yes; under Turkish law amendment may be made once in the same action (Code of Civil Procedure art. 176). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, however, an amendment wholly contradicting the first defence cannot be a tool for escaping the burden of proof; it breaches the prohibition of contradictory conduct.
  - Q: What must the debtor prove for a promissory note given as an advance?
    A: Where the note does not state the reason for its issue, as a rule the debtor proves that the note was given as an advance for a relationship to be established and that this relationship was not established.
  - Q: Can the amount of a promissory note paid under threat of enforcement be recovered?
    A: Under the general principle recited by the Assembly, a debtor who has paid the debt under threat of compulsory enforcement may bring a recovery action against the creditor for the return of the money paid.
- [Bad (Dishonoured) Cheques and Criminal Liability](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/bad-cheques-criminal-liability): A comprehensive guide to bad cheques and criminal liability: the offence (causing a “dishonoured” notation, Cheque Law No. 5941, Art. 5); the elements (presentation within the legal presentation… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/karsiliksiz-cek-ve-cezai-sorumluluk
  - Q: Is there imprisonment for a bad cheque?
    A: The direct sanction is a judicial fine. However, if the fine is not paid, it is converted directly into imprisonment without a public-service order (Art. 5/11). The perception of “just a fine, no prison” is therefore misleading.
  - Q: How much is the penalty for a bad cheque?
    A: A judicial fine of up to 1,500 days is imposed for each cheque; but the fine cannot be less than the unpaid amount of the cheque. In practice the fine is usually equal to the unpaid amount. A ban on issuing cheques and opening cheque accounts is also ordered.
  - Q: What is the complaint period?
    A: By the Cheque Law’s reference, EBL Art. 347 applies: the complaint right lapses three months after the act is learned of and, in any event, one year after it is committed. The period is a forfeiture period; if missed, the lapse of the complaint right is ordered.
  - Q: If I pay the cheque amount, does the penalty fall away?
    A: Yes. Under effective remorse, if you pay the unpaid amount in full together with commercial default interest running from the legal presentation date, the case is dismissed during the trial; if the conviction has become final, the verdict is set aside with all its consequences. Withdrawal of the complaint has the same effect. Payment must be full and with interest.
  - Q: Who is criminally liable in a company cheque?
    A: If the cheque-account holder is a legal person (company), the liable person is the member of the management organ charged with financial affairs or, absent such a designation, the natural persons forming the management organ. The Court of Cassation holds that liability and the cheque ban apply to the person authorised to represent the company at the date of presentation/offence.
  - Q: Do HAGB or conciliation apply to a bad-cheque offence?
    A: No. For this offence the provisions on advance payment, conciliation and deferment of the announcement of the verdict (HAGB) do not apply (Art. 5/10). It is therefore important to set the defence strategy correctly from the outset and to assess the possibility of effective remorse.
- [Cheque Presentment Periods in Türkiye: 10-Day, 1-Month and 3-Month Rules, Post-Dated Cheques and Recourse](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/cheque-presentment-periods-legal-effect-cheque): A cheque is payable at sight; it must be presented to the bank within ten days if payable where it was issued, within one month if payable elsewhere, and, if issued in another country, within one… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/cekin-ibraz-sureleri-ve-hukmu
  - Q: How long is the presentment period for a cheque?
    A: A cheque payable where it was issued must be presented within ten days, and one payable elsewhere within one month. For a cheque issued in a country other than the country of payment, the period is one month on the same continent and three months on different continents; all periods start on the day after the date of issue written on the cheque (TCC art. 796).
  - Q: What happens if a post-dated cheque is presented early?
    A: Until 31/12/2028, presenting a cheque before the date written on it is invalid (Law No. 5941, provisional art. 3/5). No legal proceedings may be brought on a cheque presented early and not paid; for proceedings, the cheque must be presented within the presentment period calculated from the date on it and subjected to the “no funds” procedure (Law No. 5941 art. 3/8).
  - Q: Can a cheque be collected after the presentment period?
    A: If not countermanded, the bank may still pay it after the period (TCC art. 799). If it is not paid, the holder loses recourse against the drawer, endorsers and avalists; against the drawer, an unjust-enrichment claim remains to the extent of the drawer’s enrichment at the holder’s expense (TCC art. 818/1-m, art. 732).
  - Q: When does the limitation period for a cheque expire?
    A: The holder’s rights of recourse against the drawer, endorsers and other parties are time-barred three years after the end of the presentment period. For a party who paid the cheque to claim against the others, the three years run from payment or from the date an action was brought against that party (TCC art. 814).
  - Q: What should be done about a dishonoured cheque?
    A: The cheque must be presented to the bank in time and its non-payment certified by the bank’s dated declaration on the cheque (the “no funds” procedure), a protest, or a clearing house’s dated declaration (TCC art. 808). Enforcement proceedings specific to negotiable instruments may then be initiated against the drawer, endorsers and avalists (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 167); criminal proceedings are a separate route that depends on the holder’s complaint.
- [Cheques Not Presented in Time and Unjust Enrichment: Lost Recourse, Art. 732 Claim, Time Limits in Türkiye](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/cheque-not-presented-in-time-unjust-enrichment): If a cheque is not presented in time or its dishonour is not duly certified, the holder loses recourse against all parties including the drawer, and bill enforcement is annulled by the enforcement… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ibraz-suresi-gecen-cekte-haklar-ve-sebepsiz-zenginlesme
  - Q: Can enforcement proceedings be brought on a cheque not presented to the bank in time?
    A: Not through bill-enforcement proceedings; such proceedings are annulled by the enforcement court upon the debtor’s complaint within five days or upon another timely complaint or objection (Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 168, Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 170/a). The creditor may instead resort to ordinary enforcement or an action based on unjust enrichment or the underlying relationship.
  - Q: Can money be claimed from the drawer of a cheque whose presentment period has expired?
    A: Not under the law of negotiable instruments; the holder loses recourse against the drawer as well. However, the drawer remains liable to the extent that it has been enriched at the holder’s expense, and the holder may bring an unjust enrichment action for that reason (Commercial Code art. 818/1-m, Commercial Code art. 732). If there is a direct underlying relationship with the drawer, a claim based on that relationship may also be brought.
  - Q: Who bears the burden of proof in an action under Commercial Code art. 732?
    A: The drawer, who claims that it has not been unjustly enriched (Commercial Code art. 732). The drawer must prove with documents that it paid the cheque’s value in another way or that the cheque was given without consideration; the Court of Cassation reverses decisions that place the burden on the holder.
  - Q: Within what time must the unjust enrichment action be brought?
    A: Within one year from the day following the date on which the cheque became time-barred (Commercial Code art. 732). Since rights of recourse under a cheque become time-barred three years after the end of the presentment period, the one-year period starts after those three years expire (Commercial Code art. 814).
  - Q: Which court hears this action, and is mediation mandatory?
    A: An action under Commercial Code art. 732 is a commercial case and is heard by the commercial court of first instance (Commercial Code art. 5). In actions for receivables, annulment of objection, negative declaration and restitution whose subject is a sum of money, applying to a mediator before bringing the action is a precondition (Commercial Code art. 5/A).
  - Q: Can I claim against the endorser who gave me the cheque?
    A: Not under the law of negotiable instruments, and no unjust enrichment claim may be asserted against an endorser whose obligation has lapsed (Commercial Code art. 732). If you have an underlying relationship with the endorser, such as a sale, service or loan, you may bring a claim under the general rules based on that relationship.
- [Negative Declaratory Action on Negotiable Instruments in Türkiye: Promissory Notes, Cheques, Bills of Exchange](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/negative-declaratory-action-negotiable-instruments): A comprehensive guide to the negative declaratory action on negotiable instruments (promissory note, cheque, bill of exchange): what the action is and when it is brought (EBL Art. 72); the conditions… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kambiyo-senetlerinde-menfi-tespit
  - Q: What is a negative declaratory action on a negotiable instrument, and when is it brought?
    A: It is the action by which the debtor asks the court to establish that it is not indebted under a cheque/promissory note/bill of exchange. Under Art. 72 of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law it may be brought before enforcement, during enforcement, or after the proceedings have become final.
  - Q: Does bringing the action stay the enforcement?
    A: Not automatically. If the action was filed before enforcement, an injunction staying it may be granted against security of at least 15% of the claim. If filed after enforcement, it cannot be stayed; the debtor may only request, against at least 15% security, that the money in the enforcement office not be paid to the creditor.
  - Q: Who proves want of consideration in a promissory note?
    A: As a rule, the party alleging it — the debtor. If the instrument states value received “in goods” or “in cash” and one party asserts the contrary (talil), the burden shifts to the party asserting the contrary of that statement.
  - Q: Can I prove want of consideration by witness?
    A: As a rule, no. Want of consideration, that the note was given as security, or that it was completed contrary to the agreement must, under the rule of proof by deed, be proven by written evidence; a witness alone is insufficient. Payment receipts, reconciliations and correspondence are critical.
  - Q: I signed the instrument under fraud or duress; what can I do?
    A: An allegation of fraud or duress (defect of will) may be raised in the action, but it must be proven by concrete, definite and sufficient evidence. Forgery of the instrument, or that the signature is not yours, is an absolute defence assertable against everyone.
  - Q: Can I sue after paying the debt? Is there bad-faith compensation?
    A: Yes. If you were compelled to pay without an injunction, the action converts into a recovery action; you may also bring a recovery action within one year of payment. If the enforcement is found to be unjust and in bad faith, the creditor is ordered to pay bad-faith compensation of at least 20% of the claim.
- [Presentment and Enforcement of Post-Dated Cheques in Türkiye: Early Presentment Ban until 31/12/2028](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/presentment-enforcement-requirements-post-dated-cheques): Until 31/12/2028 a post-dated cheque cannot be presented to the bank before its written date; early presentment is invalid, and enforcing an unpaid cheque requires presenting it again within the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ileri-tarihli-cekte-ibraz-ve-takip-sartlari
  - Q: Can a post-dated cheque be handed to the bank before its written date?
    A: Even if handed over, it has no legal effect. Until 31/12/2028, presentment of a cheque to the bank for payment before the issue date written on it is invalid (Law No. 5941, provisional art. 3/5). Since presentment through clearing is equivalent to presentment for payment, the same result applies to a cheque sent to clearing.
  - Q: Can enforcement proceedings be brought on a cheque presented early and dishonoured?
    A: No. No legal action may be brought on a cheque presented before its written date and not paid. Enforcement requires the cheque to be presented again within the statutory presentment period calculated from its written date and certified as dishonoured (Law No. 5941 art. 3/8). Otherwise the bill enforcement is annulled by the enforcement court upon the debtor’s timely complaint or objection.
  - Q: From which date does the presentment period of a post-dated cheque start?
    A: It starts not on the day the cheque was actually delivered but on the day after the issue date written on it (Commercial Code art. 796). It is ten days for a cheque payable at the same place and one month for a cheque payable elsewhere; the starting day is not counted (Commercial Code art. 817).
  - Q: What happens if the last day of the presentment period falls on a Sunday or a public holiday?
    A: The period is extended to include the first business day that follows; intervening holidays are counted in the period (Commercial Code art. 816). Since presentment can only be made on a business day, a cheque dated on a Sunday cannot be presented on that day.
  - Q: What is the presentment period of a cheque issued abroad and payable in Türkiye?
    A: It is one month if the places of issue and payment are on the same continent and three months if they are on different continents; a cheque issued in a European country and payable in a country with a Mediterranean coast is deemed issued on the same continent (Commercial Code art. 796). The effects of a later date and the presentment period are determined by the law of the country of payment, i.e. Turkish law (Commercial Code art. 822).
  - Q: Why is the QR code important when accepting a post-dated cheque?
    A: A QR query reveals the number and amount of the drawer’s cheques not yet presented to banks and its dishonoured cheques in the last five years (Commercial Code art. 780). Registration of the cheque in the system by the payee also ensures that changes of company representatives after registration do not remove the liability of the account-holding company (Law No. 5941 art. 3/10).
- [Promissory Note (Bono) Validity under Turkish Law: Mandatory Elements, Missing Elements and Limitation Periods](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/validity-requirements-promissory-note): A detailed examination of the mandatory elements of a promissory note (bono) under art. 776 of the Turkish Commercial Code, the consequences of missing elements under art. 777, the bill-of-exchange… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bono-senedin-gecerlilik-sartlari
  - Q: What elements must an instrument contain to be treated as a promissory note?
    A: Under art. 776 of the TCC, the instrument must contain the words 'bono' or 'emre yazılı senet,' an unconditional promise to pay a specific sum, the maturity, the place of payment, the payee's name, the date and place of issue, and the maker's signature. Even if the maturity or place elements are missing, the presumptions of art. 777 apply; however, the absence of the note clause, the promise to pay, or the signature deprives the instrument of its character as a promissory note.
  - Q: Is a note invalid if it does not state a maturity date?
    A: No. Under art. 777/2 of the TCC, a note without a stated maturity is treated as payable at sight; it must be presented within one year of its date of issue, but failure to present it in time does not relieve the maker of liability until the limitation period has expired.
  - Q: Does a note given as security lose its character as a negotiable instrument?
    A: No. According to the settled case law of the Court of Cassation, the fact that a note was given as security does not remove its character as a negotiable instrument as long as it contains the mandatory elements of art. 776 of the TCC; objections based on the underlying security relationship can only be proven by documents relating to that relationship (written evidence).
  - Q: What happens if a note lacks the maker's signature?
    A: Under art. 776/1-g of the TCC, the maker's signature is a mandatory element; an instrument without a signature does not have the character of a promissory note, and enforcement by the special attachment procedure for negotiable instruments cannot be based on it.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for claims arising from a promissory note?
    A: Art. 749 of the TCC, applied to promissory notes by reference through art. 778/h, provides that claims against the maker become time-barred after three years from the maturity date, claims of the holder against an endorser after one year, and an endorser's recourse claims against other endorsers or the maker after six months.
  - Q: Is a note invalid if it does not state the date or place of issue?
    A: A note that does not state the place of issue is deemed to have been issued at the place indicated next to the maker's name (art. 777/4), and this omission does not invalidate it. By contrast, the complete absence of a date of issue, according to Court of Cassation case law, deprives the instrument entirely of its character as a promissory note under art. 776/1-f of the TCC.

### Insurance Law

- [Breach of the Duty of Disclosure in Turkish Insurance: Can the Insurer Refuse the Claim? (TCC Arts. 1435–1440)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/insurance-duty-of-disclosure-breach-turkiye): Under Turkish law, if the policyholder fails to disclose material matters, the insurer may withdraw within fifteen days or request an additional premium before the insured event; after the event, the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sigortada-beyan-yukumlulugunun-ihlali-ve-tazminatin-reddi-ttk-1435
  - Q: Can an insurer in Türkiye refuse the whole claim if the duty of disclosure was breached?
    A: As a rule, no; under Turkish law the outcome depends on when the breach was discovered and on the degree of fault. If discovered before the insured event, the insurer may withdraw within fifteen days or ask for an additional premium (Turkish Commercial Code arts. 1439/1, 1440). After the event, negligence leads to a reduction according to its degree where the breach could affect the indemnity or the occurrence of the event. The insurer is released entirely only where an intentional breach is connected with the event that occurred; if there is no connection, the claim is paid in the ratio of the premium paid to the premium that should have been paid (art. 1439/2).
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a life insurance sum paid if a pre-existing illness was not disclosed?
    A: Under Turkish law, it depends on the connection between the undisclosed illness and the death. Under the Code, where the breach is intentional and the undisclosed matter is connected with the insured event, the obligation to pay ceases; without a connection, the sum is paid proportionally by premium ratio (Turkish Commercial Code art. 1439/2). In the Court of Cassation decisions reviewed, an insured who knew of the illness and did not disclose it was treated as at fault to the degree of intent, and a proportional calculation was required where the illness was unconnected with the death. The connection is a medical question; in the case before it, the Assembly of Civil Chambers required it to be determined by a panel report from the Forensic Medicine Institute.
  - Q: What is the time limit for an insurer in Türkiye to withdraw from the contract for breach of the duty of disclosure?
    A: Under Turkish law, withdrawal must be notified to the policyholder by a declaration within fifteen days from the date the insurer learned of the breach (Turkish Commercial Code art. 1440). Instead of withdrawing, the insurer may request an additional premium; if it is not accepted within ten days, the contract is deemed withdrawn from (art. 1439/1). An insurer that already knew the true position cannot withdraw; the policyholder bears the burden of proving this (art. 1438). For an aggravation of risk during the contract, the period for termination or additional premium is one month (art. 1445/1).
  - Q: Under Turkish law, does the duty of disclosure in insurance disappear if no health declaration form was filled in?
    A: In a 2023 decision adopted by majority, the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held under Turkish law that the absence of a completed form does not remove the insured’s duty to disclose health matters. Where the insurer has given a written list of questions, however, the policyholder cannot be held responsible for matters outside the list, unless an important matter was concealed in bad faith (Turkish Commercial Code art. 1436/1). Who filled in the form, how, and whether it was signed are assessed separately in each case.
  - Q: Can a claim refused for breach of the duty of disclosure be taken to the Insurance Arbitration Commission in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, yes, if the insurer is a member of the insurance arbitration system; no special clause in the contract is needed (Insurance Law No. 5684 art. 30/1). The claimant must first have applied to the insurer and documented a full or partial refusal; the insurer’s failure to answer in writing within fifteen working days is also sufficient (art. 30/13). A dispute already before a court or a consumer arbitration committee cannot be brought to the Commission (art. 30/14). Objection and appeal against arbitral awards depend on monetary thresholds set in the Law and subject to updating (arts. 30/12, 30/22).
- [Motor Own-Damage (Kasko) Insurance Disputes in Türkiye: Exclusions, Alcohol, Total Loss, Recourse, Limitation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/motor-own-damage-insurance-disputes): Motor own-damage (kasko) insurance disputes under Turkish law: the scope of cover and the insurer’s burden of proving exclusions (Commercial Code art. 1409), allegations of intent and bad faith… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kasko-sigorta-uyusmazligi
  - Q: What losses does kasko insurance cover?
    A: Cover is determined by the policy and the General Conditions of Kasko Insurance. Under article A/1 of the General Conditions as set out in Court of Cassation decisions, property damage to the vehicle caused by accidents such as impact, collision, overturning, falling or rolling, by malicious acts of third parties, and by fire, theft or attempted theft is covered. The insurer must prove that a loss falls outside the cover (Commercial Code art. 1409).
  - Q: Does kasko pay if the driver was under the influence of alcohol?
    A: Alcohol alone is not enough. According to the 4th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, for the loss to be excluded the accident must have occurred exclusively as a result of the alcohol, and the insurer must prove this; no exclusivity report is required for a driver who is wholly at fault and has 1.00 promille or more of alcohol. In a case where the other driver was primarily at fault, the loss was held to be covered.
  - Q: When must the kasko insurer pay the indemnity?
    A: The indemnity falls due once the documents have been delivered and the investigations completed, and in any event forty-five days after the notification of the loss; if the investigations are not completed within three months, the insurer pays an advance of at least fifty per cent. Once the indemnity is due, the insurer is in default without the need for a notice (Commercial Code art. 1427).
  - Q: Does kasko cover the diminished value of my vehicle?
    A: This depends on the policy conditions. The Court of Cassation held, in a file where the special conditions of the policy expressly excluded all diminished value, that the insurer was not liable for it. Diminished value may separately be claimed from the operator and the compulsory traffic insurer of the vehicle that caused the accident.
  - Q: Where should a kasko dispute be taken?
    A: A written application must first be made to the insurer. If the request is rejected or not answered within fifteen working days, an application may be made to the Insurance Arbitration Commission (Insurance Law art. 30). The courts are also available: the consumer court has jurisdiction if the insured is a consumer, and otherwise the commercial court of first instance; the rules on mediation before suing and, for consumer disputes below the set threshold, on the consumer arbitration committee must be observed.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a kasko indemnity?
    A: Claims arising from the insurance contract are time-barred two years from the date on which the claim fell due, and claims for the indemnity are in any event time-barred six years from the date on which the risk occurred (Commercial Code art. 1420). The Court of Cassation holds that the extended criminal limitation period does not apply to kasko claims.
- [Recourse in Turkish Traffic Insurance: Claims Against Insured and Driver, Exclusive Causation, Limitation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/recourse-traffic-insurance): Recourse in Turkish compulsory motor third-party liability (traffic) insurance: the paying insurer’s claim against the policyholder under Highway Traffic Law art. 95, matters outside cover (art. 92)… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/trafik-sigortasinda-rucu
  - Q: When can a traffic insurer claim back from its insured the compensation it paid?
    A: The insurer cannot raise against the injured party circumstances arising from the contract that remove or reduce the compensation, and it pays; after payment it may claim against the policyholder to the extent that it could have obtained the removal or reduction of the compensation (Highway Traffic Law art. 95). The recourse grounds are listed in clause B.4 of the General Conditions (General Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/1713, K. 2021/1042); in a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, the lower courts applied the version of the General Conditions in force on the policy date (4th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/5871, K. 2024/10214). An intoxicated or unlicensed driver and unlicensed passenger carriage are among these grounds.
  - Q: If the driver was intoxicated, can the insurer always claim recourse?
    A: No. According to the General Assembly of Civil Chambers, recourse requires that the accident occurred solely (exclusively) under the influence of alcohol, without any other contributing factor; the burden of proof is on the insurer and the blood-alcohol level alone does not decide the outcome. A report from an expert panel including a neurologist and a traffic expert was required for this determination (General Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2013/626, K. 2014/146).
  - Q: In recourse for an unlicensed driver, must it also be proven that the accident resulted from the lack of a licence?
    A: In the decisions examined here, unlike the alcohol ground, the Court of Cassation did not require exclusivity for unlicensed driving. The General Assembly of Civil Chambers recognised the insurer’s right of recourse for an accident caused with a vehicle driven by a certificate holder who had not obtained a driving licence (General Assembly of Civil Chambers, E. 2017/1713, K. 2021/1042). The recoverable amount is, however, limited to the driver’s fault ratio and the injured party’s actual loss (4th Civil Chamber, E. 2021/26622, K. 2024/2700).
  - Q: Is leaving the scene of the accident a recourse ground in itself?
    A: The former 17th Civil Chamber held that, for an insured who later gave a statement to the police admitting fault, merely not being present at the scene does not give the insurer a right of recourse (former 17th Civil Chamber, E. 2016/5264, K. 2019/1876). The General Conditions that entered into force on 01.06.2015 listed leaving the scene other than in cases of necessity, and breaching the duty to have documents such as the accident report and the alcohol report drawn up, as a separate recourse ground; in a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, the lower courts held that this provision could not be applied to a 2006 policy (4th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/5871, K. 2024/10214).
  - Q: Which court hears a recourse action?
    A: Where the insured qualifies as a consumer under Law No. 6502, the Court of Cassation found it correct that the insurer’s recourse action was heard by a consumer court (4th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/5871, K. 2024/10214). If the insured is not a consumer, jurisdiction is determined under the general rules. For a claim against a driver who is not a party to the policy, the Court of Cassation based the driver’s liability on tort and did not treat the relationship as commercial (4th Civil Chamber, E. 2025/10883, K. 2025/15619).
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a recourse action?
    A: Under Highway Traffic Law art. 109/4, the rights of recourse of persons liable to pay compensation in motor vehicle accidents against one another are time-barred two years from the day on which they fully performed their own obligations and learned of the person against whom recourse is to be had. In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation, it was accepted that, in an insurer’s recourse action against its insured, the period starts with payment to the injured party, and the request to apply the extended criminal limitation period did not affect the outcome (4th Civil Chamber, E. 2022/12393, K. 2025/8608).
- [Workplace Fire and the Insurer's Liability](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/workplace-fire-insurers-liability): Fire-insurance indemnity after a fire at a workplace: the conditions of the insurer's liability and the scope of cover (Fire Insurance General Conditions); the burden of proving an exclusion lies… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-21; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/isyeri-yangini-ve-sigortacinin-sorumlulugu
  - Q: The insurer refuses to pay, saying the loss is “excluded”; what can I do?
    A: The burden of proving an exclusion lies with the insurer (TCC Art. 1409/2). A risk not stated as excluded in the policy is, as a rule, within the cover. If the insurer cannot prove the exclusion, it must pay; if the refusal is unjustified, you may sue or go to Insurance Arbitration.
  - Q: I notified the insurer of the fire late; do I lose my indemnity entirely?
    A: No. Late or deficient notice is a ground for a proportionate reduction according to the degree of fault, and only if it increased the indemnity payable; it does not wholly extinguish the indemnity. If the insurer had already actually learned of the fire, it cannot benefit from this reduction (TCC Art. 1446).
  - Q: The insurer claims I started the fire; who must prove it?
    A: The insured's intent is an exclusion that removes the cover, and the burden of proving it lies with the insurer; unless intent is proven, the insurer must pay. Fault (negligence) falling short of intent does not, unless otherwise agreed, remove the cover (TCC Art. 1429). Nor can direct fault be imputed to the insured where the cause is one to which no fault can be attributed, such as electrical/short-circuit.
  - Q: I had declared a low stock value; will I receive full indemnity?
    A: If the sum insured is below the real value, there is under-insurance and, absent a contrary agreement, the insurer pays the loss in proportion of the sum insured to the insured value. It is therefore important to set the sum insured in line with the real value.
  - Q: My workplace closed because of the fire; can I claim turnover/profit loss?
    A: If business-interruption (loss-of-profit) cover was added to the policy, where the activity stops beyond the period stated in the policy, this indemnity is also paid, subject to the policy's conditions and limits.
  - Q: By when must I file the indemnity action?
    A: Claims arising from the insurance contract are subject to a two-year limitation; claims for the insurance indemnity are in any event subject to a six-year limitation from the date the risk (the fire) materialised (TCC Art. 1420).

### Inheritance Law

- [Annulment of a Will in Türkiye: Requirements, Formal Rules and Court of Cassation 7th Civil Chamber Precedents](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-annulment-will): An action for annulment of a will seeks to set aside a testamentary disposition that reflects the decedent's final wishes where it is tainted by incapacity, a defect of intent, illegality or… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/vasiyetnamenin-iptali
  - Q: On what grounds may a will be annulled?
    A: Article 557 lists four exhaustive grounds: testamentary incapacity; a defect of intent from mistake, fraud, duress or undue influence; unlawful or immoral content or burdens; and non-compliance with the statutory form.
  - Q: Who may bring an action to annul a will?
    A: Any heir with a legal interest in the annulment (a reserved share is not required) or any legatee. The defendant is the person or persons who benefited under the will.
  - Q: Within what time must the action be brought?
    A: One year from when the claimant learns of the will, the ground for annulment and his own entitlement; in any event ten years (good-faith defendant) or twenty years (bad-faith defendant) from the will's opening or the succession's opening. Invalidity may still be raised at any time as a defence.
  - Q: Which court and Court of Cassation chamber hear the case?
    A: The civil court of first instance at the decedent's last domicile. Since the Grand General Assembly's decision of 26 June 2025 (No. 2025/1), jurisdiction lies with the 7th Civil Chamber; files combined with a title-annulment/simulated-transaction claim may still reach the 1st Civil Chamber.
  - Q: What makes a holographic will valid?
    A: It must state the year, month and day, and be written and signed entirely in the decedent's own hand. Disputes over authorship are resolved by a Forensic Medicine Institute examination.
  - Q: What is the difference between annulment and abatement?
    A: Annulment sets aside a defective will in whole or part and may be sought by any interested heir. Abatement reduces only the portion of a valid disposition that exceeds the reserved share, and may be sought only by heirs entitled to that share.
- [Certificate of Inheritance and Disclaimer in Türkiye: Notary or Court, Three-Month Period, Estate Debts](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/certificate-of-inheritance-and-disclaimer): How a certificate of inheritance is obtained from a notary or a civil court of peace, the cases in which a notary cannot issue it and the action for its annulment (Civil Code art. 598, Notaries Law… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/mirascilik-belgesi-ve-mirasin-reddi
  - Q: Where is a certificate of inheritance obtained?
    A: Statutory heirs may obtain it from a notary or from the civil court of peace (Civil Code art. 598). However, if issuing the certificate requires adjudication, if the civil registry records are insufficient or if the certificate is requested by foreigners, a notary cannot issue it and the application is made to the civil court of peace (art. 71/B of Law No. 1512). For heirs appointed by will and legatees, the certificate is issued by the civil court of peace.
  - Q: What can be done if the certificate of inheritance is wrong?
    A: The invalidity of a certificate of inheritance may be asserted at any time (Civil Code art. 598). According to the Court of Cassation, the certificate does not constitute res judicata in the substantive sense, but in an annulment action conditions such as legal interest and res judicata are observed and everyone shown as an heir in the certificate must be a party. Persons whose interests are infringed may object to a certificate issued by a notary before the civil court of peace.
  - Q: How long do I have to disclaim an inheritance?
    A: An inheritance may be disclaimed within three months. For statutory heirs the period starts, as a rule, on the date on which the death became known or, if it is proven that the heir learned later that he or she was an heir, from that date; for heirs appointed by will it starts on the date of official notification of the disposition (Civil Code art. 606). The period is peremptory; where there are important reasons, the judge of the civil court of peace may extend it (Civil Code art. 615).
  - Q: How is an inheritance disclaimed?
    A: The disclaimer is made by an oral or written declaration to the civil court of peace and must be unconditional (Civil Code art. 609). This actual disclaimer is not directed against the creditors. Once the declaration reaches the court, it cannot be withdrawn unilaterally.
  - Q: Can I still avoid the debts if the three months have passed?
    A: If it can be proven that the estate was insolvent at the date of death, a deemed disclaimer may be sought (Civil Code art. 605/2).
