According to the Court of Cassation, this claim is not subject to a time limit and is raised in an action against the creditors; however, an heir who has intermeddled with the estate, concealed estate assets or appropriated them cannot disclaim (Civil Code art. 610).
  - Q: If I disclaim the inheritance, do my children become heirs?
    A: If one heir disclaims, his or her share passes to those entitled as if he or she had not been alive when the succession opened (Civil Code art. 611); a share disclaimed by one of the deceased’s children therefore passes, as a rule, to that child’s descendants (Civil Code art. 495), and their period for disclaimer runs from the date on which they learned that the earlier heirs had disclaimed. If all the descendants disclaim, their shares pass to the surviving spouse (Civil Code art. 613). If all of the nearest statutory heirs disclaim, the estate is liquidated by the civil court of peace under the bankruptcy rules and, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, does not pass to the heirs of the next class (Civil Code art. 612).
- [Decedent's Simulated Transactions (Muris Muvazaasi): Title Annulment in Turkish Court of Cassation Case-Law](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/decedents-simulated-transactions): Muris muvazaasi (concealing assets from heirs) occurs where a decedent transfers real property that is in truth a gift, disguising it as a sale or a care-until-death contract. This article explains… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-02; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/muris-muvazaasi-mirastan-mal-kacirma
  - Q: Who may bring a muris muvazaasi action?
    A: Every heir whose inheritance right is infringed, whether or not entitled to a reserved share. The right of action arises upon the decedent's death; no action lies during the decedent's lifetime.
  - Q: Is the action subject to a limitation period?
    A: No. It may be brought at any time; transactions dating from 1957 and 1970 were litigated in the reviewed decisions. An alternative abatement (tenkis) claim, however, is subject to statutory periods.
  - Q: Which transactions fall within the action?
    A: The decedent's transfer of registered land disguised as a sale or care-until-death contract. Cooperative share transfers and cases where the decedent paid the price but title was registered directly in another's name fall outside; there, abatement may be available.
  - Q: By what criteria is simulation established?
    A: Local customs, the ordinary course of events, whether the decedent had a just and reasonable ground to sell, the transferee's means, the gap between price and true value, and the personal relationship between the parties — provable by any evidence, including witnesses.
  - Q: Is a transfer made in return for care simulated?
    A: The price need not be money; services or labour suffice. Where the transfer was made out of gratitude in return for care rather than to defraud heirs, the action is dismissed. However, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, a child’s ordinary care for a parent is a moral duty; care counts as consideration only where special attention beyond ordinary care was needed.
  - Q: How long does the case take?
    A: In the reviewed decisions, files passing through the current three-tier system were completed in roughly 2.5–4 years, while older files involving reversals stretched to about 9 years from the transaction to the final decision.
- [Foreign Heirs and Property in Türkiye: Inheritance Certificate, Land Registry Transfer, Acquisition Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/inheritance-of-property-in-turkiye-by-foreign-heirs): Under Turkish law, how a foreign heir inherits real estate in Türkiye: Turkish law under art. 20 of Law No. 5718, the certificate of inheritance from the civil court of peace, the country and area… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-14. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yabanci-uyruklu-mirasci-tapu-intikali-ve-edinim-sinirlamalari
  - Q: Can a foreign heir inherit real estate in Türkiye under Turkish law?
    A: Yes, if the conditions of art. 35 of the Land Registry Law are met. Under the current text, the heir must be a national of a country designated by the President, and the limits of ten per cent of the district’s area and thirty hectares per person must not be exceeded. According to the text of the article, immovable property inherited outside these restrictions is, if not liquidated by its owner within the period granted, liquidated and converted into money, and the proceeds are paid to the person entitled.
  - Q: Can a foreign heir obtain a certificate of inheritance from a notary in Türkiye?
    A: No. Under art. 71/B of the Notaries Law, a notary may not issue a certificate of inheritance where it is requested by foreigners. The application is made to the civil court of peace; according to the Court of Cassation, naming the Treasury as a party in such proceedings is not compulsory.
  - Q: Can a certificate of inheritance issued abroad be used for a land registry transfer in Türkiye?
    A: Not on its own. Under art. 37 of the Land Registry Law, transfers for foreigners are based on certificates of inheritance issued by Turkish courts, or issued by foreign authorities and confirmed by a Turkish court as conforming to Turkish law. The Court of Cassation also considers a certificate from a Turkish court mandatory for immovables.
  - Q: Does a foreign heir lose the inheritance share in real estate in Türkiye if the acquisition conditions are not met?
    A: No. Under the last paragraph of art. 35 of the Land Registry Law, immovable property inherited outside the restrictions is, if not liquidated by its owner within a period of no more than one year to be granted, liquidated and converted into money, and the proceeds are paid to the person entitled. The reasoning of a regional court of appeal upheld by the 7th Civil Chamber in a 2023 decision also provides, for a death under the pre-2012 text, that the certificate should state the foreign heirs’ entitlement to the liquidation proceeds.
  - Q: Does a foreign heir who inherits real estate in Türkiye pay inheritance and transfer tax under Turkish law?
    A: The transfer by inheritance of real estate in Türkiye is subject to the tax (Law No. 7338, art. 1); the heir’s nationality does not change this. Whether any tax is actually payable, and how much, is determined under the other provisions of the Law. The exception in the Law concerns a foreigner without a domicile in Türkiye who acquires a Turkish citizen’s property located outside Türkiye.
- [How the Turkish Inheritance Law System Works: Heirs, Spouse’s Share, Reserved Shares, Wills, Division](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/inheritance-law-system): Under Turkish inheritance law the succession opens on death and the estate passes to the heirs as a whole by operation of law; absent a will, the descendants, the parents’ order and the grandparents’… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/turk-miras-hukuku-sistemi
  - Q: How is an inheritance divided?
    A: If there is no will, the estate goes, in the order of the parentelic system, first to the descendants, then to the parents and their descendants, then to the grandparents and their descendants; the surviving spouse takes a separate share alongside every order (Civil Code arts. 495-499). Until division the heirs own the estate jointly, and each heir may ask the civil court of peace for division (Civil Code art. 642).
  - Q: How much does a spouse inherit?
    A: The surviving spouse takes one quarter of the estate alongside the descendants, one half alongside the parents’ order, three quarters alongside the grandparents and their children, and the whole estate if none of these exist (Civil Code art. 499).
  - Q: Do siblings inherit?
    A: Yes, in the second order: if there are no descendants and a parent died before the deceased, the siblings take that parent’s share (Civil Code art. 496); if there is a spouse, this order receives one half of the estate. Since siblings have no reserved share, they can be excluded entirely by will (Civil Code arts. 505-506).
  - Q: What is the reserved share and who has one?
    A: The reserved share is the part of the statutory share guaranteed by law to descendants, parents and the surviving spouse: one half of the statutory share for descendants, one quarter for each parent, and for the spouse the whole statutory share alongside descendants or the parents’ order and three quarters in other cases (Civil Code art. 506).
  - Q: Can an heir be completely excluded from the inheritance by will?
    A: An heir with a reserved share cannot, as a rule, be excluded; an heir whose reserved share is infringed may sue for abatement (Civil Code art. 560). Complete exclusion is possible only by disinheritance based on a statutory ground stated in the disposition (Civil Code arts. 510-512).
- [Lifelong Care Contracts in Türkiye (TCO Arts. 611–618): Form, Duty of Care, Termination, Simulation, Abatement](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/lifelong-care-contract-turkiye-form-simulation-abatement): Under Turkish law, a lifelong care contract is invalid unless it is made in the form of a contract of inheritance, before an official and two witnesses. The article explains the duty of care, the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/olunceye-kadar-bakma-sozlesmesi-sekil-muvazaa-ve-tenkis
  - Q: Is a lifelong care contract valid in Türkiye if it is not made before a notary with two witnesses?
    A: As a rule, no. Under art. 612 of the Turkish Code of Obligations, a lifelong care contract is invalid unless it is made in the form of a contract of inheritance, even if it does not appoint an heir; art. 545 of the Turkish Civil Code describes that form as the form of an official will and requires the parties to sign before the official and two witnesses. The only exception is a contract made by a state-recognised care institution in compliance with the conditions set by the competent authorities; written form is then sufficient.
  - Q: How is a lifelong care contract terminated under Turkish law if the caregiver does not provide care?
    A: If a breach of the duty of care makes the continuation of the contract unbearable, either party may terminate it without notice; the party at fault returns what it received and pays appropriate compensation (Code of Obligations art. 617). Instead of termination, the judge may end their life in a common household and award the care recipient a life annuity. The 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation treats the right to terminate for failure to provide care as a right the care recipient must exercise during his or her lifetime.
  - Q: Can heirs in Türkiye challenge a lifelong care contract after the care recipient's death by claiming that no care was provided?
    A: According to the cited decision of the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, in an action for cancellation of title and registration brought by the caregiver against the heirs, the heirs' defence that the duty of care was not performed is not heard, because the right of termination must be exercised by the care recipient during his or her lifetime. This does not prevent heirs from relying on other grounds such as simulation or a defect of form.
  - Q: How do heirs in Türkiye prove that a lifelong care contract was a simulated transaction by the deceased?
    A: The burden of proof lies on the heir alleging simulation; since heirs are third parties to the contract, they may rely on any evidence, including witnesses. The Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation requires an examination of whether the deceased had a justified and reasonable ground for the contract, his or her relations with the caregiver and the other heirs, age, health and the proportion of the transferred property to the whole estate. If the intention to deprive heirs is not proven, the Unification Decision of 01.04.1974 does not apply.
  - Q: Can heirs in Türkiye bring an abatement action for real estate transferred under a lifelong care contract?
    A: Art. 615/3 of the Turkish Code of Obligations expressly reserves the heirs' right to bring an abatement action against a lifelong care contract. In practice heirs usually ask first for cancellation of title on the ground of the deceased's simulated transaction and, failing that, for abatement. A principle of the Court of Cassation on how far abatement applies to this contract for consideration has not been verified in this article; the 7th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation upheld, with general reasoning, a regional court of appeal decision holding that the contract is not subject to abatement because it is for consideration. The view that the value exceeding the care provided may be subject to abatement is this article's own inference.
- [Turkish Abatement (Tenkis) Actions: Reserved Shares, Calculation, Cassation 7th Civil Chamber Precedents](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/abatement-actions): An abatement (tenkis) action is a formative action that reduces, to the disposable limit, only the portion of a decedent's testamentary or lifetime gift that infringes the reserved shares of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tenkis-davasi
  - Q: What is an abatement action and who may bring it?
    A: It reduces, to the extent of the infringement of the reserved share, testamentary or lifetime gifts exceeding the disposable portion. Only heirs whose reserved share is infringed — descendants, parents, and the surviving spouse — may sue.
  - Q: What are the reserved-share ratios?
    A: One-half of the statutory share for descendants; one-quarter for each parent; for the surviving spouse, the whole statutory share when inheriting with other classes, otherwise three-quarters.
  - Q: Which gifts are subject to abatement?
    A: Every testamentary gift exceeding the disposable portion. Lifetime gifts only where they fall within Article 565 — for instance a disguised gift plainly made to nullify the reserved-share rules.
  - Q: How does abatement differ from a muris muvazaasi action?
    A: The 1974 Unification Decision applies only to the decedent's transfer of his own registered land. Where the decedent paid the price but title was registered directly in a relative's name, that is a disguised gift, to which only abatement — not the Unification Decision — may apply, if its conditions are met.
  - Q: Within what time must an abatement action be brought?
    A: One year from learning that the reserved share was infringed; in any event ten years from the will's opening or, for other dispositions, from succession's opening. A plea of abatement may still be raised at any time as a defence.
  - Q: How is the abatement calculation performed?
    A: The net estate (assets less debts and expenses) is first determined, then increased by the lifetime gifts subject to abatement to yield the notional estate. Abatement proceeds first from testamentary dispositions, then, if needed, from lifetime gifts from the most recent backward; for an indivisible asset the beneficiary exercises an election.
- [Turkish Inheritance and Transfer Tax for Heirs Abroad: Filing, Additional Periods, Instalments, Tax Clearance](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/turkish-inheritance-tax-heirs-living-abroad): Under Turkish law, inheritance and transfer tax for heirs living abroad: the four-, six- and eight-month filing periods under art. 9 of Law No. 7338, the additional periods under art. 342 of the Tax… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disindaki-mirascinin-veraset-ve-intikal-vergisi
  - Q: I live abroad and my father died in Türkiye; what is my filing period?
    A: If the death occurred in Türkiye and you are in a foreign country, the period is six months from the date of death (Law No. 7338, art. 9/1-a). For heirs in Türkiye it is four months.
  - Q: How do the periods work if the death occurred abroad?
    A: Six months for heirs in Türkiye, four months for heirs in the same country as the deceased, and eight months for heirs in another foreign country (art. 9/1-b).
  - Q: I missed the filing period; will a penalty be imposed immediately?
    A: No. The administration waits fifteen days after the period ends, then grants a further fifteen-day period by notification. If the return is filed within these periods, no tax loss is deemed to have occurred. The Council of State holds that a penalty assessment made without the second period is unlawful (Tax Procedure Law art. 342).
  - Q: Can the property be registered in our names before the tax is paid?
    A: Yes; registration in the heirs’ names is made without waiting for the assessment. However, if the immovable is to be sold or a right in rem is to be created over it, the tax attributable to it must be paid in full or security provided (art. 19).
  - Q: Is the tax paid in one go?
    A: No. The tax is paid over three years from assessment, in six equal instalments in May and November each year (art. 19).

### Health Law

- [Lack of Informed Consent in Cosmetic Surgery in Türkiye: Surgeon's Liability, Proof, Limitation (TBK Art. 147)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/informed-consent-cosmetic-surgery-liability-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a patient must be informed of the risks, complications and alternatives before a medical intervention; the Court of Cassation's 3rd Civil Chamber places the burden of proving this… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/aydinlatilmis-onam-eksikligi-ve-estetik-ameliyatta-hekimin-sorumlulugu
  - Q: Under Turkish law, who must prove that the patient was properly informed when informed consent is disputed?
    A: In its decision of 09.03.2026, the Court of Cassation’s 3rd Civil Chamber, relying on Law No. 1219 art. 70 and article 26 of the Rules of Medical Professional Ethics, stated that the burden of proof for informed consent lies with the doctor or the hospital, and quashed a dismissal given without allowing the defendant doctors to prove it. The patient, as a rule, must still prove the breach of contract, the damage and the causal link; the Assembly of Civil Chambers stressed that the presumption of fault concerns fault only.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a consent form signed by the patient sufficient on its own for informed consent?
    A: As a rule, no. Under art. 26 of the Patient Rights Regulation, the consent form must contain the information listed in art. 15 (such as complications, alternatives and the consequences of refusal), and that information must also be conveyed to the patient orally. Whether the form contains this information and whether it was actually explained is assessed in each case; the existence of a signed form does not by itself end the dispute over disclosure (this is the article’s own inference).
  - Q: Is cosmetic surgery treated as a contract for work or as a contract of mandate in Turkish Court of Cassation decisions?
    A: Surgery performed for an aesthetic purpose (to achieve a better appearance) was classified as a contract for work in the decisions of the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers of 02.11.2022 and of the 6th Civil Chamber of 25.06.2025; the contractor (physician or hospital) was held bound to achieve the agreed result, and disclosure and management of complications were also treated as the contractor’s responsibility. Where the operation is for treatment (for example nose surgery for a breathing problem), the relationship was treated as a mandate both in a regional court decision and in the reasoning of the 3rd Civil Chamber upholding it.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a damages claim arising from cosmetic surgery in Türkiye?
    A: Claims arising from a contract for work are, as a rule, subject to a five-year limitation period; however, the case where the contractor fails to perform its obligations at all or properly through gross fault is excluded from that five-year period (TBK art. 147). Unless the law provides otherwise, the general period is ten years (TBK art. 146). If the claim is based on defective performance, the two-year periods in TBK art. 478 and Law No. 6502 art. 16 may also apply. Tort-based claims are subject to two- and ten-year periods (TBK art. 72). Which period applies depends on the nature of the relationship and the defendant; limitation is considered only if the defendant raises it.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, can a lack of informed consent be raised for the first time on appeal in a damages action?
    A: It is not safe to do so. In its decision of 21.05.2026, the Court of Cassation’s 3rd Civil Chamber treated a lack of consent raised later, without having been pleaded in the statement of claim, as an extension of the claim and dismissed the appeal ground. Although the Assembly of Civil Chambers, in a 2021 decision concerning a different procedural situation, required disclosure to be taken into account, the safest course is to plead the lack of consent expressly in the statement of claim.
- [Malpractice Time Limit Runs from End of Treatment: Turkish Council of State on Five-Year Full-Remedy Period](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/public-hospital-malpractice-claim-five-year-time-limit): Under Turkish law, the 10th Chamber of the Council of State held unanimously, in a full-remedy action brought for a child whose treatment was continuing and where it was alleged that the necessary… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tibbi-hata-tam-yargi-davasinda-bes-yillik-sure-kesin-rapor
  - Q: When does the time limit start in a full-remedy action for medical error at a public hospital?
    A: Under Turkish law, according to the 10th Chamber of the Council of State, for bodily harm the period in Administrative Procedure Law art. 13 starts on the date on which the treatment process is completed and the final health report is given to the person concerned.
  - Q: Is the five-year period always calculated from the date of the event?
    A: Not in this decision. The Chamber stated that calculating the one-year and five-year periods without taking into account the date on which the administrative action was completed and the damage fully emerged would be incompatible with the freedom to seek legal remedies.
  - Q: Does a disability report valid for two years start the time limit?
    A: In this decision, the Chamber treated the report, which was valid for two years and not of a permanent nature, as an indication that treatment was continuing, and quashed the decision based on the ground that the action had been brought after the five-year period. In another case the outcome depends on the nature of the report and the course of treatment.
  - Q: Can an action be brought directly, or must an application first be made to the administration?
    A: For damage caused by administrative actions, Administrative Procedure Law art. 13 requires a prior application to the administration. If the request is rejected or not answered within thirty days, the action is brought within the sixty-day time limit in art. 7.
  - Q: Does this decision mean that the plaintiffs will receive compensation?
    A: No. The decision was quashed only on the time-limit point; the administrative court had found that there was no service fault; the merits of the claim will be examined by the regional administrative court in the appeal review.
- [Medical Error in Turkish Public Hospitals: Full-Remedy Action, Prior Administrative Application, Time Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/medical-error-public-hospital-full-remedy-action-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an action for harm caused by medical treatment in a state or university hospital is as a rule brought against the administration, not the physician, as a full-remedy action before… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kamu-hastanesinde-tibbi-hata-tam-yargi-davasi-ve-sureler
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is an action for medical error in a state hospital brought against the doctor or the administration?
    A: As a rule, against the administration. Under Constitution art. 129/5 and Law No. 657 art. 13, an action for damages arising from a fault committed by a public official while performing the duty is brought against the administration running the service, as a full-remedy action before the administrative courts. In its decision of 25.01.2022 (given with rectification of the decision still available; it may not be final), the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held that in a claim of faulty treatment connected with the duty the proper defendant is the administration, not the physician. According to the same decision this protection does not cover personal fault clearly separable from the duty; in that case the action is heard against the physician before the civil courts.
  - Q: What is the time limit for a full-remedy action for medical error in a public hospital in Türkiye?
    A: An application to the administration comes first: within one year of learning of the act and in any event within five years of the act (Law No. 2577 art. 13). If the administration refuses, the time limit for the action runs from the day following notification of the refusal; if it does not reply within thirty days, from the date on which that period expires. Unless a special law sets a separate period, the time limit is sixty days before the administrative court (Law No. 2577 art. 7).
  - Q: Which court hears a medical error action against a public hospital in Türkiye?
    A: The administrative court. Under Law No. 2577 art. 36/b, if the harm arose from an action of the administration, the competent court is the administrative court of the place where the service was provided or the action was carried out; as a rule this is where the hospital is located (the conclusion this article draws from the text of the provision). If the action is mistakenly brought before the civil courts and dismissed for lack of jurisdiction, an action may be brought before the administrative court within thirty days from the day after that decision becomes final (art. 9), and the prior application to the administration is not required (art. 13/2).
  - Q: Is a medical error action against a public hospital in Türkiye dismissed if the Forensic Medicine Institute report finds no fault?
    A: Not always. In its decision of 14.01.2026 the Council of State 10th Chamber quashed a dismissal based on a report that answered the parties’ allegations without sufficient reasons and required a new report from the Higher Board of Forensic Medicine. In its decision of 06.11.2025 the Council of State Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers likewise concluded, by majority, that the required medical care had not been shown although the reports stated that no certain causal link could be established. These decisions are specific to their facts; quashing does not mean the action will be upheld.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, can the administration recover from the physician the compensation it paid for a medical error?
    A: It can, but the decision is taken by the Professional Liability Board. Under additional art. 18 of Law No. 3359, the Board decides within one year whether recourse is to be had and in what amount, having regard to whether the person concerned abused his or her office by acting contrary to the requirements of the duty and to the degree of fault. For those working in state universities the final decision is taken by the university within six months.
- [The Physician's Liability in Medical Malpractice](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/physicians-liability-medical-malpractice): The physician's civil liability in medical malpractice: the nature of the patient–physician relationship (mandate for a private/independent physician — TCO Art. 502; a public-law relationship in a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-26; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tibbi-malpraktiste-hekimin-sorumlulugu
  - Q: My treatment ended badly; is every adverse outcome malpractice?
    A: No. Adverse outcomes that may arise despite compliance with the rules and requirements of medicine are complications and, as a rule, do not give rise to liability. Liability requires faulty conduct of the physician contrary to the duty of care, a loss, and a causal link between them. Even where there is a complication, the physician's duty to inform beforehand and to manage the process correctly continues.
  - Q: I signed a consent form before the operation; can I still sue?
    A: Yes, you can. Mere “consent” is not enough; the risks, complications and alternatives of the intervention must have been explained to you in a way you can understand. General/standard forms are not valid informed consent if they do not concretely set out the risks specific to the intervention. The burden of proving that disclosure was duly made lies with the physician.
  - Q: I was harmed in a public hospital; whom and where do I sue?
    A: In public hospitals there is no contract between patient and physician; the relationship is public-law. The action is therefore brought not against the physician but against the administration, as a full-remedy action before the administrative judiciary. The administrative application and action time-limits also matter; obtain advice early so as not to miss the deadline.
  - Q: Which court is competent for a private hospital or an aesthetic procedure?
    A: Actions against a private hospital/independent physician mostly fall within the jurisdiction of the consumer court. As aesthetic procedures are treated as a works contract, where the result is not achieved the defect provisions of the works contract (price reduction, remedy, damages) apply.
  - Q: If the physician has compulsory insurance, can I recover from the insurer?
    A: Compulsory Financial Liability Insurance for Medical Malpractice (Law No. 1219, Additional Art. 12) is mandatory for physicians. Where the conditions are met, the loss may be covered from this insurance within the policy limits; disputes in which the insurer is a party are heard, owing to their commercial nature, in a separate judicial branch.
  - Q: How is the existence of fault determined?
    A: Whether the medical practice complied with the rules and whether there was fault is, as a rule, determined by expert examination (panels from the Forensic Medicine Institution and/or relevant specialist departments of universities). Medical records, discharge summaries, images and consent documents are therefore very important as evidence.
- [Türkiye’s “Full-Time Law” for Physicians: Private Practice Restrictions from 1928 to 2026 and Today](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/full-time-law-physicians): The chronological development of Türkiye’s Full-Time Law for physicians: Laws No. 224 and 2162, Law No. 2368, the Full-Time Law No. 5947, the Constitutional Court’s annulment decisions of 2010, 2012… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-25; updated: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tam-gun-yasasi-kronolojik-gelisim-ve-guncel-durum
  - Q: May a specialist with a permanent post at a state hospital open a private clinic after hours?
    A: No. Law No. 657 art. 28 prohibits civil servants from opening clinics and working in private workplaces. Public physicians without managerial duties may act as occupational physicians only with permission and for up to thirty hours a month.
  - Q: May a physician faculty member at a state university work in a private hospital?
    A: As a rule, no. Professors and associate professors may be employed out of hours under an institutional contract of up to one year concluded by the university and within quotas (Law No. 2547 art. 36/7). A personal clinic or individual contract is not covered.
  - Q: What is the position of faculty members who had a clinic before 2014?
    A: Faculty members who were lawfully running a clinic or working in a private institution on 18.01.2014 may continue, because the transitional provision was annulled. According to the Council of State, this protection does not extend to those without a clinic on that date or who became faculty members later.
  - Q: In how many places may a physician working in a private hospital work at the same time?
    A: Since Law No. 7557, in no more than two health institutions, within Ministry workforce planning and within one sub-paragraph each (Law No. 1219 art. 12/3); private-sector work is insured employment.
  - Q: May a clinic physician operate on a patient in a private hospital?
    A: Yes, if the patient pays and nothing is claimed from the SGK. The Private Hospitals Regulation requires a contract with the hospital, a quota of one third of the specialty’s posts and an exception for physicians over 60; case-based permits may be granted where needed.
  - Q: What is the sanction for working in breach of the ban?
    A: Suspension of step advancement for civil-servant physicians (Law No. 657 art. 125), and suspension of step advancement or a multiple salary deduction for academic staff (Law No. 2547 art. 53). A faculty member breaching an institutional contract may not be so employed for one year, or five years on repetition.

### Family & Divorce Law

- [Action for Denial of Paternity in Turkish Law: Presumption, the Mother’s Right, Time Limits, DNA Testing](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/action-denial-paternity): Action for denial of paternity under the Turkish Civil Code (arts. 282-291): the right of action granted to the husband, the mother and the child by Law No. 7531, the presumption of paternity and the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/soybaginin-reddi-davasi
  - Q: Who can bring an action for denial of paternity?
    A: Under art. 286 of the Civil Code, as amended by Law No. 7531, the husband, the mother or the child; the action is directed at the other persons entitled to bring it. If the husband dies, is declared absent or permanently loses the capacity to discern before his time limit expires, the person claiming to be the father and the husband’s descendants, mother or father may also sue (art. 291).
  - Q: Within what time must the action be brought?
    A: The husband must sue within one year of learning of the birth and that he is not the father or that the mother had relations with another man at the time of conception; the mother within one year from the birth; the child within one year from reaching majority. If the delay rests on a justified reason, the period starts when that reason ceases (art. 289). The Court of Cassation treats this period as a peremptory time limit observed by the judge ex officio.
  - Q: Does my suspicion that the child is not mine start the time limit?
    A: No. According to the Court of Cassation, suspicion is not sufficient for learning; where learning occurs through an out-of-court paternity test, the period starts at that moment. The one-year period should therefore not be missed once a private DNA result is known.
  - Q: What happens if the other party does not attend the DNA test?
    A: Under art. 284/2 of the Civil Code the judge may draw an adverse inference against a defendant who refuses the examination, while art. 292 of the Code of Civil Procedure provides that the examination is carried out by force if blood or tissue is refused without a justified reason. The Assembly of Civil Chambers held that a report must be obtained by applying coercive measures, including arrest and compulsory appearance where necessary, against a defendant who avoids testing.
  - Q: Is a DNA report from a private laboratory sufficient for the case?
    A: A private report may be important in fixing the moment of learning, but the Court of Cassation holds that judgment cannot rest solely on a report submitted by the claimant and that a report must be obtained from the Forensic Medicine Institute by referring the persons concerned at the same time.
  - Q: If the defendant accepts the claim, will the court simply grant it?
    A: No. Because descent concerns public order, acceptance does not by itself produce effect; the judge investigates the facts ex officio and orders a genetic examination (Civil Code art. 284; Code of Civil Procedure art. 308/2). A claimant’s waiver, by contrast, led to dismissal of the action in a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation.
- [Breaking Off an Engagement: Material and Non-Pecuniary Damages and the Return of Gifts](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/breaking-off-engagement): Breaking off an engagement under Turkish law: material and non-pecuniary damages and the return of gifts under Civil Code arts. 118-123 — formation of the engagement, no action to compel marriage… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/nisanin-bozulmasi-tazminat-ve-hediyelerin-iadesi
  - Q: Must gold and jewellery given at the engagement be returned when it is broken off?
    A: As a rule, yes. Gold, jewellery and ornaments other than the engagement ring (wedding band) are gifts beyond the customary and may be reclaimed by those who gave them when the engagement ends for a reason other than marriage (TCC art. 122); fault is not required in this action. The engagement ring and items that wear out through wearing and use cannot be reclaimed.
  - Q: Does the party who breaks off the engagement always pay compensation?
    A: No. Material and non-pecuniary damages depend on fault: the party at fault — who breaks off the engagement without just cause or causes it to end for a reason attributable to them — is liable (TCC arts. 120-121). A party who breaks off the engagement for just cause may claim compensation from the other party who caused the break-up. Fault and loss must be proven.
  - Q: Can non-pecuniary damages be claimed for a broken engagement?
    A: Only if personal rights were infringed as a result of the breaking off of the engagement (TCC art. 121). According to the Court of Cassation, the natural distress caused by a broken engagement is not sufficient. Non-pecuniary damages have been accepted where the person’s standing in society was harmed, for example after the invitations had been distributed or when only a very short time remained before the wedding.
  - Q: Can engagement and wedding preparation expenses be recovered?
    A: If the engagement was broken off through the other party’s fault, appropriate compensation may be claimed for expenses incurred with a view to marriage in accordance with good faith and for material sacrifices made (TCC art. 120). Wasted expenses and actual loss must be proven; the price of items that remain with the claimant and can be used by them cannot be claimed in addition. Parents may also claim their own expenses under the same conditions.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for claims arising from a broken engagement?
    A: Rights of action arising from the termination of the engagement become time-barred one year after the termination (TCC art. 123). Limitation is a defence that must be raised by the defendant; the court must establish the date on which the engagement was broken off and decide on the defence as a preliminary issue.
  - Q: Which court hears disputes over a broken engagement?
    A: Claims for damages and for the return of gifts arising from a broken engagement are family-law cases heard by the family court (Law No. 4787 art. 4); where no family court has been established, they are heard by the civil court of first instance designated under the Law. The court of general venue is the court of the defendant’s domicile on the date the action is filed (Code of Civil Procedure art. 6).
- [Collecting Maintenance from Debtors Abroad in Türkiye: Enforcing Foreign Orders, New York, Hague Conventions](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/collecting-maintenance-from-debtor-abroad-turkiye): Under Turkish law, collecting maintenance when creditor and debtor live in different countries: enforcement and execution in Türkiye of a foreign maintenance order under MÖHUK arts. 50 and 54, why… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disindaki-borcludan-nafaka-tahsili
  - Q: My former spouse lives abroad and does not pay maintenance; what can I do?
    A: If the maintenance order was given abroad and the debtor has property or income in Türkiye, you can have the order enforced in Türkiye and start enforcement proceedings (MÖHUK art. 50). If the debtor has income only abroad, collection follows that country’s procedure and international conventions.
  - Q: Can I start enforcement in Türkiye directly on a German maintenance order?
    A: No. An enforcement judgment must first be obtained from a Turkish court. The Court of Cassation has stated that the 1956 New York Convention does not make a foreign maintenance order directly enforceable in Türkiye.
  - Q: Which court hears the enforcement action?
    A: In practice the family court; venue lies at the debtor’s domicile in Türkiye, failing that the place of residence, failing that Ankara, Istanbul or Izmir (MÖHUK art. 51).
  - Q: My child has come of age; can he or she seek enforcement of past maintenance?
    A: Yes. The Court of Cassation has accepted that children who have come of age have a legal interest in enforcing maintenance awarded for their benefit.
  - Q: The debtor says part has been paid; will enforcement be refused?
    A: No. The Court of Cassation affirmed an enforcement judgment, stating that payments made can be assessed during execution; the amount paid is deducted at the execution stage.
- [Contested Divorce and Grounds in Turkish Law: Adultery, Degrading Treatment, Desertion, Breakdown, Separation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/contested-divorce-grounds): Contested divorce under Turkish law: the special grounds (adultery, attempt on life and degrading treatment, criminal offence and dishonourable life, abandonment, mental illness — Civil Code arts.… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/cekismeli-bosanma-ve-bosanma-sebepleri
  - Q: How long does a contested divorce take?
    A: The law sets no fixed duration. It depends on the pleadings stage (two weeks for the reply, two weeks each for the reply to the reply and the rejoinder: Code of Civil Procedure art. 127, Code of Civil Procedure art. 136), the preliminary examination, the collection of witness and other evidence and the appeal stages. The periods for appeal and for appeal on points of law are two weeks each from service (Code of Civil Procedure art. 345, Code of Civil Procedure art. 361).
  - Q: When must a divorce action for adultery be brought?
    A: Within six months of learning of it and in any event within five years of the act (Civil Code art. 161). According to the Court of Cassation, in a continuing relationship the six-month period starts when the last act ends. A spouse who has forgiven has no right of action; if the period has expired and breakdown was claimed in the alternative, the claim is assessed under breakdown of the union (Civil Code art. 166/1).
  - Q: Can messages on my spouse’s phone be used as evidence?
    A: Evidence must be obtained lawfully; unlawfully obtained evidence may not be taken into account in proving a fact (Code of Civil Procedure art. 189/2). The Court of Cassation treated messages obtained by forcibly taking the phone and breaking its password as unlawful and held that the court must consider this of its own motion even if the other party does not object.
  - Q: Can a spouse at fault sue for divorce?
    A: Yes. According to the Court of Cassation, one need not be entirely without fault or less at fault to seek divorce; however, for divorce to be granted, at least some fault on the part of the defendant must be proven. If the claimant’s fault is heavier, the defendant may object; if the objection is an abuse of right and no interest worthy of protection remains in continuing the marriage, divorce may still be granted (Civil Code art. 166/2).
  - Q: How many years of separation are needed for divorce on the ground of de facto separation?
    A: Under Civil Code art. 166/4 as amended by Law No. 7532, if common life has not been re-established within one year of the finality of a decision dismissing an earlier divorce action, divorce is granted at the request of either spouse. Without an earlier dismissal, merely living apart does not trigger this provision.
  - Q: Can compensation and maintenance be awarded where the spouses are equally at fault?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation, no material or non-pecuniary compensation may be awarded to a spouse equally at fault (Civil Code art. 174). Poverty maintenance, however, may be claimed if the claimant’s fault is not heavier and he or she would fall into poverty because of the divorce (Civil Code art. 175).
- [Custody and Contact (Personal Relationship) in Türkiye: Child's Best Interests, Custody Change, Contact Orders](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/custody-personal-relationship): Custody and the child’s personal relationship (contact) under Turkish law: to whom custody belongs (TMK arts. 336, 337), the paramount criterion of the child’s best interests (UNCRC art. 3)… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/velayet-ve-kisisel-iliski
  - Q: Is custody always awarded to the mother in divorce?
    A: No. The decisive factor is the child’s best interests. Because young children are presumed to need maternal care, custody is often awarded to the mother; but this presumption applies only where it does not conflict with the child’s best interests. If the expert report and the evidence show that the child’s interest lies with the father, custody may be awarded to him.
  - Q: At what age is a child’s opinion sought?
    A: The law sets no fixed age; the criterion is that the child be of an ‘age of discernment’. A discerning child must be heard in person by the judge on custody and contact. In practice this generally arises from around the age of eight.
  - Q: What happens if the custodial parent refuses to allow contact?
    A: Persistent, unjustified obstruction of contact is a ground for changing custody, provided this is not contrary to the child’s interest; the court warns the parties of this in its decision. Enforcement of a contact order is carried out through the judicial support and victim services directorates.
  - Q: Can custody be changed?
    A: Yes. A custody decision is not res judicata. If new and serious facts — a parent’s remarriage, relocation or death, or misuse of the custodial function — endanger the child’s welfare, an action to change custody may be brought.
  - Q: Is joint custody possible in Türkiye?
    A: Within marriage custody is already exercised jointly. In divorce the rule is to award custody to one spouse; however, the Court of Cassation also permits joint custody where the parents are willing to cooperate and it serves the child’s best interests. Each case is assessed on its own facts.
  - Q: Can a contact order be changed later?
    A: Yes. Contact orders do not create res judicata; in the child’s interest they may be re-regulated, extended or removed according to changing circumstances.
- [Division of Property in Divorce: The Participation in Acquired Property Regime](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/division-property-divorce): How property is divided in divorce, acquired vs personal property, the participation claim and value-increase share, and which date's value governs — in light of the Turkish Civil Code and Court of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-08; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bosanmada-edinilmis-mallara-katilma-rejimi
  - Q: Can I get a share of the home registered in my spouse's name?
    A: If the home is acquired property obtained during the marriage, you may claim half the surplus value even if it is not registered to you; if you prove a contribution to its acquisition, you may also claim a value-increase share.
  - Q: Must I prove I contributed to the home for the participation claim?
    A: No. The participation claim arises by law; no proof of contribution is needed. Proof of contribution matters only for an additional value-increase share.
  - Q: Do inheritance or wedding gold fall into the division?
    A: Assets from inheritance or gift, and personal-use items, are personal property and as a rule are excluded; their income, however, is acquired property.
  - Q: Which date governs the asset's value?
    A: In the Court of Cassation's practice, the asset's value at the date closest to the judgment (current/market value) governs, so value increases are reflected in the division.
  - Q: Do I lose my right if my spouse sold the home before divorce?
    A: No. Transfers aimed at reducing the participation claim are included in liquidation as if the asset still existed (added value).
  - Q: What is the limitation period?
    A: In the Court of Cassation's practice, ten years, starting as a rule when the divorce judgment becomes final.
- [Divorce in Türkiye for Turkish Citizens Abroad: Competent Court, Applicable Law and Attending the Hearing](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/divorce-in-turkiye-for-turkish-citizens-living-abroad): Under Turkish law, divorce proceedings in Türkiye for Turkish citizens living abroad: the competent court under MÖHUK art. 41, the applicable law under MÖHUK art. 14, the judge's duty to hear the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disinda-yasayanlarin-turkiyede-bosanmasi
  - Q: I live abroad; can I bring divorce proceedings in Türkiye?
    A: Yes. If the action has not been or cannot be brought in your country of residence, it can be brought before the court with territorial jurisdiction in Türkiye or, failing that, at your place of residence, your last domicile, or in Ankara, Istanbul or Izmir (MÖHUK art. 41).
  - Q: Is attending the hearing mandatory in an uncontested divorce?
    A: Yes. Turkish Civil Code art. 166/3 requires the judge to hear the parties in person and be satisfied that they expressed their will freely. Both spouses therefore need to be present at the hearing.
  - Q: We live in Germany; which law will the Turkish court apply?
    A: If both spouses are Turkish citizens, Turkish law applies. If they have different nationalities, the law of their common habitual residence (for example German law) applies, failing which Turkish law (MÖHUK art. 14). Interim measures are always governed by Turkish law.
  - Q: Can I conduct a contested divorce without travelling to Türkiye?
    A: Yes; there is no personal hearing requirement in a contested case. A lawyer can conduct the case under a power of attorney drawn up at a consulate or before a foreign notary and duly certified; witnesses abroad can be heard through letters rogatory.
  - Q: Will a divorce obtained in Türkiye be valid in my country of residence?
    A: The judgment is entered in the Turkish civil register; whether it takes effect in your country of residence depends on that country’s recognition rules. You may need to apply separately to the competent authority there.
- [Foreign Divorce Validity in Türkiye: Administrative Registration at the Consulate and the Recognition Action](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/foreign-divorce-recognition-turkiye-civil-registry): Under Turkish law, a Turkish citizen who divorced abroad can correct the Turkish civil register in two ways: administrative registration at a consulate or civil registry office under art. 27/A of Law… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yurt-disinda-alinan-bosanma-kararinin-turkiyede-taninmasi
  - Q: Is my foreign divorce automatically valid in Türkiye?
    A: No. For the decision to be entered in the Turkish civil register, either administrative registration at a consulate or civil registry office (Law No. 5490, art. 27/A) or recognition by a court (MÖHUK art. 58) is required. Until then you continue to appear as married in Turkish records.
  - Q: What happens if my former spouse will not join the application?
    A: Administrative registration requires two Turkish-citizen spouses to apply together (or through attorneys). If your former spouse will not join, is not a foreign national and has not died, you need to bring a recognition action, in which the statement of claim is served on your former spouse together with the hearing date.
  - Q: Do I have to travel to Türkiye for the recognition action?
    A: No. A lawyer can conduct the case on the basis of a power of attorney drawn up at a consulate or before a notary in your country of residence and duly certified. If you have no domicile in Türkiye, the action is brought at the other party’s domicile in Türkiye or, failing that, in Ankara, Istanbul or Izmir.
  - Q: I divorced years ago; can I still seek recognition?
    A: Yes. Turkish law sets no time limit for recognition. The Court of Cassation accepted the recognition of a divorce that became final in 1991, even though the spouses later lived together.
  - Q: Which documents are needed?
    A: The original of the divorce decision certified by that country’s authorities or a copy certified by the court that gave it, a certified document showing that the decision is final, and certified Turkish translations of both. If documents are missing, the court gives time to complete them.
- [Increasing and Decreasing Maintenance: Conditions, PPI-Rate Increase and Removal of Alimony](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/increasing-decreasing-maintenance): Increasing and decreasing maintenance under Turkish law: the legal basis (TMK art. 176/4 — financial change or equity), the PPI-rate increase and balance principle absent an extraordinary change… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/nafakanin-artirilmasi-ve-azaltilmasi
  - Q: How many years must I wait to increase maintenance?
    A: There is no waiting period. An increase action may be brought at any time; each is assessed on the parties’ circumstances as at the filing date. In practice most awards already provide for an automatic annual PPI-rate increase.
  - Q: Does maintenance increase automatically each year?
    A: If the original decision ordered an annual increase at the TurkStat PPI rate, it applies automatically and no further action is needed. Absent such a clause, an increase action must be brought.
  - Q: I lost my job — can I reduce the maintenance?
    A: If you prove an extraordinary and lasting fall in your income, you may seek a decrease on the ground of changed financial circumstances. The court reassesses the creditor’s needs against your means and sets an equitable amount.
  - Q: My ex-spouse lives with someone — can I remove the poverty maintenance?
    A: The creditor living as if married without marrying is a ground for removal. But the Court of Cassation requires concrete, convincing proof that a common life just like marriage has been established; occasional meetings or friendship alone are not enough.
  - Q: Can child support be increased as the child grows?
    A: Yes. As the child’s age and needs (especially education costs) grow, an increase in child support may be sought. The judge weighs the child’s needs against the parents’ means.
  - Q: Can I take a maintenance decision to cassation?
    A: Not always. If the annual increase/decrease brought to cassation is below the finality threshold set in the HMK, the appellate decision is final and cassation is closed. Amounts above the threshold may be taken to cassation.
- [Recognition and Enforcement of Foreign Court Judgments](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/recognition-enforcement-foreign-court-judgments): A foreign judgment (e.g. a divorce) has no automatic effect in Türkiye; enforcement requires tenfiz, and conclusive-evidence effect requires recognition. Conditions, reciprocity and the révision bar… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-16; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yabanci-mahkeme-kararlarinin-taninmasi-ve-tenfizi
  - Q: I divorced abroad; must I sue again in Türkiye?
    A: No. To make a foreign divorce effective and register it, a recognition action is, as a rule, sufficient; a fresh divorce action is not needed. If the statutory conditions are met and the parties apply together (or, where one has died or is a foreign national, the Turkish-citizen party alone), the decision can also be registered by a Turkish mission abroad or a civil registry office without a court action (Population Services Act art. 27/A).
  - Q: What is the difference between recognition and enforcement?
    A: Recognition gives the judgment conclusive-evidence/res judicata effect (e.g. registering a divorce). Enforcement allows it to be executed in Türkiye (e.g. collecting maintenance or damages).
  - Q: Is reciprocity always required?
    A: No. Reciprocity is required only for enforcement, not for recognition (Art. 58).
  - Q: Does the court examine whether the foreign judgment is just?
    A: No. The enforcement judge cannot review the merits/substantive correctness; only the enforcement conditions are checked.
  - Q: Which documents are needed?
    A: The certified original of the foreign judgment, the finality annotation, a sworn translation and, within applicable treaties, an apostille.
  - Q: Which court hears the action?
    A: As a rule the civil court of first instance; for family-law judgments such as divorce, the family court.
- [Removal and Protection Orders under Law No. 6284 in Türkiye: Objection and Coercive Detention (Arts. 9, 13)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/law-6284-removal-order-objection-coercive-detention): Under Turkish law, preventive measures such as removal from the home under Law No. 6284 are issued initially for at most six months and may be extended; an objection lies to the family court within… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/6284-sayili-kanun-koruma-ve-uzaklastirma-karari-itiraz-ve-zorlama-hapsi
  - Q: Is evidence required for a removal order under Law No. 6284 in Türkiye?
    A: The rule that “no evidence or document is required” (art. 8/3) is written in the Law for protective measures. Removal from the home is a preventive measure (art. 5/1-b). According to the Constitutional Court of Türkiye, that exception is not provided for preventive measures; referring to the legislative rationale, the Court noted that a preventive measure requires facts showing exposure to violence or a risk of it. Even so, a preventive measure is ordered without delay, and a more flexible approach to reasoning may be accepted in the first decision.
  - Q: What is the maximum duration of a protection or removal order under Law No. 6284 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, a protection order may be issued for at most six months the first time (art. 8/2). If violence or the risk of violence is found likely to continue, the judge may, ex officio or on the request of the protected person, the Ministry or the police, change the duration or form of the measure, lift it or continue it unchanged. Six months is an upper limit; a shorter period may also be set.
  - Q: How and within what time limit can a removal order under Law No. 6284 be challenged in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an objection is lodged with the family court within two weeks of the date of pronouncement or service (art. 9/1). The file is sent to the next-numbered family court in the same place, to the civil court of first instance if there is only one family court, or to the nearest civil court of first instance if both courts have the same judge. The objection authority decides within one week and its decision is final (art. 9/3); according to the Court of Cassation’s 2nd Civil Chamber, a decision rejecting the objection cannot be appealed.
  - Q: How long is the coercive detention for breaching a protection order under Law No. 6284 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, a person who breaches the order is subject by judicial decision to coercive detention of three to ten days for the first breach, depending on the nature of the breached measure and the gravity of the breach (art. 13/1). For each repetition the period is fifteen to thirty days; the total may not exceed six months (art. 13/2). The decisions are enforced by the chief public prosecutor’s office.
  - Q: Does coercive detention for breaching an order under Law No. 6284 prevent a criminal case for the same act in Türkiye?
    A: As a rule, no. The Law provides for coercive detention “even if the act constitutes a crime” (art. 13/1). The Constitutional Court of Türkiye held that coercive detention is not a penalty for a crime but a disciplinary detention, and that where the breach is also a crime there is no double punishment because different legal interests are violated; three members dissented.
- [Return of a Child under the Hague Convention in Türkiye: Application, One-Year Period, Grounds for Refusal](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/hague-child-abduction-return-of-child-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the return action under the 1980 Hague Convention and Law No. 5717 where a child is brought to Türkiye, or taken from Türkiye, without the other parent's consent: the Central… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/lahey-sozlesmesi-cocuk-kacirma-ve-cocugun-iadesi
  - Q: My spouse took our child to Türkiye and will not return; what can I do?
    A: You can file a return application under the Hague Convention with the central authority of your country or with the Central Authority in Türkiye. In Türkiye the action is brought by the chief public prosecutor’s office and heard urgently under the simple procedure (Law No. 5717, arts. 4 and 9).
  - Q: If a year has passed, can return no longer be requested?
    A: It can; but for applications made within one year, prompt return is the rule. After one year the Convention allows an additional ground for refusal, so applying without delay matters.
  - Q: I obtained custody in Türkiye; can a return order still be made?
    A: Yes. A custody order given after the return application is not a ground for refusing return (Law No. 5717, art. 13). The Court of Cassation has held that temporary custody obtained in Türkiye is of no significance under the Convention.
  - Q: When is return refused?
    A: On the grounds listed in the Convention, most often a grave risk that return would expose the child to physical or psychological harm, or the objection of a child of sufficient age and maturity. The risk must be shown by concrete evidence; young age alone is not enough.
  - Q: Does the return case stop during the judicial recess?
    A: No. These cases are heard during the judicial recess and time limits are not extended because of it. The period for appealing a regional court of appeal’s decision is two weeks from service.
- [Types of Alimony in Divorce: Interim, Poverty and Child Support](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/types-alimony-divorce): Types of alimony in divorce: interim maintenance during the case (Art. 169), poverty alimony after divorce (Art. 175) and child support (Art. 182); the conditions and mode of payment of poverty… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-07-17; updated: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bosanmada-nafaka-turleri
  - Q: How many types of alimony are there in divorce?
    A: Three main types: interim maintenance paid during the case, poverty alimony paid after divorce to a spouse who falls into poverty, and child support for the child. These differ from the other financial consequence of divorce — pecuniary and non-pecuniary damages.
  - Q: Must one be faultless to receive poverty alimony?
    A: No. It is enough that the requesting spouse’s fault is not greater than the other’s. The paying spouse need not be at fault; even a faultless spouse may pay poverty alimony in proportion to their means.
  - Q: Is poverty alimony paid for life?
    A: The current text of the law provides that poverty alimony may be claimed for an indefinite period; however, by its decision of 04.06.2026 (2025/156 E., 2026/131 K.) the Constitutional Court annulled the words “for an indefinite period”, and the annulment takes effect on 30.06.2027. Until then the current rule applies; what happens to new and existing alimony after 30.06.2027 depends on how the legislature regulates the matter. Even indefinite alimony does not mean absolute lifelong payment: it ceases automatically on the creditor’s remarriage or death; it may be terminated by court decision on grounds such as living as if married, the end of poverty or a dishonourable life, and reduced if circumstances change.
  - Q: When does interim maintenance begin and end?
    A: The judge may order it of their own motion when the case is filed, and it continues throughout the proceedings. The amount paid until the divorce becomes final is, by nature, interim maintenance; poverty alimony takes effect only after the judgment is final.
  - Q: Does child support end when the child turns 18?
    A: As a rule, child support ends when the child comes of age. If the adult child’s education continues, they may claim assistance maintenance through an action they bring themselves.
  - Q: Is there a time limit to bring a maintenance action after divorce?
    A: Rights of action arising from the finalisation of the divorce are subject to a one-year limitation period. If poverty alimony was not claimed with the divorce case, it must be sought in a separate action within one year of finalisation. Child support, however, is not bound by this period; the parent with custody may claim it at any time.
- [Uncontested Divorce in Turkish Law: Requirements, Protocol, Procedure, Competent Court, Withdrawing Consent](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/uncontested-divorce): Uncontested divorce under the Turkish Civil Code (art. 166/3): a marriage of at least one year, a joint application or acceptance of the action, the judge hearing the spouses in person and approval… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/anlasmali-bosanma-sartlari-ve-sureci
  - Q: How long does an uncontested divorce take?
    A: The law sets no fixed duration. The length depends on the scheduling of the hearing, the spouses being heard in person, the service of the reasoned judgment and the appeal periods. The appeal period is two weeks from service of the judgment on each party (Code of Civil Procedure art. 345); the parties cannot waive the right of appeal before the judgment is served on them (Code of Civil Procedure art. 349/1). Judgments concerning family law cannot be enforced until they become final (Code of Civil Procedure art. 350/2).
  - Q: Is an uncontested divorce possible without a lawyer?
    A: Yes. Anyone with capacity to sue may bring and pursue an action personally or through an attorney (Code of Civil Procedure art. 71/1). Even when represented by a lawyer, however, the spouses must be heard in person by the judge at the hearing; the Court of Cassation does not consider the attorneys’ statement that an agreement exists to be sufficient. Since the protocol is enforced as an annex to the judgment after finality, its clauses should be drafted clearly and completely.
  - Q: Is an uncontested divorce possible before the marriage has lasted one year?
    A: No. The marriage must have lasted at least one year and, according to the Court of Cassation, the period must be completed on the date of filing; completion of the year after filing is not enough. In an action filed before one year has passed, the claimant is asked whether he or she will continue as a contested divorce (Civil Code art. 166/1-2) and, if so, the evidence is collected.
  - Q: Can a spouse withdraw from the uncontested divorce or the protocol?
    A: Yes, until the judgment becomes final. According to the Court of Cassation, no provision prevents the spouses from withdrawing their will regarding the divorce and the protocol before finality; the action is then heard as a contested divorce. After finality, the protocol is deemed an annex to the judgment and cannot be departed from.
  - Q: Which court hears an uncontested divorce?
    A: The family court (Law No. 4787 art. 4); where there is no family court, the designated civil court of first instance. Venue lies with the court of the domicile of either spouse or of the place where they lived together during the last six months before the action (Civil Code art. 168).
  - Q: Can division of property and compensation be regulated in the protocol?
    A: Yes. In a decision upheld by the Court of Cassation it was accepted that the protocol may regulate matters such as the division of jewellery and household goods, the liquidation of the matrimonial property regime, the transfer of immovables, the payment of money and the transfer of shares, and that upon finality these obligations become an inseparable part of the divorce. Material and non-pecuniary compensation (Civil Code art. 174) are also among the financial consequences of divorce.
- [Unpaid Maintenance in Türkiye: What Happens? Enforcement, Salary Attachment, Coercive Detention, Limitation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/unpaid-maintenance-enforcement-detention-turkiye): Under Turkish law, if court-ordered maintenance is not paid the creditor may collect it through enforcement proceedings and, where the debtor fails to pay current maintenance claimed in those… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-13. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/nafaka-odenmezse-ne-olur-icra-takibi-ve-tazyik-hapsi
  - Q: Can you go to prison for not paying maintenance?
    A: Under Turkish law, if a debtor fails to pay the current monthly maintenance claimed in enforcement proceedings based on a maintenance decision, the enforcement court may, on the creditor’s complaint, order coercive detention of up to three months (EBL art. 344). This is not a penalty but a means of compelling payment: a debtor who pays after detention begins is released, and if payment of the debt is established the penalty lapses.
  - Q: How is interim maintenance enforced?
    A: According to the 12th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, an interim decision ordering interim maintenance during divorce proceedings is neither a judgment nor a document having the character of a judgment; it cannot be enforced as a judgment but may be enforced by way of general attachment without judgment. Interim maintenance ends when the divorce decision becomes final; no maintenance can be claimed under it for the period after finality.
  - Q: Can coercive detention be sought for accrued (arrears) maintenance?
    A: The Assembly of Criminal Chambers of the Court of Cassation, in a file where only past-period maintenance was claimed and the enforcement order for that request was not served on the debtor, treated the claim as an ordinary claim and held that the elements of the art. 344 offence were not formed. Under the Assembly’s conditions, coercive detention requires at least one month of current maintenance accrued between service of the enforcement order and the complaint. Arrears are collected by attachment.
  - Q: How much of a salary can be attached for maintenance?
    A: The amount attached may not be less than one quarter of the salary (EBL art. 83); for workers’ wages and social security pensions the law reserves the rights of maintenance creditors. For retirement pensions, the 12th Civil Chamber accepted that current monthly maintenance is deducted in full each month and arrears at not less than one quarter of the remainder.
  - Q: When does a maintenance claim become time-barred?
    A: Where a debt has been established by a court decision, the new period that begins when limitation is interrupted is ten years (TCO art. 156/2). The 12th Civil Chamber applies this period in maintenance enforcement; since maintenance accrues over time, it holds that monthly instalments accrued more than ten years before the date of enforcement are time-barred, while those of the last ten years may be claimed. Enforcement proceedings based on a judgment also become time-barred if no step is taken for ten years (EBL art. 39).
  - Q: Can a coercive detention decision be challenged?
    A: Yes. An objection may be lodged within two weeks of notification of the enforcement court’s decision on coercive detention; the authority designated by law examines it and its decision is final (EBL art. 353). A coercive detention decision is not enforced once two years have passed from its finality (EBL art. 354).
- [Wedding Jewellery (Ziynet) Claims on Divorce in Turkish Law: Who Owns the Gold, Proof, Return, Limitation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/wedding-jewellery-claims-divorce): Wedding jewellery (ziynet) claims on divorce under Turkish law: who owns the gold given at the wedding (agreement, local custom and the new principled criteria of the Court of Cassation’s 2nd Civil… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bosanmada-ziynet-esyasi-alacagi
  - Q: Who keeps the gold given at the wedding after a divorce?
    A: Under the 2nd Civil Chamber’s new principled view, the court first looks at any agreement between the parties and, absent an agreement, at a local custom that has been alleged and proven. Failing both, everything of economic value attached to the man and to the woman belongs, as a rule, to each of them; an item specific to women or to men is deemed given to that sex. Items placed in the jewellery chest or bag that suit both sexes are treated as jointly owned.
  - Q: How can I prove that my spouse took my gold?
    A: As a rule, you bear the burden of proving that the jewellery existed and that your spouse took it. Wedding photographs and footage, witness statements and expert examination are assessed together, but statements of witnesses who only repeat what they heard from you are not considered sufficient. Relying on the oath in your statement of claim matters if the other evidence proves insufficient.
  - Q: Can I claim back gold that was sold to buy a house or a car?
    A: Yes. According to the Court of Cassation, unless it is alleged and proven that the jewellery was given on the understanding that it would not be claimed back, the person who took it must return it, and what it was spent on does not affect the return. If the husband argues that it was given to him on condition that it would not be returned, he must prove this; an abstract witness statement unsupported by other evidence has not been considered sufficient.
  - Q: Can a jewellery claim be brought together with the divorce action?
    A: Yes; in practice it is usually raised together with the divorce action or as a counterclaim and heard by the family court. However, because the jewellery claim is not an ancillary of the divorce, a proportional fee must be paid on the value of the jewellery claimed; if the fee is short, the court must give a proper time limit.
  - Q: Can a jewellery claim be brought years after the divorce?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation, if the jewellery is found to exist, the dispute is based on ownership and limitation does not arise; if it no longer exists, the ten-year limitation period applies, and limitation does not run between spouses while the marriage continues (Code of Obligations art. 146 and art. 153). How the period is calculated in a particular case must be assessed separately.
  - Q: On what date is the value of the gold calculated?
    A: This depends on the relief sought. The Court of Cassation states that where return in kind is ordered and proves impossible, the current value of the jewellery can be taken into account at the enforcement stage; under Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law art. 24, if the value is not stated in the judgment or is disputed, the enforcement officer assesses it at the market value on the date of attachment. The court cannot award the value on the date of the action if it was not claimed.

### Consumer Law

- [A Defective Zero-km Vehicle (Consumer Rights)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/defective-zero-km-vehicle): The consumer's rights when a zero-km vehicle turns out defective: the definition of defective goods (Law 6502, Art. 8), hidden defects and the six-month presumption of proof (Art. 10), the four… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-17; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sifir-km-aracin-ayipli-cikmasi-tuketici
  - Q: Can I have my defective zero-km vehicle replaced with a new one?
    A: Yes. Where a defect exists you choose one of four elective rights; one of them is replacement of the vehicle with a defect-free equivalent (same make/model, zero-km). Especially for important defects originating in manufacture and not remediable by repair, the Court of Cassation finds replacement equitable.
  - Q: Who must prove the vehicle is not defective?
    A: For defects emerging within six months of delivery, the burden of proving the vehicle is defect-free lies with the seller (Art. 10). This is a strong presumption in the consumer's favour.
  - Q: The service repaired it repeatedly but the fault recurs; what can I do?
    A: Even if you used the repair right, if the vehicle malfunctions again within the warranty, the maximum repair time is exceeded, or repair is impossible, you may move to the other elective rights (replacement or rescission); the seller cannot reject this (Art. 56/3).
  - Q: Can I direct my demand only to the dealer, or also to the producer/importer?
    A: Free repair and replacement may be used against the producer or importer too; seller, producer and importer are jointly and severally liable (Art. 11/2).
  - Q: Which court should I apply to?
    A: A zero-km vehicle is a consumer transaction; since the price far exceeds the consumer arbitration committee's monetary threshold, the competent court is the consumer court (or the civil court of first instance acting as such). Applying to a mediator before suing is a precondition to suit.
  - Q: I bought the vehicle years ago; has the period expired?
    A: As a rule, liability for defective goods is two years from delivery. But where the defect was concealed by gross fault or fraud, the limitation provisions do not apply (Art. 12/3).
