- [Applying to the Consumer Arbitration Committee](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/applying-consumer-arbitration-committee): An analysis of when application to the consumer arbitration committee is mandatory, the applicable monetary threshold, the application procedure, the binding effect of its decisions, the prohibition… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tuketici-hakem-heyetine-basvuru
  - Q: When is application to the consumer arbitration committee mandatory?
    A: Without prejudice to the parties’ rights under the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law, application to the consumer arbitration committee is mandatory for disputes valued below the monetary threshold set by the Law and increased every year by the revaluation rate (Law No. 6502 art. 68/1 and 68/4); the Court of Cassation treated this requirement as a condition of action to be examined by the consumer court of its own motion (Court of Cassation 13th Civil Chamber, E. 2016/9334, K. 2016/14097). No application can be made to the committee for disputes above the threshold; in such disputes, applying to a mediator before filing suit in the consumer court is, as a rule, a condition of action (Law No. 6502 art. 73/A). The current year’s amount should be confirmed from official sources before applying.
  - Q: Where, and within what time limit, must an objection to the committee’s decision be filed?
    A: The parties may object, within two weeks of service of the decision, before the consumer court of the place where the consumer arbitration committee or the consumer’s domicile is located (Law No. 6502 art. 70/3). The Court of Cassation has accepted that venue in an objection action is not exclusive (Court of Cassation 5th Civil Chamber, E. 2024/5656, K. 2024/9689).
  - Q: Does filing an objection automatically suspend enforcement of the decision?
    A: No. An objection does not suspend enforcement of the consumer arbitration committee’s decision; however, upon request, the judge may suspend enforcement by way of an interim measure (Law No. 6502 art. 70/3).
  - Q: Can a consumer arbitration committee decision be enforced directly?
    A: Yes. Consumer arbitration committee decisions are enforced in accordance with the provisions of the Enforcement and Bankruptcy Law on the enforcement of judgments (Law No. 6502 art. 70/2); an objection to the decision does not automatically suspend enforcement either (Law No. 6502 art. 70/3).
  - Q: Can the consumer court’s decision on an objection be challenged further?
    A: No. The decision of the consumer court on an objection is final (Law No. 6502 art. 70/5). The Court of Cassation likewise held that no appeal lies against such a decision (Court of Cassation 13th Civil Chamber, E. 2017/154, K. 2017/521).
  - Q: What happens if an action is filed directly, without applying to the committee?
    A: Where a dispute falls within the threshold that makes application mandatory and an action is filed without applying to the committee, according to the Court of Cassation the action must be dismissed on procedural grounds for want of a condition of action, without examining the merits (Court of Cassation 13th Civil Chamber, E. 2016/9334, K. 2016/14097). The provisions on mediation as a condition of action do not apply to disputes within the committee’s remit (Law No. 6502 art. 73/A/1).
- [Early Repayment of a Consumer Loan in Türkiye: Interest and Cost Reductions, Compensation Cap, Restructuring](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/early-repayment-consumer-loan): Early repayment of consumer loans under the Turkish Consumer Protection Law (arts. 27 and 37): the lender’s duty to reduce interest and other cost elements when all or part of a loan is repaid early… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tuketici-kredisinde-erken-odeme
  - Q: If I repay my personal loan early, must the bank reduce the interest?
    A: Yes. The consumer may pay instalments not yet due or the whole debt early, and the lender must make the necessary reduction in all interest and other cost elements in proportion to the amount repaid early (Consumer Protection Law art. 27). The reduction is calculated on the basis of the payment date, which may not be changed to the consumer’s detriment (Consumer Credit Agreements Regulation art. 15/2).
  - Q: Can an early closure fee be charged on a personal or vehicle loan?
    A: The Law provides for early repayment compensation only for housing loans (Consumer Protection Law art. 37/2); Consumer Protection Law art. 27, which governs general consumer loans, provides for no such compensation and regulates only the duty to reduce the cost. Under the repealed Law No. 4077, the Court of Cassation also stressed that there was no provision allowing an early repayment commission on consumer loans.
  - Q: How much early repayment compensation can the bank charge when I pay off my housing loan?
    A: It may be charged only on fixed-rate loans and only if the agreement provides for it. It may not exceed one per cent of the amount repaid early for loans with a remaining term of up to thirty-six months, and two per cent for longer remaining terms; it may not be charged on variable-rate loans (Consumer Protection Law art. 37/2). Under the Regulation, it also may not exceed the total reduction granted to the consumer.
  - Q: Do I pay early repayment compensation if I restructure my loan with the same bank?
    A: In a decision concerning the period of the repealed Law No. 4077, the Assembly of Civil Chambers held that refinancing agreed with the same bank is not early repayment and that no early repayment fee may therefore be claimed; the bank may claim certain costs only if the consumer is informed, gives express written approval and the bank proves that the costs reflect reality. Paying off the loan with a loan from another bank, by contrast, is early repayment.
  - Q: What happens if I pay off the loan within 14 days of taking it out?
    A: There is a fourteen-day right of withdrawal from a consumer credit agreement. If the whole credit debt is repaid early within that period, the withdrawal rules apply without any separate notice; the consumer pays the principal and the contractual interest for the period of use, and nothing may be claimed beyond the costs paid to public bodies or third parties (Consumer Protection Law art. 24).
  - Q: How can I recover an amount the bank overcharged?
    A: For disputes below the statutory monetary threshold, application to a consumer arbitration committee is mandatory; for disputes above the threshold an action may be brought in the consumer court after applying to a mediator (Consumer Protection Law art. 68 and Consumer Protection Law art. 73/A). As the threshold is updated every year, the amount applicable on the date of application should be checked.
- [Liability for Defects in the Sale of a Second-Hand Vehicle](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/liability-defects-sale-second-hand-vehicle): The buyer's rights when a second-hand vehicle turns out to have a hidden defect (accident/salvage/Tramer record, odometer fraud, engine-transmission fault): the seller is liable even if unaware of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-06-21; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/ikinci-el-arac-satisinda-ayiptan-sorumluluk
  - Q: The contract says “I saw it, approved it, took it as-is”; do I lose my rights?
    A: No. According to the General Assembly of Civil Chambers, such a statement removes the seller's liability only for patent defects; for hidden defects (e.g. a heavy-damage/salvage record) the seller's liability continues, and the seller need not have given a separate warranty.
  - Q: I later learned of an accident/salvage record or that the odometer was rolled back; what can I do?
    A: These are typical hidden defects and usually show the seller's gross fault (indeed fraud). A grossly-at-fault seller cannot escape liability by claiming late notice (TCO Art. 225) and cannot rely on the two-year limitation (TCO Art. 231). You may seek rescission, a price reduction, or loss-of-value damages.
  - Q: Must I investigate hidden defects before buying?
    A: No. The Court of Cassation holds that the buyer has no duty to investigate hidden defects; nor are you obliged to examine the traffic/insurance records one by one. You only need to notify the seller within the shortest time consistent with good faith once the defect appears.
  - Q: I bought from a dealer; where do I sue?
    A: A purchase from a dealer/commercial seller is a consumer transaction; the competent court is the consumer court (or the civil court of first instance acting as such). For low-value disputes you must first apply to the consumer arbitration committee; before suing in the consumer court, applying to a mediator is a precondition to suit. In sales between individuals, the action is heard in the civil court of first instance.
  - Q: I bought the vehicle a year ago; has the period expired?
    A: As a rule the warranty period is two years from transfer (at least one year for a second-hand purchase from a commercial seller). But if the seller was grossly at fault or concealed the defect by fraud, limitation does not run, so a limitation defence will not bar you.
  - Q: I had the vehicle inspected before the sale; can I still sue?
    A: Faults clearly appearing in the expert report count as a “known defect” for which the seller is not liable (TCO Art. 222). By contrast, your rights continue for hidden defects not detected in the inspection that emerge later.
- [Right of Withdrawal in Turkish Distance Contracts: Fourteen Days, Exceptions, Refunds, Marketplace Liability](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/right-withdrawal-distance-contracts): The right of withdrawal in distance contracts under the Turkish Consumer Protection Law (art. 48) and the Distance Contracts Regulation: when the fourteen-day period starts for goods and services… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-10. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/mesafeli-sozlesmede-cayma-hakki
  - Q: Within how many days can I return a product bought online?
    A: In distance contracts the consumer may withdraw within fourteen days without giving reasons and without paying a penalty. For goods the period starts on the day of delivery, for services on the day the contract is concluded (Distance Contracts Regulation art. 9). After sending the withdrawal notice you must send the goods back within fourteen days, unless the seller has offered to collect them (Distance Contracts Regulation art. 13/1).
  - Q: When must the seller refund the money I paid?
    A: The seller must refund all payments received, including delivery costs, in a single payment and in a manner appropriate to your means of payment, within fourteen days of the date on which the goods are handed to the carrier specified for returns in the pre-contractual information; if you use a different carrier, the period starts when the goods reach the seller (Distance Contracts Regulation art. 12).
  - Q: Who pays for return shipping?
    A: If you send the goods back with the carrier specified by the seller for returns, you cannot be held liable for the return costs; if the seller did not specify any carrier for returns in the pre-contractual information, no return costs can be claimed from you either (Distance Contracts Regulation art. 12/5). The current wording of this rule applies from 1/1/2026.
  - Q: Can I return a product whose box I have opened or which I have tried out?
    A: Opening the packaging does not remove the right of withdrawal for every product; the cases in which the right cannot be exercised once the packaging is opened are listed in the Regulation (for example goods unsuitable for return for health and hygiene reasons, and books, digital content and computer consumables on a tangible medium). You are not liable for changes resulting from trying the product out to a normal extent; for use beyond normal use, however, you may be held liable for the seller’s loss. The Court of Cassation held that for a mobile phone used for six days this must be determined by an expert report.
  - Q: What happens if the seller did not inform me about the right of withdrawal?
    A: The seller bears the burden of proving that the information was given. If you were not properly informed, you are not bound by the fourteen-day period; however, the right ends in any event one year after the withdrawal period expires (Consumer Protection Law art. 48/4). The Court of Cassation has accepted that a consumer not informed of the right of withdrawal may withdraw by bringing an action within that one-year period.
  - Q: Can I claim the refund from the platform for a product bought on a marketplace?
    A: If the platform collected the price on the seller’s behalf and the price has not been transferred to the seller after delivery, the platform is jointly and severally liable with the seller for the refund obligation relating to the right of withdrawal. The remedies for defects are outside this joint liability (Consumer Protection Law art. 48/6).
- [Tourist Damages and the Liability of Tourism Businesses: Package Tours, Hotel Theft, Pool Accidents in Türkiye](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/tourist-damages-liability-tourism-businesses): Liability of package tour organisers, intermediaries (travel agencies) and hotels for damage suffered by tourists in Türkiye: price reduction, withdrawal and compensation, including for wasted… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/turist-zararlari-ve-turizm-isletmelerinin-sorumlulugu
  - Q: Can compensation be obtained if the holiday is not as expected?
    A: Yes. The tourist may claim a price reduction, withdraw in the event of a serious failure, claim compensation for the damage and appropriate compensation for wasted holiday time (Consumer Protection Law art. 51/7-8). The Court of Cassation treated the failure to provide the environment necessary for a holiday as an attack on personality rights and reversed the dismissal of a claim for non-pecuniary damages.
  - Q: Is the hotel liable for belongings stolen at the hotel?
    A: Yes. Even without fault the hotel is liable up to three times the daily room rate, and without that cap where it or its staff are at fault (Code of Obligations art. 576). The damage must be reported to the hotel as soon as it becomes known (Code of Obligations art. 578).
  - Q: Who is liable for a pool accident?
    A: Where safety measures are lacking, the hotel may be held liable under contract and tort rules, including for its staff’s fault (Code of Obligations art. 66); the injured person’s own fault may reduce compensation (Code of Obligations art. 52). In a judgment upheld by the Court of Cassation, a hotel that kept no lifeguard was found 40% at fault.
  - Q: In a package tour, is the travel agency or the hotel liable?
    A: Towards the tourist, the organiser is liable, including for failures caused by the hotel; the Regulation also holds the intermediary (travel agency) liable, and where the organiser has no representative in Türkiye the intermediary is liable as if it were the organiser (Consumer Protection Law art. 51; Package Tour Contracts Regulation art. 13). The hotel may in addition be held directly liable for guests’ belongings (Code of Obligations art. 576) and for its own culpable acts (Code of Obligations art. 49).
  - Q: Which time limits matter for complaints?
    A: A deficiency must be reported immediately during the holiday (Package Tour Contracts Regulation art. 12/1), and damage to belongings at a hotel as soon as it becomes known (Code of Obligations art. 578). Liability for a defective service is as a rule time-barred two years after performance of the service (Consumer Protection Law art. 16), and a tort claim two years after knowledge and in any event ten years after the act (Code of Obligations art. 72).

### Contract Law

- [Adaptation of Contracts in Türkiye: Excessive Difficulty (TBK Art. 138), Leases, Currency Debts, Works](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/adaptation-contracts): Adaptation of contracts for excessive difficulty of performance under Turkish law: the conditions of Code of Obligations art. 138 (unforeseeability, a cause not attributable to the debtor, an… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sozlesmenin-uyarlanmasi-asiri-ifa-guclugu
  - Q: Under what conditions can adaptation of a contract be requested?
    A: Under Code of Obligations art. 138, an extraordinary situation that was not foreseen and could not have been expected to be foreseen when the contract was concluded must arise for a reason not attributable to the debtor, must change the circumstances to the debtor’s detriment to such an extent that demanding performance would be contrary to good faith, and the debtor must not yet have performed or must have performed under reservation of its rights. All conditions are required together.
  - Q: Is an increase in the exchange rate sufficient for adaptation of a contract?
    A: On its own it is generally not considered sufficient. The Court of Cassation has treated exchange-rate changes as foreseeable in a foreign-currency-indexed housing loan and in a commercial lease in euro (Court of Cassation Civil General Assembly, E. 2014/1614, K. 2014/900; Court of Cassation 3rd Civil Chamber, E. 2024/1255, K. 2025/813). Where a long period, a fundamental change in the parties’ circumstances and the debtor’s lack of foreign-currency income coincide, however, adaptation may be accepted (Court of Cassation Civil General Assembly, E. 2023/582, K. 2025/24).
  - Q: The rent has fallen behind; can an action for adaptation be brought instead of an action for determination of rent?
    A: In residential and roofed commercial leases, inflation, rent inflation and changes in comparable rents are the subject of the action for determination of rent under Code of Obligations art. 344 and are not separately grounds for adaptation under Code of Obligations art. 138. If the conditions of Code of Obligations art. 138 are met, the contractual rent increase rate may be the subject of an action for adaptation (Court of Cassation 3rd Civil Chamber, E. 2026/1213, K. 2026/1751).
  - Q: I paid the rent without any reservation; can I still request adaptation?
    A: Since rent is performed periodically, payment without reservation of rights does not bar an action for adaptation (Court of Cassation 3rd Civil Chamber, E. 2018/7863, K. 2019/534), although the other conditions must also be met. For obligations performed in a single act, adaptation after performance can, as a rule, be requested only if rights were reserved.
  - Q: Which court hears an adaptation action, and is mediation mandatory?
    A: The civil court of peace has jurisdiction over actions arising from a lease relationship (Code of Civil Procedure art. 4/1-a) and, for other contracts, unless otherwise provided, the civil court of first instance (Code of Civil Procedure art. 2/1); besides the court of the defendant’s domicile, the court of the place of performance also has venue (Code of Civil Procedure art. 10). In lease disputes, applying to a mediator before filing suit is a precondition to suit (Law No. 6325 art. 18/B).
  - Q: Can a contractor ask for an increase of a lump-sum price in a works contract?
    A: As a rule, no (Code of Obligations art. 480/1). However, if circumstances that could not be foreseen at the outset, or that could have been foreseen but were not taken into account by the parties, prevent the work from being produced for that price or make it extremely difficult, the contractor may ask the court for adaptation or, where this is not possible, withdraw (Code of Obligations art. 480/2).
- [Agency (Mandate) Contract and Revocation of Authority](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/agency-contract-revocation-authority): An analysis of the agency (vekalet/mandate) contract under Turkish law — its definition, the parties' obligations, unilateral termination (revocation and withdrawal) and the specific consequences of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/vekalet-sozlesmesi-ve-azil
  - Q: When may an agent be justifiably revoked?
    A: Conduct that undermines the relationship of trust — such as failing to pursue the matter diligently, departing from instructions, or breaching the duty of loyalty — may amount to justified cause for revocation. The Court of Cassation has, on the facts of a particular case, treated an attorney's demand for a fee before the matter concluded as conduct undermining trust and therefore justifying revocation. The burden of proving justified cause lies with the party relying on it.
  - Q: If revocation is unjustified, is the attorney entitled to the full fee?
    A: Under Article 174/2 of the Attorneys Act, in the case of unjustified revocation the attorney is entitled to the entire fee for the undertaken matter, regardless of the stage it had reached. Conversely, in the case of justified revocation the attorney may claim a fee only for matters concluded and become final as of the date of revocation; no fee is due for matters still pending at that date.
  - Q: May an agent always resign, and is there a cost to doing so?
    A: Under Article 512 TCO, both the principal and the agent may terminate the agency unilaterally at any time, reflecting the personal, trust-based nature of the relationship. However, a party who terminates at an inopportune time is obliged to compensate the other party for the resulting (negative) loss.
  - Q: Does the death of the principal or agent automatically end the agency?
    A: Under Article 513 TCO, unless otherwise apparent from the contract or the nature of the affair, the death, loss of capacity, or bankruptcy of either party automatically terminates the agency. However, if termination would endanger the principal's interests, the agent (or the agent's heir) must continue performing the agency until the matter can be taken over.
  - Q: When does the agent's duty to account become time-barred?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation, limitation does not run on the agent's duty to account and return what was obtained for as long as the agency relationship continues; the claim falls due once the agent renders account or the relationship ends. Claims arising from an agency contract are generally subject to a five-year limitation period under Article 147/5 TCO.
  - Q: What does a substitution clause in a power of attorney given to an attorney mean?
    A: Under Article 171 of the Attorneys Act, where the power of attorney authorises substitution, the attorney may pursue the matter jointly with, or hand it over entirely to, another attorney; even so, the original attorney's liability toward the client continues, jointly and severally with the substitute attorney.
- [Limitation vs Preclusive Periods in Turkish Law: General and Five-Year Periods, Suspension and Interruption](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/limitation-periods-preclusive-periods): The differences between limitation and preclusive periods in Turkish law (defence and objection, consideration by the judge of its own motion, suspension and interruption), the ten-year general and… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-10-19; updated: 2026-11-08. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/zamanasimi-ve-hak-dusurucu-sureler
  - Q: What is the basic difference between limitation and a preclusive period?
    A: Limitation does not extinguish the claim; it gives the debtor a defence preventing the claim from being enforced by action or enforcement proceedings, and it cannot be taken into account by the judge unless raised (Code of Obligations art. 161). A preclusive period extinguishes the right itself, is taken into account by the judge of its own motion and, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation, is not subject to suspension or interruption.
  - Q: Can a person who paid a time-barred debt claim the money back?
    A: No. Enrichment resulting from the performance of a time-barred debt cannot be reclaimed (Code of Obligations art. 78/2). Limitation does not extinguish the debt; it removes the possibility of collecting the claim through legal channels.
  - Q: In which cases is limitation interrupted, and what happens then?
    A: Limitation is interrupted if the debtor acknowledges the debt (in particular by paying interest, making part performance, giving a pledge or providing a surety) or if the creditor applies to a court or an arbitrator by way of action or defence, initiates enforcement proceedings or lodges a claim in bankruptcy (Code of Obligations art. 154). On interruption a new period begins to run; if the debt has been acknowledged in a written instrument or established by a court or arbitral decision, the new period is always ten years (Code of Obligations art. 156).
  - Q: When does limitation start in tort, and when does the criminal limitation period apply?
    A: A claim for compensation becomes time-barred two years after the injured party learned of the damage and of the person liable and in any event ten years after the act was committed; if the compensation arises from an act requiring punishment for which the criminal laws provide a longer limitation period, that period applies (Code of Obligations art. 72). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, whether the act requires punishment is, as a rule, determined by the civil judge.
  - Q: Can the limitation period be extended or shortened by contract?
    A: The periods laid down in the limitation section of the Turkish Code of Obligations cannot be changed by contract (Code of Obligations art. 148), and limitation cannot be waived in advance (Code of Obligations art. 160). The Assembly of Civil Chambers likewise considers it impossible for the parties to change or abolish preclusive periods by contract.
- [Seller at Gross Fault Loses 2-Year Defect Limitation: Turkish Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/seller-gross-fault-two-year-limitation-defective-goods): Under Turkish law, the Assembly of Civil Chambers of the Court of Cassation unanimously held that a seller whose devices, bought in a commercial sale, kept breaking down was at gross fault within the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-07. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/agir-kusurlu-satici-ayipli-malda-iki-yillik-zamanasimindan-yararlanamaz
  - Q: How long does a buyer have to sue over defective goods?
    A: Under Code of Obligations art. 231/1, every action arising from a defect in the thing sold is time-barred two years after the thing sold is transferred to the buyer, even if the defect comes to light later. If the seller has undertaken a longer period, that period applies.
  - Q: Does the two-year period apply if the seller is at gross fault?
    A: No. Under art. 231/2, a seller at gross fault in transferring the thing sold in a defective state cannot benefit from the two-year period. In this decision the Assembly of Civil Chambers did not treat as time-barred an action filed more than two years after the devices were delivered or replaced.
  - Q: What does gross fault mean?
    A: According to the Assembly, gross fault consists of intent and gross negligence; fraud may also be included. The Assembly stated that grossly faulty conduct aimed at preventing the buyer from notifying the defect may also fall within the exception in Code of Obligations art. 225/1.
  - Q: Does the buyer lose its rights if the defect was not notified in time?
    A: As a rule, the buyer must notify the defect in time (Code of Obligations art. 223; in commercial sales, Commercial Code art. 23/1-c). A seller at gross fault, however, cannot escape liability by asserting that the defect was not notified in time (Code of Obligations art. 225/1).
  - Q: Which right did the buyer use in this case?
    A: Among the elective rights in Code of Obligations art. 227, the buyer chose to withdraw from the contract and claimed the price it had paid, against return of the devices; the Assembly accepted that it had this right.

### Citizenship & Foreigners Law

- [Acquiring Turkish Citizenship after Birth: Routes, Conditions and Challenging a Refusal](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/acquiring-turkish-citizenship-routes-conditions-refusal): Under Turkish law, citizenship may be acquired after birth through the general route (five years of uninterrupted residence), marriage (at least three years), the exceptional route, re-acquisition… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/turk-vatandasliginin-sonradan-kazanilmasi-ve-ret-kararina-karsi-dava
  - Q: How many years of residence are required to apply for Turkish citizenship under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, the general route requires five years of uninterrupted residence in Türkiye counted back from the date of application (Law No. 5901 art. 11/1-b). Within that period the applicant may be abroad for a total not exceeding twelve months (art. 15). For an application through marriage the test is not a period of residence but a marriage that has lasted at least three years (art. 16). Meeting the conditions does not by itself give a right to citizenship (art. 10/1).
  - Q: When and on what conditions can a foreigner married to a Turkish citizen apply for Turkish citizenship through marriage?
    A: Under Turkish law, marriage does not directly confer citizenship. A foreigner who has been married to a Turkish citizen for at least three years and whose marriage continues may apply; the applicant must live in family unity, must not engage in any activity incompatible with the marital union and must not be in a situation that constitutes an obstacle in terms of national security and public order (Law No. 5901 art. 16/1). Even where the conditions are met, the administration has discretion (art. 10/1).
  - Q: Is the amount required for Turkish citizenship by investment written in the Turkish Citizenship Law?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, the Law only provides for the exceptional route and the decision of the President (Law No. 5901 art. 12/1-b); the amounts are in article 20 of the Regulation on the Implementation of the Turkish Citizenship Law. According to the text of the Regulation as retrieved on 14 November 2026, the purchase of, or a notarised promise-of-sale contract for, immovable property requires at least 400,000 US dollars and a three-year annotation in the land registry, and the other listed investment options require at least 500,000 US dollars or the creation of employment for at least 50 people. These are provisions of a regulation and have been amended by Presidential decisions in the past; the current text should be checked before applying.
  - Q: If an application for Turkish citizenship is refused, in which court and within how many days can an action be brought in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an annulment action against the Ministry’s refusal may be brought before the administrative court within sixty days from the day after written notification (Administrative Procedure Law art. 7). As there is no special rule, the court with territorial jurisdiction is the administrative court of the place where the authority that issued the act is located (art. 32). Where the contested act is a decision of the President, the Council of State is the court of first instance (Law No. 2575 art. 24/1-a). The administrative court’s decision may be appealed to the regional administrative court within thirty days of notification (art. 45).
  - Q: If a court annuls the refusal of a Turkish citizenship application, does the applicant automatically become a Turkish citizen?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, administrative courts may not review expediency and may not give decisions that remove the administration’s discretion (Administrative Procedure Law art. 2/2). In its decision of 15.10.2025, which may not yet be final, the 10th Chamber of the Council of State stated that annulling the refusal does not result in the applicant being directly granted citizenship, and that the administration may make a fresh assessment in respect of the other matters required by the legislation and issue a new act that may be favourable or unfavourable.
  - Q: Can Turkish citizenship acquired after birth be cancelled later under Turkish law?
    A: Yes. Under Turkish law, if the decision granting citizenship resulted from a false statement by the person concerned or from the concealment of important matters forming the basis for the acquisition, it is cancelled by the authority that gave the decision (Law No. 5901 art. 31). Where the cancellation is made by a decision of the President, the action is heard at first instance by the Council of State.
- [Challenging a Deportation Order and a Residence Permit Refusal in Türkiye: Law No. 6458 Arts. 53, 57 and 25](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/deportation-order-residence-permit-refusal-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an action against a deportation order must be brought before the administrative court within seven days of notification; the court's decision is final and, unless the foreigner… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sinir-disi-etme-karari-ve-ikamet-izni-reddine-karsi-dava-6458
  - Q: What is the time limit for challenging a deportation order in Türkiye under Law No. 6458?
    A: Under Turkish law, an action against a deportation order must be brought before the administrative court within seven days of notification of the order (Law No. 6458 art. 53/3). This is much shorter than the general sixty-day limit for administrative actions and, as a special provision, applies first. The claimant also notifies the authority that issued the order. Subject to the foreigner’s consent, the foreigner is not deported during the time limit or, if an action is brought, until the proceedings end.
  - Q: Is the administrative court’s decision on a deportation order in Türkiye final, or can it be appealed?
    A: Under Law No. 6458 art. 53/3, the administrative court’s decision in an action against a deportation order in Türkiye is final; no regional appeal or appeal on points of law lies. In decision 2024/116, given on the text before Law No. 7533, the Constitutional Court found this rule not contrary to the Constitution; the rule is retained in the current text. After a final decision, alleged violations of fundamental rights may, where its conditions are met, be raised by an individual application to the Constitutional Court; those conditions must be examined separately.
  - Q: Where and how can administrative detention at a removal centre in Türkiye be challenged?
    A: Under Turkish law, administrative detention is challenged before the criminal judge of peace, not the administrative court (Law No. 6458 art. 57/6). The foreigner, a legal representative or a lawyer may apply; the application does not suspend the detention. The judge decides within five days and the decision is final. A fresh application may be made on the ground that the conditions of detention have ceased or changed.
  - Q: What is the time limit for an action against the refusal, cancellation or non-renewal of a residence permit in Türkiye?
    A: Law No. 6458 sets no separate time limit for an action against the refusal, cancellation or non-renewal of a residence permit in Türkiye. In this article’s view, the general sixty-day limit in the Administrative Procedure Law (Law No. 2577) art. 7 therefore applies as a rule, starting on the day after written notification. A foreigner whose extension application is refused and who does not leave within ten days may be made subject to a deportation order (art. 54/1-j), so that ten-day period must also be watched.
  - Q: How long does an entry ban on a foreigner deported from Türkiye last, and can it be lifted?
    A: Under Law No. 6458 art. 9, the entry into Türkiye of a foreigner deported from Türkiye is banned by the Directorate General or the governorates. The Law also provides that in certain cases no entry ban decision need be taken (art. 9/4 and 9/5). An entry ban lasts at most five years; where there is a serious threat to public order or public security it may be extended by at most ten more years. The Directorate General may lift the ban or, with the ban remaining in place, allow entry for a specified period.
- [Work Permits for Foreign Nationals in Türkiye: Challenging a Refusal and Fines (Law No. 6735 Arts. 21, 23)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/work-permit-refusal-objection-unpermitted-employment-fine): Under Turkish law, an objection against a work permit refusal may be filed with the Ministry within thirty days of notification, and the administrative courts may be seized if the objection is… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/yabancilarin-calisma-izni-reddi-ve-kacak-yabanci-calistirma-para-cezasi-6735
  - Q: What is the time limit for objecting to a work permit refusal in Türkiye?
    A: Under art. 21 of Law No. 6735, an objection may be filed with the Ministry within thirty days of notification against the Ministry’s decisions under that Law. If the objection is rejected, the administrative courts may be seized. Since the Law sets no separate time limit for the action, this article infers that, as a rule, the sixty-day period in art. 7 of the Administrative Procedure Law applies; the date on which the rejection of the objection is notified should be taken into account.
  - Q: On what grounds is a work permit application refused under Turkish law?
    A: Art. 9 of Law No. 6735 lists the grounds; they include inconsistency with international labour force policy, false or misleading documents, insufficient justification for employment, professions reserved for Turkish citizens, objections on grounds of public order, public security or public health, and applications made out of time or left incomplete. The sub-paragraph applied should be identified in the refusal decision.
  - Q: How much is the administrative fine for an employer who employs a foreigner without a work permit in Türkiye?
    A: Under art. 23(5) of Law No. 6735, an employer or employer’s representative who employs a foreigner without a work permit is fined six thousand Turkish lira for each foreigner. This is the amount in the statutory text; under art. 17(7) of the Misdemeanours Law it is increased every year by the revaluation rate. In one decision of the Court of Cassation’s 7th Criminal Chamber, 8,821 TL per foreigner was applied for 2019. A repeated offence doubles the fine.
  - Q: What sanctions apply to a foreigner who works in Türkiye without a work permit?
    A: According to the statutory text, a foreigner working for an employer without a permit is fined two thousand four hundred Turkish lira, and an independent worker four thousand eight hundred; these amounts are updated every year. In addition, a foreigner found working without a permit is reported to the Ministry of Interior for deportation under art. 23(8) of Law No. 6735. The Law regulates the report at this stage; a deportation decision is a separate assessment (this article’s view).
  - Q: Where and within what time limit can a fine for employing a foreigner without a permit be challenged in Türkiye?
    A: Under art. 27 of the Misdemeanours Law, an application against an administrative fine may be made to the criminal court of peace within fifteen days at the latest of notification or announcement; if the period is missed, the decision becomes final. In the Court of Cassation decisions discussed, applications against fines under Law No. 6735 were examined by criminal judgeships of peace. If a decision within the jurisdiction of the administrative courts was given in the same act, art. 27(8) sends the fine to the administrative courts together with that act.

### Tax Law

- [Company Tax Debt in Türkiye: Shareholder and Legal Representative Liability (Law 6183 Art. 35), Payment Orders](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/company-tax-debt-shareholder-legal-representative-liability): Under Turkish law, the shareholder's proportional liability and the legal representative's duty-based liability for a limited company's unpaid tax debt, the order of enforcement, the relevant period… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/sirket-vergi-borcundan-ortak-ve-kanuni-temsilcinin-sorumlulugu-odeme-emri
  - Q: Up to what amount is a shareholder liable for a limited company’s tax debt in Türkiye?
    A: A limited company shareholder is liable, in proportion to its capital share, for tax debts that cannot be collected from the company or are understood to be uncollectable (Law No. 6183 art. 35). In a 2025 decision, the Council of State Assembly of Tax Law Chambers characterised this liability as secondary and strict. The transferor and transferee of a share are jointly and severally liable for debts relating to the period before the transfer.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a company’s tax debt first claimed from the company or from the shareholder or legal representative?
    A: It is first claimed from the company. The Constitutional Court has stated that the legal representative’s liability is secondary and that the public receivable must first be sought to be collected from the legal person. Decisions given or upheld by the Council of State Assembly of Tax Law Chambers have required due service of a payment order on the company followed by an adequate asset search.
  - Q: Can a limited company shareholder who is not a manager be held liable for the company’s tax debt in Türkiye?
    A: Yes, as a rule. Law No. 6183 art. 35 does not tie the shareholder’s liability to the capacity of manager; the Council of State Assembly of Tax Law Chambers has stated that no condition other than being a shareholder is required. The liability is limited to debts of the shareholding period that cannot be collected from the company, in proportion to the capital share.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a limited company shareholder who transferred its shares liable for a tax penalty imposed after the transfer?
    A: It depends on the circumstances. The Council of State Assembly of Tax Law Chambers has accepted that liability for the taxes of the shareholding period continues after the transfer. In one decision, however, it held by majority that a former shareholder cannot be held liable for a tax loss penalty imposed for a breach of voluntary disclosure committed after the shareholding ended.
  - Q: What is the time limit for an action against a tax payment order issued to a shareholder or legal representative in Türkiye?
    A: The time limit is 15 days from service of the payment order (Law No. 6183 art. 58). Because the statutory term “objection commission” means the tax court and “objection” means filing an action, the action is filed with the tax court (Law No. 2576 art. 13). If only part of the debt is challenged, that part and its amount must be clearly indicated.
- [Tax Assessment Limitation in Türkiye: How Many Years Back Can the Tax Office Go?](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/tax-assessment-limitation-period-turkiye): Under Turkish law: the five-year period within which the tax office may assess tax and penalties for past years, when it starts, the service requirement, electronic notification, suspension before… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-24. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tarh-zamanasimi-vergi-idaresi-kac-yil-geriye-gidebilir
  - Q: How many years back can the tax administration in Türkiye assess tax?
    A: Under Turkish law, a tax must be both assessed and served on the taxpayer within five years from the beginning of the year following the calendar year in which the tax receivable arose (Tax Procedure Law art. 114). A tax not served within that period becomes time-barred. If the case is referred to the assessment commission, the period may be suspended for at most one year.
  - Q: When does the limitation period for assessing Turkish taxes for the year 2021 end?
    A: For tax receivables that arose in 2021, the period starts on 01.01.2022 and ends on 31.12.2026. The assessment notice must be served on the taxpayer by that date. For periods referred to the assessment commission, the suspended time is also taken into account.
  - Q: Does a tax audit in Türkiye suspend the limitation period for tax assessment?
    A: No. Tax Procedure Law art. 114 lists only the tax office’s application to the assessment commission for an estimate of the tax base as a ground suspending limitation. The other suspension grounds in the Law (force majeure, art. 15; a mutual agreement application, additional art. 18) do not cover a tax audit either. If the audit does not result in assessment and service within the period, the tax becomes time-barred; the Constitutional Court has also stated that no tax audit can be carried out once the period has passed.
  - Q: On what date is a Turkish tax assessment notice sent by electronic notification deemed served?
    A: Electronic notification is deemed made at the end of the fifth day following the date it is transmitted to the addressee through the system (Tax Procedure Law art. 107/A). For example, a notice transmitted on 26 December is deemed served at the end of 31 December, and one transmitted on 28 December on 2 January of the following year.
  - Q: How long does the Turkish tax office have to collect a tax after it has been assessed?
    A: For a final tax, the limitation period for collection is five years from the beginning of the year following the calendar year in which the due date falls (Law No. 6183 art. 102). Events such as payment, attachment, service of a payment order or placing the debt on a payment plan interrupt the period, and a new five-year period starts from the beginning of the following year (art. 103).
  - Q: Does the taxpayer have to raise tax limitation separately in Türkiye?
    A: No. Under Tax Procedure Law art. 113, limitation takes effect regardless of whether the taxpayer has applied. The Council of State Assembly of Tax Law Chambers also holds that tax courts must examine of their own motion whether the tax and penalty have become time-barred. Even so, it is useful to raise it expressly in the petition.
- [Tax Loss Penalty and Settlement in Türkiye: How a Settlement Request Affects the Time to Sue (VUK, İYUK)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/tax-loss-penalty-settlement-tax-court-deadline-turkiye): Under Turkish law, the tax loss penalty is as a rule one times the tax; post-assessment settlement is requested within thirty days of service of the tax notice and, if no settlement is reached, the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/vergi-ziyai-cezasi-uzlasma-ve-vergi-davasi-acma-suresi
  - Q: What is the rate of the tax loss penalty under Turkish law?
    A: Under Turkish law, the tax loss penalty is, as a rule, equal to one times the tax lost (Tax Procedure Law art. 344). If the loss arises from the smuggling acts in art. 359, the penalty is three times the tax, and one times for those who participate in those acts. For returns filed after the deadline, but before a tax audit begins and before referral to an assessment commission, the penalty is applied at fifty per cent. Since 2 August 2024, the penalty is increased by fifty per cent where loss is caused by activity carried on without registering as a taxpayer.
  - Q: By when must post-assessment settlement of a tax loss penalty be requested in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, post-assessment settlement must be requested within thirty days of the date of service of the tax notice (Tax Procedure Law additional art. 1). If no settlement is reached or it cannot be secured, settlement cannot be requested again for the same penalty. If no agreement was reached in pre-assessment settlement, post-assessment settlement cannot be requested after the penalty is imposed (additional art. 11). Some penalties, such as those imposed for tax loss through smuggling acts, cannot be settled at all.
  - Q: How does a settlement request affect the time limit for filing a tax action in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an action against a penalty for which settlement was requested may be filed only if no settlement is reached (Tax Procedure Law additional art. 7). If, when the record stating that no settlement was reached is served, the thirty-day period has expired or less than fifteen days remain, the period is extended to fifteen days from service of the record. If more than fifteen days remain, the original period continues. As the current text states this rule for actions against the penalty, filing the action against the principal tax within thirty days is the cautious course; this is this article’s inference.
  - Q: Can an action be brought against a settled tax loss penalty under Turkish law?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, settlement records are final; the taxpayer may not bring an action or complain to any authority about matters settled and recorded in a record (Tax Procedure Law additional art. 6). The same rule applies to an agreement reached in pre-assessment settlement (additional art. 11). In a 2025 decision, the Council of State’s 9th Chamber upheld, as to the outcome, the dismissal of a case seeking to reclaim settled and paid tax and penalty through a correction and complaint application, on the ground that no action may be brought about a settled matter.
  - Q: Can the art. 376 reduction and settlement be used together for the same tax loss penalty under Turkish law?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, no other reduction applies to settled penalties, and the settlement provisions do not apply to penalties for which the art. 376 reduction has been used (Tax Procedure Law additional art. 9). A taxpayer who has requested settlement may withdraw it until signing the record and ask for art. 376 to be applied. The art. 376 reduction requires applying within thirty days of service of the tax notice and paying the tax and the remaining half of the penalty on the due date; filing an action cancels the reduction.

### Expropriation & Zoning Law

- [How to Challenge a Zoning Fine and a Demolition Order in Türkiye: Zoning Law No. 3194 Arts. 32 and 42](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/zoning-fine-demolition-order-challenge-law-3194-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an action against a stop-work report, a demolition order and a zoning fine for an unlicensed building is, as a rule, brought within the sixty-day period in art. 7 of the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/imar-para-cezasi-ve-yikim-kararina-karsi-dava-3194-m-32-42
  - Q: What is the time limit for bringing an action in the administrative court against a zoning fine and a demolition order in Türkiye?
    A: Because the Zoning Law sets no separate period, the sixty-day period in art. 7 of the Administrative Procedure Law applies as a rule under Turkish law; it starts on the day after written notification. In one decision the Council of State’s 6th Chamber ran the period against a council decision containing the demolition and the fine from its proper notification, and the period against the stop-work report from the date the report was posted on the building. Whether notification was proper is examined separately in each file.
  - Q: Is a separate action needed against the stop-work report (yapı tatil tutanağı) under Turkish zoning law?
    A: The Law provides that the stop-work report is deemed notified to the building owner when it is posted at the building site (Law No. 3194 art. 32/2). In one decision the Council of State’s 6th Chamber ran the time limit against the report from that posting date and held that the part of an action filed later should have been dismissed as time-barred. In this article’s view it is therefore safer to challenge the report within sixty days of its posting, together with the council decision where needed.
  - Q: Can a zoning fine in Türkiye be imposed on the owner when a tenant built the unlicensed structure?
    A: According to the Constitutional Court and the Council of State’s Assembly of Administrative Law Chambers, the “owner of the building” in art. 42 of Law No. 3194 is the person who carried out or commissioned the construction, whether or not they own the land; a tenant falls within this. The administration may not fine the registered owner without investigating who built the unlawful structure. The Assembly found no breach of this principle, however, where the fine was imposed on an owner named as building owner in the permit who took part in the construction by consenting to it.
  - Q: How is a zoning fine calculated under art. 42 of Zoning Law No. 3194 in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, the base fine is the unlawful area in square metres multiplied by the unit amount set in the Law for the building’s class and group; these are the amounts in the statutory text and are increased every year by the revaluation rate. Where the breach cannot be measured by area, twenty per cent of the cost under the unit price list is used. Each aggravating factor, such as being unlicensed (180 per cent) or being in an area with an implementation zoning plan (20 per cent), is calculated separately on the base fine and added. An additional fine equal to the unlawful area multiplied by the minimum square-metre value for property tax is also imposed.
  - Q: Can a criminal case also be brought under art. 184 of the Turkish Criminal Code for an unlicensed building in Türkiye?
    A: Yes. A person who builds or has built a building without a permit or contrary to the permit faces imprisonment of one to five years; as a rule this applies within municipal boundaries or in areas under a special zoning regime. If the building is brought into line with the zoning plan and the permit, no public prosecution is brought and a pending one is dropped. A zoning fine collected is refunded without interest to a person convicted for the same act (Law No. 3194 art. 42).
- [Zoning Readjustment under Art. 18 of Law No. 3194 in Türkiye: Contribution Share, Objection, Annulment Action](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/zoning-readjustment-article-18-law-3194-turkiye): Under article 18 of the Zoning Law (Law No. 3194), a municipality in Türkiye may merge and re-parcel land and plots without the owners’ consent and may deduct up to forty-five per cent of the… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/imar-uygulamasi-18-madde-duzenleme-ortaklik-payi-ve-parselasyona-itiraz
  - Q: Under Turkish law, how much land can be deducted as the readjustment contribution share (DOP) in a zoning readjustment under article 18 of the Zoning Law?
    A: Under article 18(2) of the Zoning Law (Law No. 3194), the readjustment contribution share may not exceed forty-five per cent of the pre-readjustment area of the land and plots included in the readjustment. As a rule, the share is taken from a parcel only once; where a plan decision increases building conditions and population, the rate of the first readjustment may be topped up to 45% provided that the parcel’s value does not fall. If public service areas exceed that limit, the excess is transferred to the public by the methods in article 13 of the Law, for example by expropriation.
  - Q: How is an objection made during the posting period against a parcellation plan (article 18 readjustment) in Türkiye?
    A: Article 19 of the Zoning Law states that a parcellation plan is posted at the relevant administration for one month and becomes final at the end of that period; the objection procedure is set out in the Regulation on Land and Plot Readjustment. Under article 26 of the Regulation, amended on 11/9/2025, the posting period is thirty days, owners are notified via e-Government and objections may also be filed through the PARSİD system. The administration assesses the objection; if it is rejected, the plan becomes final by a reasoned administrative decision.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, when must an action for annulment be brought against a zoning readjustment (parcellation act)?
    A: The time limit for an action before an administrative court in Türkiye is sixty days unless a special law provides otherwise (Administrative Procedure Law art. 7). In a 2017 posting, the Council of State’s 6th Chamber held that where an objection is made during the posting period and the administration does not reply within the sixty-day period then in force, the action must be brought within the second sixty-day period following the last day of the posting, and that a later rejection letter does not start a new period. Since 14/7/2021 the reply period under article 11 has been thirty days; the effect of the 2025 amendment to the Regulation was not addressed in the decisions reviewed. The period should therefore be counted from the earliest possible starting point; this is the article’s own inference.
  - Q: In a zoning readjustment in Türkiye, must the new parcel be allocated where the old parcel was located?
    A: As a rule, yes. Under article 18 of the Zoning Law, parcels are allocated first from where the property was located and, if that is not possible, from the nearest equivalent area. The Council of State’s Plenary Session of Administrative Law Chambers found it contrary to the legislation to allocate a share in another block where no legal, physical or technical necessity prevented allocation at the original location. Where, for example, the parcel falls on a public service area or is too small to form a building parcel on its own, allocation elsewhere or as a shared parcel is possible.
  - Q: Under Turkish law, if a court annuls a zoning readjustment, is the land returned to the original parcels?
    A: Under article 37 of the Regulation on Land and Plot Readjustment, if the parcellation plan or its approval is annulled, as a rule a reversal is carried out and the original (root) parcels are restored; where the annulment concerns only a specific parcel, a correction may be made for the parcels concerned. The Council of State’s 6th Chamber stated that reversal must be applied where there is a general ground of annulment. If returning to the root parcels is legally or physically impossible, article 18 of the Law provides for a new readjustment on the registered zoning parcels, failing which allocation of a suitable site or payment of the market value.

### Competition Law

- [Challenging Competition Board Decisions in Türkiye: Administrative Fines, Competent Court and Time Limits](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/competition-board-fines-judicial-review-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an action against an administrative sanction decision of the Competition Board is brought before the competent administrative court; the time limit is sixty days from notification… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/rekabet-kurulu-kararlarina-karsi-dava-ve-idari-para-cezasi
  - Q: Which court hears an action against a Competition Board decision in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an action against an administrative sanction decision of the Competition Board is brought before the competent administrative court (Law No. 4054 art. 55/1). The Law does not name the court. Under art. 32 of the Administrative Procedure Law, the competent court is the administrative court of the place where the authority that took the act is located; since the seat of the Competition Authority is in Ankara (art. 20), this rule points to the Ankara administrative courts. This conclusion rests on reading the two provisions together.
  - Q: What is the time limit for bringing an action against a Competition Board decision under Turkish law?
    A: Since art. 55 of Law No. 4054, which governs judicial review, sets no separate period, the general sixty-day time limit for administrative courts applies (Administrative Procedure Law art. 7). In Competition Board decisions, time limits start from the date on which the reasoned decision is notified to the parties (Law No. 4054 art. 54); the period runs from the day after notification. The thirty-day period of the expedited procedure does not apply to these actions, because Board decisions are not on the list in art. 20/A of the Administrative Procedure Law.
  - Q: Does bringing an action against a Competition Board fine suspend collection of the fine in Türkiye?
    A: No. Under Turkish law, applying to the courts against Board decisions does not suspend the implementation of the decisions or the pursuit and collection of administrative fines (Law No. 4054 art. 55/2). To suspend collection, a stay of execution must be requested from the court; the court may grant it where irreparable or hardly reparable harm and manifest unlawfulness exist together, and as a rule requires security (Administrative Procedure Law art. 27).
  - Q: Can an undertaking that settles with the Competition Board still challenge the fine in court under Turkish law?
    A: As a rule, no. Under Turkish law, a reduction of up to twenty-five per cent may be applied to the administrative fine as a result of the settlement procedure; if the process ends in settlement, the administrative fine and the matters set out in the settlement text cannot be challenged in court by the settling parties (Law No. 4054 art. 43). Settlement is possible, until the investigation report is notified, with an undertaking that accepts the existence and scope of the infringement. The other procedures and principles of settlement are set by a regulation issued by the Board.
  - Q: Does the Competition Board need a judge’s decision for an on-site inspection in Türkiye?
    A: Under art. 15 of Law No. 4054, the Board may carry out on-site inspections where it deems necessary; a decision of a criminal judge of peace is required only where the inspection is obstructed or likely to be obstructed. In 2023 the Constitutional Court held, in an individual application, that an on-site inspection carried out without a judge’s decision violated the right to inviolability of domicile. In 2025, however, the Court rejected by majority the request to annul the phrase “where it deems necessary” in the same article. The article is in force.
- [Competition Damages in Türkiye: Treble Damages, Proof, Board Decisions, Limitation (Law No. 4054 Arts. 56-59)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/competition-law-damages-action-treble-damages-turkiye): Under Turkish law, buyers and competitors harmed by an anti-competitive agreement or an abuse of dominance may claim damages under arts. 56–59 of Law No. 4054: invalidity, calculation of loss, treble… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/rekabet-ihlalinden-dogan-tazminat-davasi-uc-kat-tazminat
  - Q: Are treble damages always awarded in a competition law damages action under Turkish law?
    A: No. Under art. 58(2) of Law No. 4054, the damage must result from an agreement or decision of the parties or from their gross negligence, and the injured party must request it; the judge “may” award treble damages, so the court has discretion. In a 2023 decision the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation rejected an objection on this point because the reasons for the treble award had been stated in the judgment. In this article’s assessment, a treble award without reasons may be challenged on appeal.
  - Q: Must a Competition Board decision be final before a competition damages action is filed in Türkiye?
    A: In a 2016 decision the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that finality of the Competition Board decision is not a precondition for the action and required the outcome of the Board proceedings and the objection to be treated as a preliminary question. However, where the regional court of appeal stated that the underlying Board decision had been found unlawful by the administrative courts, appellate judgments dismissing the action were upheld; an appellate judgment requiring a Board finding where no Board decision existed was also upheld. The Board proceedings and any annulment action should therefore be followed closely.
  - Q: What is the limitation period for a competition law damages action under Turkish law?
    A: Arts. 56–59 of Law No. 4054 contain no separate period; the general rule is the two-year and ten-year periods in art. 72 of the Turkish Code of Obligations, but if the act requires a penalty subject to a longer limitation period under criminal laws, that period applies. In a 2022 decision the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that the eight-year period in the Misdemeanours Law applies to an abuse of dominance that entails a proportional administrative fine. When the period starts is assessed separately in each case.
  - Q: Can a competitor that did not complain to the Competition Board claim damages for a competition law infringement in Türkiye?
    A: Yes, according to a 2023 decision of the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation. The Chamber stated that the legislator did not make an application to the Competition Authority a precondition for damages and that, since the Board decision finding an abuse of dominance had become final, everyone injured may claim compensation. On that ground it reversed the dismissal of the claim of a competitor company that was not listed as a complainant in the Board decision.
  - Q: Which court hears a competition damages action against a bank for cartel interest in Türkiye?
    A: In a 2017 decision the 19th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that the court may not decline jurisdiction without first obtaining the parties’ contract and determining whether it is a consumer contract; if it is, the consumer court decides the merits. However, in the cartel interest cases reviewed, appellate judgments dismissing the claims were upheld in 2024 and 2026 because the regional court of appeal stated that the underlying Board decision had been found unlawful by the administrative courts.
- [Price-Fixing, Information Exchange and Concerted Practices in Türkiye (Law No. 4054 Art. 4): Fines, Leniency](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/price-fixing-information-exchange-concerted-practice-turkiye): Under Turkish law, joint price-setting, exchange of competitively sensitive information and concerted practices between competitors are prohibited by Article 4 of Law No. 4054, and the Competition… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/rakipler-arasi-fiyat-anlasmasi-bilgi-degisimi-uyumlu-eylem-4054
  - Q: Is sharing price information with competitors in itself an infringement of Article 4 of Law No. 4054 under Turkish law?
    A: Not every exchange of information is an infringement; according to the Turkish Competition Board, information exchange may also generate efficiencies, but an exchange that reduces competitors’ uncertainty about future conduct is sufficient to show coordination. Sharing future prices, price increases, campaigns or costs with a competitor, however, may amount to a concerted practice; the Competition Board may classify information exchanges aimed at price-fixing, or facilitating the functioning of a cartel, directly as a cartel. Where the information flow is one-sided, proof of common intent may be required; in one decision reviewed, no infringement was found because this could not be proven.
  - Q: What is the presumption of concerted practice in Law No. 4054 in Türkiye, and how can an undertaking rebut it?
    A: Where an agreement cannot be proven, similarity of price changes or operational regions to those in markets where competition is restricted is a presumption of concerted practice (art. 4/3). An undertaking is relieved of liability if it proves, based on economic and rational facts, that it did not engage in a concerted practice (art. 4/4); cost, exchange-rate and demand data may be used for this purpose. In a 2025 Council of State decision, price parallelism alone, without evidence of communication or contact, was not accepted as sufficient proof.
  - Q: How large an administrative fine can the Turkish Competition Board impose for a price-fixing agreement between competitors?
    A: Under art. 16/3, the Board may impose an administrative fine of up to ten percent of annual gross revenue at the end of the financial year preceding the final decision (or, if that cannot be calculated, the financial year closest to the decision); this is an upper limit. The actual rate is calculated under the Board’s fining regulation, taking into account the starting rate, duration and aggravating and mitigating factors. Managers and employees with a decisive influence on the infringement may additionally be fined up to five percent of the undertaking’s fine.
  - Q: Can a price-fixing investigation in Türkiye be closed by offering commitments to the Competition Authority?
    A: No. Article 43/3 expressly provides that commitments are not accepted for clear and serious infringements such as price-fixing, territory or customer allocation or restriction of supply between competitors. Commitments may be used for certain other issues under Articles 4 and 6; in such cases the Board may make the commitments binding and decide not to open an investigation or to end an investigation already opened.
  - Q: What is the difference between settlement and leniency (active cooperation) in a Turkish competition investigation?
    A: In leniency (the statutory term is active cooperation), an undertaking or its manager actively helps uncover a cartel; under art. 16/6, no fine or a reduced fine may be imposed depending on the nature, effectiveness and timing of the cooperation. Settlement takes place after the investigation has started and until the investigation report is notified, with the existence and scope of the infringement admitted; the fine may be reduced by up to twenty-five percent, but the fine and the matters in the settlement text cannot be challenged in court by the settling party (art. 43).
- [Resale Price, Exclusivity and Non-Compete in Dealership Agreements in Türkiye: Five-Year Limit (Law No. 4054)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/dealership-resale-price-exclusivity-non-compete-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a fixed or minimum resale price removes a dealership agreement from the block exemption, and a non-compete obligation exceeding five years cannot benefit from it. The article… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/bayilik-sozlesmesinde-yeniden-satis-fiyati-munhasirlik-rekabet-etmeme
  - Q: Can a supplier set the dealer’s resale price in a dealership agreement in Türkiye?
    A: As a rule, no. Under Communiqué No. 2002/2, preventing the dealer from freely setting its own sale price removes the whole agreement from the block exemption. The supplier may set a maximum sale price or recommend a price, but that price must not turn into a fixed or minimum price through pressure or incentives. The Competition Board has treated practices such as a floor price list and cancelling discounts as resale price maintenance.
  - Q: What is the maximum duration of a non-compete clause in a dealership agreement under Turkish law?
    A: To benefit from the block exemption, a non-compete obligation imposed on the dealer must not exceed five years; an indefinite obligation, or one tacitly renewable beyond five years, falls outside the exemption. If the premises belong to the supplier, or the supplier has obtained a right of use over them from third parties unconnected with the dealer, the term may be linked to the period of use of the premises. A post-term non-compete is at most one year under the conditions in the Communiqué.
  - Q: Is a usufruct agreement exceeding five years valid in a fuel dealership in Türkiye?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, the part exceeding five years cannot benefit from the block exemption, as the dealership and the related usufruct and lease agreements form a single vertical agreement. Agreements made before 18.09.2005 exceeding five years benefit from the exemption until 18.09.2010 at the latest, and those made afterwards for at most five years from the date they were made. The outcome is assessed according to the contractual structure of each case.
  - Q: Can money paid under a dealership agreement contrary to competition law be claimed back under Turkish law?
    A: Under Article 56 of Law No. 4054, an agreement contrary to Article 4 is invalid and restitution of obligations already performed is subject to the rules of unjust enrichment. The Assembly of Civil Chambers has accepted that, where long-term usufruct and lease agreements made before 18.09.2005 became invalid as of 18.09.2010, benefits conferred are unwound under the rules of unjust enrichment. However, a restitution action brought while the agreement and the usufruct right were still in force was dismissed on the merits; where investment costs are claimed, proof that the investment added value to the property has also been required.
  - Q: Does granting territorial exclusivity in a dealership agreement infringe competition law in Türkiye?
    A: Not in itself. Communiqué No. 2002/2 allows restrictions on active sales into an exclusive territory or to an exclusive customer group reserved by the supplier to itself or allocated to another dealer. By contrast, prohibiting the dealer from selling to customers who approach it (passive sales) is not covered by this exception and may remove the exemption. The supplier’s market share must also not exceed 30%.

### Trademark Law

- [Copyright Infringement in Türkiye: Triple Fee, Damages, Complaint, Time Limits (Law No. 5846 Arts. 66–71, 75)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/copyright-infringement-turkiye-triple-fee-damages): Under Turkish law, a person whose copyright is infringed may seek removal and prevention of the infringement without proof of fault, up to three times the contractual fee or market value for economic… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/telif-hakki-ihlali-fsek-tazminat-uc-kat-bedel-ve-ceza-sikayeti
  - Q: Must the infringer be at fault for a right holder to claim the triple fee for copyright infringement in Türkiye?
    A: No. In a 2020 decision, the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers held that the fee under art. 68 of Law No. 5846 can be claimed without fault and that this claim differs from tort damages. By contrast, if material damages are claimed under art. 70/2, the infringer’s fault is required.
  - Q: Can a judge in Türkiye award less than three times when the triple fee under FSEK art. 68 is claimed?
    A: This is disputed under Turkish law. The Constitutional Court stated that three times is an upper limit and that the judge has discretion within that limit; in two decisions of the Court of Cassation’s 11th Civil Chamber that were read, however, regional court reasoning stating that three times must be awarded where claimed was upheld by majority; in the decisions read, the Chamber majority takes this view.
  - Q: What is the time limit for filing a criminal complaint for copyright infringement under Turkish law?
    A: The offences in art. 71 of Law No. 5846 depend on a complaint, and the complaint period is six months under art. 73 of the Turkish Criminal Code; it runs from the day the act and the offender become known. Under art. 75 of Law No. 5846, documents and evidence proving ownership of the rights must also be submitted to the Chief Public Prosecutor’s Office within this period; otherwise a decision of non-prosecution is given.
  - Q: Which court hears a copyright infringement action in Türkiye?
    A: The competent courts in cases arising from Law No. 5846 are the intellectual and industrial property civil and criminal courts referred to in art. 156/1 of the Industrial Property Code. Where these courts have not been established, the civil and criminal courts of first instance designated by the Council of Judges and Prosecutors hear the cases. The author may also bring an action for removal or prevention at his or her place of residence (Law No. 5846 art. 66).
  - Q: What happens under Turkish law if unauthorised use continues after the triple fee under FSEK art. 68 has been obtained?
    A: According to the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, the art. 68 fee forms a notional contract only for the uses in dispute that occurred before the action was filed. Uses that continue after the filing of the action or are likely to be repeated do not become lawful; an action for prevention (Law No. 5846 art. 69) and new claims are available for them. Infringements of moral rights are not covered either.
- [Design Protection in Türkiye: Registered and Unregistered Designs, Novelty, Infringement (IPC Arts. 55-81)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/design-protection-registered-unregistered-designs-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a design is protected as a registered design if registered and as an unregistered design if first made available to the public in Türkiye; in both cases it must be new and have… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/tasarim-hakkinin-korunmasi-tescilli-ve-tescilsiz-tasarim-smk
  - Q: How long is an unregistered design protected under the Industrial Property Code in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an unregistered design is protected for three years from the date on which it was first made available to the public (Law No. 6769 art. 69/2). To benefit from this protection, the design must be new, have individual character and have been made available to the public for the first time in Türkiye (arts. 55/4, 56). A registered design is protected for five years from the filing date, renewable in five-year periods up to a total of twenty-five years (art. 69/1).
  - Q: Is a design first exhibited abroad protected as an unregistered design in Türkiye?
    A: As a rule, no. IPC art. 55/4 ties unregistered design protection to the design being made available to the public for the first time in Türkiye; in decision K. 2025/7420 the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation also stated that protection begins when a design not disclosed anywhere in the world is first disclosed in Türkiye. Registration is assessed separately: a disclosure by the designer within the twelve months before filing does not affect novelty or individual character (art. 57/2).
  - Q: Does selling a design before filing an application invalidate a design registration under Turkish law?
    A: A sale counts as making the design available to the public (IPC art. 57/1). If the disclosure by the designer or a person with the designer’s consent falls within the twelve months before filing, it does not affect novelty (art. 57/2). If that period has passed, the design may lack novelty and be declared invalid (art. 77/1-a).
