In the regional court reasoning upheld by Court of Cassation decision K. 2026/5010, a lace pattern sold about twenty-six months before registration was held to lack novelty for this reason, and the first-instance court’s invalidity ruling was found correct.
  - Q: What must the claimant prove in an unregistered design infringement action in Türkiye?
    A: The claimant must show when and where the design was first made available to the public and that the other party copied it; absolute novelty is also examined by the court of its own motion, while individual character is assessed on the evidence the parties submit. An unregistered design is protected only against copying of an identical design or one that does not produce a different overall impression; an independent design by a designer who could not reasonably have known the protected design is not a copy (IPC art. 59/2). In the reasoning upheld by decision K. 2026/5010, the claim failed because the time and place of first disclosure were not shown.
  - Q: Can unfair competition rules be relied on alongside the Industrial Property Code in a design infringement case in Türkiye?
    A: In decision K. 2026/928, given by majority, the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation held that for an unregistered design protected under the IPC, the unfair competition provisions of the Turkish Commercial Code cannot also be applied to the same facts and that cumulative protection no longer applies; one judge dissented. Unregistered designs made available before the IPC entered into force on 10.01.2017 are, according to the Chamber’s decision K. 2025/7420, assessed under TCC art. 55/1-a-4.
- [Trademark Invalidity and Revocation in Türkiye: Grounds, Time Limits, Authority, Non-Use (IPC Arts. 25-26)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/trademark-invalidity-and-revocation-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a trademark invalidity action rests on an obstacle that existed at registration and is heard by a court, while revocation for grounds arising later, such as non-use, becoming a… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/markanin-hukumsuzlugu-ve-iptali-smk-25-26
  - Q: What is the difference between a trademark invalidity action and trademark revocation under Turkish law?
    A: Invalidity rests on an obstacle that existed at registration; a court decides, and the decision takes effect from the filing date of the trademark (Industrial Property Code arts. 25 and 27/1). Revocation rests on grounds arising after registration, such as non-use or the trademark becoming a common name; the Turkish Patent and Trademark Office decides on request and, as a rule, the decision takes effect from the date of the request (arts. 26 and 27/2).
  - Q: Is revocation of a trademark for non-use in Türkiye requested from the Turkish Patent and Trademark Office or from a court?
    A: Under art. 26/1 of the Industrial Property Code, revocation is decided by the Office on request. That article entered into force seven years after the Code was published; revocation actions pending before the courts on that date are concluded by the courts (art. 192/1-a, provisional art. 4). The request is filed on a signed form, with the fee, separately for each trademark (Implementing Regulation art. 30/A). The Ankara Civil Court of Intellectual and Industrial Property Rights is the competent court with jurisdiction over actions against the Office’s decisions (art. 156/2).
  - Q: How is the five-year period for non-use revocation of a trademark calculated under Turkish law, and who proves use?
    A: A trademark is revoked if, without a justified reason, it has not been put to genuine use in Türkiye within five years of registration, or if use has been interrupted for five consecutive years (art. 9/1). Genuine use between the expiry of the five years and the request prevents revocation; use in the last three months made in anticipation of the request does not count (art. 26/4). The Implementing Regulation asks the proprietor for evidence (art. 30/A-10); in decisions upheld by the Court of Cassation the burden of proof was likewise placed on the proprietor.
  - Q: Is there a time limit for bringing a trademark invalidity action in Türkiye?
    A: The text of art. 25 of the Industrial Property Code sets no general time limit for an invalidity action. However, an earlier proprietor who, while knowing or having to know that a later trademark was in use, has remained silent for five consecutive years cannot rely on its trademark as a ground of invalidity, unless the later registration was in bad faith (art. 25/6). In one decision the 11th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation treated these five years as a forfeiture period.
  - Q: Which court hears a trademark invalidity action in Türkiye, and against whom is it brought?
    A: The competent court is the civil court of intellectual and industrial property rights; where none has been established, the designated civil court of first instance hears the case (art. 156/1). The action is brought against the person recorded in the register as proprietor on the date of the action, or that person’s legal successor; the Office is not named as a party (art. 25/3). In actions brought by third parties against a right holder, the court of the defendant’s domicile has, as a rule, territorial jurisdiction (art. 156/5).

### Urban Regeneration & Construction Law

- [Contractor Delay in Flat-for-Land Construction in Türkiye: Delay Damages, Termination, Title (TCO Art. 125)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/flat-for-land-contract-delay-termination-turkiye): Under Turkish law, when the contractor delays delivery the landowner may await performance and claim delay compensation at least equal to the market rent under art. 125 of the Code of Obligations, or… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15; updated: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/kat-karsiligi-insaat-sozlesmesinde-gecikme-fesih-ve-arsa-sahibinin-haklari
  - Q: What compensation can a landowner claim under Turkish law if the contractor delays delivery under a flat-for-land construction contract?
    A: Under Turkish law, the landowner may, as a rule, await performance and claim compensation for the delay (Turkish Code of Obligations art. 125/1). According to the Court of Cassation Assembly of Civil Chambers, this requires the contractor's default, the landowner's loss and the contractor's fault. Even if the contract sets no amount, compensation may be claimed at least at the level of the monthly local market rent; if the contract sets a rent compensation, that clause applies. If a penalty for late delivery was also agreed, the creditor may claim it together with performance unless he expressly waived it or accepted performance without reservation (art. 179/2).
  - Q: Until what date is delay compensation calculated under a flat-for-land construction contract in Türkiye?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, delay compensation may as a rule be claimed from the agreed delivery date until the unit is delivered complete and fit for use, or until the landowner starts actually using it. If delivery is tied to the occupancy permit and there was no earlier actual delivery, it is calculated until the permit date. If the contractor proves actual delivery, letting or sale before the permit, it is calculated until that date.
  - Q: Can a landowner terminate a flat-for-land construction contract in Türkiye by unilateral notice?
    A: According to decisions of the Court of Cassation's 6th Civil Chamber, no: because the contract includes a promise to transfer land shares, it ends only by agreement of the parties or, where there is just cause, by a court judgment of termination. The landowner's notice of termination to the contractor still matters; the Chamber used the date of that notice when calculating the contractual penalty in a forward-looking termination.
  - Q: What is the difference between retroactive and forward-looking termination of a flat-for-land construction contract under Turkish law?
    A: In retroactive termination the parties return to their position before the contract: the landowner reclaims shares transferred as an advance, and the contractor may claim the value of lawful work. In forward-looking termination the contractor receives units or shares in proportion to the work done, and the landowner may also claim delay compensation and the penalty attached to performance. The Court of Cassation's 6th Civil Chamber holds that retroactive termination cannot be ordered once construction reaches 90 percent or more; the landowner may also seek forward-looking termination at a lower level.
  - Q: Is a flat-for-land construction contract in Türkiye invalid if it was not drawn up by a notary in official form?
    A: As a rule, yes. According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, because the contract includes a promise to transfer land shares, drawing it up by a notary in official form is a condition of validity (Turkish Civil Code art. 706). However, if the parties have performed their obligations fully or to a significant extent, or the land share has been transferred to the contractor, the lack of form cannot be invoked. Whether this exception applies depends on the facts of each case.
- [Reserve Building Area Designation Annulment (Law No. 6306): Time Limit and Stay of Execution in Türkiye](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/reserve-building-area-annulment-law-6306-turkiye): Under Turkish law, a reserve building area is an area designated by the Ministry for use in implementations under Law No. 6306; an action may be brought before the administrative courts within thirty… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/rezerv-yapi-alani-ilani-ve-iptal-davasi-6306
  - Q: What is the time limit for an action against a reserve building area designation in Türkiye?
    A: Under Turkish law, an action may be brought in the administrative courts within thirty days of notification against administrative acts taken under Law No. 6306 (art. 6/9). In a decision given by majority, which may not be final, on an urgent expropriation decision taken by Presidential decision, the Council of State’s 6th Chamber applied the general sixty-day limit because the special time limit under art. 20/A of the Administrative Procedure Law had not been stated in the act; it did not address the thirty-day limit in art. 6/9. When the period starts depends in each case on notification, publication or the date of knowledge.
  - Q: Can a stay of execution be requested in an action against a reserve building area designation in Türkiye?
    A: Yes. The original text of Law No. 6306 barred a stay of execution in actions brought under it; the Constitutional Court annulled that sentence by its 2014 decision E.2012/87, K.2014/41. Today, as a rule, a stay may be ordered under art. 27 of the Administrative Procedure Law where the act is clearly unlawful and its implementation would cause irreparable or impossible-to-remedy harm, both conditions being met together.
  - Q: What did Law No. 7471 change in the definition of a reserve building area under Law No. 6306?
    A: Law No. 7471 of 7/11/2023 removed the words “as a new settlement area” from the definition. Under Turkish law as now worded, a reserve building area is an area designated by the Ministry, at the request of TOKİ or the Administration or of its own motion, for use in implementations carried out under the Law (art. 2/1-c). In a decision recording this amendment, the Council of State’s 6th Chamber found lawful the designation of an empty parcel declared at the co-owners’ request.
  - Q: For how long can zoning and building procedures be suspended on property designated a reserve building area in Türkiye?
    A: Under art. 4/1 of Law No. 6306, the Presidency or, where it carries out the implementation, TOKİ or the Administration may temporarily suspend zoning and building procedures in reserve building areas for two years; where the implementation requires, this may be extended by one more year. The original text had no time limit; the Constitutional Court annulled the open-ended zoning ban as contrary to the right to property, and the provision was later re-enacted with these periods.
  - Q: Can urgent expropriation be carried out in a reserve building area under Turkish law?
    A: According to the 6th Chamber reasoning upheld by the Council of State’s Plenary Session of the Administrative Law Chambers, declarations concerning a reserve building area have the character of a settlement project, and urgency exists provided the other expropriation requirements are met. The decision was given by majority; the dissent argued that whether the designation rests on concrete, technical and scientific data must also be reviewed. The outcome may differ in each case depending on the reasons for the act and the documents in the file.

### Mediation & Arbitration

- [Mediation Completed Before the File Reaches the Competent Court Meets the Precondition: Court of Cassation](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/mediation-after-lack-of-jurisdiction-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, where an employment action was filed in a court lacking jurisdiction and mediation was concluded after the decision of lack of… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/gorevsiz-mahkemede-acilan-davada-sonradan-arabulucuya-basvuru
  - Q: What happens in Türkiye if an employment action is filed without applying to a mediator?
    A: Under Turkish law, if it is found that the action was filed without applying to a mediator, it is dismissed on procedural grounds for lack of a precondition without any further step (Law No. 7036 art. 3(2); Law No. 6325 art. 18/A(2)). According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, no time is given to the claimant to apply to a mediator in that case.
  - Q: Does applying to a mediator after a decision of lack of jurisdiction satisfy the mediation precondition in Türkiye?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers decision of 21.01.2026, where an action was filed in a court without subject-matter jurisdiction and the mediation was concluded before the file was registered on the docket of the competent labour court, the precondition is deemed satisfied and the competent court proceeds to the merits.
  - Q: Is an employment action dismissed immediately in Türkiye if the final mediation report is not attached?
    A: No. If a mediator was applied to but the final report was not attached, the court gives a one-week final time limit; if the report is not submitted within that time, the action is dismissed on procedural grounds without the statement of claim being served on the other side. The Assembly of Civil Chambers characterises this as a precondition that can be completed.
  - Q: Is an employer's damages claim against an employee also subject to mandatory mediation in Türkiye?
    A: Yes. Law No. 7036 art. 3(1) makes mediation a precondition for employee or employer receivables and compensation. According to the statutory reasoning quoted by the Assembly of Civil Chambers, claims in tort or unjust enrichment arising from the employment relationship are also covered; damages actions arising from work accidents and occupational diseases are excluded by art. 3(3).
  - Q: Does mandatory mediation in Türkiye affect limitation and preclusive periods?
    A: From the application to the mediation office until the final report is drawn up, limitation is suspended and preclusive periods do not run (Law No. 7036 art. 3(17)).
- [Unpaid Mediation Settlement Can Open the Way to Suing for the Whole Claim: Turkish Court of Cassation Assembly](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/unpaid-mediation-settlement-action-turkiye): Under Turkish law, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, where a mediation settlement is conditional on full payment and allows all claims to be sued for in case of non-payment, the whole… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-19. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/arabuluculuk-anlasma-belgesi-odenmezse-dava-acilabilir-mi
  - Q: What can an employee do in Türkiye if the amount agreed in mediation is not paid?
    A: Under Turkish law a settlement document signed together by the parties, their lawyers and the mediator is treated as a document equivalent to a judgment without an enforceability annotation (Law No. 6325 art. 18(4)); enforcement proceedings can be started for the unpaid part. If the settlement provides that all claims may be sued for in case of non-payment, according to the Assembly of Civil Chambers an action may also be filed for the entire claim after deducting what was paid.
  - Q: Can an action be filed in Türkiye for matters agreed in a mediation settlement?
    A: As a rule, no; Law No. 6325 art. 18(5) prohibits actions on matters agreed. In Assembly of Civil Chambers decision K. 2025/329, however, the settlement was made conditional on full and complete payment and an instalment was not paid, so it was held that the dispute had not been resolved permanently by the settlement.
  - Q: Is the action dismissed in Türkiye if the debtor pays the last instalment after the action is filed?
    A: According to the Assembly of Civil Chambers, every action is decided on the factual and legal grounds existing on the date it was filed. Payment of the last instalment after filing did not change the fact that payments were not made fully and on time, and could not justify a procedural dismissal.
  - Q: On what grounds can the invalidity of a mediation settlement document be raised under Turkish law?
    A: According to the 9th Civil Chamber of the Court of Cassation, since the settlement document is not res judicata in the substantive sense, absolute nullity, excessive advantage, mistake, fraud, duress and forgery may be raised. If there was no real dispute before mediation, the document is not a settlement within the meaning of art. 18(5). Whether invalidity must be pursued in a separate annulment action or examined as a preliminary issue in the claim itself is disputed within the Chamber.

### Mining & Energy Law

- [Mining Licence Cancellation in Türkiye: Forfeited Security and Fines (Law No. 3213 Arts. 10, 13, 17, 24)](https://capar.av.tr/en/article/mining-licence-cancellation-administrative-fine-turkiye): Under Turkish law, an action against an administrative fine imposed under the Mining Law must be brought before the administrative court within thirty days of notification, and the action does not… Jurisdiction: Türkiye (Turkish law). Published: 2026-11-15. Turkish original: https://capar.av.tr/makale/maden-ruhsatinin-iptali-teminat-irat-kaydi-ve-idari-para-cezalarina-karsi-dava
  - Q: What is the time limit under Turkish law for suing against an administrative fine imposed under the Mining Law?
    A: Under Mining Law No. 3213 art. 13, an action against these fines may be brought before the administrative court within thirty days of notification. This special limit replaces the general sixty-day limit in Law No. 2577 art. 7. The day on which electronic notification is deemed made and extensions such as the judicial recess must be calculated separately; the 8th Chamber of the Council of State made this calculation in detail in one decision.
  - Q: Does suing against a Mining Law administrative fine in Türkiye stop collection of the fine?
    A: No. Law No. 3213 art. 13 expressly states that recourse to judicial remedies against administrative fines does not stop enforcement and collection. The fine is payable within one month of notification. Bringing an action alone is not enough to stop collection; a separate decision of the court is needed. This article does not examine the conditions for a stay of execution.
  - Q: Within what time limit and before which court is a mining licence cancellation challenged in Türkiye?
    A: Cancellation of a licence is an administrative act and is challenged by an action for annulment before the administrative court. Since no time limit specific to licence cancellation was found in the Mining Law articles examined for this article, the general sixty-day limit in Law No. 2577 art. 7 applies as a rule; this is the article’s own inference. If the act also contains an administrative fine, it is safer to rely on the thirty-day limit. The competent court is, as a rule, the administrative court where the authority that made the act is located (Law No. 2577 art. 32).
  - Q: Under Turkish law, is a mining licence cancelled immediately if the licence fee is not paid by the end of January?
    A: As a rule, no. Under the current text of Law No. 3213 art. 13, the portion unpaid at the end of January may be paid by the last day of June of that year, increased at the late-payment surcharge rate; if this is not done, the licence is cancelled without separate notice. In its decision of 27.02.2025, the 8th Chamber of the Council of State upheld a judgment finding a cancellation made before the end of June unlawful.
  - Q: When is a mining licence security deposit recorded as revenue under Turkish law today?
    A: Law No. 6592 (2015) abolished the security deposit and fee practice and introduced the licence fee; the current art. 13 also provides for a rehabilitation fee. Today Law No. 3213 art. 17 provides for the security deposit to be recorded as revenue for exploration licences where activity reports are not submitted, minimum activities are not carried out or the investment is not realised. For past security deposits that could not be recorded as revenue, provisional art. 21 provides for an administrative fine equal to that security deposit revenue.
  - Q: Is a fine imposed under the Turkish Mining Law if the permits required for the operating permit are not obtained in time?
    A: Under Law No. 3213 art. 24, if permits such as the environmental impact assessment decision, the ownership permit and the workplace opening and operating licence are not submitted within three years of the operating licence entering into force, an administrative fine is imposed each year. In its decision of 16.01.2025 the Constitutional Court did not find the rule unconstitutional, but stated that the fine cannot be applied where no fault can be attributed to the licence holder for the delay.
